## eami-agent
**Purpose:** Lightweight endpoint scanner (Windows primary, macOS/Linux ports per ADR-015). Runs on interval, detects AI tooling footprint, ships JSON reports to the collector.
**Status:** STABLE (core), WORKING-BUT-FRAGILE (Linux DNS correlation, macOS — no test hardware).
**Key files:** `cmd/agent/main.go`; `internal/detection/{ai_apps,ai_processes,browser,cloud_clients,file_changes,gpu,mcp_servers,models,network_activity,nodejs_ai,python_envs,scheduled_tasks}/`; `internal/{payload,collector,config,service}/`; `internal/{nativemsg,nmregister,nmlauncher}/` (B-034, native messaging host).
**Interfaces:** `POST <collector-url>/ingest` — gzipped JSON `Report{}` (endpoint id, hostname, per-detection-domain findings), API-key header auth. `eami-agent --native-messaging-host` (B-034) — browser native-messaging host mode, stdin/stdout, no live caller yet (no B1 extension).
**Data owned:** none (stateless scanner; report shape defined by `internal/payload`).
**Test coverage:** 5 `_test.go` files vs 55 source files (~11:1 file ratio). Not executed this session.
**Known limitations:**
- `internal/detection/network_activity/scanner_linux.go:181` — `linuxDNSCache()` runs `resolvectl statistics` but discards output, always returns `nil` (comment: "resolvectl doesn't expose individual hostnames"). DNS-hit correlation unimplemented on Linux; rest of network scanner (proc-based connection mapping) is real.
- macOS build path exists (ADR-015) but has never run on real macOS hardware — `.pkg` install/uninstall smoke test deferred per `tasks/TASK-054-results.md`.
- Scheduled-tasks scanner exists (`internal/detection/scheduled_tasks/`) despite `ROADMAP.md` listing it as "deferred to v1.1" — it shipped early.

**Interfaces — B-034 (2026-07-26), native-messaging host for real-time paste-event relay:** new `eami-agent --native-messaging-host` mode so a future browser extension (B1, not built yet) can hand off captured paste events in real time over stdin/stdout, using the browser's standard length-prefixed-JSON native messaging protocol — not a network port (smaller attack surface, no listening socket, standard per-browser registration). Zero changes to any existing scanner or the poll loop: this mode is a wholly separate process invocation (`main.go` dispatches straight to it and returns before `runLoop` is ever reached).
**Identity finding that shaped the design:** investigated where `eami-agent` stores its registered identity, per this task's explicit instruction to verify rather than assume — found `eami-agent` has **no `org_id` anywhere** (config, report payload, or remote-config response), only `agent_id` (local config, defaults to hostname). `org_id` is resolved entirely server-side today. Combined with `eami-collector`/`eami-api`/B-032's ingestion endpoint all being frozen for this task, paste events are forwarded via the **existing** `collector.Sender.Send()` — immediately, out-of-band from the 5-minute poll cycle, not invented as a new direct link to the backend. Today this lands in the existing agent-report pipeline's raw JSON blob (not literally in B-032's `paste_events` table yet) — completing that server-side wiring is logged as a new follow-up, B-035 (see `BACKLOG.md`), not silently left undone.
**New packages:**
- `internal/nativemsg` — the protocol: `readMessage`/`writeMessage` (4-byte little-endian length prefix + JSON, capped at 1MB — rejected before any allocation, guarding against a hostile/malformed length value), `PasteEventMessage` (no field can carry raw pasted text, mirrors B-032's guarantee), `RunHost` (the message loop, per-message panic recovery via `handleOne` so one bad message can't crash the process — B-027's discipline, including the stack-trace-in-log convention that a code-review pass caught missing from the first version and this session fixed).
- `internal/nmregister` — Windows: writes a native-messaging manifest and points Chrome's/Edge's `NativeMessagingHosts` registry keys at it. `AllowedExtensionID` is a literal `PLACEHOLDER_EXTENSION_ID_REPLACE_AFTER_B1` — there is no B1 yet, so this manifest cannot be matched by any real extension origin until that placeholder is replaced, the correct default for a not-yet-existing caller. Non-Windows: a stub directing to the shell-script-based registration instead (no registry involved on those platforms).
- `internal/nmlauncher` — defense-in-depth parent-process verification (see security-review section below).
**Real bug caught and fixed by this session's own testing, before it could ever reach a real browser:** the native-messaging manifest's `"path"` originally pointed straight at `eami-agent.exe`/`eami-agent`. Chrome/Edge's manifest schema has no `args` field — the browser execs whatever `path` names with only its own fixed arguments, never anything the manifest supplies — so a real browser would have launched the real binary with **no** `--native-messaging-host` flag at all, silently falling through to the ordinary poll loop instead of acting as a host. Caught by the mandatory code-review pass, not by any test in the original changeset (every test explicitly passed the flag on the command line itself, masking the gap). Fixed with a launcher indirection: `nmregister.Install` (Windows) and the Linux/macOS postinstall scripts now create a **hard link** (not a copy — same file, zero extra disk space, can't fall out of sync on upgrade) named `eami-agent-nmhost`/`eami-agent-nmhost.exe` next to the real binary, and the manifest's `"path"` points at that name instead. `cmd/agent/main.go`'s `invokedAsNativeMessagingLauncher()` detects invocation under that name (checking both `os.Args[0]` and the resolved `os.Executable()` path) and behaves exactly as if `--native-messaging-host` had been passed explicitly. Live-verified end-to-end after the fix: built the real binary, ran `--register-native-messaging install`, confirmed the hard link + manifest + both registry keys are correct, then invoked `eami-agent-nmhost.exe` with **no explicit flag** and confirmed it entered host mode, correctly parsed a real length-prefixed message, and forwarded it to a mock collector — proving the actual real-world browser-invocation path, not just the flag-based shortcut every automated test uses.
**Second real bug caught by this session's own testing:** adding the parent-process-check log line (below) surfaced that `main.go`'s logger wrote to `os.Stdout` — the exact stream the native messaging protocol uses for length-prefixed framing. Any log line (a warning, an error, or this new info line) interleaved with protocol bytes desyncs the browser's parsing with no way for the reader to distinguish log text from a length prefix. This was a **latent bug in the original implementation too** (RunHost's own `log.Warn`/`log.Error` calls on malformed input or a forwarding failure), just not triggered by earlier tests, which happened not to exercise those exact log-emitting paths at the subprocess level. Fixed: `main.go` now routes all logging to `os.Stderr` specifically in `--native-messaging-host` mode (every other mode keeps logging to stdout unchanged). New regression test (`TestNativeMessagingHost_LoggingNeverCorruptsStdout`) sends a malformed length prefix (the exact case that logs a warning) and asserts stdout still parses as a clean, correctly-framed response while the warning appears on stderr.
**Security review finding, fixed (confused-deputy telemetry forgery):** the mandatory security review found that nothing prevented an arbitrary local process from invoking `--native-messaging-host` directly and having it forward fabricated paste-event data using the agent's own already-configured collector API key — native messaging's `allowed_origins` restriction is enforced by the browser before it launches the host, not by the host process itself, so it provides no protection against direct invocation of the binary. Fixed with `internal/nmlauncher.VerifyLaunchedByBrowser()`: checks the immediate parent process (Windows via a `CreateToolhelp32Snapshot` walk — live-verified against this machine's real process tree, correctly resolving `go.exe` as the parent during `go test`; Linux via `/proc/<ppid>/exe`; macOS via `ps -o comm=` — neither of the latter two exercised on a live host this session, this machine is Windows). Policy: if the parent can't be determined at all, **fails open** with a loud warning (an inconclusive check must not break the feature entirely); if it can be determined and isn't a recognized browser, **fails closed**. `EAMI_NM_SKIP_PARENT_CHECK=1` is a documented operator override (same fail-closed-with-escape-hatch convention as B-023's SSRF guard, B-025's secret validation) — explicitly **not** a bulletproof fix (a capable local attacker could rename their process or launch as a child of a real browser), but a meaningful defense-in-depth improvement over no check at all.
**Test coverage — B-034:** `internal/nativemsg/nativemsg_test.go` (17 tests: message framing edge cases, validation, `RunHost`'s panic-recovery/malformed-input/EOF handling via a fake `Sender`), `internal/nmlauncher/nmlauncher_test.go` (browser-name matching, fail-open/fail-closed/skip-env-var policy via an injectable `parentProcessNameFunc` seam) + `parentproc_windows_test.go` (real, live Windows toolhelp lookup — not seam-injected), `cmd/agent/main_integration_test.go` (4 tests: builds the real binary, drives it as a genuine subprocess over real stdin/stdout pipes with a mock HTTP collector — end-to-end message parsing + forwarding, garbage input doesn't crash the process, and the stdout-purity regression above). **Verified 2026-07-26 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean on native Windows and cross-compiled for `linux/amd64` and `darwin/arm64`; `go test ./...` clean, all packages.** Both reviewer and security subagent passes ran (mandatory, new local IPC surface); both real findings above were fixed and re-verified live, not just patched and assumed.
**Known limitations:** ~~no B1 (browser extension) exists yet~~ — **B1 now exists, see the new `eami-browser-extension` section below.** `nmregister.AllowedExtensionID` is updated with B1's real, stable ID (`ngmdfnecljeoleiancdedbmhjdihaoaa`) — no longer a placeholder. Linux/macOS registration (`postinstall.sh`/`postinstall` manifest+launcher creation, `preremove.sh`/`uninstall.sh` cleanup) and the corresponding `/proc`/`ps`-based parent-process lookups are implemented but still not exercised on a live Linux/macOS host as of B1 either (this development machine remains Windows-only). ~~Completing the server-side wiring so a paste event actually lands in B-032's `paste_events` table... still requires `eami-collector`/`eami-api` changes... tracked as B-035, still open.~~ **B-035 is now done (2026-08-04, see the `eami-api` section) — and required zero changes to `eami-agent` or `eami-collector` after all**, since both already forwarded paste events through the existing pipeline unmodified; the fix was entirely server-side in `eami-api`.
**CI-failure correction (2026-07-26):** the `Product.wxs` `CustomAction` wiring above was originally shipped with two real WiX v4 syntax bugs, both caught by CI's "Build MSI" step (not caught locally at the time — no .NET SDK was available in this environment, only the runtime): (1) the added comment block used this session's own "--" em-dash convention, invalid inside XML comments (`WIX0104`); (2) `<InstallExecuteSequence>`'s two `<Custom>` elements used WiX v3-style inner-text conditions, but WiX v4 requires a `Condition="..."` attribute instead (`WIX0400`) — confirmed authoritatively from WiX's own compiler source (`ParseSequenceElement`/`InnerTextDisallowed` in `wixtoolset/wix`'s `Compiler_Package.cs`), not guessed. Both fixed. **This time actually verified, not just fixed and re-flagged as untested:** installed a real .NET SDK + WiX 4.0.6 locally (`winget`/`dotnet tool install`, matching CI's exact WiX version), reproduced both CI errors locally byte-for-byte before fixing either, then confirmed clean with both a direct `wix build` and the project's real `installer/build.ps1` script (CI's actual invocation path) against a real cross-compiled Windows binary — producing a genuine, valid, installable 2.5MB MSI. The "not compiled/run through a live wix build" limitation from B-034's original entry no longer applies to the `CustomAction`/`InstallExecuteSequence` XML itself (still not run through a live `msiexec` install/uninstall cycle, which would need real elevation/service-install side effects beyond what was asked here). See `BACKLOG.md`'s B-034 CI-failure correction entry for the full writeup.
**Manual-testing bug found and fixed (2026-08-03), `internal/nmlauncher` parent-check too strict:** B1's manual test checklist (step 5, `flush()`) failed against a real browser with Chrome's generic `"Error when communicating with the native messaging host."` after B0 was freshly re-registered on this machine. Root-caused by directly invoking the real registered `eami-agent-nmhost.exe` launcher binary from a non-browser parent process and reproducing the identical failure signature: the process exits immediately (code 1) having written zero bytes to stdout — exactly how Chrome surfaces a native-messaging host that failed before sending any response. That traced to `nmlauncher.VerifyLaunchedByBrowser()`'s fail-CLOSED branch: it only allow-lists a short hardcoded set of Chrome/Edge process names and refuses everything else, including — evidently — the user's own real browser invocation on this machine (the exact process-tree shape wasn't chased further, to avoid repeating the earlier chromedp/elevated-shell/`taskkill` incident from B1's own build). **Fixed:** the "parent determined but not recognized" branch now fails OPEN with a loud `WARN` log instead of refusing outright, matching the policy this package already used for "parent couldn't be determined at all" — a hardcoded browser-name allowlist can't realistically stay complete against every Chromium-family browser/OS/version combination in the field, and this check was always documented as best-effort defense-in-depth, not a hard guarantee; continuing to hard-block on an unrecognized name is no longer justified now that it's demonstrated to break the primary feature it exists to protect. `EAMI_NM_SKIP_PARENT_CHECK=1` still exists for operators who want to silence the warning, but is no longer load-bearing for the allow/block decision. `Result` gained a `Warn bool` field so callers (`main.go`) can log fail-open-with-reduced-assurance outcomes at `WARN` instead of `INFO`. Re-verified end-to-end after the fix (not just unit-tested): rebuilt `eami-agent.exe`, recreated the hard-linked launcher in the live local test registration, then drove a real length-prefixed native-messaging protocol round trip against it from a deliberately non-browser parent (the exact failure condition) — got `{"status":"ok"}` back plus a `WARN` log instead of a refusal, and confirmed the event landed in `endpoint_reports` in the real `docker compose` Postgres stack. `nmlauncher_test.go`'s `TestVerifyLaunchedByBrowser_UnrecognizedParent_Blocked` renamed to `..._FailsOpenWithWarning` and updated to match. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean across `eami-agent`. **Confirmed working by the user's own live retest (2026-08-03):** all 5 steps of B1's `MANUAL_TESTING.md` checklist passed on a real Chrome/Edge install after this fix, including `flush()` sending and the event landing in `endpoint_reports` — not just this session's own synthetic protocol round trip. **This fail-open behavior is explicitly accepted as a dev-testing convenience only, not the final production security posture** — see `BACKLOG.md`'s **B-037** for the required follow-up (make the allowlist genuinely reliable across real browser installs/OS versions, then restore fail-closed enforcement) before any real customer install. ~~fail-open behavior is a dev-testing convenience only, not the final production security posture~~ — **B-037 done, see immediately below.**

**B-037 (2026-08-04), fail-closed restored with a genuinely researched allowlist:** closes the gap flagged directly above. **Investigated the root cause rather than guessing, per the task brief**, and one hypothesis was directly refuted: research (Chromium's own release-channel documentation) confirmed Chrome and Edge use the **identical executable name across every channel on Windows** — `chrome.exe`/`msedge.exe` cover stable/beta/dev/canary alike, cross-checked against this real machine's actual running processes at the time. The Windows entries in `knownBrowserExecutables` were never actually incomplete; that specific theory from the original incident does not hold. Also researched Chrome's native-messaging-host launch mechanism directly and confirmed that since Chromium 113 (2023), an `.exe` host is launched directly by the browser with no `cmd.exe` intermediary, ruling out an intermediary-process theory for any reasonably current install. Real gaps *were* found and closed: Linux and macOS both use distinct per-channel process/bundle names (`google-chrome-stable`/`-beta`/`-unstable`, `microsoft-edge-stable`/`-beta`/`-dev` on Linux; separate `.app` bundles like `Google Chrome Beta`/`Canary` and `Microsoft Edge Beta`/`Canary` on macOS) — none of these were on the list before, now added (researched, not exercised on a live Linux/macOS host, flagged per this package's existing per-OS honesty convention). Also added `msedgewebview2`, a real Edge-family process confirmed running on this machine.
**The exact original failure was never captured and cannot be retroactively diagnosed** — stated plainly as an open question, not hidden behind "fixed it anyway." Leading theory, given every other explanation was specifically checked and ruled out: the user's real default browser is most likely a different Chromium-family browser (Brave/Opera/Vivaldi/etc.), not Chrome or Edge. **Explicit scope decision, confirmed with the user before building** (not assumed): kept the allowlist to Chrome/Edge only, matching this package's existing browser-detection scope and this task's own acceptance criteria — did not widen to other Chromium-family browsers, so a genuinely different browser would still fail closed; `EAMI_NM_SKIP_PARENT_CHECK=1` remains the documented escape hatch for that case. The logged `Reason` string still names the actual detected parent on every refusal, so if this recurs, checking that name against the theory above is the first step, not re-diagnosing from scratch.
**The fix itself:** `VerifyLaunchedByBrowser`'s "parent determined but not recognized" branch is fail-CLOSED again (`Allowed: false`), reverting the dev-testing-only weakening. "Parent could not be determined at all" keeps its original fail-open-with-warning behavior unchanged — confirmed, not just carried over, that this asymmetry (confident non-browser → refuse; genuinely inconclusive → don't hard-break the feature) was the correct original design, and only the second branch was ever wrong to weaken. `EAMI_NM_SKIP_PARENT_CHECK=1` is unchanged.
**Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory (org/trust-boundary-adjacent security control):** code review caught a real **Medium** (4 of the new allowlist entries — `google chrome dev`, `microsoft edge dev`, `chromium-browser`, bare `microsoft-edge` — had no corresponding test case) and a **Low** (the `chromium` entry was grouped under a "Windows" comment header despite being cross-platform) — both fixed. Security review found no High/Medium issues (confirmed the restored fail-closed logic has no bypass, confirmed `isKnownBrowser`'s exact-match normalization can't be tricked by whitespace/path/homoglyph games, confirmed the Chrome/Edge-only scope decision is consistently documented with no overclaiming elsewhere) and one **Low** (the `msedgewebview2` entry's doc comment overclaimed verification of WebView2's own origin-enforcement behavior) — fixed by rewording the comment to state plainly that this entry carries the same name-recognition-only trust level as every other list entry, no stronger.
**Test coverage:** `nmlauncher_test.go`'s `TestVerifyLaunchedByBrowser_UnrecognizedParent_FailsOpenWithWarning` reverted to `..._Blocked`, now asserting `Allowed: false`/`Warn: false`. `TestIsKnownBrowser`'s table extended to cover every new allowlist entry across all three platforms, plus an explicit `brave.exe → false` case documenting the scope decision as a tested contract, not just a comment. `TestVerifyLaunchedByBrowser_InconclusiveCheck_FailsOpen` and `TestVerifyLaunchedByBrowser_SkipEnvVar_AlwaysAllowed` unchanged (still correct). **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-agent` module.** Re-verified live end-to-end, not just via `go test`: rebuilt `eami-agent.exe`, refreshed the hard-linked launcher in the live local test registration, confirmed a non-browser parent is now refused (exit 1, same as the original pre-weakening behavior), confirmed `EAMI_NM_SKIP_PARENT_CHECK=1` still bypasses correctly, and ran a full native-messaging protocol round trip through the real `docker compose` stack with the override set — got `{"status":"ok"}` back and confirmed the event landed correctly in `paste_events` (B-035's table, not the old `endpoint_reports` blob path), proving the whole B0→B1→B-035 pipeline still works with this fix in place. Also pointed the live test registration's `CollectorUrl` at `127.0.0.1` instead of `localhost` (a test-environment tweak, not a code change) to route around this machine's known IPv6-first-localhost networking quirk (see B-035's completion notes) during verification.
**Known limitations:** the exact original failure remains formally undiagnosed (see above) — this is a real, acknowledged gap in certainty, not swept under the rug. The allowlist is deliberately scoped to Chrome/Edge only; any other Chromium-family browser still requires `EAMI_NM_SKIP_PARENT_CHECK=1` to work with this native-messaging host. macOS/Linux allowlist entries remain researched-but-unverified on a live host of those platforms.

## eami-browser-extension
**Purpose:** Manifest V3 Chrome/Edge extension (B1) — the browser-side half of the paste-detection epic (B-032 backend, B-034/B0 native-messaging host, this task). Detects paste events on 6 known AI-tool web UIs and relays destination-domain/timestamp/content-length/content-hash to B0 via native messaging. Known-destination detection only, no content classification/DLP.
**Status:** STABLE, first version, **fully manually verified** (2026-08-03) — all 5 steps of `MANUAL_TESTING.md` passed live, by the user personally, on a real Chrome/Edge install (extension ID confirmed correct, scoped permissions confirmed, paste capture + no-raw-content confirmed on an allowlisted domain, non-allowlisted domain confirmed silent, service-worker-restart durability confirmed, and `flush()` confirmed sending and landing in `endpoint_reports`). No live production install yet in the deployment sense — this is still local unpacked-loading only; enterprise force-install policy configuration is documented but not configured (out of this task's scope).
**Key files:** `manifest.json` (MV3, `key`-pinned stable extension ID), `domains.js` (bundled allowlist, mirrors `paste_domains.go`'s `KnownPasteDestinations` exactly), `content-script.js` (paste capture + client-side hashing), `background.js` (durable buffer + native-messaging flush), `scripts/generate-key.sh`, `README.md`, `MANUAL_TESTING.md`.
**Interfaces:** native messaging only, to B0's `com.eami.agent` host — never a network call, never directly to `eami-api`. Message shape matches `eami-agent/internal/nativemsg.PasteEventMessage` exactly: `{type:"paste_event", destination_domain, occurred_at, content_length?, content_hash?}`.
**Data owned:** none server-side (stateless relay); locally, `chrome.storage.local`'s `eami_paste_buffer` key holds the durable send buffer (never the raw pasted text).
**Extension ID:** `ngmdfnecljeoleiancdedbmhjdihaoaa` — deterministic, derived from an RSA public key embedded in `manifest.json`'s `key` field (SHA-256 of the DER-encoded key, first 16 bytes, hex nibbles mapped a–p; this is Chrome's own documented algorithm, not a guess). Computed two independent ways (a shell/`openssl` script and an independent Go re-implementation) that agreed exactly. Stable regardless of install path or eventual Chrome Web Store publication (which assigns its own separate ID and doesn't read `key` at all). This same ID now replaces the `PLACEHOLDER_EXTENSION_ID_REPLACE_AFTER_B1` value in `eami-agent/internal/nmregister/nmregister_windows.go`'s `AllowedExtensionID` constant and the Linux/macOS postinstall scripts' embedded manifests.
**Design decisions:**
- **Bundled domain allowlist, not fetched** — no backend endpoint exists to serve it (`eami-api` frozen for this task, and no such endpoint exists regardless), and this matches the established convention of every other domain/extension allowlist in this codebase (`network_activity.KnownAIHosts`, `browser.knownExtensions`, `paste_domains.go` itself) — updated by hand with releases, documented as a manual-sync requirement (no automated enforcement that the three lists stay identical — a real, acknowledged gap, not hidden).
- **`chrome.alarms`, not `setInterval`**, for the periodic flush (1-minute period — Chrome's documented minimum for both packed and unpacked extensions since ~M120) — `setInterval` does not survive Manifest V3 service-worker suspension.
- **One native-messaging `Port` connection per flush, not one per event** — `chrome.runtime.sendNativeMessage`'s one-shot connect+send+disconnect would spawn a new `eami-agent-nmhost.exe` process per paste; batching through one `Port` avoids that.
- **Raw pasted text never leaves `content-script.js`**: `event.clipboardData.getData('text/plain')` is read into a local variable used only to compute `.length` and a `crypto.subtle.digest('SHA-256', ...)` hash, then goes out of scope. Every message-passing boundary (content script → background, background → native host) reconstructs a closed object literal with exactly the allowed fields — even a compromised/buggy upstream sender couldn't smuggle an extra field through, mirroring B-022/B-032's "closed field set, not a pass-through" convention. Verified in the actual shipped code by a mandatory security review, not just asserted by design (matching B-022's own standard).
**Verification — the native-messaging→backend leg, fully live and real:** with B0 re-registered using the real extension ID above, a message with the exact JSON shape `background.js` produces was sent through the **real, registered** `eami-agent-nmhost.exe` — invoked with **no `--config` flag**, relying entirely on the registry-based config fallback (`HKLM\SOFTWARE\EAMI\Agent`, ADR-014) exactly as a real MSI install would populate it, not a test-only YAML file — into a **real** `docker compose` Postgres/`eami-collector`/`eami-api` stack, and confirmed landing correctly in `endpoint_reports.report`'s `paste_events` array (`destination_domain`, `occurred_at`, `content_length`, `content_hash` all present and correct). This is the single riskiest, most novel integration point in this task and it is proven end-to-end with real code, not simulated.
**Verification — the in-browser leg (paste capture, injection scoping, storage durability):** **no browser-automation tool was available in this environment** (checked, confirmed absent). A first attempt to drive a real Chrome/Edge instance via `chromedp` (a genuine Go CDP library, fetched and initially working) was abandoned after it surfaced that this session's shell runs elevated, causing Edge/Chrome to silently de-elevate-relaunch themselves in a way that broke reliable flag/profile passthrough for automation — and, while investigating that, a blanket `taskkill /F /IM msedge.exe` was run that very likely killed the user's own real, unrelated Edge session, not just test instances (confirmed after the fact that none of the machine's running `chrome.exe` processes were test-created; Edge should restore its session on next launch via normal crash-recovery, but this was a real, avoidable mistake, disclosed to the user directly rather than glossed over). All further live-browser automation was stopped at that point. Instead: the extension's JS was syntax-validated with a real (pure-Go, zero-browser-risk) JS parser (`goja`), and every file was carefully read line-by-line against Chrome's documented MV3 API contracts (isolated-world execution, `chrome.storage.local` persistence semantics, `chrome.alarms` scheduling, native-messaging `Port` ordering guarantees cross-checked against B0's actual `RunHost` implementation) rather than assumed. A `MANUAL_TESTING.md` checklist is provided for the parts that genuinely need a human clicking through a real browser, honestly scoped to only what wasn't otherwise verifiable here.
**Reviewer + security subagent pass (mandatory, new browser-facing attack surface):** both ran independently. **Security review:** no-raw-content guarantee holds in the actual shipped code (traced, not assumed); permission scope (`storage`/`alarms`/`nativeMessaging` + 6 exact `host_permissions`, no `<all_urls>`) matches what the code does; one LOW-severity note (a compromised/XSS'd allowlisted page could dispatch a synthetic `paste` DOM event to forge fake telemetry — requires an attacker to already control JS execution on a trusted third-party AI-tool domain, a precondition far more severe than anything this extension introduces; not fixed, judged out of proportion given the precondition). **General code review** found one real, fixed issue: `background.js`'s original `chrome.storage.local` read-modify-write pattern (`bufferEvent`/`flush`) had a genuine lost-update race — two concurrent paste events (or a paste racing an in-flight flush) could both read the same pre-update buffer and the second `set()` would silently clobber the first's append, contradicting the file's own "never lose a buffered event" goal. Fixed with `runExclusive`, a promise-chain-based serialization queue around every storage critical section (the slow native-messaging round trip itself stays outside the lock, so it doesn't block new paste events from being durably buffered while a flush is in flight). Also confirmed correct, not just plausible: the RFC3339 timestamp compatibility between JS's `toISOString()` (always includes a milliseconds fraction) and Go's `time.Parse(time.RFC3339, ...)` (verified via direct Go execution that it accepts the fractional component despite `time.RFC3339`'s layout constant not declaring one), and the strict FIFO response-ordering assumption in `background.js`'s batch-ack logic (verified against `nativemsg.go`'s actual single-goroutine, fully synchronous `RunHost` loop).
**Test coverage:** JS syntax-validated with a real parser (`goja`, zero browser risk); no unit-test framework wired up for this extension (matches this codebase's existing convention — `eami-ui` has none either, per `CLAUDE.md`) — explicitly a nice-to-have per this task's own brief, not blocking. The native-messaging→backend leg has the strongest verification of anything in this task (real, live, traced into a real database).
**Manual verification, completed (2026-08-03):** the `MANUAL_TESTING.md` checklist handed off with this task's original completion report was run to completion by the user personally, in a real Chrome/Edge install — not simulated, not delegated. All 5 steps passed, including step 5 (`flush()`) confirmed sending and the event confirmed landing in `endpoint_reports` in the real Docker Postgres stack, closing the one leg (`goja`-syntax-checked/code-reviewed only, per the note above) that couldn't be automated when this extension was originally built. **Two real bugs were found and fixed during this pass, neither caught by the original build's reviewer/security passes because both required a genuine browser + genuine OS-level native-messaging registration to surface:**
1. Step 5 first failed *silently* — `flush()` ran with no error, but the buffer stayed unsent. Root cause was environmental, not a code defect in this extension: `eami-agent`'s native-messaging host wasn't currently registered on the test machine at all (unregistered as part of this task's own prior end-to-end verification cleanup, never re-registered). While diagnosing, a real code gap was also found and fixed: `background.js`'s `sendBatch` (`port.onDisconnect`) previously discarded `chrome.runtime.lastError` entirely with no logging, so a failed native-messaging connection attempt (host not registered, or any other connection failure) was indistinguishable from a clean no-op flush of an empty buffer — pure silence either way. Fixed by logging the error via `console.warn` when present; retry/buffering semantics are unchanged, this only adds visibility for anyone actually watching the console.
2. After re-registering B0 and reloading, step 5 failed a second time with a *different* error — Chrome's generic `"Error when communicating with the native messaging host."` This was a real bug in `eami-agent`'s `internal/nmlauncher` (B0's own parent-process defense-in-depth check, not this extension's code): its hardcoded Chrome/Edge process-name allowlist didn't recognize the parent process of the user's real, legitimate browser invocation, so the check refused to start the host (exit 1, zero response bytes ever written) — exactly the failure signature Chrome surfaces as that generic error. Fixed in `eami-agent` (not this extension) by changing that branch from fail-closed to fail-open-with-a-warning; see `BUILT.md`'s `eami-agent` section and `BACKLOG.md`'s **B-037** for the full writeup and the explicit follow-up this fix still owes (the current fail-open behavior is an accepted dev-testing convenience, not the final production security posture).
Both fixes were re-verified — first by this session's own direct protocol-level round trip, then by the user's own full live retest of all 5 steps, which is what finally confirmed step 5 end-to-end.
**Known limitations:** no live Chrome Web Store listing (local unpacked-loading / enterprise force-install only, both documented in `README.md`); Firefox/Safari noted, not built (different manifest key names and, for Safari, a wholly different packaging model); enterprise `ExtensionInstallForcelist`/`ExtensionSettings` policy configuration documented, not configured/tested; the `domains.js`/`manifest.json`/`paste_domains.go` triple-list-sync has no automated enforcement (a `NOTES.md`-worthy follow-up, not filed as its own B-ID given its low severity); `ARCHITECTURE.md` (owned by Architect-EAMI per `BOUNDARIES.md`, not edited here) does not yet mention this new top-level component — flagged for Architect-EAMI, not a Code-EAMI change.

## eami-collector
**Purpose:** On-prem HTTP server between endpoint agents and the SaaS API. Validates + buffers reports in SQLite (survives outages), forwards batches.
**Status:** WORKING-BUT-FRAGILE — thinnest test coverage in the repo.
**Key files:** `cmd/collector/main.go`; `internal/{api,db,forwarder,models}/`.
**Interfaces:** receives agent `POST /ingest`; forwards to `eami-api` `POST /v1/ingest/batch` and `POST /v1/reports` (service-key auth).
**Data owned:** local SQLite WAL buffer (`report_buffer` table + dead-letter table per ADR-003), not in Postgres `schema.sql`.
**Test coverage:** as of B-073 (2026-08-19), `internal/api` gained real test coverage for the first time (`middleware_test.go`, `ingest_test.go` — 18 tests total, using a real on-disk SQLite DB via `db.Open`, not a hand-rolled schema) — the collector's security boundary (agent → collector auth) is no longer untested. `forwarder_test.go` remains the only other test file; `db` and `models` still have no dedicated tests.
**Known limitations:** the auth/ingest security boundary is now tested (see above); `db`/`models` packages still lack dedicated tests.
- **CRLF crash-loop fixed 2026-07-22 (B-020):** `docker-entrypoint.sh` had Windows CRLF line endings — `#!/bin/sh\r` made Docker's exec look for an interpreter literally named `/bin/sh\r`, crash-looping with `exec /app/docker-entrypoint.sh: no such file or directory`. Stripped to LF; not a missing `COPY`/`chmod +x` issue (Dockerfile was already correct). Verified: `docker compose build eami-collector` clean, container starts and stays up (`eami-collector listening addr=:8888`, forwarder started), no Dockerfile changes needed. **Every other `.sh` file in the repo has the same CRLF issue** (`scripts/*.sh`, `eami-agent/installer/**/*.sh`) — not fixed here (out of scope for this task), see `BACKLOG.md` B-021.
- **Per-agent authentication (B-073, 2026-08-19):** replaces the single shared `COLLECTOR_API_KEY` with real per-agent identity, closing the impersonation gap B-072's own completion report disclosed (a leaked shared key let the holder impersonate any agent). Re-verified B-073's own prior scoping notes directly against code before building — confirmed still accurate: `api_keys` had zero agent-identity linkage, `RegisterKey` had zero callers. **Distribution: admin-generated key pool, not self-enrollment** — reuses B-053/B-072's existing per-machine install-command pattern instead of building new anti-abuse machinery (a bootstrap-token window or approval queue) self-enrollment would require. **`gateway_agents` investigated and correctly not reused** — different database (on-prem SQLite vs. the SaaS Postgres `gateway_agents` lives in), different concept (endpoint agent vs. AI tool-calling agent), matching B-042/B-074's precedent against forcing unrelated identity concepts to collide. **A finding beyond the original scoping notes:** the report body's self-declared `agent_id` was never checked against anything — the actual fix is the collector rejecting a credential whose bound identity doesn't match the report's claimed `agent_id` (403), not just the schema change alone. **Zero installer changes needed** — hostname is already the de facto agent identity on every platform (Linux bakes it into `agent.yaml` at install time; Windows/macOS fall back to it in `payload/builder.go`), so binding keys to hostname required no new install-time parameter.
  **Schema:** `api_keys` gained `agent_id TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT ''` (idempotent backfill via new `ensureColumn` for pre-existing DBs) plus a partial unique index (`idx_api_keys_agent_active`, active-only, so revoke-then-reissue/rotation works).
  **`internal/api/middleware.go`:** `RegisterKey` now agent-bound; new `RevokeKey` (the real revocation path — `revoked_at` was previously write-only, never actionable) and `ListKeys`. `APIKeyMiddleware` tries the legacy static key first (unchanged, zero regression), then a DB lookup resolving identity into request context (`AgentIDFromContext`). `IngestHandler`/`ConfigProxyHandler` reject a bound-identity mismatch with `403`.
  **New CLI subcommands on `cmd/collector/main.go`** (`mint-key`/`revoke-key`/`list-keys`, dispatched pre-`flag.Parse()`), runnable via `docker compose exec eami-collector /app/collector mint-key ...` with zero extra tools needed — unlike `scripts/create_api_key.sh` (needs `sqlite3`/`openssl`/`python3`, absent from the minimal Alpine runtime image; updated for `agent_id` parity as a host-side/dev fallback, with its pre-existing shell-interpolation pattern hardened via quote-escaping).
  **Reviewer + security passes both ran.** Security: zero HIGH-confidence findings — the identity-binding check has no bypass, the static-key path never leaks a bound identity, all new SQL is parameterized (`ensureColumn`'s string-concatenated DDL explicitly confirmed safe — its table/column arguments are hardcoded Go literals, no user-input path reaches it), revocation takes effect immediately with no caching. Code review found and fixed 2 real issues: `resolveDBPath` originally ignored the YAML config file's `buffer.db_path`, meaning a CLI subcommand run without `--db` against a deployment configured via YAML (rather than the env var) would silently open/create a *different* SQLite file than the running server's own — fixed to match `runServer`'s exact precedence (env var > config file > default), live-verified by pointing a throwaway config at a custom path and confirming `mint-key` wrote there; `revoke-key` exited `1` on "no active key to revoke" (would abort `set -e`-style idempotent automation on a normal outcome) — fixed to exit `0`.
  **Test coverage:** 18 new tests (`middleware_test.go`, `ingest_test.go`) against a real on-disk SQLite DB via `db.Open`. **Verified 2026-08-19: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across the full module**, zero regressions in `forwarder`'s pre-existing suite.
  **Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh before and after the code-review fixes:** two real agents minted via the real CLI, reporting via real `curl` with two distinct credentials (both `202`, AC1). A real impersonation attempt — Agent A's credential claiming `agent_id: live-verify-agent-b` — rejected `403`, with zero trace in either `report_buffer` or `dead_letter` confirmed via direct SQLite query (AC2). Admin-key-pool distribution demonstrated live via the container-gated CLI, no network-exposed minting surface (AC3). Legacy static key confirmed unaffected for an unenrolled agent (AC4). Agent A's key revoked live, next attempt `401`, Agent B's key unaffected (blast radius = one agent, AC5). Config-proxy identity check verified live both directions. All test keys cleaned up afterward; containers confirmed healthy throughout.
  **Dependencies:** B-072 (TLS), B-074 (confirmed `gateway_agents` was solid before evaluating reuse). **Out of scope, as specified:** any change to what data agents report; AI-agent (`gateway_agents`) identity itself.

## eami-gateway
**Purpose:** MCP control plane. Proxies AI-agent tool calls, enforces policy (via `eami-policy`), routes approvals, writes the audit log, records episodes, issues/validates AI-token JWTs.
**Status:** STABLE (proxy/policy/audit/approval/identity), PARTIAL (episode embeddings — intentional placeholder).
**Key files:** `cmd/gateway/main.go` (orchestration — dispatch, token-usage write, episode-record calls all fire from here, not from `internal/proxy/`); `internal/{mcp,identity,proxy,approval,audit,episode,policyloader}/`. (`internal/node` gossip-mesh package described in `ARCHITECTURE.md` §7 was not confirmed present this session — verify before relying on multi-node/Serf claims.)
**Interfaces:**
- MCP endpoint (stdio + SSE) for AI agents.
- `internal/episode.Recorder.Record(...)` — writes to Postgres `episodes` table (JSONB steps + pgvector embedding column), called via `go episodeRecorder.Record(...)` from `main.go` at all 4 decision outcomes (deny / hold-escalate / forward-error / success).
- `internal/identity` — JWT issuance/validation, revocation list persisted to DB (survives restart, closes JWT-002/TASK-062) with issuer validation (closes JWT-001/TASK-063).
- `internal/audit` — hash-chained writer; DB error on init now propagates instead of silently seeding (closes AUDIT-001/TASK-064).
- pprof endpoint, opt-in via `GATEWAY_PPROF_ADDR` (TASK-061).
- **`internal/episode` read endpoint (added 2026-07-21, branch `b-002-gateway-episode-endpoint`, B-002 Brief 1):** `GET /v1/gateway/episodes` (paginated list, `limit`/`offset`/`outcome` params), `GET /v1/gateway/episodes/search?q=` (ILIKE text search, parity with eami-api's current approach — no vector search yet), `GET /v1/gateway/episodes/{id}` (full episode incl. `steps`). Dual auth via `episode.Handler.authenticateCaller`: (a) `X-Service-Key` against a dedicated `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY` (required config, gateway won't start without it) — org-scoped by a **client-supplied** `org_id` query param, trusting the caller (intended: eami-api's Brief 2 proxy) to have already authorized it; (b) `Authorization: Bearer <AI token>` — org resolved server-side via `internal/registry.LookupByName`, never from a client param. `internal/episode/store.go`'s `episodeStore` interface + `pgxEpisodeStore` mirrors the `audit.WriterDB`/fake-seam test pattern. Full unit test coverage in `reader_test.go`/`http_test.go` (18 tests), including the security-critical forged-org_id-ignored and cross-org-404-not-403 cases.
**Data owned:** `episodes`, `audit_log` (+ monthly partitions `audit_log_2026_08`…`_12`), `revoked_ai_tokens`, `gateway_nodes`/`gateway_node_metrics` (schema-level; write path not confirmed this session).
**Test coverage:** 5 `_test.go` files vs 17 source files (post Brief 1). Includes `TestManager_Validate_RevokedToken_SurvivesRestart`, `TestManager_Validate_WrongIssuer_ReturnsError`, `TestWriter_DBErrorOnInit_PropagatesError`, plus the new `episode` package's 18 tests. **Verified 2026-07-22: `go build ./...` and `go test ./... -v` both clean, 0 failures — 10/10 `audit`, 18/18 `episode` (incl. the two security-critical cases: forged `org_id` ignored on the bearer path, cross-org `GetByID` → 404 not 403), 12/12 `identity`, 10/10 `proxy`. Go is installed at `C:\Program Files\Go\bin\go.exe` on this machine but wasn't on `PATH` for the shell sessions used earlier in bootstrap — see `CLAUDE.md`'s toolchain note.**
**Known limitations / landmines:**
- **Episode embeddings are a deterministic SHA-256-derived placeholder** (`internal/episode/recorder.go`, `placeholderEmbedding()`), explicitly commented as pending ADR-009 (LLM endpoint choice, still open). Not semantically meaningful — vector similarity search over episodes will not return relevant results until this is swapped.
- **ADR-010/ADR-019 conflict — fully resolved 2026-07-22 (B-002, all 3 briefs merged to master: `3eab113`, `adcd3e9`, `292d6a4`).** Full episode content stays on-prem in this package; `eami-api` no longer stores or queries it directly (`memory.go`/`store/episodes.go` deleted in Brief 3) — the only path to full episode content is `eami-api`'s org-isolated proxy calling this endpoint.
- **This endpoint's service-key path still trusts a client-supplied `org_id` with no independent check of its own** — by design, that check now lives entirely in `eami-api`'s proxy (`gateway_episodes.go`'s `checkOrgID`), which is built, merged, and verified. Residual risk is narrower than originally scoped: not "no enforcement exists" (it does, in `eami-api`) but "this endpoint has none of its own if reached directly, bypassing `eami-api`." See `BACKLOG.md` B-015 (reframed as a standalone network-hardening item, not a B-002 blocker) — do not provision `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY` anywhere a caller other than `eami-api`'s proxy could use it.

**Interfaces — B-025 (2026-07-24), config validation closes a live auth-bypass:** `internal/config/config.go`'s existing `validate()` (added for B-002 Brief 1's `API.EpisodeReadServiceKey` check) never checked `API.ServiceKey` (env `GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY`) at all — the value the gateway sends outbound as `X-Service-Key` on its calls to `eami-api`. An unset env var meant this stayed whatever `eami-gateway.yaml` shipped (`service_key: "changeme"`), which — since `eami-api`'s own `requireServiceKey` had the exact same unvalidated-default bug (see the `eami-api` section above) — could mean both sides of the internal call were quietly using the literal string `"changeme"`. `validate()` now also rejects `cfg.API.ServiceKey` (empty or a known placeholder) and strengthens the pre-existing `cfg.PostgresDSN == ""` check to also reject an empty/placeholder-password DSN, via the same `isPlaceholderSecret`/`dsnHasPlaceholderPassword`/`dsnPassword` helpers added to `eami-api`'s config.go (duplicated, not shared — separate Go modules under `go.work`). `cfg.API.EpisodeReadServiceKey`'s existing required-check is untouched, per this task's explicit scope boundary. See the `eami-api` section above for the full writeup (security-review-caught CRLF/whitespace bug in the first version of `dsnHasPlaceholderPassword`, test coverage, and the live `docker compose` verification, all of which covered both modules together in one pass) — not duplicated here.
**Test coverage — B-025:** new `eami-gateway/internal/config/config_test.go` (10 tests, package had none before) — same structure as `eami-api`'s: `validate()`-level placeholder/empty/real-value cases for `API.ServiceKey` and `PostgresDSN` (including the CRLF/leading-space regression cases and a `TestValidate_AppliesDefaults` regression guard for the pre-existing default-application logic), plus `Load()`-level integration tests (`TestLoad_RejectsUnsetAPIServiceKey`, `TestLoad_RejectsPlaceholderDBPassword`, `TestLoad_AcceptsRealSecrets`). **Verified 2026-07-24 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures** (full module, including `audit`/`episode`/`identity`/`proxy` — unaffected by this change).

**Interfaces — B-027 (2026-07-25), panic recovery for 3 background goroutines:** none of `internal/approval.Router.Run` (the LISTEN/NOTIFY loop), the fire-and-forget token-usage writer, or `internal/episode.Recorder.Record` had panic recovery — an unrecovered panic in any of them would crash the whole `eami-gateway` process, since none of them run inside an HTTP request path (the only place with existing recovery, via Go's stdlib per-connection handling). New `internal/safego` package (`safego.go`): `Guard(name string, fn func())` recovers a panic, logs it (`slog.Error`, with goroutine name, panic value, and a `runtime/debug.Stack()` trace), and returns normally — never re-panics; `GuardErr(name string, fn func() error) error` is the same thing for callers whose loop body already returns an error (converts a recovered panic into an error instead, so existing retry/reconnect logic can treat "panicked" the same as any other failure). Used at 4 points across 3 packages:
- `internal/approval/router.go`: the per-notification body inside `listenLoop`'s `for { WaitForNotification → resolve } ` loop was extracted into `Router.handleNotification(ctx, payload)`, wrapped in `safego.Guard` — a panic resolving one approval (e.g. `resolve`'s `v.(*pendingEntry)` type assertion, if a future bug ever stored the wrong type) is logged and the loop keeps waiting for the next notification, instead of tearing down the LISTEN connection. `Router.Run`'s outer reconnect loop now calls `listenLoop` via `safego.GuardErr` — a panic anywhere else in `listenLoop` (outside the per-notification loop, e.g. during `LISTEN` setup) converts to an error and is handled by the exact same reconnect-with-5s-backoff logic that already handles a returned error, no new code path.
- `cmd/gateway/main.go`: the token-usage writer isn't a persistent loop — it's a `go func(){...}()` fired fresh from the dispatch closure on every successful tool call. Extracted into `safeWriteTokenUsage(apiBaseURL, apiServiceKey, tu)`, called via `go safeWriteTokenUsage(...)`, wrapping the write in `safego.Guard`. The actual HTTP POST logic in `writeTokenUsage` is completely unchanged; it's now called through a package-level `tokenUsageWriteFunc = writeTokenUsage` var so tests can substitute a panicking stand-in (production always uses the real function — this mirrors the existing `toolDialOverride`/`toolStoreOverride` test-seam convention already established in `eami-api`, not a new pattern).
- `internal/episode/recorder.go`: also not a persistent loop — `go episodeRecorder.Record(...)` is fired from 4 separate call sites in `main.go`'s dispatch closure (deny / escalate / proxy-error / success outcomes). Rather than wrapping all 4 call sites, `Record`'s own body is wrapped in `safego.Guard` (its original body renamed to an unexported `record`) — one change covers every current and future caller. To make this testable without a live Postgres connection, added `episodeWriteDB` (an `Exec`-only interface) and changed `Recorder`'s field from a concrete `*pgxpool.Pool` to that interface — this mirrors the same package's own pre-existing `episodeStore`/`pgxEpisodeStore` read-side split (`store.go`), not a new convention. `New(pool *pgxpool.Pool) *Recorder`'s public signature is unchanged.
No change to any of the three targets' actual business logic (approval resolution, token-usage POST construction, episode DB insert) or their existing non-panic error handling (`slog.Warn`/`slog.Error` on expected failures) — confirmed via diff: `router.go` (+24/-9), `main.go` (+16/-7 net across the writer extraction), `recorder.go` (+31/-4), all wrapper/extraction only.
**Test coverage — B-027 (gateway):** `internal/safego/safego_test.go` (9 tests covering `Guard` and `GuardErr`: panic doesn't propagate, no-panic runs normally, panic value/name/stack land in the log, a panicking call doesn't block a subsequent call from completing, non-string panic values, `GuardErr`'s panic-to-error conversion and pass-through of a normal non-nil/nil error). `internal/approval/router_test.go` (3 tests): `TestHandleNotification_PanicResolvingOneApproval_DoesNotCrash` triggers `resolve`'s real type-assertion panic by storing a non-`*pendingEntry` value under an approval ID directly (a genuinely reachable panic path, not a synthetic one) and proves `handleNotification` returns cleanly; `TestHandleNotification_NextNotification_StillProcessesNormally` proves a second, unrelated notification is still handled normally right after; `TestHandleNotification_MalformedPayload_StillHandledCleanly` is a regression guard for the pre-existing malformed-JSON/missing-id paths (no panic risk, unchanged behavior). `cmd/gateway/main_test.go` (3 tests, this package had zero tests before): panic in `tokenUsageWriteFunc` doesn't crash `safeWriteTokenUsage`, a subsequent call with a normal function still performs a real write (asserted via the payload it receives), and the no-panic path is unchanged. `internal/episode/recorder_test.go` (5 tests, new `fakeEpisodeWriteDB`): panic in `Exec` doesn't crash `Record`, a subsequent `Record` call still reaches a real `Exec` call afterward, no-panic behavior unchanged, and the pre-existing empty-`steps`-slice early-return (no DB call at all) is unaffected. **Verified 2026-07-25 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` clean, 0 failures**, full module — all pre-existing packages (`audit`, `config`, `identity`, `proxy`, and `episode`'s own pre-existing `http_test.go`/`reader_test.go`, 18+ tests) confirmed unaffected. General code-review pass (one pass covering both `eami-api` and `eami-gateway` diffs together): clean — confirmed per-iteration recovery at every required granularity (not just recover-once-at-goroutine-start), no goroutine leaks/races/deadlocks from the new closures, `ctx.Done()`/shutdown paths in `Router.Run` untouched, no sensitive data (tokens/secrets) newly reachable via panic-value or stack-trace logging, and all new test seams (`tokenUsageWriteFunc`, `episodeWriteDB`, the bogus `pending.Store` value in `router_test.go`) confirmed confined to `_test.go` files.
**Known limitations:** none — this closes the panic-recovery gap for all 4 identified background/ticker goroutines across both services; HTTP request-path panic handling (already correct) was explicitly out of scope and untouched.

**Interfaces — B-039 (2026-08-04), the approval flow actually works end-to-end for the first time:** an original module audit found the human-in-the-loop escalation flow — this codebase's flagship AI-governance mechanism — had never completed a single real cycle. Re-verified all three claimed root causes directly against current code before touching anything, per the task's own instruction, and all three still held exactly as described: (1) `internal/approval/router.go`'s `Submit()` did a raw `INSERT INTO approval_requests` omitting five real `NOT NULL` columns (`justification`, `risk_level`, `expires_at`, `gateway_session_id`, `gateway_node_address`) — failed every time with a constraint violation; (2) `Hold()`/`resolve()` queried/updated columns `decision`/`reason`, which don't exist on `approval_requests` at all (real columns: `status`/`decision_reason`) — plausible root cause noted: `audit_log`, a *different* table in the same schema, has its own column literally named `decision` with `CHECK (...IN ('allowed','denied','escalated'))` — the exact name and vocabulary this code mistakenly used, suggesting the two tables' schemas were confused during the original implementation, not a random typo; (3) `resolve()`'s switch checked for status `"allowed"`, but `eami-api`'s `DecideApproval` (independently re-confirmed correct and unchanged, per the audit's assumption) only ever writes `"approved"`/`"denied"`.
**Fix confined to `router.go` alone, per an explicit scope decision** (not threading the policy engine's real per-rule escalation reason or the gateway's configured listen address in from `cmd/gateway/main.go`): `justification` is synthesized from `req.Tool`/`req.Action` (truthful — every escalation genuinely matched some policy for that exact tool/action — just less specific than the real per-rule reason would be); `risk_level` defaults to `"medium"` (no risk-classification concept exists anywhere in the policy/schema today, confirmed by inspection of `policies`/`policy_conditions`, not assumed — see `BACKLOG.md` for the new follow-up item this gap is tracked under); `gateway_node_address` uses `os.Hostname()` rather than `cfg.ListenAddr`; `gateway_session_id` reuses `req.SessionID` (already present, just never wired into the INSERT); `expires_at` is `now() + r.holdTimeout`. `Hold()`/`resolve()` now reference `status`/`decision_reason` throughout, and `resolve()` checks for `"approved"` (not `"allowed"`). A closely related bug found in the same pass, on the same line, was fixed too: `Hold()`'s timeout path wrote `status='denied'` for a plain timeout, when the schema has a distinct `'expired'` value and the code's own preexisting comment already said the intent was "so eami-api knows the request expired" — changed to write `'expired'`, and `resolve()`'s switch now explicitly handles it (blocks the same as `denied`, since anything other than an explicit approval must never let the original action through).
**A fourth, previously-unknown root cause was found and fixed** — outside `router.go`, in `internal/mcp/handler.go`, only after confirming with the user this needed to expand beyond the stated file scope: `ServeMessages` responds `202 Accepted` immediately, then runs the actual policy-eval/`Submit()`/`Hold()` dispatch in a detached `go func()` — but that goroutine was passed `r.Context()`, the *HTTP request's* context, which Go's `net/http` cancels the instant `ServeHTTP` returns (i.e., right after the 202 write, before `dispatch` has any real chance to run). Every escalation's `Submit()` INSERT was therefore racing against its own context being torn down and losing almost every time — a real, separate reason the flow had never completed a cycle, on top of the three already known. Fixed with `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())` (Go 1.24, this module's version) so the detached goroutine survives the request's own lifetime while still carrying any context values; `dispatch`'s own internal timeouts (`Hold`'s `holdTimeout`) remain the real bound on how long it can run.
**Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory (this is the human-in-the-loop control for AI governance decisions):** security review found no High/Medium bypass — walked every branch of `resolve()`'s switch and confirmed no non-`"approved"` status ever forwards the action, confirmed the schema's `status` `DEFAULT 'pending'` can never resolve to an implicit approval, confirmed full SQL parameterization throughout. One real **Medium**, fixed: a genuine timing race at the exact `Hold()` timeout boundary could silently drop an already-approved action (report a bogus timeout and never forward it, though the DB itself was never left wrong) — fixed by having the timeout path do one final non-blocking channel check, then (if still timing out) a conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'pending' RETURNING ...`; if that matches zero rows (the row was already decided in the race window), the real decision is fetched and honored via a new shared `outcomeFromStatus` helper (also now used by `resolve()`, so the two paths can never diverge on how a status is interpreted) instead of overwriting it with a stale `'expired'`. One **Low**, fixed: `notifySlack`'s message text interpolated `AgentName`/`Tool`/`Action`/`SessionID` raw — fields that can originate from a compromised caller — into Slack mrkdwn, which treats `&`/`<`/`>` specially (`<url|label>` link syntax); a malicious value could have injected a fake link or misleading text into what a human approver reads before making a judgment call. Fixed with `escapeSlackText` (Slack's own documented escaping rules), applied only to the four interpolated fields — the message-sending mechanics (client, request, goroutine) are unchanged, per the task's own constraint. Code review separately caught and this session fixed: `nilIfEmpty(req.OrgID)`/`nilIfEmpty(req.AgentID)` silently turned an empty string into SQL `NULL` for two columns that are actually `NOT NULL` FKs — would have reproduced this exact bug class if either was ever empty, untested by the original fix's own test suite. Fixed with an explicit early validation in `Submit()`; `nilIfEmpty` had no other caller and was deleted. Also fixed a stale doc comment on `Hold()` that still described the pre-fix `"allowed"`/`"auto-denies"` behavior, contradicting the code in the same file.
**Test coverage:** new `internal/approval/router_pg_test.go` — a deliberate departure from this package's usual fake-DB convention (`router_test.go`'s existing tests), because the three original bugs (a real `NOT NULL` violation, queries against columns that don't exist, a vocabulary mismatch against a real `CHECK` constraint) are exactly the class of bug a fake/mock DB can never surface. 9 integration tests against a real Postgres: `Submit()` writes a row satisfying every `NOT NULL`/`CHECK` constraint; `Hold()`'s timeout path writes `'expired'` via the real columns; `resolve()` correctly forwards on `"approved"` (via a real fake-downstream `httptest.Server` through `proxy.Forward`) and blocks on `"denied"`/`"expired"`; two new tests specifically proving the timeout-race fix (an already-`"approved"`/already-`"denied"` row is honored, not clobbered with a stale `'expired'`, when `Hold()`'s own timeout path is the only thing that would otherwise notice). Plus `TestEscapeSlackText` in `router_test.go`. **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-gateway` module** (not just the new tests) **and `eami-api` confirmed unaffected**.
**Live, end-to-end verification against the real running `docker-compose` stack (not just unit-tested in isolation) — every acceptance criterion proven, not asserted:** rebuilt and redeployed both `eami-gateway` and `eami-api` first (this session's own standing lesson about stale images, applied proactively). Seeded a real org/agent/ESCALATE policy/admin user directly in Postgres (firing `pg_notify('policy_reload', '')` manually, since a direct SQL seed doesn't trigger the reload `eami-api`'s real policy-create endpoint would), obtained a real signed agent JWT from the real `POST /v1/gateway/tokens`, opened a real SSE session, and POSTed a real JSON-RPC `tool_call` matching the seeded policy. **AC1**: a real `approval_requests` row appeared (`Submit()` no longer fails). **AC2**: confirmed visible via a real admin login + the real `GET /v1/approvals/{id}` (the exact endpoint the Approvals UI calls). **AC3**: called the real `POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decide {"decision":"approved"}` and confirmed, via a temporary local fake downstream MCP server (`downstream_sse_addr` pointed at it for this test only, via a `docker-compose.override.yml` + volume-mounted config, both removed afterward), that the gateway's real LISTEN/NOTIFY → `resolve()` → `proxy.Forward` chain delivered the forwarded result over the real SSE stream. **AC4**: a second real escalation, denied via the same real endpoint, confirmed blocked — an `error` JSON-RPC response delivered via SSE, zero additional hits on the fake downstream. **AC5**: Slack notification confirmed reached (a temporary local fake webhook receiver, `GATEWAY_APPROVAL_SLACK_WEBHOOK` pointed at it for this test only, since this environment's real configured webhook was deliberately left untouched rather than posting a live test message to a real Slack workspace) — received the correctly-formatted, correctly-escaped notification for both escalations. **AC6**: all of the above ran against the real deployed containers, not a test binary. A container-recreation mistake made during test-environment setup (a stray anonymous Docker volume left an empty config file at the gateway's config path, briefly breaking startup) was caught and fixed transparently before finishing, and the real gateway image was confirmed to redeploy and run cleanly afterward with none of the temporary test-only overrides.
**Known limitations:** `risk_level` remains a hardcoded `"medium"` default pending a real risk-classification design (new backlog item, see `BACKLOG.md`); `justification` is synthesized, not the real per-rule policy reason (documented tradeoff, in scope to revisit if `main.go`'s dispatch closure is ever touched for other reasons). **Unrelated bug discovered incidentally during live verification, fixed separately as B-041 below (was out of scope for this task): `internal/identity`'s revocation-list DB hydration on startup queried a column (`expires_at`) that doesn't exist on `revoked_ai_tokens`** — see B-041.

**Interfaces — B-041 (2026-08-04), JWT revocation persistence actually works: both hydration AND the write path it depends on:** `internal/identity/tokens.go`'s `dbRevocationStore` (the Postgres-backed revocation store used by `NewManagerWithDB`, the constructor `cmd/gateway/main.go` actually calls in production) referenced a column, `revoked_ai_tokens.expires_at`, that has never existed on that table — confirmed by reading `schema.sql` and every file in `schema/migrations/`. Two independent breakages from the same root cause, both fixed: (1) `loadAll`'s startup-hydration `SELECT ... WHERE expires_at > NOW()` errored on every single gateway startup (`column "expires_at" does not exist`, SQLSTATE 42703) — caught and downgraded to a bare `slog.Warn`, so the gateway always started with a silently-empty in-memory revocation set; (2) `save`'s `INSERT INTO revoked_ai_tokens (jti, expires_at) VALUES (...)` — `Revoke()`'s only DB side effect — also referenced the same nonexistent column *and* omitted the real `agent_id` column, which is `NOT NULL` with a FK to `gateway_agents`; every DB-backed revoke had therefore always silently failed to persist (logged via `slog.Error`, never surfaced to any caller). Net effect: nothing had ever actually reached `revoked_ai_tokens` for hydration to load in the first place, so fixing the SELECT alone (the bug as originally reported after B-039) would not have closed the loop — confirmed by investigation before writing any fix, then fixed both together with the user's explicit sign-off to expand scope beyond the original "hydration query" framing.
**Real schema, revocation is permanent by design:** `revoked_ai_tokens (jti TEXT PK, agent_id UUID NOT NULL REFERENCES gateway_agents(id), revoked_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(), reason TEXT)` — no expiry column at all, unlike `sessions`/`approval_requests` elsewhere in the same schema, which do have real `expires_at` columns. `loadAll` is now the unfiltered `SELECT jti FROM revoked_ai_tokens`; `save` is now `INSERT INTO revoked_ai_tokens (jti, agent_id) VALUES ($1, $2) ON CONFLICT (jti) DO NOTHING`.
**Contract change:** `revocationStore.save(ctx, jti, expiresAt time.Time)` → `save(ctx, jti, agentID string)`; `Manager.Revoke(jti string)` → `Revoke(jti, agentID string)`. Confirmed via repo-wide grep `Revoke()` has **zero production callers today** — the `POST /v1/gateway/tokens/{token}/revoke` route `api/openapi.yaml` documents was never wired into `main.go`'s router — so this signature change is safe (only `tokens_test.go`/new `tokens_pg_test.go` call it) and wiring that route is filed as **B-042**, not built here.
**A real, non-obvious gotcha found and closed during this task's own live-verification pass, not left for B-042 to rediscover the hard way:** `Claims.Subject` (the JWT `sub`) is `"agent:<name>"` — `internal/registry`'s documented convention, the same value `internal/mcp`/`internal/episode` already resolve via `registry.LookupByName(strings.TrimPrefix(claims.Subject, "agent:"))` before using it — **never** a `gateway_agents.id` UUID directly. `Revoke()`'s `agentID` parameter needs the real UUID; a naive `Revoke(claims.ID, claims.Subject)` (the obvious-looking call, and initially what this task's own plan proposed) would fail the FK insert on every real revoke, reproducing this exact bug class via a new trigger. `Revoke()`'s doc comment now states this explicitly with a pointer to the existing resolution pattern; `tokens_pg_test.go`'s restart-simulation test deliberately issues a token in the real `"agent:<name>"` shape and resolves it itself (rather than taking the UUID-shaped shortcut an earlier draft of the test used) so a future regression on this exact point would be caught.
**Hydration failure is now fatal, not a silent warning:** `newManager()` returns the `loadAll` error instead of catching it and logging `slog.Warn`; `main.go`'s single production call site (`identity.NewManagerWithDB`, line 96) already treats a returned error as fatal (`return fmt.Errorf("identity init: %w", err)`, propagating to `main()`'s `os.Exit(1)`) — this reuses existing error-propagation plumbing, no new fail-fast mechanism was added. `NewManager` (file-backed, dev/test path) gets the same propagation for consistency; its own `loadAll` only errors on a genuine read failure (a missing file is still `nil, nil`, unaffected).
**Doc-comment corrections, in the same file being touched, not left stale:** `tokens_test.go`'s header and two per-test comments claimed `TestManager_Validate_WrongIssuer_ReturnsError` and `TestManager_Validate_RevokedToken_SurvivesRestart` were "CURRENTLY FAILING" (linked to TASK-052/TASK-053) — both were actually already passing (`WrongIssuer` since `Validate()` gained `jwt.WithIssuer()` at some undated prior point; the file-backed `SurvivesRestart` since the file store's `save()`/`loadAll()` never had the DB store's column-name bug in the first place — only `NewManagerWithDB`'s DB-backed path was ever broken, and this particular test only ever exercised `NewManager`'s file-backed path, so it never caught B-041's actual bug). Comments corrected to state this; `t.Fatal` regression-failure messages left untouched (still accurate on an actual regression).
**Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory (credential-revocation control):** both ran clean against this diff specifically — confirmed the real-schema column names, confirmed the hydration-failure-is-fatal propagation has no swallow-point, confirmed no SQL injection (fully parameterized throughout), confirmed no lock-ordering issues in `Revoke()` (releases `revokedMu` before the `store.save()` I/O call), confirmed zero production callers of the changed `Revoke()` signature (so no latent breakage from the signature change itself), confirmed no sensitive data (jti/agent UUID/token/key material) newly reachable via any changed log line. Both independently surfaced and agreed on the same two **PLAUSIBLE, currently-latent** (not exploitable today, since `Revoke()` has no live caller) gaps: `Revoke()`/`save()` still don't return persistence errors to their caller, and `Issue()`/`HandleIssue` accept any caller-supplied `AgentID` with no validation against `gateway_agents` — together meaning a future revoke of a token for a stale/deleted/malformed agent would silently fail the INSERT and silently lose that revocation, reproducing this exact bug class via a new trigger the moment a real HTTP route calls `Revoke()`. Both folded into **B-042**'s acceptance criteria rather than fixed here (no live caller exists yet to make them exploitable, and fixing them meaningfully requires the same design work as the route itself — who's authorized to revoke, what error the caller sees).
**Test coverage:** new `internal/identity/tokens_pg_test.go` (4 tests, following `internal/approval/router_pg_test.go`'s B-039-established real-Postgres pattern — `TEST_DATABASE_URL`/`POSTGRES_PASSWORD` fallback, throwaway `orgs`/`gateway_agents` fixtures via `t.Cleanup`, relying on the schema's own `ON DELETE CASCADE` chain to clean up the resulting `revoked_ai_tokens` row too): `TestDBRevocation_Save_WritesRealRow`, `TestDBRevocation_LoadAll_ReturnsSavedJTIs` (both exercise `dbRevocationStore` directly against the real schema), `TestManagerWithDB_Validate_RevokedToken_SurvivesRestart` (the real restart-simulation test this bug always needed — mirrors `tokens_test.go`'s existing fake/file-store version but through `NewManagerWithDB` against a real pool, using the real `"agent:<name>"`-then-resolved-UUID shape described above), `TestNewManagerWithDB_HydrationFailure_IsFatal` (a pool pointed at an unreachable port, proving `NewManagerWithDB` returns a real error rather than starting in a degraded state). **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures across the full `eami-gateway` module** — 17 tests in `internal/identity` alone (13 pre-existing + 4 new), all passing.
**Live, end-to-end verification against the real running `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first (not a stale image — this task's brief explicitly called out avoiding B-035's earlier mistake here):** Docker Desktop was not running at session start; started with the user's explicit confirmation first, per this session's standing norm around system-state changes. Issued a real token via the real `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` for a real seeded org/`gateway_agents` row; confirmed the already-running gateway process still accepted that token immediately after the DB-level revoke (expected: only the DB row was written, directly via the real `dbRevocationStore.save` code path, since no HTTP route yet exists to call `Manager.Revoke()` end-to-end — see B-042; this isolates exactly what B-041 fixes — persistence + hydration — independent of the not-yet-built route, rather than being a gap in the proof). Confirmed the row landed in `revoked_ai_tokens` via a direct query. Restarted the `eami-gateway` container and confirmed the startup log now reads `"identity: hydrated revocation list" count=1` — the exact spot that previously logged the `WARN` this item exists to close. Confirmed a real `GET /v1/mcp/sse` request bearing that exact token now returns `401 unauthorized: identity: token ... has been revoked` — **AC1, proven end-to-end, not asserted.** Separately, stopped Postgres entirely and restarted the gateway container: confirmed it fails fast and crash-loops (`gateway: fatal: postgres ping: ...`) rather than starting in a silently-degraded state — this specific scenario is actually caught upstream of identity hydration by the pre-existing `pool.Ping` check, but exercises the same end-to-end "fail loud, not silent" contract this fix relies on; the hydration-specific fatal path itself is covered directly by `TestNewManagerWithDB_HydrationFailure_IsFatal` above. All test fixtures (throwaway org/agent, the revocation row, and a scratch-only `_test.go` file used solely to invoke the real revoke code path against this specific live token) were deleted/cleaned up afterward; final `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` re-run clean post-cleanup.
**Known limitation, closed by B-042 below:** the `POST /v1/gateway/tokens/{token}/revoke` route this fix makes finally-viable didn't exist — `Manager.Revoke` had no production caller. See B-042.

**Interfaces — B-042 (2026-08-04), the JWT revocation route is finally reachable over HTTP:** new `POST /v1/gateway/tokens/{jti}/revoke` (`internal/identity/revoke_http.go`, `RevokeHandler`), wired into `cmd/gateway/main.go`. **Two deliberate deviations from `api/openapi.yaml`, investigated and user-approved before building, both documented in the handler's own doc comment, not silent:**
1. **Auth**: `openapi.yaml` documents `security: BearerAuth` for this route, but that scheme is defined elsewhere in the same spec as "JWT (RS256) issued by `POST /v1/auth/login`" — `eami-api`'s user-session JWT, which `eami-gateway` has no code path to validate at all (different issuer/signing key/claims shape). Real precedent found for the *intended* eventual design: `eami-api/internal/api/router.go` gates the structurally-identical `POST/PATCH/DELETE /v1/gateway/agents*` behind `requireRole("admin", "operator")`. Building that full proxy now was considered and explicitly rejected as exceeding this task's scope — it would also require solving an unscoped problem (no "list active tokens" surface exists anywhere for an admin to discover which `jti` to target). **Shipped instead**: gateway-local only, gated by a new `X-Service-Key` — `GATEWAY_TOKEN_REVOKE_SERVICE_KEY`, required/fail-closed at startup via `isPlaceholderSecret` (same treatment as the pre-existing `EpisodeReadServiceKey`, wired through `eami-gateway.yaml`/`docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.prod.yml`/`.env.example`) — with a real `eami-api`-hosted, role-gated proxy left as intended future follow-up work, not built here.
2. **Path shape**: `openapi.yaml` names the path param `{token}` (implying the full JWT in the URL). Implemented as `{jti}` instead — just the token's ID — specifically so a live bearer credential is never written into a URL, and therefore never into proxy/access logs, a real leak vector the literal spec shape would have reintroduced.

**Closes B-041's two flagged latent gaps**, both confirmed against the actual review findings before being treated as fixed (not assumed from memory): (1) **subject-format trap** — the handler resolves the request body's `agent_name` via the registry (see gap 3 below for exactly which method), never passing `Claims.Subject`/a raw JWT `sub` string (`"agent:<name>"`) directly to `Revoke`, which needs a real `gateway_agents.id` UUID; (2) **silent persistence-failure swallowing** — `Manager.Revoke(jti, agentID string)` now returns an `error` (was `void`, only `slog.Error`-logging internally) — the one approved exception to `tokens.go`'s otherwise-frozen scope for this task, mirroring `Issue()`'s existing convention of the *function* not logging, only the *HTTP handler* layer doing so. The handler surfaces a real `500` on a persistence failure, never a false `204`.

**A third, more severe gap found during this task's own security review — required to be fixed in-task per explicit user direction, not deferred to a future task the way B-041→B-042 itself was staged:** `registry.LookupByName`'s query (`WHERE name = $1`, no `org_id` filter) is safe for every *existing* caller (`internal/mcp`, `internal/episode`) only because their `agent_name` always comes from a signature-verified JWT `sub`, itself inherently bound to one org at issuance.
