This new handler is the **first** caller passing a raw, client-supplied `agent_name` with no such cryptographic binding — since `gateway_agents.name` is only unique per `(org_id, name)` (`schema.sql`'s own `UNIQUE` constraint), an Org A caller could otherwise resolve, and cause the permanent-audit-record revocation of, Org B's identically-named agent's token. **Fixed** with a new, purely additive `internal/registry.LookupByNameAndOrg(ctx, name, orgID string)` — direct `WHERE name = $1 AND org_id = $2` query, deliberately uncached (avoids any interaction with `LookupByName`'s existing name-only cache, and this is a low-frequency admin action, not a hot path) — `LookupByName` itself and all its existing callers are completely untouched, confirmed via `git diff` (pure 38-line addition, zero deletions). The revoke request body now requires `org_id` (validated as a well-formed UUID via `uuid.Parse` before any query, 400 otherwise) — caller-supplied and trusted, matching `episode/http.go`'s own already-shipped, already-documented precedent for its service-key path's `org_id` query param, not a new trust-model pattern invented for this task.

**Also correctly preserved, not accidentally dropped in the port:** `registry.LookupByName`'s existing behavior of returning a *populated* `AgentRecord` even when the agent's status is `suspended`/`revoked` (only a genuinely-unresolvable name returns `nil`) — `LookupByNameAndOrg` matches this exactly. The handler deliberately treats revoking a token for an already-suspended agent as a valid action, not an error (an existing session established before suspension may still hold a live, revocable token) — traced and confirmed correct, not assumed.

**Reviewer + security subagent passes ran twice**: an initial pass against the base implementation (service-key auth, gap 1/2 fixes) came back clean on the auth/persistence-error design, and separately flagged three findings — the org-scoping gap above (fixed, see above), the multi-node broadcast gap (real, pre-existing, logged as new **B-043**, not fixed here — out of scope for wiring one HTTP route), and transient-DB-errors-collapsing-to-`403`-with-raw-error-text-in-the-response (confirmed to be a pre-existing pattern already shipped in `episode/http.go`/`mcp/handler.go`, not a regression introduced here — left as-is, matching this codebase's convention of not fixing unrelated pre-existing patterns outside a task's own diff). A focused follow-up pass, run specifically against the org-scoping fix after it was built, came back fully clean.

**Test coverage:** 9 new tests. `internal/identity/revoke_http_pg_test.go` (5 tests, real Postgres, real `registry.Registry`, following B-041's established pattern): `TestRevokeHandler_HappyPath_RevokesRealToken`, `TestRevokeHandler_WrongServiceKey_Returns401` (both wrong-key and missing-key cases, plus confirms the token is untouched), `TestRevokeHandler_UnknownAgent_Returns403`, `TestRevokeHandler_CrossOrgAgentName_Returns403` (creates a real second org, submits the real agent's name with the *wrong* real `org_id`, confirms `403` and the token stays valid — proven together with the happy-path test to demonstrate correct scoping, not a "reject everything" bug), `TestRevokeHandler_PersistenceFailure_Returns500NotSilent204` (a `fakeResolver` test seam returns a fabricated, nonexistent agent UUID that genuinely violates the real `NOT NULL` FK on `INSERT`, proving the `500` path is real, not simulated). `internal/config/config_test.go` gained `TestValidate_RejectsPlaceholderTokenRevokeServiceKey` plus updates to the shared `validConfig()`/`clearSecretEnv()`/`TestLoad_*` fixtures for the new required secret. **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures across the full `eami-gateway` module** — `internal/identity` alone now has 26 tests (17 pre-B-042 + 9 new).

**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first (not a stale image):** seeded a real org/`gateway_agents` row, issued a real token via `POST /v1/gateway/tokens`, confirmed it valid via a real `GET /v1/mcp/sse` request. **AC2 (unauthorized rejection)**: a revoke attempt with the wrong `X-Service-Key` returned real `401`, and the token was confirmed still valid immediately after. **AC1 (legitimate revoke)**: a request with the real service key, correct `agent_name`, and correct `org_id` returned real `204`; the row was confirmed present in `revoked_ai_tokens` with the correct `agent_id`; a subsequent `GET /v1/mcp/sse` with that exact token returned `401 unauthorized: identity: token ... has been revoked`. **Went further than the brief's minimum**: restarted the `eami-gateway` container and confirmed this specific HTTP-route-driven revocation survives the restart too (`"identity: hydrated revocation list" count=1`, token still rejected afterward) — proving the full B-041→B-042 chain works together end-to-end, not just each half verified in isolation. All test fixtures (throwaway orgs/agents, the revocation row) deleted/cleaned up afterward; final `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` re-run clean post-cleanup.

**Known limitation, tracked as new B-043, not built here:** `Manager.Revoke()` only updates the in-memory revoked-set on whichever gateway node handles the request — other nodes don't learn of a revocation until their own restart. `schema.sql`'s `revoked_ai_tokens` comment and `NewManagerWithDB`'s own doc comment both claim Serf-based multi-node broadcast; a repo-wide grep found no such mechanism anywhere. Predates B-042 (the gap has existed since multi-node support was first claimed) — B-042 is simply the first task where it became concretely reachable/consequential, since it's the first time `Revoke()` has had a live HTTP caller at all. Also not built here (explicit, approved scope boundary): the real `eami-api`-hosted, role-gated proxy layer (matching `/v1/gateway/agents*`'s `requireRole` precedent) that would be this endpoint's real long-term caller.

**Interfaces — B-044 (2026-08-06), dynamic MCP tool routing for `rest_api`-type `gateway_tools`:** `proxy.Forward` previously forwarded every tool call to one hardcoded static URL (`cfg.Proxy.DownstreamSSEAddr`), regardless of which tool was named — `gateway_tools` (built by B-022/B-023) had zero live consumers anywhere in `eami-gateway`, confirmed by a routing investigation via repo-wide grep. New `internal/toolrouter` package (`Router.Resolve`, org-scoped `gateway_tools` lookup by name; `Router.Forward`, dispatch to a resolved `rest_api` row's real `base_url` using real decrypted credentials) is consulted by `dispatch` (via new `resolveDynamicTool` in `cmd/gateway/main.go`) before policy evaluation and before the existing `ActionAllow` forward branch. **Two of the task brief's own stated assumptions were corrected before building** (kickoff line's own instruction): B-023's SSRF guard (`safeDialContext`/`isBlockedTestTarget`) and B-022's credential cipher (`toolcreds.Cipher`) both live in `eami-api/internal/...` — `eami-gateway` and `eami-api` are separate Go modules under `go.work`, so neither is importable regardless of export status (Go's `internal/` visibility is scoped to the module tree). Matched this repo's own B-025 precedent (duplicate small security-relevant helpers into each module rather than build shared infrastructure) instead: `toolrouter/dial.go` and `toolrouter/creds.go` are deliberate, documented duplicates — `creds.go`'s `Cipher` is decrypt-only (`eami-gateway` never writes credentials); `eami-api`'s originals are completely untouched, still needed for its own connectivity-test feature.
**A genuine design fork, presented to the user rather than decided unilaterally:** when a tool_call's name matches no `gateway_tools` row for the caller's org (the case for 100% of today's live traffic), dynamic routing is **strictly additive/opt-in** — falls through to the exact same static `fwdProxy.Forward` behavior as before this brief, never a new rejection path for a previously-working call. `proxy.go` and `internal/approval/router.go` (the escalation/approval path) are completely untouched by this task — dynamic routing applies only to `dispatch`'s immediate-allow branch.
**Policy engine, additive:** `eami-policy`'s `ActionContext.ToolServerID string` / `Conditions.ToolServerIDs []string`, new matcher block in `structural.go` mirroring the existing `ToolNames` pattern exactly — lets a rule pin to a resolved `gateway_tools.id` (immutable) in addition to a tool name (mutable). Migration `009_tool_server_routing.sql` adds `policy_conditions.tool_server_ids TEXT[]`; `policyloader.go`'s `queryRules` scans/populates it the same way as every other condition column.
**A real correctness bug found and fixed during code review, before shipping:** `Router.Forward` dereferenced `row.Type` with no nil-guard — the single production call site is already correctly guarded, so this couldn't panic today, but `Router` is an importable package and its own doc comment promises no failure path ever panics. Fixed with a one-line check + `TestForward_NilRow_Rejected`.
**Reviewer + security subagent passes both ran** (mandatory — new outbound dispatch to admin-supplied destinations, at least B-023 rigor): **both clean, no exploitable vulnerabilities, no blocking findings.** Security review re-derived (not trusted from comments) that the duplicated SSRF guard is byte-for-byte equivalent in protective effect to the original (including the resolve-once/dial-the-validated-IP DNS-rebinding protection); confirmed no code path reaches the network without the guarded dialer (the only unrestricted dialer is confined to `router_pg_test.go`, verified via grep); confirmed Go's default redirect-following does NOT bypass the guard (implemented as the `Transport`'s `DialContext`, re-invoked on every redirect hop, not a one-time pre-check); confirmed no decrypted credential ever reaches a log line or error message; confirmed `toolrouter.Cipher`/`toolcreds.Cipher` are genuinely interoperable (compared line-by-line, verified with a real round-trip test). One informational note, not a vulnerability: the guard's correct, conservative blocking of RFC1918 addresses also blocks an on-prem gateway's legitimate access to a customer's own internal APIs — flagged for product as a future allowlist decision, not fixed here (fails closed correctly in the meantime). Code review's two other minor findings (no `gateway_tools.status` filter in `Resolve`; `db_connection_string` `auth_type` silently ignored on `rest_api` rows, same class as B-023's already-accepted `basic`-auth gap) left as documented, non-blocking limitations.
**Test coverage:** 22 new tests. `internal/toolrouter/router_pg_test.go` (11 real-Postgres tests, following this session's established pattern, including an injectable-dialer test seam mirroring B-023's own `dialContextFunc` convention — production `New()` always uses the real guard; only `_test.go` files construct the unrestricted one used to reach a local `httptest` server without tripping the loopback block): `Resolve` found/not-found/cross-org; `Forward` real REST round-trip, real credential decrypt+header application, undecryptable-credentials/empty-base_url/wrong-type/nil-row clean rejections, and the real SSRF guard rejecting `127.0.0.1`/`169.254.169.254` through the actual production dialer. `cmd/gateway/main_pg_test.go` (6 tests for `resolveDynamicTool`'s every branch). `eami-policy/policy_test.go` (`TestMatchesRule_ToolServerIDs`, 5 cases). `eami-gateway/internal/policyloader/loader_pg_test.go` (1 real-DB round-trip test). **Verified 2026-08-06 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-gateway`, `eami-policy`; `eami-api` confirmed unaffected** (untouched module).
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first:** seeded a real org/agent/`gateway_tools` row (real AES-256-GCM-encrypted credentials, real `TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY`), issued a real token, opened a real SSE session, posted a real `tool_call`. **AC1**: routed to the tool's real `base_url` against a real public echo endpoint (`postman-echo.com/post`); confirmed the real decrypted API key arrived as a real `Authorization: Bearer` header at the real destination. **AC3, discovered organically, not just designed as a test case**: the first live-verification attempt used `host.docker.internal` (a local echo server) as the tool's `base_url` — the real SSRF guard correctly rejected it, since Docker Desktop's `host.docker.internal` resolves to a private-range address inside Docker's own network. Genuine, unplanned live proof the guard is active on the real dispatch path. **AC4**: a second real org/agent calling the identical tool name fell through to the exact same static-fallback error shape as an ordinary unregistered call, proving cross-org isolation live. **AC6**: a real `mcp`-type `gateway_tools` row, called live, also fell through to the identical static-fallback path, confirming `mcp`/`database`-type registration doesn't accidentally trigger dynamic dispatch. `audit_log` correctly recorded all four live calls (`allowed` for the real successful dispatch, `denied` for the SSRF-blocked/cross-org/mcp-fallback attempts). All test fixtures cleaned up afterward; final `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` re-run clean post-cleanup.
**Known limitations, documented not silently left:** no `gateway_tools.status` filter (a disabled tool is still routable); `db_connection_string` auth on a `rest_api` row sends no auth header; on-prem customers' legitimate internal REST targets are unreachable by the SSRF guard's design (no allowlist mechanism, flagged for product); ~~no per-endpoint action-to-path mapping (every action for a tool POSTs to the same single `base_url`...)~~ — **closed by B-046, see immediately below.** `mcp`/`database`-type dynamic routing and MCP server capability discovery remain future, separate work.

**Interfaces — B-046 (2026-08-06), per-endpoint action-to-path mapping for `rest_api`-type `gateway_tools`:** closes the gap B-044 itself flagged, confirmed live during that task's own manual testing (a non-`/`-suffixed action against `base_url` alone 404'd — real REST APIs have more than one endpoint). New `gateway_tools.action_paths JSONB` column (migration `010`, `{"<action_name>": {"path": "...", "method": "..."}}`) — JSONB chosen over a new table, matching this schema's own established convention for small structured per-row config (`alert_rules.condition_config`, `notification_config.config`, `episodes.steps`) and avoiding a join on every dispatch for data always read as one small whole per tool. `toolrouter.Router.Forward` (`router.go`): when a resolved tool has `ActionPaths` entries, an incoming action matching one dispatches to `joinURLPath(base_url, entry.Path)` with `entry.Method` (default `POST`, matching the flat-behavior default) instead of always POSTing straight to `base_url`. Request body envelope unchanged (`{tool, action, params, session_id}`) — only the URL path and HTTP method vary per mapped action; deliberate scope boundary to avoid building a generic API-schema importer, per this task's own brief.
**Fallback behavior for an unmapped action, a deliberate product decision, not an oversight:** a tool with **no** `action_paths` at all keeps B-044's exact original flat behavior (every action POSTs to `base_url`) — fully additive, zero effect on any tool that doesn't define mappings. A tool that **has** other mappings defined, called with an action that isn't among them, is a **clean hard rejection** (`toolrouter: tool ... has action mappings defined but none for action "..." -- refusing to fall back to base_url for an unmapped action`), confirmed explicitly with the user before building (not assumed either way) — routing an unmapped action to a URL/credential context the admin never authorized for it was judged worse than a clear failure, matching this same file's existing precedent of rejecting cleanly on unusable state (nil row, wrong type, missing `base_url`, bad creds) rather than silently falling through.
**Security review finding, traced and empirically verified, not just asserted:** the one real question worth answering carefully was whether an admin-supplied `action_paths[action].path` value could redirect a dispatch to a different host than `base_url`'s own (an SSRF-guard bypass) — `joinURLPath` is pure string concatenation (`strings.TrimRight(base,"/") + "/" + strings.TrimLeft(path,"/")`) forming **one** URL string parsed **once**, never a `url.ResolveReference`-style relative resolution (the pattern that has known scheme-relative `"//host"` bypass pitfalls). Verified empirically with a standalone Go program feeding `joinURLPath` six candidate malicious `path` values (`//evil.example.com/steal`, `http://evil.example.com/steal`, `https://evil.example.com/steal`, `@evil.example.com/steal`, a `..%2F..%2F@evil.example.com` traversal attempt) against `base = "https://good.example.com"` and parsing each result with `net/url` — every single one resolved to `Host="good.example.com"`, confirming the SSRF guard (which validates the dialed address derived from the final request URL's host) cannot be bypassed via a crafted mapped path. Added a defense-in-depth write-time guard anyway in `eami-api` (below) rejecting scheme-qualified paths — not because it's exploitable, but because it's a real, easy admin misconfiguration worth catching early with a clear error. `dial.go`/`creds.go` (SSRF guard, credential decrypt) confirmed byte-for-byte untouched by this task, per its explicit scope boundary.
**Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory:** both automated subagent passes failed on a platform-wide session-limit error before completing (not a finding, a tooling outage — resets time noted in-session). Substituted with a direct, careful manual review of the actual diff plus the empirical `joinURLPath` verification described above, rather than skipping the mandatory review step.
**Test coverage:** `internal/toolrouter/router_pg_test.go` gained 5 new real-Postgres tests (`TestResolve_ParsesActionPaths`, `TestForward_ActionPathMapping_RoutesCorrectly` — two different mapped actions, two different methods, both routed correctly; `TestForward_UnmappedActionOnMappedTool_CleanRejection`; `TestForward_NoActionPathsAtAll_FlatBaseURLBehaviorUnchanged`; `TestForward_SSRFStillBlocked_WithActionMapping` — the guard still applies on the mapped-path dispatch branch, not just the flat one) plus a new `insertToolWithActionPaths` fixture helper. **Verified 2026-08-06 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-gateway` module** (17 tests in `internal/toolrouter` alone, all passing against real Postgres).
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first (`eami-api`/`eami-gateway`/`eami-ui` images):** migration `010` applied to the running Postgres; created a real `rest_api` tool (`b046-live-verify`, `base_url=https://postman-echo.com`) via a real admin JWT with two real action mappings (`echo_get`→`GET /get`, `echo_post`→`POST /post`); issued a real agent token, opened a real MCP SSE session, posted three real `tool_call`s. **AC1**: `echo_get` reached `https://postman-echo.com/get` (confirmed via the echoed `"url"` in the real response) with the real decrypted `Authorization: Bearer` header attached; `echo_post` reached `https://postman-echo.com/post` with the correct unchanged envelope in the request body. **AC3**: a third call to an undefined action on that same tool returned the exact designed rejection error over the live SSE stream, not a 200 or a silent fallback. **AC2**: a separate pre-existing tool with zero `action_paths` (left over from B-044's own manual testing) was called with an arbitrary action name and still flatly POSTed to its `base_url`, completely unaffected. **AC4 (credentials/SSRF unchanged)**: real decrypted credentials were injected correctly in every call above; the SSRF guard's continued correctness on the mapped-path branch is covered by the automated real-Postgres test above (not separately re-proven live, since it requires a genuinely blocked target and the empirical `joinURLPath` trace-through already answers the theoretical question directly). One test-environment note: the existing `dev@example.com` admin account's password was reset to a known value to obtain a login token for this verification (no other credential was available) — flagged here since the user may want to reset it again.

**Interfaces — B-061 (2026-08-12), real `tools/list` support for MCP discovery:** `internal/mcp`'s SSE transport previously implemented only the non-standard `tool_call` JSON-RPC method — any other method, including the real spec's `tools/list`, got a `-32601 method not found`, so a connecting agent had to already know exact tool/action names in advance (standing assumption since B-044). Per the MCP Discovery investigation's Part A/B findings, added real `tools/list` so an agent can discover its own org's actual `rest_api` connectors and their known actions. New `ToolDefinition{Name, Description, InputSchema}` (matches the real spec's shape), `ListToolsHandler` (a 4th callback-injection parameter on `mcp.NewHandler`, mirroring `DecisionHandler`'s existing DB-agnostic pattern — `internal/mcp` still has zero direct DB access). `ServeMessages` gained an early `if req.Method == "tools/list"` branch dispatching to new `serveToolsList`; the pre-existing `tool_call` branch below it is byte-for-byte unmodified (confirmed by direct diff review, not assumed). `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s new `listGatewayTools(ctx, pool, orgID)` mirrors `resolveDynamicTool`'s exact org-scoping shape (`WHERE org_id=$1 AND type='rest_api'`), building one `ToolDefinition` per `action_paths` key (`Name="<connector>/<action>"`, matching `splitToolAction`'s primary separator; `Description` synthesized from the real stored method+path, e.g. `GET /get on connector "b046-live-verify"`; `InputSchema={"type":"object"}`, honestly generic since `action_paths` carries no parameter schema).
**Design decision:** only `type='rest_api'` rows are eligible — `ai_provider` connectors dispatch via a completely different mechanism with no `action_paths` concept (representing one as "zero actions" would be misleading, not honestly empty); `mcp`/`database` types have no working direct-dispatch path in `main.go` today. A `rest_api` row with no `action_paths` set (B-044's flat-POST fallback) naturally contributes zero entries — not a separate rule, just the consequence of building the list from `action_paths` keys. `serveToolsList` follows `tool_call`'s exact 202-then-SSE-`message` response mechanism and the same `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())` pattern (B-039) for its detached goroutine.
**Reviewer + security passes, both clean, zero findings.** Code review confirmed `tool_call` is genuinely untouched, `context.WithoutCancel` is applied correctly, and the mandatory `t.Cleanup(pool.Close)`-before-DB-cleanup ordering convention (CLAUDE.md) is followed in every new test helper — no recurrence of the B-056-class bug. Two nitpicks noted, left as-is: non-deterministic map-iteration order in the returned tool list (harmless for `tools/list` semantics); a pre-existing, non-regression nil-handler-panic pattern shared with `dispatch`. Security review confirmed `orgID` is always read from `sess.Agent.OrgID` (registry-resolved from the JWT-validated agent at session creation, never client-supplied on the `tools/list` request itself), the query is fully parameterized, no credential material (`credentials_encrypted`/`base_url`) is ever read into a `ToolDefinition`, and the synthesized `Description` string is safely JSON/SSE-escaped via `encoding/json`.
**Test coverage:** `cmd/gateway/main_tools_list_pg_test.go` — 5 real-Postgres unit-shape tests on `listGatewayTools` (multi-action tool → one definition per action with real method/path; cross-org isolation; `mcp`/`database`/`ai_provider` types all contribute zero; a `rest_api` tool with no `action_paths` contributes zero; empty orgID short-circuits with no query) plus 2 new real end-to-end SSE tests — genuinely new test infrastructure (`readSSEFrame`, no prior test in this module opened a live SSE stream): a real agent JWT opens a real `GET /v1/mcp/sse`, POSTs a real `tools/list`, and reads back the real `message` SSE event, asserting the exact `{"tools":[{"name","description","inputSchema"}]}` shape a real MCP client would parse, built from a real seeded connector with a real `ai_provider` row proven absent from the result; plus a `tool_call` regression test over the same live harness. **Verified 2026-08-12 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -count=1` clean across the full `eami-gateway` module** (14 packages).
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh:** a real agent JWT issued via `POST /v1/gateway/tokens`, a real `GET /v1/mcp/sse` opened, a real `tools/list` POSTed and its real `message` SSE event read back — result showed exactly the two real `b046-live-verify` actions (`echo_get`, `echo_post`) with their real method/path in each description; the org's real `claude` `ai_provider` connector and the two `rest_api` tools with no `action_paths` configured (`manual_verify_tool`, `Test`) correctly did not appear. A follow-up real `tool_call` to `b046-live-verify/echo_get` over the same live transport still dispatched correctly (real response from `postman-echo.com`), confirming no regression outside the automated suite too.
**Known limitations, documented not silently left:** `ai_provider`/`mcp`/`database` tool discovery is out of scope (a natural, separate future brief); no real pagination (`nextCursor` never populated); `InputSchema` is the generic `{"type":"object"}` for every tool, not derived per-parameter (no schema exists in `action_paths` to derive from); no agent-facing client in this codebase actually consumes `tools/list` yet — this closes the server-side protocol gap only.

**Interfaces — B-070 (2026-08-17), per-agent-identity rate limiting on workflow-run:** `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{workflowId}/run` (B-059) had no rate limiting at all — a real resource-exhaustion vector, since each run is fully synchronous and can block for up to 10 minutes on an escalation `Hold()`. New `internal/workflow/ratelimit.go`: a small in-memory, per-key fixed-window `rateLimiter` (deliberately duplicated from `eami-api/internal/api`'s identical type — `eami-gateway`/`eami-api` are separate Go modules under `go.work`, matching this repo's own B-025/B-044 precedent for this exact situation) plus `RateLimitRunMiddleware(idm, resolver, limit, window, next)`, wrapping `workflowHTTP.HandleRun` at its `mux.HandleFunc` registration in `cmd/gateway/main.go` — **this module has zero chi dependency at all** (routing is stdlib `http.ServeMux`, confirmed by grep before building), so the brief's own assumption of reusing "chi's rate-limiting middleware" didn't apply here; a hand-rolled wrapper around the raw `http.HandlerFunc` was the only option. `HandleRun`/`workflow/http.go` are **byte-for-byte unmodified** — the middleware duplicates exactly two read-only, side-effect-free things `HandleRun` already does (bearer JWT validation via `identity.Manager.Validate`, agent-name resolution via the same `AgentResolver`) purely to learn the calling agent's identity before deciding whether to rate-limit; missing/invalid auth or an unresolvable agent name is NOT rejected by the middleware — it passes through unchanged so `HandleRun` produces its own real 401/403, avoiding two slightly different auth-error code paths. New `config.RateLimitConfig` (`WORKFLOW_RUN_RATE_LIMIT_PER_AGENT`/`WORKFLOW_RUN_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS`, defaults 5/60s — chosen because a single run can legitimately hold resources for up to 10 minutes, so the real threat is a bursty retry/runaway-agent loop starting many overlapping runs, not sustained high-frequency polling).
**Ambiguously-confirmed single-instance assumption, checked not assumed:** ADR-020 Model A (the only deployment model that exists today, `DECISIONS.md`) is a fully self-contained one-process-per-service appliance — an in-memory limiter is the whole story here, not a partial one, same reasoning already established for B-055's setup-wizard limiter.
**A real cross-org rate-limit bypass found by this task's own mandatory security review, before shipping, not after:** the first version keyed the limiter on the raw JWT `claims.Subject` (an agent name, e.g. `"agent:researcher"`). `gateway_agents.name` is unique only per `(org_id, name)` (`schema.sql`'s own `UNIQUE` constraint), **not globally** — two different orgs can each register an agent named `"researcher"`, and both tokens would carry the identical `claims.Subject`, so one org's agent exhausting its quota would also rate-limit the other org's identically-named agent, which had made zero requests. Fixed by keying on the **resolved** `agentRec.ID` (a real, globally-unique registry UUID) instead of the bare JWT subject — the same class of fix B-042's `registry.LookupByNameAndOrg` made for the token-revoke path, applied here more simply since a resolved `*registry.AgentRecord` already carries a real unique primary key.
**A second real finding from the same review, fixed:** `rateLimiter.Allow` indexed `kept[0]` once `len(kept) >= rl.limit`, which is immediately true (`0 >= 0`) for a non-positive `limit` on the very first request — a misconfigured (e.g. typo'd `"0"`) env var wouldn't disable limiting, it would **panic every request** to the guarded route. Fixed two ways: `Allow` now fails closed (denies, doesn't panic) for `limit <= 0`, independent of config; `config.go`'s `validate()` also now explicitly rejects a negative `WORKFLOW_RUN_RATE_LIMIT_PER_AGENT`/`_WINDOW_SECONDS` at startup (0 is still treated as "use the default," matching this file's own existing convention for every other zero-valued field).
**Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory (explicit security-hardening brief):** code review's automated pass found the two real issues above (cross-org key collision, non-positive-limit panic) plus a third finding shared with the `eami-api` side (see that module's B-070 entry) — all three fixed before shipping, not deferred.
**Test coverage:** `internal/workflow/ratelimit_test.go` — 7 tests against a fake `AgentResolver` (avoids a real registry/DB; these tests are about the rate-limit decision itself) and a real ephemeral-keypair `*identity.Manager`: same-agent threshold trip with `Retry-After`; independent quotas for two genuinely different agents; **`TestRateLimitRunMiddleware_SameName_DifferentOrgs_IndependentLimits`**, the regression test for the cross-org fix — two tokens with the identical `claims.Subject` shape, resolved via a context-value-keyed fake resolver to two distinct `AgentRecord`s (same `Name`, different `ID`/`OrgID`), proving org A exhausting its quota never touches org B's identically-named agent; single-run-not-blocked; invalid-auth and unresolvable-agent pass-through (both confirm `HandleRun`'s own error path is reached, not intercepted). **Verified 2026-08-17 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./... -count=1` clean across the full `eami-gateway` module**, including this module's pre-existing real-Postgres suites (`internal/identity`, `internal/toolrouter`, `internal/approval`, etc.), confirmed unaffected.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (before AND after the security-review fixes):** issued a real agent JWT via `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` for a real registered agent (`manual-test-agent`), then rapid-fired 6 real `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{workflowId}/run` calls (a bogus `workflowId`, so `HandleRun` itself returns a real `400` — proving the request genuinely reached the handler each time, not blocked earlier): the first 5 returned real `400`s, the 6th returned a real `429` with `Retry-After: 60`, exactly matching the configured window. See the `eami-api` B-070 entry below for the shared login/setup-wizard verification run in the same session.
**Known limitations, disclosed not hidden:** distributed/multi-instance rate limiting is explicitly out of scope (ADR-020 Model A is single-instance; matches this brief's own stated boundary); no rate limiting on any other gateway route (MCP `tool_call`/`tools/list`, episode read, token issue/revoke) — a natural, separate follow-up, not attempted here.

**Bugfix — B-099 (2026-08-23), escalated (approval-held) tool calls now record token usage — closes the gap B-097's AC4 found live.** `extractTokenUsage`/`safeWriteTokenUsage` previously existed only in the immediate-Allow dispatch branch (`main.go`); the escalate branch returned its `Hold()` result directly, never reaching that code, so any org with an active escalate policy on an AI-provider connector recorded zero real cost/token data for those calls, silently.
**Investigated the real design choice per the brief's own assumption, not defaulted either way:** traced `Hold()`'s return values (`result, holdErr`) and `outcomeFromStatus` (`approval/router.go`) directly — confirmed `result` is already the real downstream response body (`decisionResult{data: tr.Body, err: proxyErr}` from `dispatchApproved`'s "approved" case), identical in shape to the Allow branch's `tr.Body`, and `holdErr == nil` if and only if the escalation was genuinely approved *and* the resumed dispatch actually succeeded (every non-approved status returns `decisionResult{err: ...}` with no data). This meant the fix needed **zero changes to `approval/router.go`** — everything needed was already available in `main.go` after `Hold()` returns.
**Design: a shared helper, not a duplicated call** — new `recordTokenUsage(apiBaseURL, apiServiceKey string, body json.RawMessage, ac mcp.ActionContext)` (`main.go`, next to `extractTokenUsage`), called from both the Allow branch (replacing its inline two lines) and the Escalate branch (new, gated on `if holdErr == nil`, right after `Hold()` returns). Chosen over duplicating the two-line snippet because the exact failure mode this brief exists to fix — one branch's post-dispatch step silently not existing in the other — is precisely what a shared helper structurally prevents from recurring.
**AC2 (denied escalation records no usage) was already correctly handled by existing code, not newly built** — a denied/expired/failed-resume decision already produces `holdErr != nil` via `outcomeFromStatus`'s existing `default` branch; the fix only needed to gate the new call on that pre-existing signal.
**B-057's TOCTOU/resume logic is untouched by construction, not just by discipline** — the fix only reads `Hold()`'s existing return values; no line in `approval/router.go` changed.
**Reviewer + security passes both ran (mandatory, touches the dispatch closure directly).** Security review: zero findings — no new input source, no new trust boundary, no new destination; the new call site reuses the exact same already-reviewed write path with the same-shape data the Allow branch has always sent. **Code review found a real, pre-existing, HIGH-severity bug independent of this fix, not introduced by it — logged separately as B-099, not fixed here (see `BACKLOG.md`):** `Hold()`'s own timeout backstop (`router.go:334-352`) can call `dispatchApproved` a **second time** in a narrow race window (a decision landing right at the ~600s hold-timeout boundary while the real dispatch triggered by `resolve()`'s `LISTEN/NOTIFY` path is still in flight) — a governed action can genuinely execute twice against a real downstream system, silently. This diff's own `recordTokenUsage` call can't itself double-fire (`Hold()` only ever returns once), but the deeper bug means the second real dispatch's spend goes unrecorded regardless — a downstream symptom of a resume-path bug, not something fixable within this brief's `MUST NOT MODIFY: ... the resume-path fix itself` boundary. **Traced and confirmed directly against the real code before logging**, not taken on the reviewer's word alone. Also logged **B-101** (Low): the `holdErr == nil` gate isn't independently unit-tested outside live verification, since `dispatch` is an inline closure inside `run()`, not a testable unit without a refactor out of this brief's scope.
**Test coverage:** `cmd/gateway/main_test.go` gained `TestRecordTokenUsage_ExtractsAndWrites` (the shared helper performs the identical extract-then-write sequence the Allow branch always has) and `TestRecordTokenUsage_CalledTwiceIndependently` (proves the helper is genuinely reusable across two independent `ActionContext`s without cross-contaminating state — the exact property this fix depends on). The full escalate→approve→dispatch cycle itself is not independently unit-testable — `dispatch` is an inline closure inside `run()`, matching this exact code path's established precedent (B-039/B-042/B-097's AC4 were all verified live, never via a full closure-level Go test) — so AC1/AC2/AC4 were proven live instead, per the brief's own TEST REQUIREMENT. **Verified 2026-08-23: `go build`/`go vet` clean; full `go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module**, including every pre-existing `internal/approval` TOCTOU/`dispatchApproved` test — zero regressions.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt and redeployed with the fix:** **AC1** — the exact scenario that found this bug, re-run: real agent JWT, real MCP SSE session, real `tool_call` to the org's real `claude` connector, real approval via `POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decide` — a genuine successful Anthropic response (`usage:{input_tokens:8,output_tokens:1}`) now produces a real `token_usage` row (`model=claude-haiku-4-5-20251001, tokens_in=8, tokens_out=1, cost_usd=0.000010`), where before the fix it produced none. **AC2** — a second escalation, denied via the real API, confirmed a real denial error over SSE and zero new `token_usage` rows (count unchanged). **AC3** — a real dispatch to a non-escalated `rest_api` connector (`b046-live-verify/echo_get`, confirmed via `policy_conditions` to not match either active escalate policy) still wrote a `token_usage` row exactly as before, proving no regression to the immediate-Allow path. **AC4** — the real TOCTOU-drift scenario re-run after the fix, per explicit instruction to empirically re-confirm rather than rely on the structural argument: a third escalation's connector credentials were rotated mid-hold (backed up and restored entirely inside Postgres via a temporary table, never touching the real key bytes outside the database) — the approved resume correctly refused with the real `"configuration changed... refusing to resume against a different destination than the approver reviewed"` error, **and correctly recorded zero token usage for the refused call**, proving B-099's own gate and B-057's TOCTOU guarantee compose correctly together. Real credentials restored and confirmed working with one final real dispatch afterward. All test approval/token-usage rows left in place in Dev Org as a real audit trail of the verification, matching this codebase's established precedent for this exact live-verification pattern.
**Dependencies:** B-097 (found this gap). **Out of scope, as specified:** B-100 (the double-dispatch race) and B-101 (test-coverage gap), both logged separately, neither fixed here.

**Bugfix — B-100 (2026-08-23), `Hold()`'s timeout backstop can no longer double-dispatch to a downstream connector — closes the race B-099's code review found and this session's own investigation reproduced deterministically (5/5 runs) before building.**
**Confirmed mechanism, re-verified against current code before building, not assumed:** `resolve()` (the `LISTEN/NOTIFY` path) and `Hold()`'s own timeout backstop (`router.go`) could both reach `outcomeFromStatus` → `dispatchApproved` — the real downstream call — for the same `approvalID`, when a decision landed near the hold deadline while `resolve()`'s real dispatch was still in flight. Worse than first characterized: `Hold()` always returned its own **second**, redundant dispatch's result; the first, genuinely real dispatch's result was silently discarded every time.
**Fix: `sync.Once` on `pendingEntry`, not a DB-level lock — investigated and reasoned about explicitly, not defaulted to either option.** New `once sync.Once` + `result decisionResult` fields on `pendingEntry`; both call sites that reach `outcomeFromStatus` (`resolve()` and `Hold()`'s `ErrNoRows` fallback) now wrap the call in `entry.once.Do(...)`. Correct specifically because this race is provably **intra-process**: `resolve()` only ever acts on a *local* `pendingEntry` (its own doc comment already states a different node's notification is silently dropped if no local entry exists), so a DB-level lock would solve a cross-node problem that structurally cannot occur here, at real unnecessary cost. `sync.Once`'s documented happens-before guarantee makes reading the shared `entry.result` immediately after `Do()` returns race-free with no extra locking, in either the winning or losing goroutine.
**A real, substantive finding from the mandatory code-review pass, investigated and factually corrected before accepting — not rubber-stamped, not dismissed either:** the review flagged that the losing caller's block inside `Do()` is no longer bounded by its own `ctx`/`holdTimeout`, citing "a disconnected caller would have aborted the redundant dispatch faster pre-fix." Traced this claim against the actual production call chain and found it factually inaccurate for this codebase: `Hold()` has exactly one production caller (`cmd/gateway/main.go:359`), fed via `internal/mcp/handler.go`'s `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())` — already stripped of disconnect-cancellation by B-039's own established design, specifically so a multi-minute hold can outlive the original HTTP request. So client disconnect never cancelled either the pre-fix or post-fix fallback dispatch. The narrower, real point underneath the finding — the loser's wait is now bounded by the *winner's* dispatch duration (itself bounded only by the downstream HTTP client's shared ~30s default timeout across `internal/proxy`/`internal/toolrouter`/`internal/aiprovider`) rather than by anything Hold()-specific — is real but **not a regression**: pre-fix, the loser ran its own full duplicate `dispatchApproved` call in this exact same branch, using the same uncancellable `ctx`, equally unbounded by `holdTimeout`. Documented explicitly in `pendingEntry`'s own doc comment (not left implicit) so a future reader doesn't have to re-derive this. The review's second finding (initial 50ms/300ms test timing margins risked CI flakiness) was accepted and fixed directly — widened to 150ms/750ms (5x margin), confirmed stable across repeated runs.
**Security review: zero findings.** No new input source, no new trust boundary, no new destination; `entry.once`/`entry.result` are pure in-memory state scoped one-per-approval via the existing `r.pending` map. Net effect on risk is a strict reduction (closes a real duplicate-real-world-side-effect issue), not a new attack surface.
**Blast radius investigated concretely, not abstractly, before building (part of the preceding investigation, re-confirmed here):** for `ai_provider` connectors, a double-dispatch meant a real second POST to Anthropic with identical parameters — duplicate spend, and possibly a duplicate downstream action if the agent acted on the duplicate completion. For `rest_api` connectors, the identical HTTP request sent twice to the connector's real `base_url` with no idempotency key anywhere in the code — concretely, a duplicate payment against a payments API, a duplicate ticket, a duplicate message to a real recipient, indistinguishable at the network layer from two genuinely separate legitimate calls. Confirmed via `dispatchApproved`'s unconditional per-call `ComputeConfigHash` re-verification that this race never interacted with or weakened B-057's TOCTOU guarantee — orthogonal properties (destination correctness vs. dispatch count), the race never let a mid-hold config edit go undetected.
**Test coverage:** new `router_race_test.go` — `TestHold_ResolveRace_DoesNotDoubleDispatch` is a real adversarial reproduction (a slow fake downstream + a hold timeout with a deliberate 5x margin under the downstream's response latency, directly driving `Hold()` and `resolve()` concurrently against a real inserted `approval_requests` row), asserting the real downstream hit count is exactly 1. **Proven meaningful, not vacuous, by direct demonstration:** temporarily reverted the fix (`git stash`) and re-ran the test — it correctly failed with `hitCount=2` and two "approved — resuming original call" log lines, exactly reproducing B-099's original finding; restored the fix and confirmed green again before proceeding. `TestHold_ResolveRace_NormalTiming_SingleDispatch` is the negative control (normal timing, no race) proving the fix doesn't change behavior outside the race window. **`-race` could not be run** — this machine has no C compiler installed (`gcc`/`cc`/`clang` all absent), and `-race` requires cgo; disclosed rather than silently skipped. Full `internal/approval` regression suite re-run clean, including every pre-existing `TestHold_TimeoutRace_Honors*` and `TestDispatchApproved_*`/TOCTOU test — zero regressions. **Verified 2026-08-23: `go build`/`go vet` clean; full `go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module.**
**Live-verified against the real redeployed stack (AC1/AC2 proven at the Go level per the investigation's own honest assessment that a sub-second timing race isn't meaningfully provable against a 600s production default; AC3-AC6 verified live):** **AC3** — a normal escalate→approve→dispatch cycle to the org's real `claude` connector, unaffected, real successful response. **AC4** — a normal escalate→deny cycle, unaffected, zero new `token_usage` rows. **AC5** — the real TOCTOU-drift scenario re-run (credentials rotated mid-hold, backed up/restored entirely inside Postgres, never exposed outside the database): resume still correctly refused with the exact `"configuration changed"` error. **AC6** — B-099's `recordTokenUsage` confirmed firing exactly once per real dispatch (`token_usage` count incremented by exactly 1 for the approve cycle, by 0 for the deny and TOCTOU-refused cycles). Real credentials restored and confirmed working with a final live dispatch. All test approval/token-usage rows left in place in Dev Org as a real audit trail, matching this session's established precedent.
**Dependencies:** B-099 (found this gap). **Out of scope, as specified:** any cross-node/distributed locking mechanism (confirmed unnecessary — this race is provably intra-process); any other approval mechanism change.

**Refactor — B-102 (2026-08-24), `dispatch` extracted into a testable `Dispatcher` type with a branch-convergence hook mechanism — closes B-101 and structurally closes B-099's bug class.** Followed the 2026-08-23 extensibility investigation's own finding (delivered in-session that day, not re-derived): `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s `dispatch` closure was the one real site in `eami-gateway`/`eami-api` with the multi-branch fan-out shape that caused B-099 (a post-dispatch step manually repeated across independent branch returns, with no structural guarantee nothing gets missed).
**What shipped:** new `cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go` — `Dispatcher` (holds the exact same collaborators the old closure captured from `run()`), `NewDispatcher` (builds a literal two-entry hook list, `recordTokenUsageHook`/`recordEpisodeHook`, plus a variadic `extraHooks` test-only seam), `DispatchOutcome` (a real typed struct — `Decision`, `Result`, `Err`, `Dispatched`, `EpisodeSteps`, `EpisodeOutcome` — not `map[string]any`), and `Dispatcher.Dispatch` (the old closure's exact logic, restructured so every branch converges on one shared exit that runs the hook list, instead of five independent `return` statements). `main.go`'s `run()` now does `dispatcher := NewDispatcher(...)` and passes `dispatcher.Dispatch` everywhere `dispatch` used to go (`mcp.NewHandler`, `workflow.New`) — a method value with the identical `mcp.DecisionHandler` signature, so `internal/workflow` needed zero changes and inherits the fix for free (workflows call `dispatch` once per step, unmodified, per B-058/B-059's own design).
**Deliberately NOT a generic mechanism:** no Publish/Subscribe registry, no event bus, no channels, no global/`init()`-time registration — the hook list is a literal two-entry slice built once in `NewDispatcher`, mirroring `aiprovider.Router`'s own explicit `map[string]Adapter` (B-057). The 2026-08-23 investigation's exhaustive inventory found exactly one site needing this; `internal/approval/router.go` (untouched by this brief), `internal/workflow`, and every `eami-api` CRUD handler were each individually confirmed not to have the same shape.
**Mandatory code-review pass (general-purpose subagent, effort "high") caught 4 real findings before shipping, all fixed:**
1. **The Escalate branch's own `Submit()`-failure case still `return`ed independently**, bypassing the entire hook list — the exact bug class this brief exists to eliminate, reintroduced inside the mechanism meant to prevent it, and directly contradicted this file's own doc comments ("there is only one place any branch can reach to return at all"). Fixed: converges via `break` into the same single exit as every other case. Regression-tested by `TestDispatch_Escalate_SubmitFails_StillConverges`, which also confirms `Dispatched=false` and an empty `EpisodeSteps` for this case — preserving the pre-existing "Submit failure never wrote an episode" behavior exactly, not silently starting to write one.
2. **File organization:** the ~400-line mechanism was added inline into `main.go`, inconsistent with `CLAUDE.md`'s "one file per concern" Go convention and with `dispatcher_test.go` already living in its own file. Fixed: moved into `cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go` (same package, zero behavior change, `run()`'s call sites unchanged).
3. **`DispatchOutcome.Dispatched` was set independently by each branch's literal, but was always exactly `Err == nil` in every one of them** — the same "two things that must independently be kept consistent" risk this whole refactor exists to eliminate, now reintroduced inside the fix itself. Fixed: computed once, centrally (`outcome.Dispatched = outcome.Err == nil`), immediately before the hook loop, removed from every branch literal.
4. **Four `episode.Step` literals duplicated verbatim** across branches (identical shape, varying only `Decision`/`Result`). Fixed: extracted `newEpisodeStep(ac, decision, result)`.
Security review (same pass): zero findings — pure structural refactor, no new input source, trust boundary, or destination; `internal/approval/router.go` not touched at all.
**Test coverage:** 7 new tests, `cmd/gateway/dispatcher_test.go`, all real-Postgres integration tests requiring no live server: `TestDispatch_Deny/Allow/Escalate_Approved_NoFullServerNeeded` prove `Dispatch` is directly constructable and callable for every decision type (AC1); `TestDispatch_TokenUsageHook_FiresForAllow`/`FiresForApprovedEscalate` share one assertion helper (`assertDispatchedHookFired`) across both an immediate-Allow and a genuine escalate→approve→resume cycle (AC3, proving the convergence itself, not just that both branches happen to work); `TestDispatch_NewHook_FiresForAllThreeDecisionTypes` is the central proof — a test-only counter hook added only via `extraHooks` fires exactly once for Deny/Escalate/Allow with zero branch-specific code in `Dispatch` to register it (AC4); `TestDispatch_Escalate_SubmitFails_StillConverges` is the code-review regression test (finding 1 above), forcing `Submit()` to fail deterministically via its own existing empty-`OrgID` validation (`router.go:240-242`) rather than breaking connectivity. New harness helpers: `mainTestEnv.insertAgent` (`approval_requests.agent_id` is `NOT NULL REFERENCES gateway_agents(id)`, `schema.sql:273`) and `fakeEvaluator` (implements `policy.Evaluator`, returns a fixed `Decision` per test so each case can be forced directly without seeding real policy rules) — both new in `dispatcher_test.go`, built on the pre-existing `mainTestEnv`/`newMainTestEnv` harness (`main_pg_test.go`) and `mainTestEnv.insertAIProviderTool` (`main_ai_provider_pg_test.go`), following the same real-toolRouter/real-aiProviderRouter/real-approvalRouter-with-LISTEN/NOTIFY-actually-running pattern `internal/workflow/testenv_test.go` already proved for reconstructing dispatch's real pipeline (duplicated, not imported — `cmd/gateway` is Go's `main` package and cannot be imported by any other package's tests, so `workflow/testenv_test.go`'s own hand-duplication of `dispatch`'s body remains necessary and unaffected by this brief). **Verified 2026-08-24 with a real toolchain and a real Postgres (`docker compose up -d postgres`, `TEST_DATABASE_URL` set): `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test -count=1 ./...` all clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module** — every pre-existing `internal/approval` TOCTOU/`dispatchApproved`/`TestHold_ResolveRace_*` test and the full `internal/workflow` suite (17 tests) re-run clean, confirming AC5/AC6 (zero regressions, workflow execution unaffected).
**Known, pre-existing, non-blocking test artifact, disclosed not hidden:** every real-Postgres test in this package (and in `internal/workflow`'s own pre-existing suite, unrelated to this brief) logs a `WARN episode: db write failed ... violates foreign key constraint "episodes_org_id_fkey"` — `episodeRecorder.Record` is fire-and-forget (`go d.episodeRecorder.Record(...)`), so its async INSERT can race a test's own `t.Cleanup`-registered `DELETE FROM orgs`, which cascades before the goroutine's insert lands. Confirmed pre-existing (identical warning already present in `internal/workflow`'s existing, unmodified suite) and harmless (the error is logged, never propagated — matches `episode.Recorder.Record`'s own documented fire-and-forget contract); not fixed here, out of this brief's scope.
**Dependencies:** B-099 (found this gap), B-100 (confirmed unaffected). **Closes B-101** as a direct consequence (`BACKLOG.md` updated). **Out of scope, as specified:** any repo-wide event-bus/registry abstraction; the B-103 (adapter-pattern docs) and B-104 (DataTable adoption) items sequenced separately from this investigation, neither touched here.

**Docs — B-103 (2026-08-24), the `aiprovider.Adapter` pattern (B-057) is now a documented standing convention, not just tribal knowledge in a doc comment.** No code changed — `aiprovider/types.go`/`router.go` are exactly as they were since B-078.
**Placement, investigated before writing, not assumed:** the brief's own SCOPE offered `ARCHITECTURE.md` or a new ADR as the two candidate locations. `BOUNDARIES.md`'s explicit ownership table assigns `ARCHITECTURE.md` to Architect-EAMI ("PM and Architect jointly maintain") and `DECISIONS.md` (the ADR log) to PM-EAMI ("PM writes; Architect reviews") — both outside this session's file boundary per that doc's own stated "Golden Rule" ("cross-boundary changes require a written handoff task"). Documented instead as a new bullet in `CLAUDE.md`'s `## Conventions` section — not listed in `BOUNDARIES.md`'s ownership table at all, and the one doc guaranteed to be read by every future session (unlike `ARCHITECTURE.md`, which `ADR-020` already found stale elsewhere — its Serf/gossip-mesh section describes infrastructure `B-080`'s investigation confirmed was never built). Confirmed with the user before writing, per this brief's own plan-then-approve kickoff.
**What the new bullet says:** when adding a new backend TYPE behind an existing dispatch surface (a second AI provider, a Skill, an A2A agent), define a small interface, implement it in one new file per backend, and register the instance in an explicit `map[string]Adapter` passed to the constructor — never global/`init()`-time registration — citing `aiprovider/types.go`/`router.go`/`ClaudeAdapter` as the real working example. Explicitly distinguishes this ("new backend TYPE") from B-102's dispatch-hook mechanism ("new cross-cutting reaction to an existing call") so the two aren't conflated by a future reader scoping a brief.
**Also discovered, flagged, not fixed (outside this brief's file boundary):** `ARCHITECTURE.md` §3.3 (eami-gateway's "Key packages" list) is missing `internal/aiprovider` entirely — a real staleness gap, consistent with the same pattern `ADR-020`/`B-080` already found elsewhere in that file. Noted here for whoever owns that file next; not touched.
**Dependencies:** none. Independent of B-102/B-104.

**Interfaces — B-098 (2026-08-24), `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` is finally authenticated:** previously zero auth — anyone reaching the gateway's port could mint a valid AI-agent token for any existing agent name, with `req.AgentID` trusted as opaque client input straight into the JWT `Subject`. `Manager.HandleIssue` deleted entirely; new `internal/identity/issue_http.go`'s `IssueHandler` (mirrors `revoke_http.go`'s `AgentResolver`-as-test-seam pattern) requires a real, unrevoked, unexpired, agent-scoped `api_keys` row (`X-API-Key` header) — a feature that existed with a real Settings UI and real storage but enforced nothing anywhere (`GetAPIKeyByHash` had zero callers). New `internal/identity/apikey.go` (`APIKeyValidator`, same-DB Postgres query, SHA-256 hash matching `eami-api/internal/auth.APIKeyFromRaw`'s exact format) and `events.go` (`TokenEventStore`, writes to new `ai_token_events` table). The requested agent is resolved via `registry.LookupByNameAndOrg` scoped to the **validated key's own org** (never client input) and rejected unless the resolved agent's real UUID equals the key's bound `agent_id` — the actual cross-agent scoping proof. JWT `Subject` is rebuilt from the resolved record's canonical name. `revoke_http.go` gains a `TokenEventStore` param, writing a `revoked` event after each successful `Revoke()`.
**Schema (`schema/migrations-v2/000010_gateway_token_auth.up.sql`):** `api_keys.agent_id UUID REFERENCES gateway_agents(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` (nullable — org-scoped-only keys, e.g. collector ingest, still work; only an agent-scoped key authorizes gateway token issuance). New `ai_token_events` table (org_id FK-cascaded; `agent_id`/`agent_name`/`api_key_id` are snapshot columns with no FK, mirroring B-087's `agent_lifecycle_events` reasoning — a machine-presented API key has no human `performed_by`, a different concern from that table's admin-CRUD scope).
**Mandatory reviewer + security subagent passes, both found and this brief fixed real issues:** security review (independent fork, full trace of cross-agent/cross-org bypass, SQL injection, hash-algorithm correctness, timing side-channel, events-table injection): zero HIGH-confidence findings, several claims empirically verified against the real running gateway. A same-org agent-name-enumeration oracle (two different 403 messages for "agent doesn't exist" vs. "agent exists but key isn't scoped to it") found independently during the same pass — unified into one message, live-reverified. Code review (general-purpose subagent, "high" effort): found `GetAPIKeyByHash` (eami-api) didn't filter `expires_at` unlike this package's own new validator for the identical row (fixed, see eami-api section), a UI raw-UUID fallback (fixed, see eami-ui section), and disclosed-not-fixed: `HandleIssue` now costs 3-4 serial Postgres round trips per issuance versus the previous single in-process sign — logged as **B-107**, out of this security-fix brief's scope.
**Test coverage:** `internal/identity/issue_http_pg_test.go` — 9 new real-Postgres tests covering all 6 acceptance criteria (missing/unknown/revoked/expired key, cross-agent rejection, correct-scope success) plus an org-scoped-only-key case. `revoke_http_pg_test.go` updated for the new `TokenEventStore` param, all 5 pre-existing tests still pass. **Verified 2026-08-24: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module** against a real Postgres, zero regressions across every pre-existing package.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt and restarted before and after the code-review fixes:** all 6 ACs proven via real `curl` against the real containerized gateway — no key → 401; a key scoped to a different real agent in the same real org → 403 (the centerpiece AC2 proof); correctly-scoped key → real 200 with a real signed JWT; revoked key → 401; expired key → 401; not-yet-expired key → 200. Both `issued` and `revoked` `ai_token_events` rows confirmed live via direct `psql`, matching the returned `jti`. The unified-error-message fix reconfirmed live after rebuild. All fixtures cleaned up afterward (cascaded via `org_id ON DELETE CASCADE`).
**Known limitations:** see **B-107** (round-trip count, disclosed not fixed) and **B-106** (`openapi.yaml` doesn't yet document `agent_id`/`expires_at`/`jti`, Architect-EAMI-owned, out of this session's boundary) in `BACKLOG.md`.

**Interfaces — B-108 (2026-08-25), `tokenUsagePayload` gains `ToolName`:** `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s `extractTokenUsage` now sets `ToolName` from `ac.Tool` unconditionally, at construction — the connector this call dispatched through, previously known at the call site but never carried into the payload eami-api writes to `token_usage.tool_name` (a column that existed but was always `NULL`). Deliberately set before the early-return for an empty/unparseable downstream result body, unlike `Model`/token counts, which do depend on that body parsing — `ToolName` doesn't. No dispatch logic touched; the full detail (query/UI side) is in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api`/`eami-ui` sections under the same B-108 entry.
**Test coverage:** `main_test.go` gains a `ToolName` assertion in the existing `TestRecordTokenUsage_ExtractsAndWrites` plus a new `TestExtractTokenUsage_ToolNameSurvivesUnparseableBody` proving the field survives both an empty body and a genuinely unparseable one. **Verified 2026-08-25: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module.**

**Bugfix — B-111 (2026-08-26), `extractTokenUsage` now parses Anthropic prompt-caching token counts:** previously only `usage.input_tokens`/`usage.output_tokens` were parsed — `usage.cache_creation_input_tokens`/`usage.cache_read_input_tokens` (plus a nested `usage.cache_creation.{ephemeral_5m_input_tokens,ephemeral_1h_input_tokens}` breakdown) were silently dropped by Go's unmarshal on every caching dispatch. `tokenUsagePayload` gains `CacheCreation5mTokens`/`CacheCreation1hTokens`/`CacheReadTokens` (raw counts only — no cost computed here, see the `eami-api` entry below for why). Parsing logic: when the nested `cache_creation` breakdown is present, trust it directly (the exact 5m/1h split); when absent, fall back to attributing the flat `cache_creation_input_tokens` total to the 5m tier (Anthropic's default TTL) rather than dropping it.
**A genuinely unresolved question at plan time, resolved empirically rather than guessed:** Anthropic's docs only showed the `cache_creation` breakdown object for a *mixed* 5m+1h example; whether it's present for a single-TTL response wasn't documented either way. Live-verified directly against the real API before finalizing this code: a real `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` dispatch with a pure 1h-only `cache_control` (no 5m mixed in) returned `"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":4812}` — the breakdown **is** present for single-TTL responses too. The flat-total fallback branch is kept as defensive-only (documented in code as not observed reachable against the real API), not deleted.
**Also re-verified, not assumed stale, before building:** 1-hour cache TTL no longer requires a beta header (a plan-time assumption carried over from earlier knowledge) — it's just a `"ttl":"1h"` field inside the request's own `cache_control` block. Since `ClaudeAdapter.Dispatch` (`internal/aiprovider/claude.go`) forwards `req.Params` verbatim, 1h caching is fully reachable through EAMI's dispatch path today, confirmed via the same live dispatch above.
**Test coverage:** `main_test.go` gains 4 new tests — `TestExtractTokenUsage_CacheBreakdownPresent_UsesExactSplit` (built from the exact real response JSON captured live), `TestExtractTokenUsage_CacheRead_NoBreakdownNeeded`, `TestExtractTokenUsage_CacheBreakdownAbsent_FallsBackToFlat5m` (the defensive branch), `TestExtractTokenUsage_NoCaching_CacheFieldsStayZero` (regression guard). **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module**, zero regressions in any pre-existing token-usage test.
**Dependencies:** none new. Full live-verification and cross-module detail in `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api` section under the same B-111 entry, and `BACKLOG.md`'s B-111 entry.

**Interfaces — B-093 (2026-08-26), `audit_log` gains `workflow_run_id`/`step_index` linkage:** `mcp.ActionContext` (`internal/mcp/handler.go`) gains `WorkflowRunID string`/`StepIndex *int32` — empty/nil for every standalone MCP `tool_call`, since only `workflow/executor.go`'s `runStep` ever sets them, right alongside its existing `stepAC.Tool =`/`.Action =` assignments (`stepAC.WorkflowRunID = runID.String()`, `stepAC.StepIndex = &step.StepOrder`). `audit.Entry`/`internal/audit/writer.go`'s `InsertEntry` thread both through as two new nullable `audit_log` columns (`schema/migrations-v2/000012`, no FK — matches `policy_id`/`approval_id`/`data_handling_designation`'s existing FK-less convention on this table exactly) — **deliberately excluded from the hash `content` string**, same B-078 `DataHandling` precedent, re-confirmed directly against `writer.go` before this migration was written. `cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go`'s `auditEntry` construction parses `ac.WorkflowRunID` (empty string parses to `uuid.Nil`, `audit.Entry`'s own "not a workflow call" sentinel) and copies `ac.StepIndex` straight through.
**A real, previously-masked pre-existing bug found while writing this brief's own tests (`eami-gateway/internal/workflow` package), not introduced by it:** the shared test helper `allowAllRules()` uses the literal string `"allow-all"` as a `policy.Rule.ID` — but `audit_log.policy_id` is a real `uuid` column, so any allow-path dispatch matching this rule fails its `audit_log` write with `invalid input syntax for type uuid`, silently, since every existing caller discards the write error (`_ = auditWriter.Write(...)`) and none previously read back the resulting row. B-093's own new tests were the first to check and hit it immediately. Confirmed test-fixture-only, not production (a real policy's ID from the `policies` table is always a genuine UUID); worked around locally via a new `allowAllRulesRealPolicyID()` helper in the two new tests, not fixed in the shared one (would touch every other already-passing test's fixture for no benefit). Logged fresh as **B-115**.
**Test coverage:** `internal/audit/writer_pg_test.go` gains `TestWriter_RealDB_WorkflowLinkage_PersistsCorrectly` (round trip, including a real "step 0" surviving as non-NULL, distinct from "absent") and `TestWriter_RealDB_HashChainRemainsValid_WithWorkflowLinkage` (AC4 — mirrors the file's own pre-existing `..._WithDataHandling` test exactly). `internal/workflow/executor_test.go` gains `TestExecutor_Run_AuditLogRecordsWorkflowRunIDAndStepIndex` (AC3 centerpiece) and `TestExecutor_Run_StandaloneDispatch_AuditLogWorkflowLinkageIsNull`. `internal/workflow/testenv_test.go`'s hand-reconstructed `dispatch` closure (real Postgres, no `cmd/gateway` import possible — see that file's own header) needed the identical two-line `WorkflowRunID`/`StepIndex` wiring applied a second time, kept in sync with the real `dispatcher.go` fix. **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module**, zero regressions; `schema/migrationtest`'s full suite (fresh/incremental parity, idempotent re-run, seeded-DB upgrade) passes with the new migration in place.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real rebuilt/restarted stack:** a real 2-step workflow (two real `rest_api` connectors to `postman-echo.com`) dispatched via a real agent JWT against `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{id}/run` — the resulting real `audit_log` rows confirmed via direct `psql` to carry the exact `workflow_run_id`/`step_index` (0, 1) (AC3). A real standalone `tool_call` through the same agent confirmed a genuinely NULL `workflow_run_id`/`step_index` row. **AC4 hash-chain re-verification done live, not just in the Go test:** pulled the 3 real rows this session's live dispatches produced, independently recomputed each hash in bash (`sha256sum`, matching Go's exact `RFC3339`-without-fractional-seconds timestamp formatting) against the documented formula — all 3 matched exactly. `audit_log` rows deliberately not deleted afterward (append-only by design, no `DELETE` RLS policy — same "keep as real audit trail evidence" precedent as prior live-verification sessions); every other fixture (API key, workflow, 2 connectors) was cleaned up, and the agent was suspended rather than hard-deleted (real audit/workflow-run history).
**Dependencies:** B-078 (hash-chain-exclusion precedent), B-063 (workflow execution engine, `runStep`'s call site).

**Fix — B-107 (2026-08-26), `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` round trips cut from 3 blocking to 1:** B-098's `IssueHandler.HandleIssue` performed `ValidateAPIKey` → `LookupByNameAndOrg` → `RecordIssued`, all serial and blocking. **Re-verified before building — the brief's own framing didn't fully hold:** `Manager.Issue()` does zero DB work (pure in-process JWT signing), so there was no fourth "`Manager.Issue`'s DB write" round trip as originally described; the real count was 3, not 3–4.
**What shipped:** (1) `ValidateAPIKey` + `LookupByNameAndOrg` combined into one new `ValidateAndResolveAgent` query (`internal/identity/apikey.go`) — `LEFT JOIN gateway_agents ga ON ga.org_id = ak.org_id AND ga.name = $2` against the `api_keys` row matched by hash, so the agent lookup is scoped by the *same validated key row's own org_id*, never a second round trip that could diverge. LEFT JOIN so "valid key, no matching agent" stays distinguishable from "no such key at all." (2) `RecordIssued` made fire-and-forget, mirroring `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s established `safeWriteTokenUsage`/B-099 pattern. The now-dead `ValidateAPIKey` interface method (confirmed zero remaining callers via repo-wide grep) was removed entirely rather than left as untested extra surface on a security-relevant interface. Net: 3 blocking round trips → 1.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes both ran; the most severe finding was a real bug this brief introduced, not a hypothetical:**
- **Code review (HIGH):** the new fire-and-forget goroutine omitted the `safego.Guard` panic-recovery wrapper that its own cited precedent (`safeWriteTokenUsage`) actually uses — `safego`'s package doc states an unrecovered panic in a detached goroutine crashes the *entire gateway process*. Fixed: wrapped in `safego.Guard("token-issuance-event-recorder", ...)`.
- **Code review + security review, independently (Medium):** `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())` never expires, so a stalled events-store DB would hang the goroutine and its pool connection indefinitely. Fixed: `context.WithTimeout(context.Background(), 5*time.Second)`.
- **Security review (Medium):** decoding the request body ahead of API-key validation (needed since the combined query takes `agentName` as a parameter) let an *unauthenticated* caller make the handler buffer an arbitrarily large body before any credential check — and an in-code comment incorrectly called this "not a security-relevant change." Fixed: `r.Body = http.MaxBytesReader(w, r.Body, 8<<10)` as the handler's first statement; the comment corrected.
- **Code review (Medium, test gap):** no test proved the cross-org join correlation holds when two different orgs each have an agent with the *identical* name (`gateway_agents.name` is unique only per-org, so this is an explicitly expected real state). Fixed: new `TestIssueHandler_SameAgentNameDifferentOrgs_ResolvesOwnOrgOnly` proves both directions.
- **Not fixed, logged fresh:** `Scope`/`Task`/`Model`/`Owner`/`RiskTier` still pass straight from client JSON into the signed JWT claims (only `Subject` is rebuilt from the DB record) — traced to be not currently exploitable (only `Subject`/`ExpiresAt` are read downstream, confirmed by grep), but a latent risk the day any consumer trusts `claims.RiskTier`. Pre-existing since B-098, not introduced by B-107 — logged as **B-116**. No rate limiting on this endpoint — pre-existing, logged as **B-118**.
**Test coverage:** `TestIssueHandler_OversizedBody_Returns400Bounded` and `TestIssueHandler_SameAgentNameDifferentOrgs_ResolvesOwnOrgOnly`, both new in `issue_http_pg_test.go`. All 10 pre-existing `TestIssueHandler_*`/`TestRevokeHandler_*` tests re-run clean, including `TestIssueHandler_KeyScopedToDifferentAgent_Returns403` (B-098's original cross-agent-scoping proof, re-confirmed unaffected) and `TestIssueHandler_SuccessfulIssue_RecordsAITokenEvent` (updated to poll via a new `waitForTokenEvent` helper instead of asserting immediately, since the write is now async). **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across all of `eami-gateway`** against a real Postgres, zero regressions.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real rebuilt/restarted stack:** a real API key created via `POST /v1/auth/api-keys` scoped to a real existing agent; a real cross-agent attempt (same key, requesting a different real agent's identity) returned a real 403 — re-proving B-098's AC2 centerpiece unaffected. The same key issuing for its own bound agent returned a real 200 with a real signed JWT, and the resulting `ai_token_events` row was confirmed present via direct `psql` within 1 second — proving the `safego.Guard`+bounded-timeout rework still lands correctly under real conditions. Test fixtures cleaned up (API key revoked via the real endpoint, which soft-deletes — `revoked=true` — not a hard delete, confirmed before reporting done).
**Dependencies:** B-098 (the code this optimizes), B-099 (the fire-and-forget/`safego.Guard` precedent this now correctly mirrors). New discovered work: **B-116**, **B-118**.

**Fix — B-116/B-118 (2026-08-27), `POST /v1/gateway/tokens` claims bound to the DB + per-agent rate limiting:** closes the two items B-107's own review passes logged. **B-116:** `apikey.go`'s `ResolvedAgent` extended with `Scope`/`Model`/`Owner`/`RiskTier` (plus `TokenTTLSeconds`, see below), populated by adding 4 columns to `ValidateAndResolveAgent`'s existing single combined `SELECT` — same query, no new round trip (B-107's constraint preserved and re-verified). `issue_http.go`'s `HandleIssue` now overwrites `req.Scope`/`Model`/`Owner`/`RiskTier` from the resolved record unconditionally, same "rebuilt not validated-then-rejected" treatment `Subject`/`AgentID` already got — a deliberate design choice, confirmed with the user before building (rejecting a mismatch would add a new client-visible failure mode with no added security benefit, since the DB wins either way). `Task` stays client-supplied (no matching column; a per-request purpose string, not an identity attribute). **B-118:** a small in-memory per-agent fixed-window(-log) `rateLimiter` (`issue_http.go`) — algorithm deliberately duplicated, not imported, from `workflow/ratelimit.go`'s B-070 precedent (mutex-protected `map[string][]time.Time`, fail-closed on non-positive limit, `Retry-After` rounded up per RFC 9110 §10.2.3). Hardcoded at 20 requests/60s per resolved agent UUID, constructed once in `NewIssueHandler` — deliberately not env-configurable like B-070's `WorkflowRunPerAgent` (would have required touching `config.go`/`main.go`, outside this brief's granted scope; full configurability logged fresh as **B-119**). Checked after API-key validation + B-098's cross-agent scoping succeed, keyed on `rec.ID` (not the client-claimed agent name — same cross-org-collision avoidance as B-042/B-070).
**Scope note:** `apikey.go` was added to this brief's MAY-MODIFY set mid-session (confirmed with the user) — B-116 is structurally unbuildable from `issue_http.go` alone without either extending the existing combined `SELECT` or reintroducing a second query (forbidden by B-107's own round-trip constraint); extending the SELECT was the only option consistent with both.
**A real finding beyond B-116's own original scope (Scope/Task/Model/Owner/RiskTier), from this brief's own mandatory code-review AND security-review passes — both independently converged on the same field:** `IssueRequest.TTLSeconds` was the one client-controlled field actually enforced (`Manager.Validate` checks `exp`, derived from it) — unlike the other four, which nothing downstream trusted yet. `gateway_agents.token_ttl_seconds` (admin-managed via the Agents UI, `NOT NULL DEFAULT 900`) was a real column `eami-gateway` never read anywhere — confirmed via repo-wide grep. Before this fix, an operator setting a tight per-agent TTL as a risk control was silently overridden by any caller sending its own `ttl_seconds` (up to the global 14400s clamp) — the fix that would have mattered most was almost shipped without it. Now bound the same unconditional way as the other four. **Known, disclosed divergence:** `api/openapi.yaml`'s `AITokenRequest` still documents `ttl_seconds` as client-settable (60-14400s) — Architect-EAMI-owned (`BOUNDARIES.md`), disclosed as now-stale rather than edited, same B-086/B-107 precedent.
**Mandatory reviewer + security subagent passes, both ran, both found real issues (the TTL finding above, independently, was the most severe from each):**
- **Code review (HIGH) + security review (Medium), independently converged:** `TTLSeconds` unbound — fixed, see above.
- **Code review (Medium):** the hardcoded 20/60s constants meant no test could cheaply prove the rate-limit window ever actually expires (all integration tests only proved the trip, none proved recovery), and the `rl.limit <= 0` fail-closed branch was unreachable/untested. Fixed: new `issue_ratelimit_test.go`, 7 pure-unit tests needing no Postgres, including a short-test-only-window expiry proof.
- **Code review (Low) + security review (Low), independently:** the 5 new pointer dereferences in `apikey.go`'s extended scan (`*agentScope` etc.) are safe only via an implicit, previously-uncommented dependency on `gateway_agents`'s `NOT NULL` constraints holding. Fixed: explicit comment documenting the dependency and the deliberate choice not to `COALESCE` (a silent empty-string fallback on an identity/auth path is worse than a loud panic if that invariant is ever broken by a future migration).
- **Code review (Low, process):** a code comment prematurely referenced "BACKLOG.md's follow-up item" before it existed. Fixed: reworded, and the item now exists (**B-119**).
- **Disclosed, not fixed (both reviews independently, correctly out of B-118's own stated scope — "one authenticated client"):** the endpoint has zero PRE-auth protection — a flood of bogus `X-API-Key` values still costs a real indexed Postgres lookup against the shared pool before ever reaching the per-agent limiter. Logged fresh as **B-120**.
