- **Disclosed, not fixed (both Low, inherited unchanged from B-070's own accepted precedent, not regressions):** the `attempts` map never evicts (bounded, same property `workflow/ratelimit.go` already has); the limiter is actually a sliding-window log, not a true fixed window (stricter, not weaker; naming inherited from B-070 for consistency).
**Test coverage:** `issue_http_b116_b118_pg_test.go` (real Postgres) — `TestIssueHandler_ForgedClaims_OverwrittenFromDB` (all 5 fields incl. TTL, `Task` proven to survive unmodified), `TestIssueHandler_RateLimit_TripsAfterLimit_PerAgent` (429 + real `Retry-After` at the real 20-request production limit), `TestIssueHandler_RateLimit_BucketIsolatedPerAgent`. `issue_ratelimit_test.go` (pure unit, no DB) — 7 tests on the limiter algorithm in isolation, including the window-expiry proof the code review flagged as missing. **Verified 2026-08-27: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module** against a real Postgres, zero regressions — including `TestIssueHandler_KeyScopedToDifferentAgent_Returns403` (B-098's cross-agent-scoping proof, re-run) and the full pre-existing suite.
**Live-verified twice against the real rebuilt/restarted gateway container + real Postgres (docker-compose, rebuilt from cold — Docker Desktop was not running at session start and was started for this):** first pass — a real forged request (`scope`/`model`/`owner`/`risk_tier` all deliberately wrong) against a real seeded agent returned a real signed JWT whose decoded claims matched the DB record exactly, not the forged values; 20 real successful issuances followed by a real 429 with `Retry-After: 36` on the 21st; cross-agent scoping re-confirmed still 403 live; a second agent's own bucket confirmed unaffected; `ai_token_events` confirmed to hold exactly 20 `issued` rows, not 21. Second pass, after the TTL fix specifically: a real agent seeded with `token_ttl_seconds=300`, a real request forging `ttl_seconds:14400`, the real returned JWT decoded — `exp - iat = 300`, not 14400. All fixtures (orgs/agents/keys/events) cleaned up after each pass, confirmed 0 remaining via direct `psql`.
**Dependencies:** B-098 (where the 5 originally-unbound fields were introduced), B-107 (round-trip constraint honored throughout). New discovered work: **B-119** (env-configurable rate-limit thresholds), **B-120** (pre-auth rate limiting/DoS protection on this route).

**Fix — B-115 (2026-08-27), `allowAllRules()`'s allow-path `audit_log` write actually succeeds now:** the shared test helper `allowAllRules()` (`internal/workflow/executor_test.go`) used the literal string `"allow-all"` as a `policy.Rule.ID` — that ID flows into `decision.PolicyID` -> `audit.Entry.PolicyID`, a real Postgres `uuid` column, so every allow-path dispatch matching this rule failed its `audit_log` INSERT with "invalid input syntax for type uuid," silently, since the write error is discarded (`_ = auditWriter.Write(...)`) and no pre-existing test read back the resulting row. Fixed: `ID` changed to `uuid.NewString()` (kept `Name: "allow-all"` — confirmed via repo-wide grep no test asserts against the ID string itself). Per this brief's explicit MUST NOT MODIFY ("any other test helper"), B-093's `allowAllRulesRealPolicyID()` local workaround was left untouched even though this fix makes it redundant — logged as **B-123**.
**A real, disclosed side effect found by this brief's own mandatory security-review pass, not fixed here:** `audit_log` has no FK to `orgs` (deliberate append-only design), so every test using `allowAllRules()` now leaves a permanent orphaned row in the shared dev database once its own org is deleted by `t.Cleanup` — previously these INSERTs failed, so nothing was ever written. Measured live: one `go test ./internal/workflow/...` run took matching rows from 43 to 59. Not fixed via reflexive deletion (would break `audit_log`'s tamper-evident hash chain) — needs a real design decision, logged as **B-122**.
**Test coverage:** new `TestExecutor_Run_AllowPath_UsingAllowAllRules_AuditLogRowPersists` (real Postgres) — a single-step allow-path dispatch through `allowAllRules()` directly, reading back the resulting `audit_log` row and asserting `decision="allowed"` and `policy_id` matches the matched rule's own (now-real-UUID) ID. **Verified 2026-08-27: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module** against a real Postgres, zero regressions to any pre-existing test using `allowAllRules()`.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both ran (per explicit user correction to this brief's own STANDING RULES, which had omitted them), both found real issues -- disclosed above (B-122, B-123) and one genuine production-code analogue, out of this brief's file scope, logged and since closed as B-121 (see this same section, below).**
**Dependencies:** none. Found by, not blocking, B-093. New discovered work: **B-121** (a real production analogue in `dispatcher.go`, Medium-High priority — DONE, see below), **B-122** (orphaned audit_log rows), **B-123** (redundant helper cleanup).

**Fix — B-121 (2026-08-27), `dispatcher.go`'s audit-write-error handling is now uniform across every decision type, not silently discarded on 3 of 4 branches:** closes the asymmetry B-115's own review found — `Dispatch`'s `Deny`, `Escalate` (pre-`Hold()`), and `Allow`-proxy-failure branches all discarded `d.auditWriter.Write`'s returned error (`_ = ...`), while only `Allow`-success checked it (inline `slog.Error`). **Investigated first, per the task brief's explicit ask:** does the fix belong in B-102's hook mechanism? Partially — `Dispatch`'s own doc comment establishes audit-write TIMING is deliberately branch-specific (Escalate writes before blocking on `Hold()`) and predates B-102, so moving the `Write()` call itself into the hook loop would have changed real behavior; what genuinely fits the hook mechanism is the resulting ERROR's handling, not the write's timing.
**What shipped:** `DispatchOutcome` gained `AuditWriteErr error` (captured at each branch's existing `Write()` call site, carried through — Escalate's two exit points share one write before `Hold()`, so both propagate the same value) and `AuditDecision string` (the literal value passed to `Write`, NOT the same as the existing `Decision` field — see the security-review finding below for why that distinction is load-bearing). One new hook, `d.logAuditWriteFailureHook`, registered FIRST in the hooks list, logs at Error level (`org_id`/`agent`/`agent_uuid`/`session_id`/`tool`/`action`/`decision`/`workflow_run_id`/`step_index`/`err`) when `AuditWriteErr != nil`; no-op otherwise. Allow-success's old inline log was removed — all 4 sites now flow through the identical mechanism. **Logging, not a stronger alert/metric, confirmed with the user before building:** `eami-gateway` has no existing metrics/alerting infrastructure to escalate into (no Prometheus, no expvar; `eami-api`'s `internal/alerting` is a separate module for business-metric rules, not a fit here).
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both found real issues, all fixed:**
- **Security review (Medium), the most severe finding:** the Allow-proxy-failure branch sets `Decision="allowed"` (the policy-branch label) but writes `auditEntry.Decision="denied"` — the first draft's log line reused `Decision`, so a lost row from this branch would have pointed an operator at exactly the wrong bucket to reconcile against. Fixed with the new `AuditDecision` field. **Live-verified against real production behavior, not just the test's assumption:** a real dispatch to an unregistered tool (no downstream configured in this dev topology) hit exactly this branch, and the real `audit_log` row's `decision` column was confirmed `"denied"` via direct `psql`.
- **Code review (Medium):** the log line's original fields (`org_id`/`agent`/`tool`/`action`/`decision`/`err`) couldn't correlate a lost row against `workflow_run_steps` (no FK to/from `audit_log` at all) or the approval subsystem. Fixed: added `agent_uuid`/`session_id`/`workflow_run_id`/`step_index`.
- **Code review (Low-Medium):** the new hook was registered last, behind two hooks with no panic recovery in the loop — a panic in an earlier hook would have silently prevented the audit-integrity signal from firing at all. Fixed: registered first, since it has zero dependency on the other two.
- **A real bug this session found in its OWN mid-fix test code, not either review pass:** implementing the code review's "don't silently swallow other log output during these tests" finding by having the test capture handler forward to the real previous default handler caused a genuine deadlock — Go's `slog.defaultHandler` re-enters the classic `log` package's non-reentrant mutex when called from inside another handler's `Handle`. Confirmed via a real hang and a `go test -timeout` panic showing two goroutines both blocked on the identical mutex, one nested in the other's stack. Reverted to a non-forwarding capture handler that dumps records via `t.Logf` only on test failure — same debuggability, no runtime handler-chain re-entrancy.
- **Disclosed, not fixed here, all out of this brief's own `MAY MODIFY: dispatcher.go`-only scope:** an approved escalation's actual downstream execution writes no `audit_log` row at all (**B-124**, Medium-High — the most significant of the four, since it's a real hole in the audit trail's core governance claim); `audit_log.approval_id`/`approved_by` are never populated despite `eami-api` reading them back (**B-125**, Medium, likely the same fix as B-124); `internal/workflow/testenv_test.go`'s reconstructed dispatch closure still has the identical discard pattern, checked as the fix sketch asked, confirmed zero production impact since production routes through the real fixed `Dispatcher.Dispatch` (**B-126**, Low, test-only); a pre-existing test race where `recordEpisodeHook`'s detached goroutine loses to org-delete cleanup, meaning episode assertions across this session's test files silently test writes that never land (**B-127**, Low, test quality, not introduced by this brief).
**Test coverage:** `dispatcher_test.go` gained `failingAuditDB` (implements `audit.WriterDB`, the package's own documented test seam) for deterministic failure injection, plus 8 new tests covering Deny/both Escalate exit points/Allow-success/Allow-proxy-failure (the `AuditDecision`-mismatch regression, via a real forced proxy failure through a closed listener, not simulated), a negative-case (successful write logs nothing), and an AC4-style proof mirroring B-102's own `TestDispatch_NewHook_FiresForAllThreeDecisionTypes` (one production change, three decision types, zero branch-specific code). **Verified 2026-08-27: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 -timeout 180s ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module** against a real Postgres, zero regressions.
**Live-verified against the real rebuilt/restarted gateway container:** a real seeded org/agent/API key/deny+allow policies, a real agent JWT, a real SSE session, two real `tool_call` dispatches (both landed on the Allow-proxy-failure branch — no downstream is configured in this dev topology — precisely the branch the `AuditDecision` bug lived in). Real `audit_log` rows confirmed `decision="denied"` via direct `psql`; `docker compose logs` confirmed zero `"dispatch: audit_log write failed"` lines under this normal, fully-successful run (no false positives). Per the same principle the user set for B-115/B-117 (deterministic fake-DB tests prove the failure-injection scenarios; live verification confirms normal behavior is unaffected), a real audit-write failure was not forced against the live stack. Fixtures cleaned up; the agent suspended rather than hard-deleted (real episode history from the live dispatch, same `DeleteAgent` guard precedent as prior sessions).
**Dependencies:** none. Found by, not blocking, B-115/B-117. New discovered work: **B-124**, **B-125**, **B-126**, **B-127**.

**Fix — B-128 (2026-08-28), logged not fixed: no `org_id` scoping exists anywhere in the policy evaluation pipeline.** Found while live-verifying B-124/125: `internal/policyloader/loader.go`'s `queryRules()` has no `org_id` predicate and doesn't even select it; `eami-policy`'s `Rule`/`Conditions`/`ActionContext` have no `OrgID` field at all. `Loader.store()` builds one global `policy.Evaluator` shared by every org in the process — confirmed live via a direct `psql` run of the loader's own query returning 3 active policies from 2+ different orgs in one priority-ordered list. URGENT/Critical per explicit user direction; a real multi-tenant isolation failure in the core enforcement surface, not test noise. Logged only, not fixed — needs a real design pass (org-scoped query + rule/context fields + either a per-org `Evaluator` or an org-filtered subset before evaluation). Full detail in `BACKLOG.md`'s B-128 entry.

**Fix — B-128 (2026-09-01), DONE.** A dedicated investigation pass (no code changes) first re-confirmed the above still held against current code post-B-129, then empirically re-proved the leak live (a throwaway probe against the real dev DB — since deleted — showed a dispatch structurally belonging to org `b121-liveverify` matched and was decided by Dev Org's own policy), and recommended the minimal additive design over a per-org-evaluator split, backed by real scale evidence (`ARCHITECTURE.md` §8's dedicated-Postgres-per-customer deployment topology). User approved; built exactly the confirmed 4-point design, no interface changes: (1) `policyloader.queryRules()` now selects `p.org_id` onto `policy.Rule.OrgID`; (2) `eami-policy/types.go` gained `OrgID` on both `Rule` and `ActionContext`; (3) `structural.go`'s `matchesRule()` gained one new, unconditional, first check (`if r.OrgID != "" && r.OrgID != ac.OrgID { return false }` — empty is a wildcard only for hand-built test literals, never a real DB-loaded rule since `policies.org_id` is `NOT NULL`); (4) `internal/mcp/handler.go`'s `ToPolicyContext()` now sets `OrgID: a.OrgID`, confirmed server-resolved from `sess.Agent.OrgID` at every real construction site, never client input. No schema migration. `policy.EvaluatorSource` (B-129) and `ReorderPolicies`/priority ordering (B-086/090) both confirmed untouched and re-verified live afterward with zero interaction.
**One real regression found by the mandatory review passes and fixed before shipping:** `internal/workflow/executor.go`'s `runStep` builds an independent, informational `policy.ActionContext` solely to populate the audit-facing `StepResult.ProjectedDecision` preview — this literal never set `OrgID`, so once every real rule gained a non-empty `OrgID`, the preview could never match any real policy again, silently showing `"allow"` on every workflow step regardless of the org's actual policy. Not a security hole (the real, enforced dispatch two lines below correctly inherits `OrgID` from `template` and was unaffected) — a silent audit-data regression, introduced by this fix, caught independently by both review passes. Fixed with one line (`OrgID: template.OrgID`); confirmed as a real regression by reverting the line and re-running the new test (failed with exactly the predicted symptom, `ProjectedDecision = "allow"` want `"escalate"`), then restoring it (passed).
**A second, more severe, pre-existing finding from the security review, disclosed and NOT fixed here — logged as B-141.** `internal/registry.go`'s `LookupByName` resolves an agent by name alone with no `org_id` predicate; since `gateway_agents` only enforces `UNIQUE(org_id, name)` (not global uniqueness), two orgs with a same-named agent can have the wrong org's row resolved, corrupting `ac.OrgID` at its source — upstream of and silently defeating this very fix's org check. Pre-existing (B-042 partially addressed this bug class for token-revoke only, with reasoning the security review found has a real hole for every other caller); a real design decision (JWT org-binding claim vs. per-call-site org-scoping), correctly out of this fix's scope. Full detail in `BACKLOG.md`'s new B-141 entry.
**Test coverage — the adversarial two-org proof, matching B-042's own standard:** `TestDispatch_OrgScoping_TwoOrgsConflictingPolicies` (`cmd/gateway/dispatcher_org_scoping_test.go`, new) — two real orgs, genuinely conflicting same-priority policies on the identical tool name, one real shared `Loader`/`Dispatcher`; each org's dispatch decided only by its own policy (AC1, centerpiece). `TestLoad_OrgScoping_CrossOrgDispatchNoLongerMatches` (`internal/policyloader/loader_pg_test.go`, new) — reproduces the investigation's exact repro and confirms it no longer matches, with a same-org positive control (AC2, negative control). `TestEvaluate_OrgScoping_PriorityOrderingIndependentPerOrg` and `TestMatchesRule_OrgID` (`eami-policy/policy_test.go`, new) — unit-level org-guard and priority-independence proofs (AC5). `TestExecutor_Run_ProjectedDecision_OrgScopedRule_StillMatches` (`internal/workflow/executor_test.go`, new) — the executor.go regression test above. The pre-existing `TestLoad_ToolServerIDs_RealDBRoundTrip` was updated to set `OrgID` on its `ActionContext` literals (a code-review catch: without it, the test would have started silently passing for the wrong reason instead of testing `ToolServerIDs` matching). **Verified 2026-09-01: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module and `eami-policy`, against a real Postgres, zero regressions** (full package list: `cmd/gateway`, `aiprovider`, `approval`, `audit`, `config`, `episode`, `identity`, `policyloader`, `proxy`, `safego`, `toolrouter`, `workflow`, plus `eami-policy`'s own 27 tests). `eami-api`'s full `Reorder`/`Policy`/`Policies` test suite also re-run live, unmodified, 100% green (confirms zero interaction with B-086/090, as expected — separate CRUD-layer code path never touched).
**Live-verified:** all of the above against the real dev Postgres; the actual `eami-gateway` Docker image rebuilt and container restarted twice (core fix, then review-pass fixes), clean startup both times (`"policyloader: loaded policies from DB","count":2"`, no query errors against the real production schema). All test fixtures (orgs/policies/agents) confirmed cleaned up via `t.Cleanup`, zero leftover rows in the dev DB.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both ran, both found real issues (the executor.go regression, independently; the security review additionally found B-141), the in-scope one fixed before shipping, the out-of-scope one disclosed and logged.**
**Dependencies:** none. **Unblocks:** nothing further — B-124/125 (already DONE) were live-verified against an environment that still had this gap; that disclosure is now moot. **New discovered work:** B-141 (Critical, logged not fixed).

**Fix — B-141 (2026-09-01), DONE: JWT org-binding + org-scoped identity resolution at all 4 confirmed call sites, closing the confirmed cross-tenant episode-content read.** Investigation first traced all 4 call sites precisely and confirmed none had an org_id available prior to the lookup itself (the lookup is what discovers it) — the two "fix directions" (JWT org-binding vs. per-call-site scoping) weren't independent alternatives; JWT-binding is the structural prerequisite that makes per-call-site scoping achievable at all. Also traced B-042's original "safe by construction" reasoning precisely: two call sites (`workflow/http.go` B-059, `workflow/ratelimit.go` B-070) were added **after** B-042 already existed as a documented precedent in the same file, and neither reused it. User approved the confirmed plan; built exactly it, B5 leading:
1. `identity/tokens.go`: `Claims`/`IssueRequest` gained `OrgID string` (`IssueRequest.OrgID` has `json:"-"`, confirmed empirically unspoofable via request body — a decode test with both `"org_id"` and `"OrgID"` keys left the field empty both times, and `HandleIssue` unconditionally overwrites it from server-side state regardless). `Manager.Issue` signs `OrgID: req.OrgID` into the token, same additive pattern as B-116.
2. `identity/issue_http.go`'s `HandleIssue` sets `req.OrgID = key.OrgID` (from `ValidateAndResolveAgent`'s already org-scoped query) before `Manager.Issue`.
3. All 4 confirmed call sites (`mcp/handler.go` ServeSSE, `workflow/http.go` HandleRun, `workflow/ratelimit.go` RateLimitRunMiddleware, `episode/http.go` authenticateCaller — the confirmed leak path) switched from `LookupByName` to the pre-existing, untouched, already-correct `LookupByNameAndOrg` (B-042).
4. **Hard cutover, confirmed before building:** a pre-fix-shaped token (empty `OrgID` claim) is rejected with 401 at 3 of 4 sites; `RateLimitRunMiddleware` deliberately doesn't hard-reject (best-effort, already falls through on any resolution failure) — confirmed safe by both mandatory review passes, which independently traced `main.go`'s real wiring and found `HandleRun` is `next` directly with nothing in between, so its own independent, unconditional `LookupByNameAndOrg`/OrgID check is the real, unbypassable boundary regardless of the middleware's own outcome.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both ran, both clean — zero correctness or security defects found in the fix itself.** Both independently converged on the same single finding: `registry.go`'s `LookupByNameAndOrg` doc comment still asserts the exact disproven "safe by construction" reasoning that produced B-141, now factually stale (its two named "safe" callers no longer use the method it defends) and a landmine for future code. `registry.go` was outside this brief's MAY MODIFY scope — disclosed, not fixed, logged fresh as **B-142** (Low).
**Test coverage — the adversarial three-surface proof, matching B-128's own two-org standard, all real Postgres:** `TestServeSSE_OrgScoping_TwoOrgsSameAgentName_ResolvesCorrectAgentEachTime` (`cmd/gateway/mcp_org_scoping_test.go`, new) captures the real dispatched `mcp.ActionContext` and confirms correct-org resolution for each of two real JWTs sharing an identical agent name across two real orgs. `TestHandleRun_OrgScoping_TwoOrgsSameAgentName_ResolvesCorrectAgentEachTime` (`internal/workflow/http_org_scoping_test.go`, new — `HandleRun`'s first-ever direct test coverage) confirms via the real `workflow_runs.org_id`/`agent_id` columns. `TestListEpisodes_OrgScoping_TwoOrgsSameAgentName_NeverLeaksAcrossOrgs` (`internal/episode/http_org_scoping_pg_test.go`, new) is both AC1's third surface and AC2's negative control — **empirically confirmed to catch the bug, not just asserted:** the fix was manually reverted (episode's resolver swapped back to unscoped `LookupByName`), the test re-run and confirmed to fail with the exact predicted leak message, then the fix restored and the test re-confirmed passing. `ratelimit_test.go`'s pre-existing same-name-different-org test was strengthened (not merely adapted): its old context-smuggling test-only device (`ctxOrgResolver`/`ctxOrgKey`, needed because production previously had no real mechanism to distinguish two same-named agents) is now genuinely obsolete and was deleted, replaced with two real tokens carrying distinct real `OrgID` claims. Dedicated pre-cutover-token tests added at all 4 sites. **Verified 2026-09-01: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module against real Postgres, zero regressions** — including a targeted re-run confirming zero interaction with B-128 (`TestDispatch_OrgScoping_*`, `TestLoad_OrgScoping_*`), B-098/B-107/B-118 (`TestIssueHandler_*`), and B-042 (`TestRevokeHandler_CrossOrgAgentName_Returns403`).
**Live-verified:** all of the above against the real dev Postgres; the actual `eami-gateway` Docker image rebuilt and container restarted, clean startup confirmed against the real production schema; all test fixtures cleaned up, zero leftover rows.
**Dependencies:** none. **New discovered work:** B-142 (Low, stale doc comment + now-dead `LookupByName` code in `registry.go`, logged not fixed).

**Fix — B-129 (2026-08-28), CRITICAL: policy hot-reload was completely non-functional on the actual dispatch path — only a full gateway restart made any policy change (create/edit/reorder/delete, any org) take effect.** Found root-causing why B-128's own investigation still couldn't get a live escalate policy to match even after two confirmed, successful `pg_notify` reloads. Ruled out stale process/cache/build first (gateway container/image freshly built, `pLoader`'s own internal state genuinely updated on both reloads, confirmed via logs) before finding the real cause: `cmd/gateway/main.go` called `pLoader.Evaluator()` **once**, at startup (2 call sites), and passed the frozen `policy.Evaluator` value into `NewDispatcher`/`workflow.New` — `Dispatch()` and `workflow.Executor` then evaluated against that one snapshot forever, never re-consulting the loader despite its own doc comment specifying exactly that per-dispatch pattern.
**Investigated before building, per explicit user direction:** performance was not a legitimate reason for the snapshot (`Loader.Evaluator()` is a bare atomic pointer load — confirmed via benchmark, ~1.5ns vs ~0.4ns cached, 0.04% of one `Evaluate()` call); the real constraint was `dispatcher_test.go`'s existing fake-evaluator test pattern, preserved via a new indirection. `workflowExecutor` has two independent instances of the bug (real enforcement via the shared `Dispatcher.Dispatch`, plus its own separate frozen snapshot for the non-enforcing `ProjectedDecision` preview) — both fixed. Checked, not assumed, that tonight's prior live-verification claims (B-086, B-090, B-098, B-116, B-118) are unaffected — none of them depend on `eami-gateway`'s live evaluator reflecting a post-startup policy change; B-121's is the one genuinely open question (didn't depend on its seeded Deny policy matching to prove its actual claim, but whether that policy was ever really exercised is undetermined), flagged rather than resolved either way.
**What shipped:** new `policy.EvaluatorSource` interface (`eami-policy/evaluator.go`); `*policyloader.Loader` satisfies it structurally, now asserted at compile time. `Dispatcher`/`workflow.Executor` hold `policyEvalSource policy.EvaluatorSource` (renamed from `policyEval policy.Evaluator`) and call `.Evaluator().Evaluate(...)` fresh every dispatch/step; `main.go` passes `pLoader` itself, not a snapshot.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes (each relaunched once after a session-limit failure), both found zero correctness/security defects in the fix.** Security review's one substantive finding: this fix changes B-128's blast radius (an over-broad cross-org policy now reaches every org's live dispatch on the very next call instead of only after a restart) — correctly attributed to B-128, not a new defect, not fixed here. Code review: two non-blocking notes (a pre-existing unrelated `gofmt` misalignment left untouched; the compile-time assertion, added).
**Test coverage:** `dispatcher_policy_reload_test.go` (real Postgres) — two tests, identical real `Dispatcher`/`Loader` setup, differing in exactly the one variable under test: `TestDispatch_PolicyReload_TakesEffectWithoutRestart` proves the fix (a Deny policy inserted and reloaded post-construction denies the very next dispatch, no restart); `TestDispatch_PolicyReload_FrozenSnapshotWiring_ReproducesTheBug` proves the old bug reproduces exactly, using a byte-for-byte stand-in for the old `main.go` pattern. `loader_bench_test.go` empirically confirms the negligible performance cost. **Verified 2026-08-28: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 -timeout 240s ./...` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module and `eami-policy`**, against a real Postgres, zero regressions.
**Live-verified against the real rebuilt/restarted gateway container, reproducing and resolving the exact scenario that blocked B-124/125's live verification:** fresh org/admin/agent/API key seeded, real JWT, real SSE session, a first dispatch (no policy yet, Allow-branch proxy failure as expected) — then a real Deny policy inserted directly into Postgres and `pg_notify('policy_reload','')` fired with **no gateway restart**. The second dispatch, same running process, was genuinely denied: `"policy denied [...]: matched rule \"b129-deny-all\" (priority 1)"`, confirmed via `psql` against real `audit_log`. All fixtures cleaned up via cascade delete, confirmed 0 remaining. A leftover fixture from the paused B-124/125 attempt (`b124-escalate-all`, priority -100, matched everything) was also found actively corrupting an unrelated pre-existing test (`TestLoad_ToolServerIDs_RealDBRoundTrip`) via this exact B-128 mechanism — removed with explicit user authorization (that one row only; B-124/125's org/agent/API-key fixtures were left untouched).
**Dependencies:** none — pre-existing. Found via the same live-verification attempt as B-128. **Unblocks:** B-124/125's live verification, resumes next.

**Fix — B-124/B-125 (2026-08-28), tamper-evident audit trail for escalation resolution outcomes: a second, real `audit_log` row is now written once an escalation genuinely resolves (approved, denied, or expired), carrying the real `approval_id`/`approved_by`.** Previously, an approved-and-executed escalation was recorded only by the pre-approval "escalated" row — whatever actually happened after a human decided (success, failure, a different result than reviewed) left no trace in the tamper-evident chain, and `audit_log.approval_id`/`approved_by` were permanently null everywhere despite `eami-api`'s audit endpoint already reading them back.
**Prerequisite completed first, per the brief's own explicit ask:** `approval.Router.Hold()`'s 4 independent exit paths converged onto one shared `HoldOutcome` construction point (mirroring B-102's own `Dispatch()` convergence) — proven by its own dedicated test before the actual fix was built, closing the exact "unconverged exits can silently diverge" risk that caused B-099.
**What shipped:** `Hold()` now returns an exported `HoldOutcome` struct (`Result`/`Err`/`Resolved`/`Status`/`ApprovedBy`/`DecisionReason`) instead of `(json.RawMessage, error)`. `DispatchOutcome` gained `ResolutionAuditEntry *audit.Entry`, built once in `Dispatch()` itself when `Hold()` genuinely resolved, written by a new `recordEscalationResolutionAuditHook` (`safego.Guard`-wrapped, `context.WithoutCancel`). Symmetric across approved/denied/expired; ctx-cancelled writes nothing by design. **The attribution fix** (found independently by both mandatory review passes): `ApprovedBy` is only copied onto the resolution row when the decision is `"allowed"` or the human's own decision wasn't `"approved"` — never for "approved but the resumed dispatch itself failed," which would otherwise misleadingly attach a real approver's identity to a `"denied"` row they never actually denied.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both found real issues, all fixed:** the attribution fix (independently converged on by both); a cross-module invariant (the genuine-timeout path's once-guard-free reasoning depends on `eami-api`'s `DecideApproval` UPDATE's `WHERE status='pending'`) made explicit in comments rather than left implicit; a silent-error-swallowing bug in `resolveHoldTimeout`'s deepest fallback fixed. **A real test-flakiness bug the session found in its own test code:** an earlier `verifyHashChain` draft snapshotted the chain head before `Dispatch()` ran, flaky under `go test ./...`'s concurrent package execution against the one global `audit_log` chain — fixed to trust the test's own first row's stored `prev_hash`, verify internal consistency of only its own rows, and (strengthened per a code-review finding) confirm that stored `prev_hash` really does correspond to an existing row or the genesis hash.
**Disclosed, not fixed, confirmed with the user:** the hash chain formula itself doesn't cover `approval_id`/`approved_by` (out of this brief's explicit MUST NOT MODIFY scope on the formula) — a tamper of either field on a resolution row wouldn't be caught by hash verification alone; `DecisionReason` has no schema column. Neither minted as a B-ID without founder confirmation.
**Test coverage:** 14+ real-Postgres integration tests across `internal/approval` and `cmd/gateway`, all 7 acceptance criteria (approved/denied/expired → 2nd row hash-verified; ctx-cancelled → no 2nd row; Hold() convergence itself; full mixed-sequence hash-chain re-verification; zero regression to B-121/B-100/normal dispatch). **Verified 2026-08-28: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across `eami-gateway`**, run repeatedly including under full concurrent-package conditions.
**Live-verified against the real rebuilt gateway container** (required B-129's fix to be usable at all — no escalate policy created after startup could be reliably exercised live before it landed): a fresh org/admin/agent/API key, a live-reloaded escalate policy, two real escalations decided via the real `POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decide` endpoint. The approved path landed directly on the attribution fix's exact edge case (no downstream configured in this dev topology, same limitation as B-121's own live verification) — human approved, resumed dispatch failed, resolution row correctly shows `decision="denied"`, a real `approval_id`, and empty `approved_by`. The denied path exercised the clean case: real `approval_id` and `approved_by` both populated correctly. All 4 real rows (2 escalations × escalated+resolution) independently re-hashed outside the application via a standalone bash/sha256sum reimplementation of the documented formula, matching the stored `hash` column exactly for all 4 — genuine external tamper-evidence proof, not trust in stored linkage. Zero resolution-audit-write-failure log lines (no false positives). Expired and ctx-cancelled paths not live-exercised (10-minute default hold timeout; ctx-cancellation isn't observable via HTTP) — proven only by the Go integration tests above, per this session's established standard. All fixtures cleaned up, confirmed 0 remaining.
**Dependencies:** B-121. **Blocked, then unblocked, by B-129** (found and fixed as its own urgent brief during this one's live-verification step). **Related, not fixed:** B-128 (pre-existing, unrelated to this fix's correctness).

**Fix — B-122/B-140/B-123/B-126/B-127 (2026-09-03), test-infrastructure hardening batch, all four DONE, test-only, zero production code touched.**

**B-122/B-140 — per-test throwaway-database isolation, closing both the permanent orphaned-`audit_log`-row accumulation (B-122) and the confirmed hash-chain-continuity test race (B-140) in one motion.** New `eami-gateway/internal/testdb` package (`testdb.go`, a plain non-`_test.go` package so every other package's own test files can import it — Go doesn't allow importing another package's `_test.go` files): `ResolveConn` (the same `TEST_DATABASE_URL`/`POSTGRES_PASSWORD` fallback every existing `*TestDSN` helper already used independently, centralized), `NewThrowawayPool(t) *pgxpool.Pool` creates a uniquely-named database, applies every real `schema/migrations-v2/*.up.sql` file against it via `pgx`'s simple query protocol (mirroring `eami-api/internal/api/bootstrap_test.go`'s already-proven pattern exactly, duplicated rather than imported since `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` are separate Go modules under `go.work`), opens a pool, and registers `t.Cleanup` for `pool.Close` then (LIFO) the database's own `pg_terminate_backend`+`DROP DATABASE`. Swapped all **11 real-Postgres pool-creation call sites across 9 packages** onto it: `cmd/gateway` (`main_pg_test.go`'s `newMainTestEnv`, shared by `dispatcher_test.go`/`dispatcher_org_scoping_test.go`/`dispatcher_policy_reload_test.go`/`dispatcher_escalation_resolution_test.go`/`main_tools_list_pg_test.go`/`mcp_org_scoping_test.go`/`main_ai_provider_pg_test.go`), `internal/workflow` (`testenv_test.go`'s `newWorkflowTestEnv`), `internal/audit` (`writer_pg_test.go`'s `newAuditTestPool`), `internal/approval` (`router_pg_test.go`'s `newApprovalTestEnv` **and** `router_race_test.go`'s separate `newRacyApprovalTestEnv`), `internal/policyloader` (`loader_pg_test.go`'s two inline test functions, no shared constructor existed), `internal/identity` (`tokens_pg_test.go`'s `newIdentityTestEnv`, shared by `issue_http_pg_test.go`/`revoke_http_pg_test.go`/`issue_http_b116_b118_pg_test.go`), `internal/episode` (`http_org_scoping_pg_test.go`'s one inline test function), `internal/aiprovider` (`router_pg_test.go`'s `newAIProviderTestEnv`), `internal/toolrouter` (`router_pg_test.go`'s `newToolrouterTestEnv`). Every now-redundant per-org/per-row `t.Cleanup(DELETE ...)` at these 11 constructors was removed (the whole throwaway database is dropped instead); a handful of *other*, non-constructor per-test `DELETE` cleanups elsewhere were deliberately left alone as harmless no-ops against a soon-to-be-dropped database, to keep the diff focused on the actual isolation mechanism rather than a repo-wide cleanup hunt. Left untouched, per explicit scope: `internal/identity/tokens_pg_test.go`'s deliberately-broken `badPool` (a fixed wrong-port connection for a negative-connection test, unrelated to this pattern). Side effect, not a separate fix: `internal/approval/router_pg_test.go`'s `approvalTestDSN` previously used `localhost` instead of every other file's `127.0.0.1` (the documented IPv6/Docker-Desktop gotcha in `CONTEXT.md`) — now moot, since that whole function was replaced by the shared helper.
**Verified 2026-09-03: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; full `go test ./... -count=1` run 5 times in a row, all green, zero flakiness, across all 9 real-Postgres packages** (`cmd/gateway` ~55-60s/run, the rest 5-35s/run each — real, disclosed slowdown from applying the full migration set per test function rather than per package, the tradeoff this brief's own task brief approved in exchange for genuine isolation). **`TestDispatch_Escalate_Resolution_Expired_WritesSecondRow` (the specific test B-140 was opened over) additionally run 20 times in isolation (`-run ... -count=20`), 20/20 pass.** Confirmed zero leaked throwaway databases afterward via a direct `psql` count against `pg_database` (`gatewaytest_%` — 0 rows).

**B-123 — removed `eami-gateway/internal/workflow/executor_test.go`'s `allowAllRulesRealPolicyID()`, a byte-identical duplicate of the now-fixed `allowAllRules()` left over from B-093's pre-B-115 workaround.** Re-confirmed still byte-identical before touching anything. Deleted the function and its stale doc comment (which still asserted the disproven "not a valid UUID" reasoning B-115 fixed); repointed its 2 callers (`TestExecutor_Run_AuditLogRecordsWorkflowRunIDAndStepIndex`, `TestExecutor_Run_StandaloneDispatch_AuditLogWorkflowLinkageIsNull`) at `allowAllRules()` directly; trimmed a third comment (on `TestExecutor_Run_AllowPath_UsingAllowAllRules_AuditLogRowPersists`) that referenced the now-deleted helper by name.

**B-126 — `internal/workflow/testenv_test.go`'s reconstructed dispatch closure no longer discards `audit.Writer.Write`'s returned error.** All 4 `_ = auditWriter.Write(ctx, auditEntry)` sites (Deny, Escalate, Allow-proxy-error, Allow-success) now route through a new test-local `logAuditWriteFailure` closure that mirrors `cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go`'s `logAuditWriteFailureHook` in spirit (same field set: org/agent/tool/action/decision/session/err, logged via `slog.Error` when non-nil, no-op on success) — purely so a future `internal/workflow` test could exercise this scenario the same way `cmd/gateway`'s `dispatcher_test.go` already can. No existing test in this package asserted on this behavior before or after; this is a latent-capability fix, not a regression fix.

**B-127 — closed the pre-existing `recordEpisodeHook` detached-goroutine test race, test-only (production `cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go`/`internal/episode/recorder.go` are both out of this brief's scope and were not touched).** New `waitForEpisodeRow(t, pool, orgID, task, timeout)` helper in `cmd/gateway/dispatcher_test.go` polls `episodes` for the row a `go d.episodeRecorder.Record(...)` goroutine would have written, by org+task, with a bounded timeout — the actual fix is that this poll runs synchronously inside the test function itself, strictly before any `t.Cleanup` fires (`t.Cleanup` only runs after the test function returns), closing the exact race B-127 identified without needing production code to expose the goroutine's completion (which would have required modifying `Dispatcher.episodeRecorder`'s concrete, non-interface type — out of scope). New regression test `TestDispatch_Allow_EpisodeRow_LandsBeforeAssertion` proves it: a real Allow-branch dispatch, then a wait-and-read of the resulting `episodes` row, asserting `outcome="success"` and the expected step content. Not fixed/exercised: `internal/workflow`'s own 3 `go episodeRecorder.Record(...)` sites in `testenv_test.go`'s dispatch closure carry the identical structural race, but no existing or new test in that package currently asserts on episode DB content, so there was nothing to make flaky there yet — flagged, not additionally instrumented, since the task brief's acceptance criterion was one targeted proof "where practical," not blanket coverage.
**Verified 2026-09-03: part of the same 5x-full-suite-green run and included in the individual `cmd/gateway` runs above** (`TestDispatch_Allow_EpisodeRow_LandsBeforeAssertion` passed in every run, no `-run`-isolated repeat done separately since it's already covered by the 5 full-suite passes).

**Dependencies:** B-115 (whose fix made B-123 possible), B-121 (the pattern B-126 mirrors), B-102/B-027 (the hook/goroutine mechanism B-127's race lives in), B-140→B-122 (the resolved fix-direction fork). **Unblocks:** nothing structurally; closes out all 5 backlog items this brief batched (B-122, B-140, B-123, B-126, B-127) as DONE.

**Fix — B-119/B-120/B-142 (2026-09-03), token-issuance rate-limit hardening + a stale doc-comment fix, all three DONE. Touches the token-issuance auth path; reviewer + security passes mandatory, both ran, both found real issues, all fixed before shipping.**

**B-142 — `internal/registry/registry.go`'s doc comments corrected first (smallest, independent item).** Re-verified via a fresh repo-wide grep (production and test code) before touching anything: `LookupByName` still has zero real callers anywhere (only a stale comment reference survives in `identity/tokens.go`), and every real production caller (`internal/mcp`, `internal/episode`'s bearer-JWT branch, `internal/identity/revoke_http.go`, `internal/workflow/http.go`, `internal/workflow/ratelimit.go`) uses the org-scoped `LookupByNameAndOrg`. Rewrote both functions' doc comments to state the real design (org id sourced from a JWT's server-set `OrgID` claim, B-141, or an explicit caller-supplied `org_id` on the service-key-only revoke path) instead of the disproven "JWT sub alone is safe" reasoning that produced B-141, and added an explicit "do not use `LookupByName` for new code" warning at the function itself, not only at its sibling. Doc-comment-only; independently re-confirmed accurate by the mandatory security review (its own independent grep matched exactly).

**B-119 — `POST /v1/gateway/tokens`'s per-agent rate-limit threshold (B-118's hardcoded 20 req/60s) is now env-configurable, mirroring B-070's `WorkflowRunPerAgent` pattern exactly.** `eami-gateway/internal/config/config.go`'s `RateLimitConfig` gained `TokenIssuePerAgent`/`TokenIssuePerAgentWindowSeconds` (env vars `TOKEN_ISSUE_RATE_LIMIT_PER_AGENT`/`TOKEN_ISSUE_RATE_LIMIT_WINDOW_SECONDS`, defaults 20/60, non-negative validation — same shape as `WorkflowRunPerAgent`). `issue_http.go`'s old `tokenIssueRateLimit`/`tokenIssueRateLimitWindow` constants removed entirely.

**B-120 — a second, pre-auth defense added to the same endpoint, protecting the shared `pgxpool` from a flood of bogus `X-API-Key` values before they ever reach `ValidateAndResolveAgent`'s real DB round trip — but NOT the mechanism the task brief's own plan initially specified, after the mandatory security review found a real, High-severity problem with it.**
- **First draft (per the approved plan): a per-remote-IP request-rate limiter,** keyed on `r.RemoteAddr` (deliberately not `X-Forwarded-For`, to avoid duplicating `eami-api`'s B-071 `trustedProxyPeer` spoofing-defense machinery, which `eami-gateway` has none of).
- **Security review finding (High), not shipped as originally planned:** this product's production topology (B-071, Caddy fronts the gateway directly) means every external caller's `r.RemoteAddr` is the proxy's own container IP — collapsing a per-IP limiter into ONE shared bucket across every org's every agent. Consequence 1: a single unauthenticated flood (costing the attacker nothing — a bogus key never needs to look plausible) could lock every legitimate agent in every org out of token issuance for a full window. Consequence 2, found by the review's own arithmetic and more concerning than the first: a handful of legitimate agents independently issuing at their own normal pace could sum to the shared threshold and trip it with **no attacker involved at all** (3 agents at B-118's own 20/60s default already sum to the first draft's proposed 60/60s pre-auth default). A second, Low finding: the shared-bucket map had no eviction, newly reachable with attacker-influenceable cardinality (source IP) rather than the previously bounded real-agent-UUID cardinality every other caller of this limiter type used.
- **Rebuilt as a bounded CONCURRENCY gate instead (the task brief's own fix sketch's second-listed option).** New `preAuthGate` type (`issue_http.go`): a non-blocking, buffered-channel semaphore bounding how many not-yet-authenticated `ValidateAndResolveAgent` calls can run concurrently (`TryAcquire`/`Release`, fail-closed on a non-positive size, same "never panic" contract as `rateLimiter.Allow`). Deliberately **global, not keyed at all** — a per-IP key would degrade to one shared gate anyway in the real topology, so keying added complexity with no real per-attacker isolation. This mechanism has neither of the rate-limiter's failure modes: it only rejects a request arriving while every slot is genuinely occupied by another in-flight real DB call, and recovers the instant those calls complete (each is one fast indexed lookup) — there is no fixed window for an attacker to fill and walk away from, and ordinary legitimate concurrency essentially never saturates a modest bound. New `config.RateLimitConfig.TokenIssuePreAuthMaxConcurrent` (env var `TOKEN_ISSUE_PREAUTH_MAX_CONCURRENT`, default 10 — chosen as generous headroom for realistic legitimate concurrency against a pool shared with dispatch/policy-loading/workflow-run traffic, while still bounding a burst).
- **Placement, precise to AC2's literal wording:** the gate brackets `ValidateAndResolveAgent`'s call specifically (acquire immediately before, release immediately after) — not the whole handler — since the stated threat is DB-pool contention, not body-decode cost (already bounded separately by B-107's `MaxBytesReader`).
- **Second, independent code-review finding, also fixed before shipping:** `NewIssueHandler`'s constructor had grown two same-typed positional pairs (`int, time.Duration, int, time.Duration`) back-to-back — a real transposition risk the compiler can't catch (the B-070 precedent this mirrors only ever had one such pair). Bundled into a new named `IssueRateLimits{PerAgentLimit, PerAgentWindow, PreAuthMaxConcurrent}` struct instead; `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s one construction call site updated to build one by field name.

**Test coverage:** `issue_http_preauth_test.go` (new, pure unit, no Postgres) — a controllable fake `APIKeyValidator` that blocks inside `ValidateAndResolveAgent` until released, letting tests hold N calls genuinely "in flight" simultaneously: proves a request arriving while every slot is saturated is rejected immediately and never itself reaches the DB layer (exact call-count assertion, not timing-based), parameterized over two concurrency limits (AC1: the threshold is a real, configurable parameter), plus a dedicated test proving `Release()` genuinely frees a slot for reuse rather than leaking it. `issue_http_preauth_pg_test.go` (new, real Postgres) — a negative control proving legitimate issuance is unaffected, plus a genuine real-Postgres proof of saturation using a real `LOCK TABLE api_keys IN ACCESS EXCLUSIVE MODE` held in a separate transaction to force one real request to genuinely block inside its real DB query, confirming a concurrent second request gets a real 429 while that lock is held. `config_test.go` gained default/override/negative-rejection/env-var-override tests for all 3 new fields, following the file's existing `t.Setenv` conventions exactly. Existing B-098/116/118 real-Postgres tests (`issue_http_pg_test.go`, `issue_http_b116_b118_pg_test.go`) updated for the new `IssueRateLimits`-struct constructor call (test constants renamed, generous pre-auth concurrency so these authenticated-path tests never interact with the pre-auth gate) — zero behavioral change to what they assert.

**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both ran, both found real issues, all fixed (see above) — not merely disclosed.** Code review: the `NewIssueHandler` transposition risk. Security review: the shared-bucket cross-tenant DoS/self-trigger finding (the reason B-120's mechanism changed entirely) and the unbounded-map finding (moot once the mechanism changed, since the concurrency gate has no per-key map at all). Both passes separately, independently re-verified B-142's comment claims via their own greps and confirmed them accurate; both confirmed the pre-auth mechanism is purely additive and doesn't reorder/weaken/skip any of B-098/107/116/118/141's existing checks.

**Verified 2026-09-03: `go build`/`go vet` clean; full `go test ./... -count=1` clean across the entire `eami-gateway` module; the new concurrency-gate tests specifically repeated 15 times in a row with zero flakiness.** `-race` unavailable on this machine (no C toolchain/cgo for the Windows Go install) — correctness argued instead via deterministic channel-based synchronization in the tests themselves (blocking fakes / real Postgres locks force genuine overlap rather than relying on timing).

**Live-verified against the real rebuilt/restarted gateway container, three separate scenarios:** (1) AC1 — with `TOKEN_ISSUE_RATE_LIMIT_PER_AGENT=3` set (a temporary `docker-compose.yml` env passthrough, reverted before commit — see B-162 below for why that was necessary at all), a real seeded org/agent/API key issued exactly 3 real successful tokens then received a real 429 with a real `Retry-After: 51` header on the 4th. (2) AC2 — with `TOKEN_ISSUE_PREAUTH_MAX_CONCURRENT=1`, a real `LOCK TABLE api_keys IN ACCESS EXCLUSIVE MODE` held against the live `eami` database genuinely blocked one real request inside its real query; a second concurrent request got a real 429 instantly, and the first only completed (a real 401, bogus key) after the lock released — the same proof as the automated test, reproduced against the actual running process rather than a test harness. A first attempt using 60 truly-parallel `curl`/`xargs -P` requests against a real (fast, local) Postgres never observed a 429, confirming empirically that a sub-millisecond-latency-protected concurrency gate cannot be reliably saturated by ordinary HTTP client bursts — the deliberate lock-based technique was necessary for a genuine, deterministic live proof, not a shortcut. (3) AC3 — with default config restored, a real legitimate issuance still returned 200 with a real token, cross-agent scoping still 403, and a bogus key still 401: zero regression to B-098/116/118. All fixtures (org, cascade-deleted agent/api_keys) cleaned up, confirmed 0 remaining.

**New discovered work, both disclosed not fixed (out of this brief's scope):** **B-161** (Low, test-only) — `identity.IssueHandler`'s fire-and-forget `RecordIssued` goroutine (B-107) occasionally lost its race against a throwaway-DB test's own teardown during this brief's own verification runs, the identical mechanism B-127 already diagnosed for `recordEpisodeHook`/episodes, just newly visible now (plausibly made easier to hit by B-122/B-140's more aggressive `DROP DATABASE` teardown vs. the old shared-DB's plain `Close`). **B-162** (Low-Medium) — none of B-070's or this brief's 6 rate-limit env vars are actually wired through `docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.prod.yml`'s `environment:` blocks, so an operator following the documented ".env" contract would see no effect; found because this brief's own live verification needed a temporary, reverted-before-commit compose edit just to prove the override worked at all.

**Dependencies:** B-118 (the mechanism B-119 makes tunable, and B-120 sits adjacent to), B-070 (the pattern B-119 mirrors, and B-120's first draft mirrored before the redesign), B-071 (the production proxy topology that made the security review's finding real, not theoretical), B-141 (the fix B-142's comment now accurately describes), B-107 (`MaxBytesReader`, still the only pre-auth defense on body size, unaffected). **New discovered work:** B-161, B-162 (both above).

## eami-policy
**Purpose:** Shared policy-evaluation library (not a service), imported by `eami-gateway`. Structural (JSON-match) and semantic (LLM-intent) rule evaluation.
**Status:** STABLE (structural), PARTIAL (semantic — intentional stub).
**Key files:** `evaluator.go`, `structural.go`, `semantic.go`, `types.go` (flat module, no `internal/`).
**Interfaces:** Go library API — `Evaluate(rules, context) Decision`. Imported directly by `eami-gateway`; no network interface. `ActionContext.ToolServerID`/`Conditions.ToolServerIDs` added B-044 (2026-08-06) — lets a rule match a resolved `gateway_tools.id`, additive to the existing `ToolNames` by-string match. `Rule.OrgID`/`ActionContext.OrgID` added B-128 (2026-09-01) — `matchesRule()` now rejects any rule whose `OrgID` differs from the evaluated `ActionContext`'s (empty `Rule.OrgID` is a wildcard, only ever true for hand-built test literals — a real DB-loaded rule's `OrgID` is never empty).
**Data owned:** none (pure library over caller-supplied rule/context data).
**Test coverage:** 1 `_test.go` file (`policy_test.go`) vs 4 source files, including a test that pins the stub behavior (`TestEvaluate_SemanticRuleSkippedByStub`) and, since B-128 (2026-09-01), `TestMatchesRule_OrgID`/`TestEvaluate_OrgScoping_PriorityOrderingIndependentPerOrg`. **Verified 2026-09-01 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go test ./...` clean, 27/27 tests.**
**Known limitations:** `semantic.go` — semantic/LLM rule evaluation **always returns no-match** (`// TODO(BE-Policy): Implement full LLM-based semantic evaluation`). Any policy relying on a semantic rule silently falls through to the next rule or default action. Blocked on ADR-009/ADR-012 (LLM endpoint choice).

## eami-api
**Purpose:** SaaS REST backend. Auth, org/user/RBAC management, gateway-resource CRUD (agents/policies/tools/nodes), audit query, FinOps analytics, alerting, discover/ingest write paths, memory/episode read paths.
**Status:** STABLE (broad — largest, best-tested module). **Data-sovereignty landmine resolved 2026-07-22** (B-002 fully closed — see below; no longer a landmine).
**Key files:** `cmd/api/main.go`; `internal/{api,auth,alerting,config,store}/`. `internal/store/*.sql.go` — one file per domain, sqlc-style, parameterized queries throughout (no `fmt.Sprintf` SQL construction found). **`internal/api/memory.go` and `internal/store/episodes.go` no longer exist** — deleted in B-002 Brief 3 (2026-07-22); see below.
**Interfaces — full route table** (from `internal/api/router.go`, more authoritative than any doc):
- Public: `GET /health`, `POST /v1/auth/login`, `POST /v1/auth/refresh`
- Service-key (collector/gateway → API, no JWT): `POST /v1/reports`, `POST /v1/ingest/batch`, `POST /v1/internal/token-usage`, `POST /v1/reports/paste-events` (B-032, no live caller yet)
- JWT + admin: org/notification settings, user management (`/v1/users*`)
- JWT + admin/operator: API keys, agents/policies/tools/nodes CRUD, agent-config, alert-rule CRUD, approval creation
- JWT + admin/operator/approver: approval decide, alert acknowledge/resolve
- JWT + admin/operator/viewer (read): agents/policies/tools/nodes list+get, `/v1/audit`, `/v1/audit/export`, `/v1/audit/verify`, `/v1/alerts*`, `/v1/finops/summary`, `/v1/finops/timeseries`, `/v1/memory/episodes`, `/v1/memory/episodes/search`, `/v1/memory/episodes/{episodeId}` (all three now served by the gateway-proxy handlers, see below), `/v1/gateway/episodes`, `/v1/gateway/episodes/search`, `/v1/gateway/episodes/{episodeId}` (same handlers, second mount, unused by the frontend), `/v1/endpoints*`, `/v1/discover/endpoints*`, `/v1/paste-events`, `/v1/paste-events/timeseries` (B-038)
- JWT + any role: `GET /v1/approvals`, `GET /v1/approvals/{id}`
**Data owned:** `orgs`, `users`, `refresh_tokens`, `api_keys`, `agents` (gateway_agents), `agent_configs`, `policies`/`policy_conditions`, `gateway_tools`, `gateway_nodes`/`gateway_node_metrics`, `approval_requests`, `audit_log` (read; write owned by gateway), `alert_rules`/`alerts`, `token_usage`, `model_pricing`, `discovered_endpoints` + `endpoint_ai_apps`/`endpoint_mcp_servers`/`endpoint_model_files`/`endpoint_reports`, `paste_events` (B-032), `notification_config`. **`episodes` table: no longer directly readable from eami-api at all** — the only path to it is via `eami-gateway`'s org-isolated proxy (`internal/api/gateway_episodes.go`, Brief 2, frozen since).
**Interfaces — B-002 Brief 2 (2026-07-22, `internal/api/gateway_episodes.go`), gateway episode proxy — frozen/unchanged by Brief 3:** `GET /v1/gateway/episodes` (+`/search`, `/{episodeId}`) proxy to `eami-gateway`'s Brief-1 endpoint via `X-Service-Key` (config `Gateway.EpisodeReadServiceKey`, env `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY` — same var name/value eami-gateway itself validates inbound; `Gateway.URL`/env `API_GATEWAY_URL`, defaults `http://eami-gateway:8080`). Both config fields optional at eami-api startup — unset means the routes 502 per-request ("gateway proxy is not configured"), not a boot failure. Org isolation (the actual point of this brief, since Brief 1's service-key path enforces none): every gateway call's `org_id` is always `claimsFromContext(r).OrgID`, never client input; an optional `org_id` query param is accepted only as a tamper-check that 403s on mismatch before the gateway is ever called. Gateway error responses collapse to a real 404 (episode not found / cross-org, same value) or a generic 502 (everything else, including network failures) — gateway's plain-text body is never read past the status code, so nothing can leak through. Exported types `GatewayEpisode`/`GatewayListResult`/`GatewayError`/`GatewayEpisodeClient` (exported only so the external `api_test` test package can construct fakes/errors — not intended as a public API).
**Interfaces — B-002 Brief 3 (2026-07-22, branch `b-002-memory-cutover`), memory.go cutover — B-002 fully closed:** `eami-api/internal/api/memory.go` and `eami-api/internal/store/episodes.go` **deleted entirely** (verified zero other callers of `store.Queries.ListEpisodes`/`SearchEpisodes`/`ListEpisodesParams`/`store.Episode` before removal). `router.go`'s `/v1/memory/episodes` and `/v1/memory/episodes/search` registrations — the actual `api/openapi.yaml`-documented, `MemoryPage.tsx`-consumed routes — now point directly at Brief 2's `ListGatewayEpisodes`/`SearchGatewayEpisodes` handlers (same functions, new mount, zero duplicated logic). Added `GET /v1/memory/episodes/{episodeId}` → `GetGatewayEpisode`, filling a route `openapi.yaml` documented but no implementation ever existed for. `MemoryPage.tsx` required **zero code changes** — verified the JSON response shapes are byte-identical between the old `memory.go` output and Brief 2's output (same field names, same envelope) before relying on that. Security review for this brief specifically re-verified the org-isolation chain (`jwtMiddleware` → `checkOrgID` → `claimsFromContext(r).OrgID`) at the new `/v1/memory/episodes*` mount points rather than assuming Brief 2's review transferred automatically — confirmed the leak is fully closed, not just superseded by a safer alternative running alongside it.
**Test coverage:** 7 `_test.go` files vs 44 source files (post Brief 3 — 2 files net change: `memory.go`'s production code gone, `memory_test.go` added). Covers agents, approvals, auth, finops, policies, gateway episode proxy (Brief 2, 11 tests), and memory cutover (Brief 3, new `memory_test.go`, 8 tests reusing Brief 2's `fakeGatewayClient`/`newGatewayTestServer` with zero duplication) — including `TestMemoryEpisodes_List_MismatchedOrgIDSupplied_Returns403_GatewayNeverCalled`, re-proving the org-isolation centerpiece at the real frontend-facing route, and `TestMemoryEpisodesDetail_ValidRequest_ReturnsFullSteps`, asserting non-empty step content actually round-trips (not just a 200 status). **Verified 2026-07-22 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures.** Two subagent reviewer/security passes per brief (Brief 2 and Brief 3) all came back clean. **Frontend (`eami-ui`) build/lint/typecheck not run for Brief 3** — Node/npm confirmed genuinely absent from this machine (checked common install locations directly, unlike Go which was merely off-`PATH`) — `MemoryPage.tsx`'s correctness rests on manual shape-verification, not a compiler run. **`docker compose up`-based manual verification (requested by Brief 3's brief) not performed** — no Docker in this environment; flagged before implementation, not discovered after. **Pre-existing, unrelated issue discovered while verifying Brief 2:** several `TestFinOpsTimeSeries_*` subtests in `finops_test.go` panic internally (nil `s.queries` dereferenced in `finops.go:269`) but still report `--- PASS` because chi's `Recoverer` catches the panic; confirmed pre-existing, not fixed (out of scope) — see `BACKLOG.md` B-016.
**Known limitations / landmines:**
- `router.go` — the historical "Memory episodes (stubs...)" comment that used to sit above these routes is gone now that the routes themselves were rewritten in Brief 3 (was previously flagged as stale in `BACKLOG.md` B-012 — worth confirming that item can close too).
- `notification_channels` table (`schema.sql`) appears unused by any Go code — superseded by `notification_config`. Likely dead schema.
- Search over episodes is `ILIKE` text match on the `task` column, not vector similarity, despite pgvector being provisioned — consistent with the embedding placeholder (both wait on ADR-009). See `BACKLOG.md` B-008.
- **New, discovered during Brief 3:** `api/openapi.yaml`'s `EpisodeStep` schema doesn't match the real step JSON shape (`eami-gateway/internal/episode/recorder.go`'s `Step` struct) — predates B-002 entirely, now visible because `/v1/memory/episodes/{episodeId}` is the first real route where the documented schema is checkable against actual output. See `BACKLOG.md` B-017.
**Interfaces — B-022 (2026-07-23), gateway tool credential encryption:** `POST /v1/gateway/tools` now actually persists the `credentials` object it was always documented (`api/openapi.yaml`'s `ToolCreate.credentials`) but never implemented — previously silently discarded (decoded, never read, never written to `gateway_tools.credentials_encrypted`, 201 returned anyway; a gap surfaced by a prior full-application audit, not previously tracked under its own B-ID). New package `eami-api/internal/toolcreds` (`toolcreds.go`) implements AES-256-GCM encrypt/decrypt with a hex-encoded 32-byte key (`TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` env var, `config.Config.ToolCredentialsEncryptionKey`) — encryption happens in the API process; Postgres never sees the key or the plaintext. Deliberately not pgcrypto (the column's own schema comment says "encrypted using pgcrypto," but that extension is otherwise unused anywhere in the codebase — using it would mean sending the key to Postgres as a query parameter on every write, a weaker posture than app-side AEAD). `Server` gets a `toolCreds *toolcreds.Cipher` field (nil if the key is unconfigured — optional at startup, same convention as the B-002 gateway proxy config) and a `toolStoreOverride`/`toolQueries()` test seam scoped to `tools.go` only (mirrors `storeIface`/`WithGatewayClient` elsewhere in this file, doesn't touch the broader `Store` interface). `CreateTool` decodes `credentials` as `json.RawMessage` and a `credentialsProvided` helper decides presence structurally (any JSON object with ≥1 key, regardless of key names) rather than via the typed `ToolCredentials` struct — this was a security-review-caught bug in an earlier version of this change: deciding presence via the typed struct meant any unrecognized field name (a typo, a future field) decoded to an all-empty struct and silently reproduced the exact bug being fixed. The raw submitted bytes are what gets encrypted and stored, not a re-marshaled struct, so nothing the client sends can be dropped by the struct's fixed field set. If credentials are submitted but no key is configured, the request fails closed (500, no store call, no tool row created) — never a silent-success-with-data-loss. Credentials are write-only: `store.GatewayTool`/`ToolResp` have no field for the encrypted blob, and no query in `tools.sql.go` selects `credentials_encrypted` back out, so there is no leak surface in `ListTools`/`CreateTool`'s responses (structurally, not just by convention).
**Test coverage — B-022:** `eami-api/internal/toolcreds/toolcreds_test.go` (9 tests: round-trip, non-deterministic nonce, tamper/wrong-key/truncated-ciphertext rejection, key-validation edge cases) + `eami-api/internal/api/tools_test.go` (10 new tests, `tools.go` had zero test coverage before this: encrypted storage + decrypt-and-verify retrieval-proof, unaffected-without-credentials, empty-object treated as no-credentials, the unrecognized-field-name regression + its fail-closed variant, non-object-credentials-rejected, response-leak checks on both `CreateTool` and `ListTools`, and a nil-store-configured regression test — `tools.go`'s other three handlers previously called `s.queries` with no nil guard, unlike every other handler file in this package; a `Server` built via `NewHandler` for another handler's tests would have nil-panicked into an opaque 500 instead of a clean error, caught by the general code-review pass). **Verified 2026-07-23 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures** (full module, not just the new packages). Two independent subagent passes: security review caught and the fix confirmed the fail-closed-bypass-via-unrecognized-field-name bug above (re-verified clean after the fix); general code review caught the missing nil-store guard (fixed) and a stray non-standard error code, `"config_error"` vs. the codebase's uniform `"internal_error"` (fixed).
**Known limitations:** Key rotation/loss is operational, not code: losing `TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` makes any already-stored `credentials_encrypted` values unrecoverable (same class of risk as losing `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`), and there is no rotation tooling (re-encrypt-with-new-key) — out of scope for this fix.

**Interfaces — B-023 (2026-07-24), real `TestTool` connectivity check:** `POST /v1/gateway/tools/{toolId}/test` no longer returns a synthetic "connected" result — it was a stub (`tools.go`'s old comment: "For now returns a synthetic 'connected' result after a brief probe" — there was no probe). New file `eami-api/internal/api/tool_connectivity.go` performs a real check per `type`: `rest_api` does a real `http.Client` GET to `base_url` (`Authorization: Bearer <api_key>` if present, HTTP Basic using `oauth_client_id`/`oauth_client_secret` as a best-effort fallback for non-standard "OAuth" APIs that accept it, `auth_type=basic` always reports `misconfigured` since `ToolCredentials` has no username/password fields to supply it) — 401/403 classify as `auth-failed`, any other completed response (including 4xx/5xx) as `connected` (reaching the server and getting *a* response proves DNS/TLS/network work, which is what the test is actually asserting). `database` does a real `pgx.ConnectConfig` handshake against `connection_string` — a successful connect already proves both reachability and valid credentials (Postgres's own auth happens during the handshake), and a failure is classified via SQLSTATE: class `28xxx` (`invalid_password`/`invalid_authorization_specification`) → `auth-failed`, anything else → `unreachable`. `mcp` always reports `misconfigured` with an explanatory message: `mcp_command` describes a local subprocess meant to run on the gateway host, not eami-api's cloud process — executing an admin-supplied command string from the SaaS backend would itself be a command-injection/RCE surface, and `eami-gateway/internal/proxy` (read for this task) confirmed the gateway's real dispatch path doesn't even consult `gateway_tools` per-tool either, so there's no existing per-tool MCP connection behavior to mirror. Every outcome is bounded by an 8-second context timeout (`toolTestTimeout`) applied once, wrapping both the decrypt step and the dial — no retries, per this task's scope. Response shape changed from the old stub's `{status, latency_ms}` to `{success, latency_ms, error}`, which is what `api/openapi.yaml` has documented for this route all along (another previously-undetected spec-vs-implementation mismatch, now closed). The richer 4-way outcome (`connected`/`auth-failed`/`unreachable`/`misconfigured`) is only in the HTTP response — `gateway_tools.status`'s DB `CHECK` constraint only allows 3 values (`connected`/`degraded`/`disconnected`, schema frozen, not touched by this task), so `toolTestOutcome.dbStatus()` maps `auth-failed` → `degraded` (reached the endpoint; credentials are what's wrong) and both `unreachable`/`misconfigured` → `disconnected` for the persisted column only.

**Security-critical addition, found by review, not in the original plan:** `eami-api` is EAMI's own cloud SaaS backend (per `ARCHITECTURE.md`), architecturally distinct from `eami-gateway`, which runs on-prem inside the customer's own network. Without a network-target guard, an org-scoped `admin`/`operator` (a role with no other legitimate reach into EAMI's cloud environment) could point a tool's `base_url`/`connection_string` at cloud metadata endpoints (`169.254.169.254`) or internal-only services and use the connected/auth-failed/unreachable distinction as a reachability oracle against EAMI's own infrastructure — a real boundary crossing this feature would otherwise introduce, not a restatement of capability the caller already has via `eami-gateway` (which runs *in* the customer's own network, where they already have legitimate reach). Added `safeDialContext` + `isBlockedTestTarget`: resolves the target host (or parses it directly if already an IP literal), rejects the request before dialing if *any* resolved address is loopback, link-local (covers `169.254.169.254` and `fe80::/10`), unspecified, or private/RFC1918/ULA (via Go's own `net.IP.IsLoopback`/`IsPrivate`/`IsLinkLocalUnicast`/`IsLinkLocalMulticast`/`IsUnspecified`), then dials the already-validated IP directly (not the original host) so a DNS answer that changes between the check and the actual connection (rebinding) can't slip through. Wired into both paths: `testRESTTool` via `http.Transport.DialContext`, `testDatabaseTool` via `pgx.ParseConfig` + `cfg.DialFunc` + `pgx.ConnectConfig` (replacing the simpler `pgx.Connect(ctx, connString)`, which has no dial-level control point). Security review re-verified the fix clean, tracing the `169.254.169.254`/RFC1918 cases end-to-end through the real (non-test) code path and confirming the new `Server.toolDialOverride` test-injection seam (needed so tests can exercise real round-trips against local `httptest` servers, which the guard would otherwise correctly reject) is assigned only in `_test.go` files — never reachable from any HTTP request.

**Other fixes from the general code-review pass, applied before commit:** (a) `safeDialContext` originally only dialed the first resolved address; fixed to fall back through every validated address in order, mirroring `net.Dialer`'s own multi-address behavior — a host whose first A/AAAA record is down but whose second works no longer incorrectly reports `unreachable`. (b) `testRESTTool`'s per-call `http.Transport` had keep-alives enabled with nothing ever calling `CloseIdleConnections()` — since the ad-hoc Transport is discarded after each call but the underlying connection and its `readLoop`/`writeLoop` goroutines stay reachable through the open connection itself, every `TestTool` call against a REST tool leaked one idle socket/goroutine; fixed with `DisableKeepAlives: true` (a one-shot connectivity check has no reuse benefit anyway). (c) `testDatabaseTool`'s deferred `conn.Close(context.Background())` had no deadline of its own — if the peer went unresponsive after a successful handshake, `Close` could hang past the 8s bound the rest of the design promises; fixed with a dedicated 2-second close-only context, independent of the (possibly already-expired) outer context.

**Credentials never leak, first production `Decrypt` caller:** a new `toolStore` interface method, `GetToolForTest`, is the only place `credentials_encrypted` is ever read back — implemented via `toolStoreWithConnectivity`, a thin wrapper running one raw SQL query through `s.queries.DB()` (the same escape hatch `finops.go` already uses for queries with no sqlc-style wrapper), so `eami-api/internal/store/tools.sql.go` needed zero changes (explicitly confirmed acceptable with the user before implementing, since it's a deviation from this codebase's usual sqlc-style convention). The decrypted plaintext only ever reaches an `Authorization` header or a Postgres connection string — `classifyHTTPError`/`classifyPgConnectError` return only static, categorical strings (`"timed out"`, `"connection failed"`, `"database rejected the credentials"`), never `err.Error()` on the underlying error, which could otherwise echo back request/DSN details. `toolcreds.Decrypt` failures (wrong/rotated key, corrupted blob) are a clean `misconfigured` result, never a panic, never exposing the underlying decrypt error text.

**Test coverage — B-023:** 30 new tests. `tool_connectivity_test.go`: `classifyPgConnectError`/`classifyHTTPError` (pure-function, no live Postgres/network needed), `testRESTTool`/`testDatabaseTool` outcome classification via real `httptest` servers and refused/timed-out listeners, `testToolConnectivity` dispatch + decrypt-failure handling (including the no-leak assertion on the decrypted plaintext), `dbStatus()`'s 4-to-3 mapping, and direct coverage of the SSRF guard itself (`TestIsBlockedTestTarget` — loopback v4/v6, the AWS/GCP metadata address, RFC1918 ×3, IPv6 ULA, unspecified, plus two real public IPs confirmed *not* blocked; `TestSafeDialContext_*` — blocks by IP literal and by hostname resolution, blocks the metadata address, blocks RFC1918, rejects a malformed address). `tools_testtool_test.go`: full HTTP-round-trip handler tests for each acceptance-criterion outcome (valid-credentials-connected, invalid-credentials-auth-failed, connection-refused-unreachable-not-a-hang, no-credentials-still-testable, mcp-type-misconfigured-not-fabricated-connected, tool-not-found-404, decrypted-credentials-never-leak-in-response). **Verified 2026-07-24 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures — 149 tests total in the module (up from 143 pre-B-023).**
**Known limitations:** none remaining — the frontend gap this created (`handleTest` not reading `success`) is fixed, see the `eami-ui` section below, B-024.

**Interfaces — B-025 (2026-07-24), config validation closes a live auth-bypass:** `internal/config/config.go`'s `Load()` **had no `validate()` at all** — `defaults()` hardcoded `ServiceKey: "changeme"` and a DSN with a `changeme` password, so an unset `API_SERVICE_KEY` env var meant `middleware.go`'s `requireServiceKey` (already correctly `subtle.ConstantTimeCompare`, untouched by this fix) would accept a literal `X-Service-Key: changeme` header from any caller against the collector write paths (`/v1/reports`, `/v1/ingest/batch`, `/v1/internal/token-usage`) — a real, working authentication bypass on a naive deployment, not a cosmetic placeholder. Added `validate(cfg *Config) error`, called at the end of `Load()` (its error already propagates to `main.go`'s existing `log.Fatalf`, so no `main.go` change was needed): rejects `cfg.ServiceKey` if empty or a known placeholder, and rejects `cfg.Database.DSN` if empty or its password segment is empty/placeholder. `defaults()` no longer ships any literal `changeme` — the default `ServiceKey`/`Database.DSN` fields are simply absent now, so an unconfigured deployment fails `validate()` instead of silently working. Two new unexported helpers, duplicated verbatim in `eami-gateway`'s config.go (separate Go module via `go.work`, no shared package introduced for ~30 lines): `isPlaceholderSecret(v string) bool` (trims + lowercases, checks against `{"", "changeme", "devpassword"}`) and `dsnHasPlaceholderPassword(dsn string) bool`, which — after a security-review-caught bug in the first version (see below) — extracts the DSN's password segment via `dsnPassword()` (`scheme://user:password@host` shape, last `:`/`@` delimiters) and runs it through `isPlaceholderSecret` rather than raw substring matching.
**Security-review-caught bug, fixed before commit:** the first version of `dsnHasPlaceholderPassword` did a raw `strings.Contains(lower, ":changeme@")`-style substring check directly against the whole DSN, with no trimming. `isPlaceholderSecret` (used for `ServiceKey`) already trimmed whitespace, but this sibling function didn't — so `API_DB_PASSWORD=" changeme"` (leading space) or `API_DB_PASSWORD="changeme\r"` (a CRLF-corrupted `.env` value — this repo has already hit a real CRLF bug once, see B-020) would build a DSN like `...: changeme@host...` or `...:changeme\r@host...`, and the un-trimmed substring check would miss it, silently starting with an effectively-default password. Fixed by extracting the password segment first and validating it through the same trimmed/lowercased `isPlaceholderSecret` path as every other secret, closing the inconsistency rather than patching the substring list.
**Test coverage — B-025:** new `eami-api/internal/config/config_test.go` (8 tests, package had none before): `validate()`-level tests for placeholder/empty rejection and real-value acceptance on both `ServiceKey` and `Database.DSN` (including the CRLF/leading-space regression cases above and an unparseable-DSN case), plus `Load()`-level integration tests (`TestLoad_RejectsUnsetServiceKey`, `TestLoad_RejectsPlaceholderDBPassword`, `TestLoad_AcceptsRealSecrets`) added after the general code-review pass flagged that pure `validate()` unit tests alone wouldn't catch a regression like someone dropping the `validate(cfg)` call from `Load()` or re-adding a hardcoded default — the `Load()` tests exercise the actual env-var wiring, the real attack surface, using `t.Setenv` and a nonexistent config path (`os.IsNotExist`-tolerated, matching how docker-compose configures every service via env vars, not by editing the shipped YAML). **Verified 2026-07-24 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures** (full module).
