Independent reviewer + security subagent passes both ran against the diff; security review's one finding (the CRLF/whitespace gap above) was fixed and the full auth-bypass trace re-verified with no surviving path. **Manually verified live, not just via `go test`:** built and started `postgres`+`eami-api`+`eami-gateway` via `docker compose up -d --build` using this machine's real, already-generated `.env` secrets — both services started clean (`eami-api: database connected`, `... listening on :8081`); then, with the stack still running, ran `docker compose run --rm -e API_SERVICE_KEY=changeme eami-api` and `-e API_SERVICE_KEY= eami-api` and confirmed both print the exact `validate()` error and refuse to start, and separately confirmed `-e API_DB_PASSWORD=devpassword eami-api` is rejected the same way; stack torn down after (`docker compose down`).
**Out-of-scope observations logged, not fixed (see `BACKLOG.md`):** `eami-api/eami-api.yaml`/`eami-gateway/eami-gateway.yaml` still contain literal `"changeme"` values baked into the Docker image's default config file — no longer a security bug (`validate()` correctly rejects them if no env override is present), but a stale/misleading example config now inconsistent with this task's `.env.example` cleanup; `scripts/seed-db.sh`/`scripts/create-audit-partition.sh` still have a `${DATABASE_URL:-postgresql://eami_app:devpassword@...}`-style fallback, same silent-weak-default pattern this task closed elsewhere. Neither file was in this task's `MAY MODIFY` scope.

**Interfaces — B-026 (2026-07-24), persistent RSA key for user-session JWTs:** `internal/auth/auth.go`'s RS256 signing key **regenerated in memory on every container restart in the actual running system** — not because no persistence mechanism existed in the code (`NewService` already had a `loadPrivateKey` path for a non-empty `keyPath`), but because `internal/config/config.go` had **no env-var override for `cfg.Auth.RSAPrivateKeyPath` at all**, and neither `eami-api.yaml` (ships `rsa_private_key_path: ""`) nor `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` set one — so the docker-deployed system was always in ephemeral dev-mode, force-logging-out every user on every restart. Mirrors `eami-gateway/internal/identity`'s `loadOrGenerateKey` pattern (read as the reference implementation, not modified by this task): new `loadOrGenerateKey(path string) (*rsa.PrivateKey, error)` in `auth.go` tries the existing `loadPrivateKey` (unchanged — still supports both PKCS1 `"RSA PRIVATE KEY"` and PKCS8 `"PRIVATE KEY"` PEM formats, a bit more flexible than gateway's PKCS1-only loader), and only if the error is specifically `os.ErrNotExist` (checked via `errors.Is`, correctly unwraps through `loadPrivateKey`'s `%w`-wrapped `fmt.Errorf`) generates a new 2048-bit key, `os.MkdirAll`s the parent dir (`0700`), and writes PKCS1-PEM at `0600`. Any other load failure (corrupt file, wrong format, permission error) propagates as a real error instead of triggering silent regeneration — deliberately, since overwriting a corrupt-but-real key file would itself invalidate every outstanding token, the exact failure mode this fix exists to prevent. `NewService` now calls `loadOrGenerateKey` instead of the old `loadPrivateKey` when `keyPath != ""`; the `keyPath == ""` fully-in-memory ephemeral branch (dev mode, never touches disk) is untouched. New `API_JWT_KEY_PATH` env var wired into `config.go` (mirrors `GATEWAY_JWT_KEY_PATH`'s existing pattern exactly) overrides `cfg.Auth.RSAPrivateKeyPath`; `main.go` gained one `else` branch logging `"eami-api: using persistent RSA key at %s"` for parity with the pre-existing ephemeral-mode warning.
**Infra:** `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` — new named volume `api_certs` (not a bind mount, not baked into the image) mounted at `/certs` on the `eami-api` service, `API_JWT_KEY_PATH: /certs/api.key` added to its environment — mirrors the pre-existing `gateway_certs`/`GATEWAY_JWT_KEY_PATH` pattern exactly, distinct volume name (no collision). `scripts/setup.sh` — new `generate_api_keypair()`, a near-duplicate of the existing `generate_gateway_keypair()` (same openssl-in-a-throwaway-alpine-container mechanism, writes to `${project_name}_api_certs` instead of `${project_name}_gateway_certs`), called immediately after the existing gateway-keypair call; `API_JWT_KEY_PATH=/certs/api.key` added to the generated `.env`'s eami-api section. `.env.example` documents the new var next to the pre-existing `API_JWT_SECRET` entry. **Discovered, not fixed (out of scope):** `API_JWT_SECRET` (`.env.example`/compose/`config.go`) appears to be dead code — defined and generated by `setup.sh` but never read by any Go code (the service is 100% RS256; no HS256 fallback path exists despite the `.env.example` comment claiming one) — pre-existing, unrelated to this fix, not logged as a new B-ID since it's cosmetic/dead-config rather than a behavior gap.
**Test coverage — B-026:** new `eami-api/internal/auth/auth_test.go` (5 tests, package had zero tests before this): `TestNewService_GeneratesAndPersistsKey_WhenMissing`, `TestNewService_LoadsExistingKey_TokenValidAfterRestart` (the core contract proof — `Service` A issues a token at a temp key path, `Service` B constructed against the *same* path, simulating a restart, successfully verifies A's token, proving B loaded the identical key rather than minting a new one), `TestNewService_CorruptKeyFile_ReturnsError_NotOverwritten` (seeds garbage bytes at the key path, confirms `NewService` errors AND the file is byte-for-byte unchanged afterward — not silently regenerated over), `TestNewService_EphemeralMode_EmptyPathStillWorks` (regression guard for the untouched `keyPath == ""` branch), `TestNewService_MissingParentDir_CreatesIt`. **Verified 2026-07-24 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` all clean, 0 failures** (full module; `eami-gateway` module also re-verified clean and confirmed untouched by this change). Independent reviewer + security subagent passes both ran against the diff (mandatory per this task's standing rules, given it generates/persists key material for the first time in this code path) — both came back clean, no findings: security review traced every `fmt.Errorf`/`log.Printf` in the changed paths and confirmed key bytes never reach a log line or error message, confirmed `0600`/`0700` permissions are applied atomically at creation (no window of looser permissions), confirmed the `errors.Is(os.ErrNotExist)` branch correctly rejects (not silently regenerates over) permission-denied/corrupt-file errors, and confirmed `api_certs` is a genuine named volume with no collision against `gateway_certs`. **Manually verified live, not just via `go test`:** built and started `postgres`+`eami-api`+`eami-gateway` via `docker compose up -d --build` (confirmed `api_certs` volume auto-created, startup log now reads `"eami-api: using persistent RSA key at /certs/api.key"` instead of the old ephemeral warning); seeded a throwaway test org/user directly in Postgres, logged in via `POST /v1/auth/login` to get a real access token, confirmed it authenticates (`GET /v1/gateway/agents` → 200) *before* restart; ran `docker compose restart eami-api`; confirmed the **same token, no re-login**, still authenticates (200) *after* restart. Went further than the brief's minimum: also ran a full `docker compose down` (no `-v`) + `up` cycle and confirmed the volume survived and the same token still worked afterward too. Test org/user cleaned up from Postgres after verification; stack torn down.

**Interfaces — B-027 (2026-07-25), panic recovery for the alerting engine's background loop:** `internal/alerting/engine.go`'s `Engine.Run` — a `time.Ticker`-driven loop evaluating active alert rules once a minute — had no panic recovery anywhere: an unrecovered panic in `ListAllActiveAlertRules` or in evaluating any single rule would crash the whole `eami-api` process (chi's `Recoverer` only protects the HTTP request path; this loop runs outside it). New unexported `guard(name string, fn func())` in a new `recover.go` (local to this package — it's the only background/ticker goroutine in this module, so no cross-package helper was needed; see the `eami-gateway` section below for the sibling module's shared `internal/safego`, used by three call sites there). Two layers, both required: `Engine.evaluateRules` (extracted from `tick`'s existing per-rule `for` loop, same body, same error handling) calls `guard` around each rule's `evaluateRule` call individually — a panic evaluating one rule is logged and the loop moves to the next rule in the *same* tick, not just "recovers once and dies." `Engine.safeTick` (new, wraps `tick`) is defense-in-depth for anything that could panic outside the per-rule loop, e.g. `ListAllActiveAlertRules` itself — `Run`'s ticker loop calls `safeTick` instead of `tick` directly. No change to `tick`/`evaluateRule`'s actual logic or any existing `log.Printf` line — confirmed via diff (31 lines changed, all wrapper/extraction, zero business-logic edits).
**Test coverage — B-027 (alerting):** new `recover_test.go` (4 tests: panic doesn't propagate, no-panic runs normally, a panicking call doesn't prevent a subsequent call from running, panic value + name land in the log) and `engine_test.go` (new `fakeDBTX` implementing `store.DBTX` lets tests deterministically panic inside `QueryRow`/`Query` without a live Postgres connection): `TestEvaluateRules_PanicInOneRule_OthersStillEvaluate` proves acceptance criterion #1 directly — two rules, the first's metric query panics, and the test asserts the second rule's `QueryRow` was still reached (2 calls, not 1); `TestEvaluateRules_NoPanic_BothRulesEvaluateNormally` is the control case; `TestSafeTick_PanicListingRules_DoesNotCrash_AndNextTickWorks` proves the outer layer — a panic in `ListAllActiveAlertRules` on the first tick doesn't prevent a second `safeTick` call (simulating the next ticker fire) from completing normally. **Verified 2026-07-25 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` clean, 0 failures**, full module including all pre-existing packages (`api`, `auth`, `config`, `toolcreds`) confirmed unaffected. General code-review pass (covering both modules' diffs together, see the `eami-gateway` section below for the rest): clean — one trivial redundant `rule := rule` loop-variable capture in an early version (harmless under Go 1.22+'s per-iteration loop vars, since this codebase's toolchain note confirms `go1.26.5`) flagged and removed before commit. See `BACKLOG.md` B-027 for the full cross-module writeup (this task touched both `eami-api` and `eami-gateway`, separate Go modules, in one session).

**Interfaces — B-032 (2026-07-25), paste-detection groundwork: `paste_events` table + batch ingestion endpoint:** backend foundation for a future browser-extension paste-detection epic, built ahead of the extension itself (no live caller yet, verification uses synthetic requests) after an investigation this session concluded `discovered_endpoints`/`POST /v1/reports` was the wrong shape for it (dedup-by-`(org,source_host,method,host,path)` tuple with a single overwritten `last_seen` — can't answer "how many paste events happened, and when"; that investigation also found `/v1/reports` currently has **zero real producers** — `eami-collector`'s forwarder only ever posts to `/v1/ingest/batch`, a separate table/pipeline). New `POST /v1/reports/paste-events` (`internal/api/paste_events.go`, `IngestPasteEvents`), service-key auth (`requireServiceKey`, same middleware as `/v1/reports`), accepts a JSON array, returns `202 {"accepted": N, "rejected": N}`.
**Schema (`schema/migrations/008_paste_events.sql` + `schema.sql`):** `paste_events` — new TimescaleDB hypertable on `occurred_at` (append-only, no `ON CONFLICT`/dedup, one row per event — deliberately unlike `discovered_endpoints`), `org_id`/`source_endpoint_id` real FKs (`orgs`/`endpoints`, both `ON DELETE CASCADE`), `destination_domain` TEXT (not a FK — no domains dimension table exists anywhere in this schema), `content_length`/`content_hash` (coarse indicators only, nullable), optional anonymised `os_username` (mirrors `eami-agent`'s browser scanner `BrowserExtension.UserPath` convention — no employee/user-directory table exists to FK against; `users` is dashboard-login accounts, a different identity space). **Hypertable, not `audit_log`'s pg_cron monthly-partition pattern:** investigated both existing time-series conventions in this codebase and picked based on query shape, not habit — `audit_log`'s manual partitioning exists for its RLS insert-only policy + hash-chain tamper-evidence requirements (compliance-driven), which don't apply here; `paste_events` is pure time-series analytics shaped like `token_usage`, so it follows that table's `create_hypertable()` pattern (no PK on `id`, same as `token_usage`/`audit_log` — the partition column must be part of any unique constraint/PK). Three indexes for the actual reporting query shapes (not a generic `org_id`/`created_at` composite): `idx_paste_events_org` (org-only trend), `idx_paste_events_org_domain` (org+domain+time — the CIO-dashboard query), `idx_paste_events_endpoint` (per-machine drill-down). **Retention:** 90-day Timescale `add_retention_policy` (background-job-enforced, unlike `endpoint_reports`' comment-only "kept for 90 days" convention it matches numerically). **Migration idempotency fixed during review** (see below) to match `005_token_usage.sql`'s established `IF NOT EXISTS`/extension-guarded convention, which the first version didn't follow.
**Source-identity resolution — deliberately NOT `UpsertAgentEndpoint`:** new `ResolvePasteSourceEndpoint` (`internal/store/paste_events.sql.go`) finds-or-creates the `endpoints` row by `(org_id, agent_id)` like the existing `UpsertAgentEndpoint` (used by `ingest.go`'s full agent-scan pipeline), but only refreshes `last_seen` on conflict — it does not touch `hostname`/`agent_version`/`os_info`. `UpsertAgentEndpoint` unconditionally overwrites all three on every call; a paste event only ever carries a hostname (never agent_version/os_info), so reusing it here would silently blank out richer metadata the full `eami-agent` scanner already collected for that machine every time a paste event arrived. Confirmed against `UpsertAgentEndpoint`'s real definition during code review (not just asserted) and now has direct regression coverage (see tests below) proving scanner-written `agent_version`/`os_info` survive a subsequent paste event untouched.
**Batching — the gap this was built to avoid repeating:** `BatchInsertPasteEvents` writes an entire batch via a single multi-row `INSERT ... SELECT * FROM unnest($1::uuid[], ...)` (parameterized arrays, no string-built SQL) — one Postgres round trip regardless of batch size, unlike `reports.go`'s `IngestReports`, which calls `UpsertEndpoint` once per event in a loop (N round trips per request, flagged as a real gap by this session's prior investigation, not fixed here since it's out of this task's scope — `reports.go` itself was explicitly untouched). Proved quantitatively, not just by code reading: a `pgx.QueryTracer`-instrumented test asserts exactly 1 query fires for both a 1-event and a 50-event batch. One real behavioral difference from `reports.go`'s per-item resilience, caught by code review and worth knowing: because the write is one batch statement, a DB failure on `BatchInsertPasteEvents` fails the *whole* batch's already-validated events in one 500, rather than `reports.go`'s per-item partial-success pattern — low practical risk since every value reaching the batch has already round-tripped through a successful `ResolvePasteSourceEndpoint` (FKs pre-validated), but not free.
**Content-scrub guarantee, structural not conventional:** `PasteEventReport` (the request type) has a fixed field set — `org_id, agent_id, hostname, destination_domain, occurred_at, content_length, content_hash, os_username` — with no field capable of carrying raw pasted text, mirroring B-022's toolcreds "no field to carry it" pattern. Verified two ways: a reflection test asserting the exact field set (fails immediately if a future edit adds a carrying field), and a live test that POSTs a crafted payload with smuggled `"content"`/`"raw_text"` keys carrying a sentinel string against the real endpoint/DB and confirms the sentinel appears in no stored column — proving Go's default unknown-field-dropping behavior actually holds end-to-end, not just in principle.
**Domain allowlist — new list, not a new convention:** `internal/api/paste_domains.go`'s `KnownPasteDestinations`/`MatchesKnownPasteDestination` mirrors `eami-agent/internal/detection/network_activity`'s `KnownAIHosts`/`MatchesKnownAIHost` shape exactly (case-insensitive exact-or-subdomain-suffix match) but is a separate list, not an import — `eami-agent`'s list lives under an `internal/` package in a different Go module (`go.work`), which Go does not allow importing across module boundaries. Covers browser-facing UI domains (`chat.openai.com`, `claude.ai`, etc.), deliberately distinct from `KnownAIHosts`' API hostnames (`api.openai.com`) since paste events are reported against the UI a human pastes into, not the API host. Enforced server-side before any write (never trusts the client's own classification) — confirmed by a security review pass specifically checking for suffix-match bypasses (e.g. `chat.openai.com.attacker.com`), none found.
**Test coverage — B-032:** new `eami-api/internal/api/paste_events_test.go`, 9 tests, all integration-level against a real Postgres+TimescaleDB (no mock/fake layer exists for `*store.Queries` in this codebase — `reports.go`'s `IngestReports` has the same property and, notably, has **zero** test coverage; this sibling endpoint is now better-tested). Covers: structural + live no-raw-content guarantee (above), batch write correctness with no dedup/collapsing (25-event batch, same domain/source, distinct timestamps — proving `paste_events` doesn't inherit `discovered_endpoints`' collapsing behavior), the one-round-trip batching proof (above), server-side allowlist rejection (mixed valid/invalid-domain batch, confirms the rejected event never reaches a row), the `ResolvePasteSourceEndpoint`-vs-`UpsertAgentEndpoint` non-clobbering regression (added after code review flagged it as untested — the single riskiest piece of new logic), a `discovered_endpoints`/`reports.go` regression test proving the two ingestion paths coexist in the same process/DB with zero behavioral change to the pre-existing upsert-by-tuple hit-count semantics, and a seeded-volume performance test (below). **Verified 2026-07-25 with a real toolchain, including a real database, not just static review:** `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean across all 5 workspace modules (`eami-api`, `eami-gateway`, `eami-policy`, `eami-collector`, `eami-agent`); `go test ./...` clean across `eami-api` (all packages, not just the new one) and `eami-gateway` (unmodified, sanity-checked). Docker Desktop was paused at session start (confirmed via the user before unpausing) — brought up via `docker desktop start`/`docker compose up -d postgres` against this repo's real `docker-compose.yml`, migration 008 applied by hand against the existing (non-fresh) dev database exactly as its own header instructs, then re-applied a second time after the idempotency fix to confirm the re-run is genuinely clean (`NOTICE: ... already exists, skipping` throughout, no errors).
**Performance verification (acceptance criterion, not assumed):** seeded 200,000 synthetic `paste_events` rows (2 orgs × 200 endpoints × 100k rows, 60-day spread, 5 rotating domains) directly via set-based SQL, then ran a real `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` on the representative CIO-dashboard query ("paste events by domain, last 30 days, for org X") through the actual HTTP-independent store path. Result: `Index Only Scan` on `idx_paste_events_org_domain` for every TimescaleDB chunk touched, zero `Seq Scan` anywhere in the plan, 5.3ms total execution time. Full plan text captured in the test log (`TestPasteEvents_ReportingQuery_UsesIndex_AtRealisticVolume`, gated on `testing.Short()` since seeding takes real time).
**Reviewer + security subagent passes (mandatory per this task's standing rules — new privacy-sensitive ingestion surface):** both ran independently against the diff. **Security review:** no HIGH/MEDIUM-confidence new vulnerabilities — traced the parameterized `unnest()` batch insert (no SQL injection surface), the structural no-raw-content guarantee, and the domain-allowlist suffix-match logic for bypasses (none found); noted the endpoint reuses the existing client-supplied-`org_id`-under-service-key trust model already used by `/v1/reports`/`/v1/ingest/batch`/`/v1/internal/token-usage`, an existing pattern this change continues rather than a new deviation. **General code review** found and this session fixed three real issues before commit: (1) migration 008 was missing the `IF NOT EXISTS`/extension-guard idempotency pattern every prior migration since 005 established — fixed and re-verified by re-running the migration live against the same DB; (2) the `ResolvePasteSourceEndpoint`-vs-`UpsertAgentEndpoint` non-clobbering behavior (the riskiest new logic) had no test — added `TestResolvePasteSourceEndpoint_DoesNotClobberScannerMetadata` (first version had a false failure comparing `jsonb` output byte-for-byte instead of semantically — Postgres's `jsonb` column re-serializes with its own canonical spacing on read; fixed to parse-and-compare); (3) a trivial `gofmt` misalignment in the new test file's helper functions, fixed. Also flagged, not fixed (deferred, see `BACKLOG.md` B-033): `api/openapi.yaml` has no entry for the new route, unlike every other `reports.go` handler — out of this task's `MAY MODIFY` scope (`openapi.yaml` is owned by Architect-EAMI per `BOUNDARIES.md`).
**Known limitations:** no `GET` endpoint exists yet for `paste_events` — reporting/dashboard consumption is explicitly out of this task's scope (ingestion groundwork only); a materialized view / pre-aggregated daily rollup table was investigated per this task's brief and *not* built — the base table's indexes already prove fast enough at 200k-row volume via the real `EXPLAIN ANALYZE` above, so a rollup is a "when it's actually needed" optimization, not a day-one requirement. `api/openapi.yaml` not updated (see above, B-033). No browser extension exists yet — this endpoint has no live caller; verification is entirely synthetic-request-based, as the brief specified. ~~No `GET` endpoint exists yet~~ — **now built, see B-038 immediately below.**

**Interfaces — B-038 (2026-08-03), read-only admin API over `paste_events` (B2):** the `GET` gap flagged directly above is now closed. New `ListPasteEvents`/`CountPasteEvents` in `internal/store/paste_events.sql.go` (mirrors `ListAudit`/`CountAudit`'s exact shape: optional `Domain`/`From`/`To` filters via `NULL`-coalescing SQL, `Limit`/`Offset` pagination, a shared `listPasteEventsWhereSQL` fragment so the list and count queries can never drift out of sync on which rows match) and `Server.PasteEventsTimeSeries` (`internal/api/paste_events.go`) — raw `time_bucket()` SQL built directly in the handler via `s.queries.DB()`, mirroring `FinOpsTimeSeries`'s exact structure (dynamic bucket interval via a fixed `switch`, never user input, so no injection surface). Two new routes, `GET /v1/paste-events` and `GET /v1/paste-events/timeseries`, mounted in the existing admin/operator/viewer read group next to `/v1/audit`. **A deliberate improvement over the `FinOpsTimeSeries` pattern it mirrors:** that endpoint only returns total cost per bucket, and `FinOpsPage.tsx` distributes it across models *proportionally* as a display-only approximation (no real per-model-per-day breakdown exists server-side); `PasteEventsTimeSeries`'s query already groups by `(bucket, destination_domain)` directly, so its response is real per-domain counts, not an estimate.
**Data-source decision, made before writing any code (per this task's own brief, which required checking rather than assuming):** confirmed live via direct `psql` query that the real `Dev Org` has **zero rows** in `paste_events` — the 800,000 rows present in the table belong entirely to B-032's own disposable `paste-events-perf-*` synthetic test orgs, still left over from prior test runs. The only real, live-captured paste events (from B1/B-036's manual test pass) sit in `endpoint_reports`' raw JSON blob, the interim path B-035 exists to retire. Built this read API against `paste_events` anyway — it is the correct, purpose-built, indexed table the task's own contract named, and the alternative (reading the blob) would be throwaway work — but this means the new UI shows an honest empty state for any real org today. **B-035 is now the explicit next task**, raised to High priority in `BACKLOG.md`, specifically because it's the only thing separating this shipped, fully-tested feature from showing real data.
**No raw content, structurally:** `PasteEventResp` (`types.go`) carries only `id`, `destination_domain`, `occurred_at`, `content_length`, `content_hash`, `os_username` — literally the only columns `paste_events` has. There is no raw-content column anywhere upstream of this struct for it to accidentally expose; a reflection-based test locks the exact field set the same way B-032's own `PasteEventReport` test does for the write side.
**`api/openapi.yaml` not touched** (owned by Architect-EAMI, same reasoning B-032 used for the ingestion route) — widened B-033 to cover these two new routes too rather than filing a separate doc-gap item. Frontend calls them via `apiFetch`'s documented escape hatch (`client.ts`) instead of the generated typed client, matching `MemoryPage.tsx`'s pre-B-002-Brief-3 precedent for exactly this situation.
**Test coverage — B-038:** new `internal/api/paste_events_read_test.go`. 6 fast integration tests against a real Postgres: domain/time filtering (plus a reflection check locking `PasteEventResp`'s field set, AC4), pagination correctness (distinct rows across pages), org isolation (a second org's viewer sees zero rows from the first org's seeded events — proven via a real signed JWT through the actual `jwtMiddleware`/`requireRole`/`claimsFromContext` chain, not a bypass; needed its own `newReadEnv` test harness since `paste_events_test.go`'s existing `pasteEventsTestEnv` was built with `authSvc=nil`, sufficient for its own service-key-only ingestion tests but not for these JWT-protected read routes), timeseries bucket/domain grouping correctness, and timeseries input validation (missing from/to, invalid granularity). Plus 2 real `EXPLAIN (ANALYZE, BUFFERS)` tests, reusing B-032's own 100,000-row-per-org seeded-volume convention (`seedPasteEventsPerfData`, a second org seeded alongside so the org filter is actually selective) rather than inventing a new one — proving the *exact* queries `ListPasteEvents` and `PasteEventsTimeSeries` issue use `idx_paste_events_org_domain`/`idx_paste_events_org` respectively, zero `Seq Scan`, at that volume (list query: 0.224ms; timeseries aggregation over 100k rows: 42ms). **Verified 2026-08-03 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./...` clean across the full module** (not just the new file) **including the two seeded EXPLAIN ANALYZE tests run without `-short`.**
**Known limitations:** none new for the backend; inherits B-035's gap noted above (empty for real orgs until that ships). ~~empty for real orgs until B-035 ships~~ — **B-035 now done, see immediately below.**

**Interfaces — B-035 (2026-08-04), paste events wired into the real `paste_events` table:** closes the gap B-034/B-036/B-038 each explicitly flagged and deferred. **Investigated rather than assumed, per this task's own brief:** re-confirmed `eami-agent` still has no `org_id` anywhere, and B-032's original client-facing `POST /v1/reports/paste-events` still trusts a per-event client-supplied `org_id` directly — not reusable as-is for agent-originated data. **Corrected the brief's own assumption**: `eami-collector` does not resolve `org_id` anywhere in its code (confirmed by grep across the whole module) — it's a pure blind relay (`/v1/ingest` → SQLite buffer → forwarder → `POST /v1/ingest/batch`), authenticated only by a single collector-wide service key. The only place `org_id` is ever resolved is `eami-api`'s `IngestBatch`, via the pre-existing `GetDefaultOrgID` ("oldest org") single-tenant placeholder. Traced the wire format end-to-end: `eami-agent`'s native-messaging relay (B0) already sends paste events through the **existing, completely unmodified** `/v1/ingest` → buffer → forwarder → `/v1/ingest/batch` pipeline (B0's `outboundReport{agent_id, hostname, collected_at, paste_events}`, forwarded as opaque bytes throughout) — so **`eami-agent` and `eami-collector` needed zero code changes**, a real simplification versus the brief's own initial sketch (which anticipated a new `eami-collector` relay route). The entire fix is confined to `eami-api`.
**The actual fix, `internal/api/ingest.go`:** `agentReport` gained a `PasteEvents []rawPasteEvent` field. `processIngestItem` now branches on `rep.PasteEvents != nil` (checked via nil-ness, not `len() > 0` — see the code-review-caught bug below) to a new `processPasteEventRelayItem`, which resolves the endpoint via `ResolvePasteSourceEndpoint` (B-032's non-clobbering upsert, only touches `last_seen`) and writes straight into `paste_events` via `BatchInsertPasteEvents` — never calling `UpsertAgentEndpoint`/`InsertEndpointReport`/any normalised-table insert for that item. A normal full-scan report's path is completely untouched. `orgID` is always the value `IngestBatch` already resolved server-side before `processIngestItem` is ever called — `agentReport` has no `OrgID` field anywhere in its struct tree, so nothing in the JSON a compromised `eami-agent` controls can influence which org its paste events land in, structurally, not by convention.
**Real correctness bug found by the design itself, before any code was written:** a paste-event-only relay report has no scan data (blank `agent_version`/platform). Routing it through the pre-existing `UpsertAgentEndpoint` (which unconditionally overwrites `agent_version`/`os_info` on every call, by design, for real scans) would have silently clobbered a real endpoint's already-known metadata — the exact bug class `ResolvePasteSourceEndpoint` was built to avoid for B-032's own direct-caller path. Avoided entirely by design, not patched after the fact: paste-relay items never reach `UpsertAgentEndpoint` at all.
**Shared validation, not duplicated:** extracted `validatePasteEvent` (`paste_events.go`) — domain-allowlist check (`MatchesKnownPasteDestination`, never trusts client classification) + RFC3339 timestamp parse + lowercasing — used by **both** the new relay path and the original `IngestPasteEvents` (refactored to call it, zero behavioral change to its existing accept/reject counting, confirmed by the full pre-existing test suite passing unmodified).
**Reviewer + security subagent passes (mandatory, org-resolution trust boundary):** both ran independently. **Security review:** no High/Medium findings. Confirmed org resolution cannot be spoofed (structurally, not just empirically — `agentReport` has no field to carry one) and confirmed the design stays safe even after `GetDefaultOrgID` is eventually replaced by real multi-tenant resolution, *as long as* whatever replaces it keeps deriving `orgID` from an authenticated channel rather than from request-body fields — flagged as the one thing to preserve, not a bug today. Confirmed full SQL parameterization throughout (`unnest()`-based batch insert, parameterized `ON CONFLICT` upsert). One **Low**: no length caps on `content_hash`/`os_username`/`destination_domain` or on the paste-events-per-item array size (this endpoint is reachable directly by anything holding the collector's service key, bypassing B0's own 1MB native-messaging protocol cap entirely) — **fixed**: added `maxDestinationDomainLen`/`maxContentHashLen`/`maxOSUsernameLen` to `validatePasteEvent` (applies to both entry points) and `maxPasteEventsPerRelayItem = 100` to the relay path. **Code review:** one **Medium-High** real bug, caught before it ever shipped: the original branch condition was `len(rep.PasteEvents) > 0`, which can't distinguish "the `paste_events` key is absent from the JSON" from "the key is present as an empty `[]`" (Go's `encoding/json` sets a slice to `nil` only in the former case) — a relay item with an explicitly-empty-but-present array would have silently fallen through to the clobbering scan-report path. **Fixed** by checking `rep.PasteEvents != nil` instead, and added `TestIngestBatch_EmptyPasteEventsArray_StillDoesNotClobber` to lock it in. Also caught and fixed a **Medium**: a hypothetical future item carrying both real scan data and `paste_events` together would have silently discarded the scan data with no log line — now logs a warning (defensive; today's two report shapes remain structurally mutually exclusive, confirmed, not just assumed). One **Low** dead-code note (an unreachable `agentID == ""` guard) cleaned up in the same pass.
**Test coverage — B-035:** new `internal/api/ingest_paste_relay_test.go`, 6 integration tests against a real Postgres, all sending the *exact* JSON shape B0's real relay produces (not a hand-simplified proxy): a relayed event lands in `paste_events` and produces zero `endpoint_reports` rows (AC1/AC4); a smuggled fake `org_id` in the relay payload is provably inert — confirmed both that the resulting row uses the real resolved org and that no org row was ever created for the fake ID (AC2); the server-side domain allowlist still rejects an unrecognized domain on this path; a normal full-scan report in the same batch shape is completely unaffected, still writing `endpoint_reports`/normalised tables exactly as before (AC5); the centerpiece, `TestIngestBatch_PasteEventRelay_DoesNotClobberScannerMetadata`, seeds real scanner metadata via a genuine full-scan item, then relays a paste event for the same `agent_id` with a *different* hostname, and confirms `hostname`/`agent_version`/`os_info` all survive untouched; and the empty-array regression test added during review. Because `GetDefaultOrgID` resolves to "the oldest org in the whole database" (not a disposable per-test org, unlike B-032's own synthetic-org tests), every test asks the server what its actual default org is via the same `GetDefaultOrgID` call `IngestBatch` itself uses, rather than assuming one, and cleans up via a uniquely-prefixed `agent_id` (`ON DELETE CASCADE` from `endpoints`) so nothing outside what each test created is ever touched. **Verified 2026-08-04 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-api` module** (not just the new file), **plus `eami-agent`/`eami-collector`/`eami-gateway` all still build clean** (confirming the "zero changes needed" claim above, not just asserting it). A genuine environment hiccup was hit and diagnosed mid-task, unrelated to this code: this machine's `localhost` resolves IPv6-first (`::1` before `127.0.0.1`), and Docker Desktop's IPv6 port-forward on this machine accepts the TCP handshake but silently drops all subsequent data — confirmed via a minimal standalone Go probe isolating the exact failure point, worked around for test runs via `TEST_DATABASE_URL` forcing IPv4 (no code changes), full Docker stack confirmed healthy afterward via `127.0.0.1`.
**Known limitations:** none — this was the last explicitly-tracked gap in the paste-detection epic (B-032→B-034→B-036→B-038→B-035, in build order). `GetDefaultOrgID`'s single-tenant-v1 design itself is unchanged and out of scope (pre-existing, not introduced here) — see the security review note above for what must be preserved if/when it's replaced.

**Interfaces — B-045 (2026-08-06), Tools page gains Edit:** the Tools admin page had `ListTools`/`CreateTool`/`DeleteTool`/`TestTool` and no way to correct a mistake short of delete-and-recreate — confirmed as a real, live gap during B-044's own manual testing (a wrong `base_url` needed a raw SQL fix). New `PATCH /v1/gateway/tools/{toolId}` (`store.UpdateTool` + `Server.UpdateTool`) is a partial update using the exact `COALESCE($n, column)` convention `UpdateAgent`/`agents.sql.go` already established in this codebase — a `nil` Go value means "leave unchanged." Applied to `credentials_encrypted` specifically: omitted `credentials` in the request → `nil` bytes passed → `COALESCE` preserves the existing encrypted blob; `credentials` present → re-encrypted via `CreateTool`'s exact same `credentialsProvided`/`s.toolCreds.Encrypt` path, same fail-closed guarantee if no cipher is configured. `type`/`auth_type` deliberately not editable (matches `UpdateAgent`'s precedent of only exposing operational fields). `api/openapi.yaml` deliberately not touched (Architect-EAMI-owned) — ships undocumented, matching B-038's precedent.
**Investigated the "is this a broader pattern" question directly, per the task brief:** Tools was the *only* resource missing Edit. `Agents`/`Policies`/`Settings` already had `PATCH`/`PUT` routes — confirmed via `router.go`'s full route table, not assumed.
**A real, necessary scope correction, confirmed with the user before building:** the frontend's generated OpenAPI client has no typed call for an undocumented `PATCH`; the documented fallback, `apiFetch()` in `eami-ui/src/api/client.ts` (CLAUDE.md's own "no raw fetch in components" escape hatch), was GET-only. Extended it with optional `{method, body}` — small, generic, reusable by future edit flows on other undocumented endpoints, not tool-specific — rather than writing a raw `fetch()` in the component.
**AC4 (no stale routing after an edit), verified not assumed:** `toolrouter/router.go` (B-044) has no caching — `Resolve` is a direct `pool.QueryRow` on every dispatch, grepped to confirm. Proven with a new real-Postgres test, `TestResolve_PicksUpEditedConfig_NoStaleCache`: dispatch to server A, edit `base_url` via a real `UPDATE` (the same effect `UpdateTool`'s `COALESCE` produces), dispatch again, confirm server B is hit.
**Reviewer + security passes both ran** (mandatory, credential handling): security review found the core logic sound (independently re-derived that `toolcreds.Cipher.Encrypt` can never produce a zero-length ciphertext, so "submitted" vs "omitted" credentials can never collide) but flagged a real gap — every credential-preservation test ran against a fake store, never real Postgres. **Closed**: new `tools_update_pg_test.go`, 4 real-Postgres tests using the real `store.Queries.UpdateTool`, reading real stored bytes back and decrypting them to confirm the actual end state (including that a rotation produces the NEW plaintext and the OLD one is genuinely gone, not just "no error"). Code review caught and this session fixed two real gaps before shipping: (1) **Medium** — `UpdateTool` had no empty-`name` validation, unlike `CreateTool`'s identical check; `{"name":""}` would have silently blanked a tool's name via `COALESCE` — fixed. (2) **Low** — the Edit UI's `mcp_args` handling treated whitespace-only input as "provided" (JS truthiness), producing an explicit `[]` that would silently wipe existing stored args — fixed to check `.trim()` first. A follow-up focused review, including running the new real-Postgres tests live, confirmed both fixes correct and complete.
**Test coverage:** 17 new — 10 fake-store unit tests (`tools_update_test.go`: persistence, credential preserve/rotate/fail-closed, no-leak response, not-found, invalid-id, empty-name rejected), 4 real-Postgres tests (`tools_update_pg_test.go`: credential preservation and rotation against real encrypted bytes, cross-org isolation, `mcp_args` preservation verifying pgx's nil-slice-to-NULL array encoding directly), 1 real-Postgres routing test in `eami-gateway` (AC4, above), plus `tsc && vite build` clean via the Docker builder-stage trick (Node/npm still absent locally), run twice — before and after the `mcp_args` fix. **Verified 2026-08-06 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across `eami-api` and `eami-gateway`.**
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh first:** edited the real "Test" `gateway_tools` row from B-044's own manual-testing session via a real admin JWT (a throwaway admin user seeded for this session, deleted afterward) — renamed it, then changed `base_url` to a distinguishable real `postman-echo.com` path. A single subsequent real `tool_call` through the live gateway proved AC2 and AC4 simultaneously: the dispatch reached the **edited** `base_url` (not stale data), while the **original** credential — set during B-044's testing, never touched by these edits — was still applied correctly as a real `Authorization` header. `base_url` restored to a clean value afterward; all throwaway fixtures cleaned up.
**Known limitations, documented not silently left:** no way to clear `base_url`/`mcp_command` to empty via the Edit panel (a blank field means "no change," matching the credential field's own established semantic, not "clear it"); a rename colliding with another tool's name in the same org surfaces as a raw `500` with the Postgres error text, not a clean `409` (pre-existing pattern shared with `CreateTool`, not introduced here).

**Interfaces — B-046 (2026-08-06), `gateway_tools.action_paths` persistence + validation:** full dispatch-side writeup in the `eami-gateway` section above. This module owns validation and storage: `ActionPathMapping` (`tools.go`) mirrors `toolrouter.ActionPathEntry`'s JSON shape exactly (a deliberate duplicate, not shared — same B-025/B-044 cross-module-`internal/`-visibility constraint). `validateActionPaths` normalizes and validates a submitted map: non-empty action name and path required, method uppercased and restricted to a fixed `GET/POST/PUT/PATCH/DELETE` allow-list (defaulting to `POST`, matching `toolrouter.Forward`'s own default), and — the one piece of hardening added directly from the security review's trace-through (see `eami-gateway` section) — a path containing `"://"` is rejected outright as a likely full-URL paste-in mistake, even though it was proven not to be an actual host-confusion vector. `GatewayTool.ActionPaths`/`CreateToolParams.ActionPaths`/`UpdateToolParams.ActionPaths` are raw JSONB `[]byte` passthrough, matching `AlertRule.ConditionConfig`'s existing convention exactly — no new wrapper type needed.
**`UpdateTool`'s tri-state semantics, following `MCPArgs`' existing precedent exactly:** `action_paths` omitted from the PATCH body (or explicit JSON `null`) → `nil` map after decode → `COALESCE` leaves existing mappings untouched. `action_paths` present as **any** object, including an empty `{}` → non-nil map → encoded as the literal bytes `"{}"` when empty so `COALESCE` still overwrites → this is how an admin explicitly clears all mappings, a real affordance intentionally added (not just left as a side effect) since without it mappings could be set but never fully removed short of raw SQL.
**Reviewer + security subagent passes:** both automated subagent passes failed on a platform session-limit error (see `eami-gateway` section) — substituted with a direct manual review plus the empirical `joinURLPath` host-confusion trace-through described there, which is what motivated the `"://"`-rejection hardening above.
**Test coverage:** 11 new handler-level tests (`tools_action_paths_test.go`, fake-store-based, mirroring `tools_test.go`/`tools_update_test.go`'s convention): valid mappings persisted with method uppercased, omitted-method defaults to `POST`, empty-path/unsupported-method/full-URL-path all rejected with the store never called, omitted-on-update leaves `ActionPaths` nil, explicit `{}` produces the literal clear signal, `ToolResp.ActionPaths` round-trips correctly and is omitted entirely (`omitempty`) for a tool with none. 3 new real-Postgres tests (`tools_action_paths_pg_test.go`, same `TEST_DATABASE_URL`/`POSTGRES_PASSWORD` convention as `tools_update_pg_test.go`): `CreateTool`'s JSONB bind round-trips exactly, `UpdateTool`'s `COALESCE` genuinely preserves real stored mappings when omitted, and genuinely clears them when given the literal `{}` — proving the tri-state semantics against real Postgres, not just a fake store. **Verified 2026-08-06 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-api` module**, including all real-Postgres tests.
**Known limitations:** duplicate action names within a single submitted `action_paths` object silently resolve to "last one wins" (ordinary Go map/JSON-object semantics, not flagged as an error) — acceptable for a hand-edited admin form, not surfaced as a UX warning in this iteration.

**Interfaces — B-055 (2026-08-10), first-boot setup wizard (B-053 follow-up):** the last open gap B-053's own README flagged ("the next brief needs its own race-safety design for whatever mechanism lets the first visitor claim admin") is closed. New `internal/api/bootstrap.go`: three pre-auth routes (no user exists yet to authenticate as) — `GET /v1/setup/status` (`{configured: bool}`, drives the frontend's post-setup redirect), `POST /v1/setup/token/validate` (read-only token check, reveals the org/admin form without consuming anything), `POST /v1/setup/bootstrap` (the real atomic operation). New `setup_tokens` table (`schema/migrations-v2/000003_add_setup_tokens.up.sql`) and a new `Queries.Begin(ctx)` helper (`internal/store/db.go`) so `Bootstrap` can run inside one real `pgx.Tx`.
**Concurrency guard, the brief's central requirement — a real DB lock, not check-then-act:** `Bootstrap` runs `SELECT id, expires_at, consumed_at FROM setup_tokens WHERE token_hash = $1 FOR UPDATE` inside its transaction. Two concurrent requests for the same token genuinely serialize on that row lock — the loser re-reads the committed `consumed_at` after the winner's transaction commits, not a stale in-memory value. The independent "system still unconfigured" check (`SELECT count(*) FROM orgs`) runs inside the *same* transaction, so it can't itself race a concurrent bootstrap either. Every failure path returns before `tx.Commit()`; the deferred `tx.Rollback()` discards any partial work (including token consumption), which is also the abandoned-session-recovery mechanism — an interrupted attempt (crash, dropped connection, VM reboot mid-request) never leaves the token spent or the system half-configured; a fresh attempt with the same still-valid token succeeds normally.
**Trust boundary:** the raw token is a `openssl rand -hex 32` (256-bit) value generated appliance-side (`appliance/scripts/eami-stack.sh`, see `appliance` section below) and printed only to the VM's console/journal output — the same channel B-053 already established as the trusted emergency-access boundary (SSH is permanently disabled). It is never transmitted, logged, or echoed anywhere over the network by `eami-api`; `BootstrapResponse` carries only `org_id`/`org_name`/`admin_email`. Rate limiting (`setupRateLimiter`, hand-rolled in-memory fixed-window, no new dependency) on `/token/validate` and `/bootstrap` is defense-in-depth only — the token's 256-bit entropy is the real defense against guessing.
**A real deviation from B-053's own stated assumption, found and required to be surfaced rather than worked around silently:** B-053's README said "whatever serves the wizard reads `/data/eami/state` directly." That's not actually possible — `docker-compose.prod.yml` is frozen for this brief too, and nothing bind-mounts `/data/eami` into any container, so `eami-api` has no way to read that host file. The real gate implemented here is DB-based (`SELECT count(*) FROM orgs`), which is also strictly more correct (always live, no separate file-sync step). `/data/eami/state` still exists and is best-effort-reconciled by `eami-stack.sh`, but is now informational only — see `appliance/README.md`'s rewritten contract section.
**TLS tradeoff, stated rather than silently accepted:** these routes run over the same plain-HTTP-only exposure every other `eami-api` route already has in this appliance (`docker-compose.prod.yml` has no TLS anywhere — `/v1/auth/login` already sends real admin passwords over plaintext HTTP today). The wizard introduces no new class of risk beyond what already ships; full TLS is correctly out of this brief's scope, tracked as the same pre-existing, already-accepted residual.
**Test coverage:** `bootstrap_test.go`, real-Postgres integration tests, each creating its own throwaway database (mirrors `schema/migrationtest`'s per-test pattern, since that module can't be imported directly — migrations applied by reading the real `schema/migrations-v2/*.up.sql` files via pgx's simple query protocol, which supports the multi-statement files the extended protocol can't run in one `Exec`). Covers: token validation (missing/unknown/expired/already-consumed/valid), a real concurrent race (`TestBootstrap_ConcurrentRequests_ExactlyOneWins` — 5 goroutines racing one token, asserts exactly 1 success and exactly 1 row in `orgs`/`users`), abandoned-session recovery (`TestBootstrap_InterruptedTransaction_TokenStaysValidForRetry` — locks and "consumes" the token in a transaction that's then rolled back instead of committed, confirms a subsequent real attempt still succeeds), the full state transition (`/v1/setup/status` false→true, a second bootstrap attempt with a *different* valid token still rejected once configured — proving the system-state check is independent of token validity), rate limiting, input validation, and a real login proof (`TestBootstrap_Success_LoginWorksAndTokenIsDeadAfterward` — creates an org/admin, logs in via the real `POST /v1/auth/login` with the created credentials, confirms a real JWT comes back, confirms the token can't be reused). **Verified 2026-08-10 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-api` module** (all 8 new tests passing against real Postgres, plus the full pre-existing suite unaffected).
**Reviewer + security subagent passes: both ran, and code review found a real, high-value gap — fixed before shipping, not glossed over.** Security review (an independent subagent pass plus this session's own direct trace-through) found no exploitable findings — confirmed no network-only path exists to create an org or to leak/derive the raw token, confirmed no SQL injection (every query parameterized), confirmed no secret is ever logged or echoed in a response.
Code review found **one real Medium-severity concurrency gap contradicting this brief's own explicit requirement**: the original design's `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the `setup_tokens` row only serializes two requests that share the *same* token — it does nothing for two requests each holding a *different*, independently valid, unconsumed token, which could both observe `orgCount == 0` under READ COMMITTED and both insert an org. In normal operation only one unconsumed token ever exists (`eami-stack.sh` deletes any prior one before minting a new one), but that's an operational invariant enforced by a shell script, not a database guarantee — exactly the "not just app-level check-then-act" gap the brief warned against. **Fixed** with a global `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('eami_setup_bootstrap'))` held for the transaction's lifetime, serializing *every* concurrent `Bootstrap` call regardless of which token it holds. **Reproduced the bug empirically before trusting the fix, not just reasoning about it**: temporarily disabled the new lock and ran the two-different-tokens regression test 5 times — it failed 3 of 5 (a genuine, non-deterministic race, exactly as theorized) — then restored the fix and confirmed it passes reliably. New test `TestBootstrap_ConcurrentRequests_DifferentValidTokens_ExactlyOneWins` added specifically to catch a regression of this exact gap (the original `TestBootstrap_ConcurrentRequests_ExactlyOneWins`, using one shared token, could not have caught it).
Three smaller findings also fixed: (1) `SetupWizardPage.tsx`'s `onSubmitBootstrap` error handler set the error message on the wrong form (`bootstrapForm`, which was about to be unmounted by the very same `setStage('token')` call) — a real bug caught in this session's own manual re-read, found before the code-review agent even ran, fixed by setting it on `tokenForm` instead. (2) The server's email regex required a literal dot in the domain, stricter than the frontend's zod `.email()` check, so a client-accepted address like `admin@localhost` could be server-rejected — loosened to match. (3) Combined with (2): `onSubmitBootstrap` unconditionally sent the admin back to re-enter the 64-character console token on *any* failure, even a fixable input error — now `apiFetch` throws a status-carrying `ApiFetchError`, and only a 401 (dead token) or 409 (already configured) triggers the return-to-token-gate; every other failure keeps the admin on the form with their already-typed org/admin details intact. One finding intentionally left as a disclosed limitation, not fixed: `setupRateLimiter`'s map never removes a key once created, even after its entries expire — real but low-severity for a single-appliance pre-auth endpoint with no realistic reason to see traffic from thousands of distinct IPs; a correct fix needs a background sweep goroutine, judged not worth the added complexity here.
**Live-verified end-to-end against a real, isolated, genuinely-empty stack, twice** (`docker compose -p eami-wizard-live-test`/`-test2`, fresh throwaway Postgres volumes, reusing the already-built local `eami-api`/`eami-gateway`/`eami-ui` images — not the shared dev stack). First pass, pre-fixes: generated a real console-style token via the exact commands `eami-stack.sh` runs, confirmed `GET /v1/setup/status` → `false`, validated the token (`204`), completed `POST /v1/setup/bootstrap` (`201`, real org+admin created), **logged in for real with the created credentials** (`POST /v1/auth/login` → `200`, real signed JWT returned — AC2's actual requirement), confirmed `/v1/setup/status` flipped to `true`, confirmed re-submitting the same token was cleanly rejected (`401 "setup token has already been used"`). Second pass, after rebuilding the image with all the code-review fixes above: repeated the identical flow end-to-end, this time deliberately using a dot-less-domain email (`admin@localhost`) to prove the email-regex fix live, not just in a unit test — bootstrap succeeded (`201`), login succeeded (`200`, real JWT), status flip and reuse-rejection both reconfirmed. Confirmed `eami-ui`'s dev server actually serves the `/setup` route and its router module resolves to the real `SetupWizardPage` component (not just type-checked in isolation). Real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` both clean, run after every code change including the post-review fixes (Node/npm newly installed this session via `winget` — previously absent on this machine, see `CLAUDE.md`'s toolchain note, now resolved). Both throwaway isolated projects fully torn down afterward (`docker compose down -v`).
**An incidental, pre-existing discovery while checking the real dev stack was untouched, not caused by this task's own code:** the dev database's org count read 29 before this session and 31 partway through — turned out to be 30 synthetic `paste-events-perf-a`/`-b` fixtures accumulated since 2026-07-25 across many prior sessions' `go test ./...` runs (`paste_events_read_test.go`'s perf-seed test registers a `t.Cleanup` that isn't reliably firing), leaving only 1 genuine org (`Dev Org`/`dev@example.com`) once cleaned up. Confirmed by name/timestamp before deleting anything, confirmed the real org was untouched afterward. The leaked rows were deleted; the underlying test-cleanup bug itself was not investigated or fixed (out of this task's scope) — logged as **B-056**.
**Not performed: interactive browser click-through** — browser automation tools were unavailable this session (the user chose not to complete the Claude-in-Chrome install); the frontend's actual served bundle and routing were confirmed live via direct HTTP requests and source inspection instead, and the wizard's own client-side logic (status check → gate → form → bootstrap) is a thin, already-build-verified consumer of the exact backend contract proven live above.
**Post-push CI failure, real and fixed — two distinct bugs, not one, confirmed by reading the actual job logs rather than assumed:** the first push broke `Test — schema/migrationtest` and `Test — eami-api` in CI. (1) `migrate_test.go` hardcoded an expected schema version of 2 in four places; B-055's new migration (000003) makes the real version 3 — a stale test expectation, not a defect in the migration mechanism itself (every other module's migration-apply CI step, including `eami-api`'s own, succeeded). Fixed all four checks, gave the fresh-vs-incremental comparison test a third `Steps(1)` call (was silently missing `setup_tokens`, causing a table-count mismatch), added `setup_tokens` to the fresh-schema spot-check list. (2) A **separate bug**, in `bootstrap_test.go` itself: its connection helper copied `migrate_test.go`'s narrower env-var pattern (checks `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is set, but reads the password from `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` separately) — CI's matrix `test` job (which runs `eami-api`) only ever sets `TEST_DATABASE_URL`, never `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` (`build.yml` already has a comment, predating this session, explaining exactly why `test-migrationtest` needs both and the matrix job doesn't). Fixed by parsing `TEST_DATABASE_URL` as a full DSN, matching every other `eami-api` real-Postgres test file's existing convention. Both fixes verified locally by reproducing CI's exact env-var setup (`TEST_DATABASE_URL` only) before the second push — **confirmed genuinely green via the GitHub API afterward: zero failed jobs, Docker image builds (gated on both test jobs) also succeeded.**
**Known limitations:** the full Packer/QEMU appliance image was not rebuilt from scratch this session (B-053 already proved that boot mechanism separately in its own session; this brief's only change to it is the token-generation step added to `eami-stack.sh`, which is itself exercised for real above via the identical commands) — a from-scratch appliance rebuild is a reasonable follow-up if the founder wants the literal "boot the actual `.qcow2`" step repeated, not done here given it was already proven mechanically sound and unrelated to this brief's actual new logic.

**Interfaces — B-058 (2026-08-12), Multi-Hop Workflows Brief 1 — schema + CRUD foundation only (Thread B epic, briefs 2-7 not built):** new `workflows`/`workflow_steps` tables (`schema/migrations-v2/000006`), new `internal/api/workflows.go` (`GET/POST /v1/gateway/workflows`, `GET/PATCH/DELETE /v1/gateway/workflows/{workflowId}`), new `internal/store/workflows.sql.go`. Role gating identical to `gateway_tools`: `requireRole("admin","operator")` for write, `requireRole("admin","operator","viewer")` for read. `api/openapi.yaml` deliberately not touched — ships undocumented, matching B-038/B-045's precedent.
**Data model:** `workflow_steps.gateway_tool_id UUID REFERENCES gateway_tools(id) ON DELETE SET NULL` (not a blocking pre-check) — `DeleteTool` was out of scope to modify this brief and deletes unconditionally today, so a default-`RESTRICT` FK would have turned that untouched flow into a raw constraint-violation the moment any workflow referenced the deleted tool. A step with a deleted connector comes back from `GetWorkflow`/`ListWorkflowSteps` with `gateway_tool_id`/`tool_name` both null — surfaced, not hidden. `input_mapping JSONB` on `workflow_steps` is a genuinely inert placeholder for a later brief (output→input mapping between hops); never read or written beyond NULL passthrough here. v1 is strictly linear (`step_order INTEGER`, `UNIQUE(workflow_id, step_order)`) — no branching columns, per the Thread B investigation's own finding that branching is unbuildable until connector output normalization exists.
**Cross-org guard — the load-bearing security-relevant piece of this brief:** `ToolBelongsToOrg` (`SELECT EXISTS(... WHERE id=$1 AND org_id=$2)`), called by `validateWorkflowSteps` for every step on both `CreateWorkflow` and `UpdateWorkflow`, before any row is written. The FK constraint alone only proves a `gateway_tool_id` exists *somewhere* in `gateway_tools` — never that it belongs to the caller's own org; without this explicit application-level check, org A could reference org B's connector id directly. `UpdateWorkflow` additionally confirms workflow ownership (`GetWorkflow(id, uc.OrgID)`) before opening a transaction, and the transaction's own `UPDATE ... WHERE id=$1 AND org_id=$2` is independently org-scoped too (no TOCTOU between the pre-check and the mutating statement).
**sqlc note:** `workflows.sql.go` is hand-written, not `sqlc generate`-produced — the sqlc CLI is confirmed not on `PATH` on this machine (same reason `tools.sql.go` predates it as hand-written). A real `.sql` source file (`internal/store/query/workflows.sql`) was added anyway so a machine with sqlc available can regenerate for real later without drift.
**Test coverage:** `workflows_test.go` — 4 real-Postgres integration tests: full CRUD lifecycle (create with 2 steps inside a transaction, list with joined step count, get with joined tool names, update via full-replace reorder/add/remove, delete with cascade verified via a direct query); cross-org isolation (Get/Update/Delete all no-op/404 for another org's workflow, plus a full HTTP-level test via a real `httptest` server + real JWT proving `CreateWorkflow` rejects a step referencing another org's real connector with a clean 400 and zero rows created); dangling-reference behavior (a real `DeleteTool` call against a referenced connector leaves the workflow intact with the affected step's `gateway_tool_id`/`tool_name` both null). Plus `workflows_internal_test.go` (3 unit tests for the FK-violation-detection helper, see code review below). **Verified 2026-08-12 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./... -count=1` all clean across the full `eami-api` module.**
**A real test-authoring bug found and fixed live, not application code but worth recording — it reproduces a bug this exact file (`BACKLOG.md`, B-056) already documents once:** the first version of `workflows_test.go` used `defer pool.Close()` alongside `t.Cleanup`-registered per-org `DELETE FROM orgs` calls. `t.Cleanup` runs in a phase strictly *after* a function's own `defer`s finish, so the pool was already closed by the time any cleanup DELETE ran — every single cleanup silently no-op'd. Reproduced live: a full test run left 14 leaked `wf-*` orgs in the shared dev database (confirmed via `psql`). Fixed by switching every test to `t.Cleanup(func() { pool.Close() })`, registered *before* any org-seeding helper call so LIFO cleanup ordering runs the org deletes first, then closes the pool last — re-ran, confirmed zero leakage, shared dev DB back to just `Dev Org` for the remainder of the session (verified repeatedly, including after the final full-suite run and the live docker-compose verification below).
**Reviewer + security passes both ran.** Security review (this session, direct data-flow trace rather than a delegated pass, since org-isolation is always security-relevant per standing rules): traced org-scoping on every query, the `ToolBelongsToOrg` cross-org guard, `UpdateWorkflow`'s pre-mutation ownership check, confirmed all SQL parameterized (no injection surface) and no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` anywhere in the new frontend code — zero findings, reported via the empty-findings path rather than manufacturing speculative issues. **Code review found 4 real code issues plus 1 real documentation-consistency gap, all fixed:**
1. `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s step-row editor (`rowsToSteps`, now `validateAndConvertRows`) silently filtered out any incomplete row on submit — including a row flagged `toolDeleted` (a deleted connector's step, exactly what AC4's recovery flow depends on an admin fixing). Editing an unrelated field without touching the broken row would silently delete it instead of preserving it for repair. Fixed: submission is now blocked with a clear "Step N is incomplete" error naming the row, never a silent drop.
2. This file's own Thread B entry said "NOT YET SCOPED, no B-ID assigned" in the very diff that shipped a fully-built B-058 under that epic — fixed (see the Thread B entry above, now reflecting Brief 1 DONE).
3. `WorkflowUpdateRequest`'s doc comment incorrectly implied `{"steps": []}` was a supported way to clear a workflow to zero steps — `validateWorkflowSteps` actually rejects empty arrays unconditionally (a workflow needs at least one step to mean anything). Comment corrected to state this plainly; no behavior change, since the "bug" was documentation, not code.
4. A real, if narrow, TOCTOU: `validateWorkflowSteps`' `ToolBelongsToOrg` checks run before the transaction opens, so a connector deleted by a concurrent request in the gap between validation and the transactional `INSERT` surfaced as a raw 500 (unhandled FK violation) instead of a clean error. Fixed with a new `isForeignKeyViolation(err) bool` helper (checks Postgres SQLSTATE `23503`, mirroring `tool_connectivity.go`'s already-established `classifyPgConnectError` convention for checking `pgErr.Code`), applied on both `CreateWorkflow`'s and `UpdateWorkflow`'s `InsertWorkflowStep` calls — now a clean 400 ("one of the referenced connectors no longer exists"). Covered by 3 new pure unit tests (`workflows_internal_test.go`, constructed `*pgconn.PgError` values, no DB needed, same pattern as `TestClassifyPgConnectError_*`).
5. `UpdateWorkflow` discarded the row its own `UPDATE ... RETURNING` produced and then issued a second, redundant `GetWorkflow` after commit to reconstruct the same data. Fixed to reuse the already-fetched row directly, cutting one full DB round-trip from every successful `PATCH`.
One finding left as a disclosed, not-fixed style note: `AddWorkflowPanel`/`EditWorkflowPanel` are near-duplicate ~65-line components — matches this codebase's own pre-existing `AddToolPanel`/`EditToolPanel` precedent in `ToolsPage.tsx`, not a new departure from convention, so not treated as a defect requiring a fix in this brief.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (before AND after the code-review fixes, to prove both the original flow and the fixes themselves):** logged in as `dev@example.com` (password reset to a known value for this session, no other credential available — same disclosed precedent as B-045/B-046/B-054; the user may want to reset it again). Created a real workflow (`b058-live-verify`) with 2 real steps against the real `Test`/`b046-live-verify` `rest_api` connectors — confirmed via List (`step_count: 2`) and Get (both steps with correctly joined `tool_name`). Edited it live: renamed, activated (`draft`→`active`), reordered the 2 existing steps, and added a 3rd step referencing the real `claude` `ai_provider` connector — proving both connector types work as workflow steps, and that reorder+add all land correctly via the full-replace update path. Deleted it: `204`, subsequent `GET` `404`, `workflow_steps` row count confirmed `0` at the DB level (cascade). Post-code-review-fix smoke test confirmed both new validation paths return clean `400`s live (`"at least one step is required"` for an empty `steps` array; the cross-org/not-found guard for a nonexistent `gateway_tool_id`) rather than 500s. **Confirmed via direct grep that `eami-gateway`'s entire module (including `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s dispatch closure) has zero references to `workflow`/`workflows` anywhere** — this brief is genuinely definition-only; nothing executes a workflow yet. `eami-api`/`eami-ui` containers logged clean (no errors/panics) throughout. Shared dev database confirmed to contain only `Dev Org` both before this session's work and after every verification step, including the test-cleanup-bug incident above.
**Known limitations, disclosed not hidden:** no live interactive browser click-through of `/gateway/workflows` was performed (confirmed the route resolves with a real `curl` against the running Vite dev server instead, `200`, and the full CRUD lifecycle was proven via direct API calls that exercise the identical handler code a browser session would trigger) — matches the standing limitation most recent `eami-ui` briefs in this environment have disclosed the same way. No automated frontend test exists for the new `validateAndConvertRows`/`StepsEditor` logic (matches this repo's pre-existing 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline, `BACKLOG.md` B-006, not a new gap introduced by this brief).
**Dependencies:** none (Brief 1 has no dependency on later Thread B briefs — see `BACKLOG.md`'s B-058 entry). **Out of scope, as specified in the task brief:** workflow execution, output→input mapping logic, per-hop TOCTOU pinning, escalation/approval integration, audit logging integration, branching/conditional logic.

**Interfaces — B-070 (2026-08-17), rate limiting on login and the setup wizard:** `POST /v1/auth/login` had zero rate limiting — a real credential-stuffing/brute-force surface, since this is the only real self-service auth path in the system. **A scope correction found during research, before building, not assumed from the task brief's own framing:** the brief's premise ("no rate limiting exists anywhere in this stack") wasn't accurate — B-055's `bootstrap.go` already rate-limited its setup routes per-IP (in-handler, not middleware), just without a `Retry-After` header. New `internal/api/ratelimit.go`: `setupRateLimiter` (B-055's original type) extracted and generalized into a shared `rateLimiter`, with `Allow` now returning `(ok bool, retryAfter time.Duration)` instead of a bare `bool`. New `internal/api/ratelimit_login.go`: `rateLimitLogin` chi middleware wraps `POST /v1/auth/login` (`router.go`'s only change to this route besides the wrapper), checking **both** a per-IP limiter (keyed on `middleware.GetClientIP`, the same non-spoofable source B-047's `ClientIPFromRemoteAddr` migration already established) and a per-account limiter (keyed on a case/whitespace-normalized email) — per-IP alone doesn't stop credential stuffing spread across many accounts from one IP; per-account alone doesn't stop one IP hammering many different accounts. The account key is extracted by peeking the request body and restoring it byte-for-byte, so **`Login()` itself has zero lines changed** — confirmed via diff. New `config.RateLimitConfig` (all six values — `LOGIN_RATE_LIMIT_PER_IP`/`_WINDOW_SECONDS`, `LOGIN_RATE_LIMIT_PER_ACCOUNT`/`_WINDOW_SECONDS`, `SETUP_RATE_LIMIT`/`_WINDOW_SECONDS` — individually env-overridable, matching this file's established pattern), defaults 20/5min (IP) and 5/5min (account) — account tighter than IP since one IP legitimately represents many real users behind NAT/a corporate proxy, while one account should never see 20 password attempts in 5 minutes from anyone legitimate; setup mirrors B-055's original hardcoded 10/15min, now configurable instead of fixed in code. `bootstrap.go`'s `ValidateSetupToken`/`Bootstrap` gained exactly one line each (`setRetryAfter(w, retryAfter)`) — their actual validation/DB-write logic is unchanged.
**Three real bugs found by this task's own mandatory security review, all fixed before shipping:**
1. **Fail-open on a body-read error.** `io.ReadAll(r.Body)` can return a non-nil `err` alongside a body it had *already fully read* (e.g. a malformed chunked-transfer trailer arriving after every real byte). The original code treated any read error as "skip rate limiting entirely, forward the request" — letting a syntactically valid, fully-readable credential-guess bypass both limiters. Fixed by ignoring the read error and always counting whatever bytes were captured against the IP limiter before forwarding.
2. **Parser disagreement let an attacker downgrade account-level protection to the looser IP-level one.** The middleware parsed the peeked body with `json.Unmarshal` (errors on trailing data) while `Login()`'s own `decodeJSON` uses a streaming `json.NewDecoder(...).Decode` (silently ignores anything after the first JSON value). A body with one byte appended (`{...}X`) would fail the middleware's parser — skipping the tighter per-account limiter — while still parsing normally in `Login()`, letting an attacker brute-force one account at the IP limit (4x the intended rate) just by appending garbage to every request. Fixed by switching the middleware to the identical `json.NewDecoder(...).Decode` call `Login()` uses, so both paths agree on what counts as "the same request."
3. **Non-positive rate-limit panic**, shared with the `eami-gateway` B-070 fix above: `rateLimiter.Allow` indexed `kept[0]` once `len(kept) >= rl.limit`, immediately true for `limit <= 0` on the very first request — a misconfigured (e.g. typo'd `"0"`) env var would crash every login attempt rather than disable limiting. Fixed with a fail-closed guard in `Allow` itself, plus `config.go`'s `validate()` now rejecting any of the six new values that are `<= 0` at startup (same "fail loudly at boot" convention as every other required secret/value in that function).
**Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory (explicit security-hardening brief):** code review's automated pass found all three issues above; fixed, then the full test suite (including 3 new regression tests specifically reproducing each bug against the pre-fix code path) re-run clean.
**Test coverage:** `internal/api/ratelimit_login_test.go` (4 black-box tests via `MockStore`/`httptest`: per-account trip after 5, per-IP trip after 20 across distinct unseeded accounts, 2 genuine typos then a real successful login unaffected, case-insensitive account-key folding); `internal/api/ratelimit_login_internal_test.go` (white-box, `package api`: the body-read-error regression via a `flakyBody` that mimics `io.ReadAll`'s real partial-data-plus-error shape; the non-positive-limit fail-closed regression via direct `newRateLimiter(0/-1/-100, ...)` construction; the trailing-garbage account-key regression via a body with one appended byte); `internal/api/ratelimit_bootstrap_test.go` (real-Postgres, reusing `bootstrap_test.go`'s exact `newBootstrapTestEnv` fixture: token-validate trips after 10 with a real `Retry-After` header, and confirms the shared per-IP limiter also blocks a *genuinely valid* Bootstrap call while tripped — not just repeats of the same invalid request; a full below-threshold status→validate→bootstrap flow unaffected); `internal/config/config_test.go` gained `TestValidate_RejectsNonPositiveRateLimitValues`/`TestValidate_AcceptsPositiveRateLimitValues`/`TestLoad_RejectsZeroRateLimitEnvVar`, plus `validConfig()`'s fixture updated to include `RateLimit: DefaultRateLimitConfig()` (a real regression this task caused and fixed in the same session: the bare zero-value `RateLimit` the old fixture left in place would have made every one of that file's pre-existing "accepts a valid config" tests fail once the new bounds check landed). **Verified 2026-08-17 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-api` module**, zero regressions in any pre-existing test.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (before AND after the security-review fixes):** rapid-fired 6 wrong-password login attempts against one real (throwaway, seeded-then-deleted-afterward) account — the first 5 returned real `401`s, the 6th a real `429` with `Retry-After: 294` — then confirmed even the *correct* password was rejected while rate-limited (the limiter gates before `Login()` ever checks credentials). Separately proved AC5 live: 2 genuine typos against that same account returned `401`, then a 3rd attempt with the correct password returned a real `200` with a real access token — a legitimate user is never falsely blocked. Rapid-fired 22 login attempts across 22 distinct unseeded accounts from one source IP — cumulative with the prior test's requests (same real IP, same real process), the per-IP limiter tripped at exactly the 21st request overall, matching the configured 20/5min threshold precisely. Rapid-fired 11 setup-wizard token-validate attempts against the (already-configured) real appliance — 10 real `409`s (already-configured, expected), the 11th a real `429` with `Retry-After: 897` (~15min window). All test fixtures (one throwaway org/admin user) created directly in Postgres and deleted afterward; final rebuild/redeploy re-confirmed both services healthy post-fix.
**Known limitations, disclosed not hidden:** no rate limiting on any other `eami-api` route (`/v1/auth/refresh`, API-key creation, etc.) — a natural, separate follow-up, matching this brief's own explicitly stated scope boundary (login + setup-wizard + workflow-run only, the three highest-risk endpoints identified). Distributed/multi-instance rate limiting is out of scope (ADR-020 Model A is single-instance).

**Interfaces — B-074 (2026-08-19), clean 409 on duplicate `gateway_agents.(org_id, name)`.** Task brief arrived assuming `gateway_agents.name` had no real per-org uniqueness enforcement and asked for a new `schema/migrations-v2` migration to add one. **Re-verified directly against the running database before building anything, per the brief's own stated assumption to check rather than trust the investigation summary — the premise was wrong.** A live `\d gateway_agents` against the actual running Postgres container showed `"gateway_agents_org_id_name_key" UNIQUE CONSTRAINT, btree (org_id, name)` already present and already enforced; a live query for duplicate `(org_id, name)` pairs returned zero rows. The constraint is declared identically in all three schema sources (`schema/schema.sql`, `schema/migrations/001_init.sql`, and `schema/migrations-v2/000001_baseline.up.sql` — the file B-051's real `golang-migrate` runner actually applies). The brief's cited culprit, `resolveDynamicTool` (`eami-gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go`), turned out to be unrelated entirely — it resolves `gateway_tools` rows (B-044's dynamic tool routing), not agent identity. The actual agent-lookup code, `eami-gateway/internal/registry/registry.go`'s `LookupByName`/`LookupByNameAndOrg`, already carries a B-042 doc comment stating the exact constraint exists and explaining why `LookupByName`'s unscoped-by-org query is deliberately safe (every caller's `name` comes from a JWT already scoped to one org) — a already-reasoned design, not a live ambiguity bug. Confirmed with the user before building; scope corrected to no migration, no `registry.go` changes.
**The one real gap that did exist:** `eami-api/internal/api/agents.go`'s `CreateAgent` didn't classify a Postgres unique-violation (SQLSTATE 23505) on insert — any DB error fell through to a flat `500 internal_error` carrying the raw driver error string, which does violate a duplicate-name attempt's expected "clean error, not a 500." New `isUniqueViolation(err error) bool` (checks `pgErr.Code == "23505"`, mirrors `workflows.go`'s existing `isForeignKeyViolation` convention exactly) now short-circuits `CreateAgent`'s error path to a `409 conflict` with a message naming the org-scoped duplicate, before falling through to the unchanged generic 500 path for every other error type.
