**Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory per this brief's own standing rules (identity-resolution-adjacent, same bar as B-042/B-058).** Security review: zero findings — traced the new `%q`-formatted error message (not an information disclosure, since it only echoes the exact name string the authenticated caller already submitted in their own request body, scoped by `uc.OrgID` from verified JWT claims, never client input) and confirmed no auth/authz/injection surface was touched. Code review found 3 minor issues, all fixed: (1) `api/openapi.yaml`'s `POST /v1/gateway/agents` path doesn't document the new `409` response — correctly **not** fixed in this brief, since `api/openapi.yaml` is Architect-EAMI-owned per `BOUNDARIES.md` ("Only Architect writes it"); logged in `NOTES.md` instead, matching this repo's own B-045 undocumented-response precedent; (2)/(3) two `gofmt` violations introduced by this diff (a missing blank line in `agents.go`, a misaligned map-literal in the new test file) — both fixed with `gofmt -w`, confirmed clean.
**Test coverage:** `agents_internal_test.go` (3 pure unit tests for `isUniqueViolation`, mirroring `workflows_internal_test.go`'s `TestIsForeignKeyViolation_*` pattern exactly — real code, other code, non-PgError). `agents_pg_test.go` — `TestCreateAgent_RealDB_DuplicateNameInSameOrg_Returns409NotDuplicate`, a real-Postgres HTTP-level test (`toolsUpdateTestDSN`/`seedTestOrg`/`seedTestUser`/`api.NewServer` convention from `workflows_test.go`): first create succeeds (201), second identical create gets a real 409 with `code="conflict"`, and — the AC1 "first row untouched" requirement, verified directly, not assumed — a post-conflict `SELECT count(*)` confirms exactly one row for `(org_id, name)` and a re-fetch of that row's `model` column confirms it's byte-identical to what was originally inserted. **Verified 2026-08-19 with a real toolchain: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean across `eami-api`; `POSTGRES_PASSWORD=... go test ./...` clean across the full module (all pre-existing suites included, zero regressions), plus a separate confirmation that `eami-gateway` (untouched by this brief) still builds/vets cleanly.**
**Live-verified, not just via `go test`:** the exact same real-Postgres duplicate-create/409/row-untouched sequence above ran directly against the shared dev Postgres container (`eaim-postgres-1`) via the real HTTP handler, then the throwaway test org (and its cascaded `gateway_agents` row) was confirmed deleted afterward — `SELECT count(*) FROM orgs WHERE name LIKE 'agents-dup-%'` and the equivalent `gateway_agents` count both `0` post-run; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Dependencies:** none. **Out of scope, as specified (and correctly so, given the corrected premise):** any schema/migration change (constraint already existed), any change to `registry.go`/`LookupByName`/`LookupByNameAndOrg` (already correct), endpoint-agent identity (B-073, separate, not started this session).

**Interfaces — B-075 (2026-08-20), OpenAPI-spec auto-discovery for connector actions (MCP Discovery Part C).** An admin can now paste/upload an OpenAPI 3.x spec (or point at a URL) for a `rest_api` connector and get real, reviewable candidate `action_paths` entries generated automatically, instead of hand-typing every path/method (B-046). New `POST /v1/gateway/openapi/discover` (stateless, writes nothing) parses the spec and returns a preview; nothing activates until the admin reviews/edits the result in the UI and saves through the pre-existing `action_paths` PATCH (`UpdateTool`, completely unmodified) — no new activation endpoint or parallel storage mechanism.

**CRITICAL FIRST-STEP FINDING (AC6), investigated before any design work, not assumed either way — B-061's `tools/list` is NOT compatible with a generic external MCP client (e.g. Claude Desktop) today.** Traced `eami-gateway/internal/mcp/handler.go`'s `ServeMessages` directly: it handles exactly two JSON-RPC methods, `tools/list` and the non-standard `tool_call` — there is **zero handling anywhere in this codebase for the spec-mandated `initialize` handshake** every conformant MCP client sends first (confirmed via a repo-wide grep for `initialize`/`protocolVersion`/`notifications/initialized`: zero matches outside this note). A real client gets `-32601 method not found` on its very first message and never proceeds to `tools/list` at all. A **second, independent** compatibility breaker: the real spec's tool-invocation method is `tools/call`, not this codebase's `tool_call` — even a client that somehow bypassed the missing handshake would still fail to invoke any tool, since the method name itself doesn't match. B-061's `tools/list` has only ever been exercised against this codebase's own internal test harness, which calls these exact non-standard method names directly — never a real external client. Per this brief's own OUT OF SCOPE clause, external-client connectivity is **not** built here — it needs its own separate, clearly-scoped follow-up brief (a real `initialize`/`tools/call` implementation, likely also needing a real OAuth or API-key auth model a remote client can actually obtain, since today's Bearer JWT is minted via EAMI's own internal `/v1/gateway/tokens` endpoint with no path an external client like Claude Desktop could reach). **Logged as B-076, QUEUED, in `BACKLOG.md`.**

**A second, related finding directly bearing on AC4's realism, traced through `eami-gateway/internal/toolrouter/router.go`'s `Forward` before designing the dispatch-facing half of this brief:** dispatch sends every `tool_call` as a fixed `{tool, action, params, session_id}` JSON envelope to `base_url + <literal action_paths.Path>` (`joinURLPath` is pure string concatenation) — there is no path-parameter substitution (`{petId}` stays literal in the URL) and no query-parameter mapping; the downstream never receives a body matching its own real schema. This is pre-existing B-044/046 behavior, explicitly out of this brief's scope to change (`MUST NOT MODIFY: B-044/046's dispatch logic`). **Practical consequence, disclosed rather than hidden:** a spec-generated action pointing at a genuine third-party REST API (a public Petstore-style spec, for instance) will not dispatch correctly regardless of how well the spec is parsed, unless the target happens to accept/echo an arbitrary JSON body — matching every prior live-verification in this codebase's history, which has always used an echo endpoint (`postman-echo.com`), never a real strict-schema API. **Handled, not ignored:** the discovery response flags every action whose path contains a `{...}` placeholder with an explicit warning ("the gateway's current dispatch mechanism does not substitute path parameters... this action will not reach the intended endpoint"), surfaced both in the API response and in the actual `ActionPathsEditor`/`OpenAPIDiscoverySection` UI (live-verified, see below) — an admin activating such an action is doing so with full knowledge it won't work as-is, not being misled. **Live-proven, not just reasoned about:** a real spec-generated action with a path parameter (`GET /get/{itemId}`) was deliberately activated and dispatched against the real `postman-echo.com` target — it genuinely failed with a real `404` (the request literally hit `.../get/{itemId}`, not `.../get/42`), confirming the warning is accurate, not theoretical.

**Backend (`eami-api`):** new `github.com/getkin/kin-openapi` dependency (MIT, mature — `go list -m -versions` confirmed 147+ published versions before adding). New package `internal/openapidiscovery`: `Parse(specBytes []byte) (*Result, error)` walks every path×method (restricted to the same 5-method allowlist `validateActionPaths` already enforces), building one `Action{Path, Method, Summary, InputSchema, HasPathParams}` per operation — action name from `operationId` if present (sanitized to `[a-zA-Z0-9_]`), else a `method_path` slug, with numeric-suffix disambiguation on collision. `buildInputSchema` synthesizes a JSON-Schema-shaped object per action from the operation's parameters (path/query/header, each schema round-tripped through kin-openapi's own JSON marshaling rather than hand-rolled conversion) plus its `application/json` request body (object bodies flattened into top-level properties; non-object bodies represented honestly as a single `"body"` property). `FetchSpec(ctx, url, dial DialContextFunc)` retrieves a `spec_url`, requiring an SSRF-guarded dialer be passed explicitly (panics on nil, deliberately, so a caller forgetting to guard it fails loudly in tests rather than silently dialing the real network). Bounds: 5 MiB max spec size (both paths), 300 max actions (truncates with an explicit warning, never a silent drop), 8s fetch timeout.
New `POST /v1/gateway/openapi/discover` (`openapi_discover.go`, same `requireRole("admin","operator")` group as `CreateTool`/`UpdateTool`/`TestTool`) — exactly one of `spec_url`/`spec_content` required; a URL is fetched through the **same, reused** `safeDialContext` SSRF guard `TestTool` (`tool_connectivity.go`) already uses, not a duplicate. `ActionPathMapping` (`tools.go`) and `toolrouter.ActionPathEntry` (`eami-gateway`) both gained an optional `InputSchema map[string]any` field — purely additive and decode-only for dispatch (`toolrouter.Forward` never reads it, confirmed by direct inspection before adding the field); `listGatewayTools` (`cmd/gateway/main.go`) uses it for `tools/list`'s `InputSchema` when present, else keeps B-061's original honest `{"type":"object"}` fallback unchanged.

**Frontend (`eami-ui`):** new `OpenAPIDiscoverySection` component (URL/paste toggle, a review table with a checkbox per discovered action, a per-action path-parameter warning, an "Add selected to action mappings" button) wired into **both** `AddToolPanel` and `EditToolPanel` — "Add selected" only ever merges into the same local `actionRows` state the pre-existing `ActionPathsEditor` already manages; nothing reaches the backend until the panel's own pre-existing Save/Create button is clicked. New `useDiscoverOpenAPI()` hook (`apiFetch`, undocumented-route escape hatch, matching B-045's precedent).

**Reviewer + security passes, both mandatory (real untrusted-input surface: admin-supplied specs, optionally server-fetched from an admin-supplied URL).** Security review found and this brief fixed one real MEDIUM issue: `spec_content`'s documented 5 MiB bound was enforced only *after* the full JSON request body had already been decoded into memory — the URL-fetch path correctly used `io.LimitReader`, but the direct-paste path had no bound until `Parse()`'s own later, in-memory check. Fixed with `http.MaxBytesReader(w, r.Body, openapidiscovery.MaxSpecBytes*2)` ahead of `json.Decode`, live-verified by POSTing a real 10MB+ body and confirming a real `400` before the process could buffer it in full. Security review independently ruled out, with concrete tracing (not assumption): SSRF bypass via redirects (every hop re-dials through the guard) or DNS rebinding (the guard resolves and validates the IP, then dials that IP directly — no check-then-connect gap); a second SSRF vector via a malicious spec's own `$ref` (kin-openapi's `IsExternalRefsAllowed` left at its default `false` rejects local-file/relative-path/remote-URL refs alike at parse time, verified empirically — see below); YAML "billion laughs"/deep-nesting DoS (traced through `oasdiff/yaml3`'s alias-ratio and 10000-depth guards, and stdlib `encoding/json`'s identical depth cap); unbounded recursion on a self-referential internal `$ref` (kin-openapi's `SchemaRef` marshaling prefers `Ref` over `Value` on a repeat, cutting recursion short by design — independently reproduced live by this session with a 10^9-scale YAML anchor structure and a self-referential schema, both completing in under a second); injection (action names are regex-sanitized, non-backtracking, ReDoS-immune); auth/authz (correctly role-gated, no org-scoping surface since the endpoint is stateless); XSS (plain JSX text interpolation throughout, no `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`). Code review found and this brief fixed 3 real issues: (1) `ActionPathsEditor`'s path-parameter warning was driven by a static `hasPathParams` flag set once at add-time, going stale the instant an admin edited a row's path (either fixing a discovered `{param}` by hand, or typing a brand-new row containing one) — fixed by deriving the warning live from `row.path.includes('{')` on every render instead, removing the stale field entirely; (2) `buildInputSchema` silently overwrote a path/query/header parameter's schema whenever a request-body property shared its name — fixed to keep the parameter's schema (never overwritten) and emit an explicit per-action warning naming the exact collision, live-verified with a real `format` query-param/body-property collision; (3) discovery was only wired into `EditToolPanel`, forcing a save-then-reopen round trip to use it during tool creation — fixed by wiring the same stateless component into `AddToolPanel` too.

**Test coverage:** `eami-api/internal/openapidiscovery` — 18 tests (valid-spec correctness including query/path/body-param extraction and flattening, no-operationId fallback naming, duplicate-slug disambiguation, the parameter/body-property collision fix, oversized/empty/malformed-spec rejection, and three real, empirically-verified `$ref` rejection tests — local file, relative path, remote HTTP — plus `FetchSpec`'s dialer-actually-invoked, dial-rejection-propagates, nil-dial-panics, non-http-scheme, oversized-response, and non-200 cases). `eami-api/internal/api/openapi_discover_test.go` — 7 handler-level tests (role gating, exactly-one-of validation, the real request/response wire shape via a real parsed spec, and the HTTP-layer size-bound regression test proving the security fix). `eami-gateway/cmd/gateway/main_tools_list_pg_test.go` gained a real-Postgres test proving `input_schema` round-trips through Postgres JSONB and `listGatewayTools` reflects it verbatim, alongside a sibling action with no `input_schema` proving the pre-B-075 fallback still applies unchanged in the same tool. **Verified 2026-08-20: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-api` module and `eami-gateway`'s `toolrouter`/`cmd/gateway` packages (real Postgres); real `npm run build`/`tsc --noEmit` clean for `eami-ui`.**

**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh before AND after the code-review fixes (AC1-5, plus the 4 specific verifications this brief's own standing rules required beyond the standard bar):**
- **SSRF guard genuinely active on the live endpoint, not just unit-tested:** real `POST /v1/gateway/openapi/discover` calls with `spec_url` pointing at `127.0.0.1:5432` and `169.254.169.254` both returned a real `400` with the guard's own rejection message; a real legitimate public spec (the official Swagger Petstore 3.0 spec) was fetched and parsed successfully in the same session, proving the guard isn't over-blocking.
- **Local-file `$ref` rejection, both as a real Go test AND live through the real running endpoint:** a spec containing `$ref: 'file:///etc/passwd#/...'` was rejected with a real `400` naming the disallowed reference, via a direct HTTP call against the deployed service (not just `go test`).
- **AC1** — a real custom spec (matching `postman-echo.com`'s real `/get`, `/post`, and a deliberately path-param'd `/get/{itemId}`) produced correct, reviewable actions including the exact expected `input_schema` shapes.
- **AC2** — the admin reviewed and selected only 2 of 3 discovered actions (excluding the path-param one) via the exact request sequence the UI generates; only those 2 landed in `action_paths` via the existing PATCH — nothing auto-activated.
- **AC3** — a real agent JWT opened a real MCP SSE session and sent a real `tools/list`: the two spec-generated actions showed their real rich schemas (query param `foo`, required body field `message` with its real description) in the same response where the **pre-existing, untouched** `b046-live-verify` connector's actions still showed the exact original generic `{"type":"object"}` fallback — AC3 and AC5 (zero regression) proven simultaneously in one live call, and independently reconfirmed via a direct `psql` read showing `b046-live-verify`'s stored `action_paths` byte-identical to before this brief (no `input_schema` key present, exactly as it was created under B-046).
- **AC4** — real `tool_call`s for both activated actions genuinely reached `https://postman-echo.com/get` and `/post`, with the real decrypted `Authorization: Bearer` credential attached and the real dispatched params echoed back; **the path-param action's predicted failure was also proven live** (see the dispatch-limitation finding above) — both the success and the disclosed-limitation cases demonstrated with real network calls, not just one cherry-picked happy path.
- **Path-parameter warning visible in the actual UI, not just the API response:** browser automation was unavailable this session (declined); verified instead by fetching the real Vite-served module directly (`curl http://localhost:5173/src/pages/gateway/ToolsPage.tsx`) and confirming both warning render points (`ActionPathsEditor`'s live `row.path.includes("{")` check and `OpenAPIDiscoverySection`'s `a.has_path_params` check) are present in the code actually served, then tracing the real logic against the real API response data captured earlier (`has_path_params: true` for the path-param action) to confirm the conditional deterministically renders — **genuine pixel-level browser confirmation was not performed**, the same standing limitation disclosed by every other `eami-ui` brief in this codebase's history; the user can confirm visually via `http://localhost:5173` directly.
- All test fixtures (tools, agent, episodes) created during verification confirmed deleted afterward; dev DB confirmed back to its exact pre-session baseline (`Test`, `b046-live-verify`, `claude`, `manual_verify_tool` — the same 4 tools that existed before this session, nothing added or removed).

**An incidental, pre-existing gap found during test-fixture cleanup, NOT fixed here (out of this brief's scope — `agents.go`'s `DeleteAgent`, untouched by B-075):** `DELETE /v1/gateway/agents/{id}` returns a raw, unclassified `500` for any agent with real `episodes` or `approval_requests`/`workflow_runs` history, since those tables' `agent_id` foreign keys are `ON DELETE NO ACTION` (unlike `revoked_ai_tokens`/`agent_configs`, which cascade) and `DeleteAgent`'s handler doesn't classify the resulting FK-violation error the way B-074 just taught `CreateAgent` to (`isForeignKeyViolation`-style handling). Reproduced live this session (a test agent with 3 real episode rows failed to delete via the API); worked around by deleting the dependent rows directly for this session's own cleanup, not by fixing the handler. **Logged as B-077, QUEUED, in `BACKLOG.md`.**

**Dependencies:** B-044/046 (`gateway_tools`/`action_paths`, extended not modified), B-061 (`tools/list`, response shape unchanged). **Out of scope, as specified, and correctly so given the AC6 finding:** external MCP-client connectivity (a real `initialize`/`tools/call` implementation — B-076, new, QUEUED); `mcp`/`database`/`ai_provider` connector types (matches B-061's own existing scope); any change to B-044/046's dispatch logic (the path-parameter/wrapper-body limitations are real and disclosed, not silently worked around).

**Interfaces — B-078 (2026-08-20), data-handling visibility for AI provider connectors.** An admin can now record what a third-party AI provider's actual data-retention agreement is per `ai_provider` connector ("Zero Data Retention enabled" / "Standard retention (no ZDR)" / "Unknown/not configured") — a real, admin-visible, auditable designation. **Explicitly a visibility feature, not a technical control**: EAMI cannot enforce what Claude/Gemini/OpenAI actually does with dispatched data once it leaves the gateway — that's governed by the customer's own commercial agreement, not by anything this code does. Mirrors B-047's `audit_mode` pattern exactly, per an investigation this session ran first and re-verified directly against current code before building (confirmed `schema/migrations-v2/000004`'s real structure, `aiprovider.ToolRow`/`main.go`'s shared `auditEntry` construction, and the audit hash-chain's exact formula all matched the investigation's own findings).
**Schema** (`schema/migrations-v2/000008_ai_provider_data_handling.up.sql`, next free migration number confirmed via directory listing): `gateway_tools` gains `data_handling_designation TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'unknown' CHECK (IN ('zero_retention','standard_retention','unknown'))` and nullable `data_handling_note TEXT` — both structured (for filtering/alerting) and free-text (for the actual agreement citation), matching the investigation's own "both, not either" reasoning. `audit_log` gains a third, deliberately unconstrained/nullable `data_handling_designation TEXT` — a per-call **snapshot**, not a live-validated field, empty/NULL for every non-`ai_provider` dispatch and every pre-migration row (backfilling a fake "unknown" onto history nobody configured would misrepresent it).
**`eami-api`:** `store.CreateToolParams`/`UpdateToolParams`/`GatewayTool` gain `DataHandlingDesignation`/`DataHandlingNote` fields (mirrors `Provider`/`AuditMode` exactly); `tools.go`'s `CreateTool`/`UpdateTool` validate against a new `validDataHandling` allowlist, defaulting to `"unknown"` (the fail-safe default — never any specific retention posture until an admin explicitly confirms one), and extend the exact same ai_provider-only type-consistency guard `provider`/`audit_mode` already use (rejecting either field outright on a non-`ai_provider` type, the precise class of gap B-047's own code review caught and fixed after the fact — closed here on the first pass instead). `DataHandlingNote`'s clear-vs-unchanged semantics: `nil` leaves it unchanged (COALESCE), a non-nil pointer to `""` is a real, meaningful "the admin explicitly cleared it" value — not the `action_paths`-style map trick, just the same `*string` COALESCE convention `Name`/`BaseURL` already use, confirmed to work correctly by both a fake-store test and a real-Postgres round-trip.
**`eami-gateway`:** `aiprovider.ToolRow` gains `DataHandling string`, resolved fresh in `Router.Resolve`'s existing query (one added column); `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s single shared `auditEntry` construction snapshots it once (`auditEntry.DataHandling = resolvedProvider.DataHandling`), the exact same call site and "applied once, covers every branch below uniformly" pattern `AuditMode`'s redaction already uses — this is what makes AC3 real: a later designation change only affects a *future* dispatch's fresh `resolvedProvider` read, never an already-built `auditEntry` for a call already in flight or already written. `audit.Entry`/`InsertEntry` gain the field as one more plain bound SQL parameter, added to `writer.go`'s hash-formula doc comment as an explicit "deliberately excluded" note.
**`eami-ui`:** new "Data handling" dropdown + free-text note field in both `AddToolPanel`'s and `EditToolPanel`'s existing `ai_provider`-conditional section (mirrors the Provider/Audit-mode fields exactly); a non-blocking amber warning badge on any `ai_provider` row in the Tools table itself where the designation is `unknown` — visible without opening the edit panel, never a creation block, matching this session's fail-safe-visibility-not-a-gate principle.
**Reviewer + security passes, both mandatory per this brief's own standing rules (touches the audit hash chain) — both came back with ZERO findings, not just "nothing serious."** Security review's explicit, required task was to independently re-derive the hash-chain-exclusion claim from the actual code, not trust the investigation's own reasoning — it read `writer.go`'s hash content string directly, confirmed `DataHandling` genuinely never appears in it, confirmed `InsertEntry` is a single parameterized `INSERT` with no `UPDATE`/`DELETE` anywhere in the diff, confirmed `GetLastHash` (chain-resume-on-restart) only ever selects the `hash` column, and separately verified the new type-consistency guard's org-scoping has no gap analogous to B-047's original one. Code review independently re-traced every SQL column list against its `Scan`/args list (an 18-column `INSERT`, a 13-column `INSERT`, 12 `COALESCE` assignments) for off-by-one errors, verified both new real-Postgres test files follow CLAUDE.md's mandatory `t.Cleanup`-only pool-lifecycle pattern correctly, and found zero correctness/simplification/convention issues — explicitly noted as "unusually well cross-checked against exactly the failure modes a reviewer would look for."
**Test coverage:** `eami-api` — 16 new handler-level tests in `tools_ai_provider_test.go` (default/explicit/invalid designation on create and update, the ai_provider-only type guard on both paths, the empty-string-clears-the-note behavior, response-shape round-trips) plus 2 new real-Postgres tests in `tools_data_handling_pg_test.go` (full create→GET→PATCH→re-GET round trip through the real HTTP handler and a real DB read-back, and the real `DEFAULT 'unknown'` proven at the column level, not just Go-side). `eami-gateway` — `Resolve`'s real round-trip for both the DB default and an explicit designation (`router_pg_test.go`), and three new tests in `internal/audit/writer_pg_test.go`: the **centerpiece** AC2/AC3 proof (two real `Write()` calls through the same long-lived `*Writer` instance, mirroring `main.go`'s real usage, the second carrying a different `DataHandling` value — confirms the *first* row's stored value survives untouched), a NULL-vs-empty-string proof for non-`ai_provider` dispatches, and a **real hash-chain-recomputation test**: 5 real rows written (mixing `DataHandling` set/unset), then independently re-derived SHA-256 end-to-end from what's actually stored in Postgres and compared against the real stored `hash`/`prev_hash` values — empirical proof AC5 holds, not just "the diff doesn't touch that code." **Verified 2026-08-20: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-api` and `eami-gateway` modules** (real Postgres); real `npm run build`/`tsc --noEmit` clean for `eami-ui`.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh, migration `000008` applied via the real `golang-migrate` runner (not just manually for local test iteration):**
- **AC1** — created a real `ai_provider` connector with no designation (`data_handling_designation: "unknown"` confirmed both in the create response and via direct `psql`); PATCHed a real designation + note change, confirmed both in the response and `psql`.
- **AC2/AC3, the centerpiece, proven with real dispatches through the real gateway process (not just the Go test harness):** dispatched a real `tool_call` through the connector while its designation was `unknown` — the resulting `audit_log` row (real, written by the real `cmd/gateway/main.go` dispatch path against a genuinely-rejected fake Anthropic API key, `decision='denied'`) recorded `data_handling_designation='unknown'`. Then PATCHed the connector to `zero_retention` and dispatched a *second* real `tool_call` — its `audit_log` row correctly recorded `zero_retention`, while a direct `psql` re-read of the **first** row confirmed it was still `unknown`, completely unaltered by the later change. This is the literal, real-world proof of the brief's central claim.
- **AC5, hash-chain integrity, proven independently in a THIRD language (not Go, not the reviewer's own trace) beyond what the automated tests already proved:** pulled the two real audit_log rows' `id`/`org_id`/`agent_name`/`tool_name`/`action`/`decision`/`timestamp`/`prev_hash`/`hash` directly via `psql`, confirmed the second row's `prev_hash` exactly equals the first row's `hash` (real chain linkage across real dispatches), then independently recomputed SHA-256 of the documented formula in a standalone Node.js script and confirmed it matched the real stored `hash` byte-for-byte. Also confirmed `audit_mode`'s existing redaction (`parameters = null` for `structural_metadata_only`, this connector's setting) held unchanged on both real rows — zero regression.
- **AC4** — confirmed via the real served Vite module (`curl http://localhost:5173/...`) that the exact warning-badge conditional is deployed, then evaluated that real conditional against real `GET /v1/gateway/tools` API data for two real connectors (one `zero_retention`, one left at the default `unknown`) and confirmed the badge condition evaluates correctly for both — **genuine pixel-level browser confirmation was not performed**, browser automation unavailable this session (declined), the same standing, consistently-disclosed limitation as every other `eami-ui` brief in this codebase's history.
- All test fixtures (2 tools, 1 agent, 2 audit_log rows, 2 episode rows) confirmed deleted afterward; dev DB confirmed back to its exact pre-session baseline (same 4 tools, same 3 agents, only `Dev Org`).
**Dependencies:** B-047 (`provider`/`audit_mode`, the pattern this mirrors), B-074 (confirmed `gateway_agents`/`CreateAgent`'s error-classification precedent this brief's own `UpdateTool` guard extends). **Out of scope, as specified:** any technical enforcement of data handling (not possible); the dashboard widget (optional follow-up, not built); redaction/tokenization (genuinely independent per the investigation's own confirmed finding, unaffected by this brief).

**Fix — B-086 (2026-08-22), `ReorderPolicies` (`eami-api/internal/store/policies.sql.go`) no longer 500s on ordinary reorder attempts.** Task brief: wire the Policies page's decorative drag-handle icon (Dead Clicks Audit) to the real reorder endpoint. **Two real, previously-unknown bugs found before the UI wiring could even be proven, both required fixing (with explicit user sign-off before touching anything outside the brief's stated scope) for the brief's own AC1/AC2 to be satisfiable at all:**
1. **Frontend/spec mismatch (fixed in `eami-ui`, see that section below):** `useReorderPolicies()` sent `{order: [...]}`; the real handler (`internal/api/types.go:167`) expects `policy_ids`. Root cause: `api/openapi.yaml:1258-1265` itself documents `order`, not `policy_ids` — the generated-client-based hook had faithfully followed a spec that doesn't match the real implementation. Spec correction logged separately as **B-089** (`openapi.yaml` is Architect-EAMI-owned).
2. **Backend transaction bug, this section:** `policies`' `UNIQUE (org_id, priority)` constraint is `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` (`schema.sql:191`) — deliberately declared so a full reorder is checked only at commit — but `ReorderPolicies` looped plain `q.db.Exec` calls with no transaction, so each row's constraint was checked immediately on its own auto-commit. Reproduced live before any fix existed: a real adjacent-pair swap (the exact shape an up/down click produces) returned a real `500 duplicate key value violates unique constraint "policies_org_id_priority_key" (SQLSTATE 23505)`. **Fixed** by wrapping the loop in a real transaction via `Queries.Begin()`/`WithTx()` — the same pattern already established by `bootstrap.go`'s setup wizard, the only other user of this codebase's transaction helper. No schema change, no change to the endpoint's request/response contract, no change to the policy evaluation engine.
**Test coverage:** new `eami-api/internal/api/policies_reorder_pg_test.go` (real Postgres, `t.Cleanup`-only pool lifecycle — `pool.Close()` registered before `seedTestOrg`'s own cleanup, per `CLAUDE.md`'s mandatory ordering). `TestReorderPolicies_RealDB_AdjacentSwap_NoSpuriousUniqueViolation` seeds 3 sequentially-numbered policies and swaps the first two — the exact intermediate-collision shape that 500'd pre-fix — confirms success and correct final order/priorities. `TestReorderPolicies_RealDB_GenuineCollision_RollsBackCleanly` proves the transaction correctly *rejects* a genuinely-invalid final state (not just the old bug's spurious rejection of valid ones) and leaves zero partial state behind. **Verified 2026-08-22: `go build`/`go vet` clean; full `go test ./...` clean across `eami-api`** (both new tests pass; zero regressions elsewhere).
**Reviewer + security passes, mandatory per this brief's own standing rules (real enforcement behavior).** Security: zero findings — the transaction wrap only changes the atomicity boundary around already-parameterized, already org-scoped SQL; no new attack surface, no auth/authorization change. Code review: two real, lower-severity findings, disclosed and logged as **B-090** rather than fixed here (out of this brief's approved "wrap in a transaction" scope) — holding row locks across all N per-row updates for the transaction's duration introduces a narrow concurrent-reorder deadlock risk (two admins reordering the same org's policies with overlapping-but-different-order id lists could hit Postgres `40P01`, surfacing as a retryable `500`); the function also still does N sequential single-row round-trips instead of one batched multi-row `UPDATE`, which would also shrink the deadlock window.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack — the brief's own required centerpiece, not just "the API call succeeds":** two real throwaway policies (`deny` priority 1000, `allow` priority 1001, identical `tool_names` condition targeting a real `rest_api` connector). A real `tool_call`, dispatched via a real agent JWT + real MCP SSE session, confirmed **deny wins first** (`-32600 "Request denied by policy"`, correct `policy_id`, real `audit_log` row `decision=denied`). Reordered via the real, fixed endpoint using the real UI's exact full-org-list request shape (the identical adjacent-pair-swap call that 500'd before the fix, now returning a real `200`). The **identical** `tool_call` dispatched again on a fresh session — confirmed **allow now wins**: a real response came back from `postman-echo.com`, and the real `audit_log` row recorded `decision=allowed` with the allow policy's id. This is the literal proof that a real reorder through the real endpoint changes real enforcement outcomes. All throwaway policies deleted afterward; the two original real policies' relative order is unchanged (their raw priority *numbers* shifted from 1/99 to 1/2 as a side effect of being included in the full-list test call — disclosed, not hidden, no behavioral impact).
**Dependencies:** none for this fix. **B-089** (openapi.yaml spec correction) and **B-090** (deadlock-risk/batching improvement) are new, separate, low-priority follow-ups — not blocking.

**Feature/Fix — B-087 + B-077 (2026-08-22), agent lifecycle: create/suspend/delete, and the delete-with-history bug that would have immediately blocked it — full frontend writeup in `eami-ui` section below.** Task brief: `AgentsPage.tsx` had no UI path to `useCreateAgent`/`useUpdateAgent`/`useDeleteAgent` despite all three being real, tested endpoints — the Dead Clicks Audit's finding that directly explained why B-077 was only ever reproduced via a raw API call.
**B-077's root cause, confirmed and fixed:** `DeleteAgent` issued a raw `DELETE` with no FK-violation classification. `episodes`/`approval_requests`/`workflow_runs`.`agent_id` are all default `NO ACTION` (deliberately not cascaded — losing that history on agent deletion would be wrong for a compliance-relevant audit trail, this item's own original reasoning, re-confirmed not re-litigated). Fixed by classifying the resulting FK violation (`23503`) into a clean `409` pointing the admin at suspend instead, reusing `workflows.go`'s already-existing `isForeignKeyViolation` helper — zero new classification logic needed, same pattern as B-074's `isUniqueViolation`.
**Suspend investigated before assuming a data-model change was needed — none was:** `gateway_agents.status` already had a real, working `CHECK (status IN ('active','suspended','revoked'))`, and suspension was already fully enforced at the dispatch layer — `eami-gateway/internal/registry/registry.go`'s `ErrAgentSuspended` plus `internal/mcp/handler.go`'s real `403` at SSE session creation, both pre-existing, both untouched by this brief. Only the UI trigger was missing.
**A false premise in the task brief, found by investigation, confirmed with the user before building against it:** the brief assumed lifecycle actions should write to `audit_log`, "consistent with" existing admin-action patterns. Grepped every existing admin-action handler (`policies.go`/`tools.go`/`approvals.go`/`agents.go`) — zero `audit_log` writes exist anywhere in `eami-api`; that table is written exclusively by `eami-gateway`'s hash-chained dispatch-decision path. **Resolution, confirmed with the user:** a new, small, separate `agent_lifecycle_events` table (migration `000009_agent_lifecycle_events`) — deliberately not a write into the hash-chained ledger (a different concern), no FK to `gateway_agents` so a `deleted` event survives the row it describes. New hand-written `eami-api/internal/store/agent_lifecycle_events.sql.go` (`InsertAgentLifecycleEvent`, matching `token_usage.sql.go`'s existing non-sqlc-generated precedent) — one row per `created`/`suspended`/`reactivated`/`deleted` transition, best-effort (mirrors `NotifyPolicyReload`'s established "side effect must not fail the primary action" convention), `performed_by` always the authenticated caller's own JWT-derived user id, never client input.
**Test coverage:** new `TestDeleteAgent_RealDB_WithRealHistory_Returns409NotRawError` (`agents_pg_test.go`, real Postgres, `t.Cleanup`-only pool lifecycle) reproduces B-077's exact original failure end-to-end through the real HTTP handler: seeds a real episode against a real agent, confirms the delete now `409`s cleanly (not `500`), confirms the agent row survives untouched, confirms zero spurious `deleted` lifecycle row from the blocked attempt, confirms suspend still succeeds as the real alternative and its own lifecycle row is written. **Verified 2026-08-22: `go build`/`go vet` clean; full `go test ./...` clean across `eami-api`** (new test passes, zero regressions across the existing agent test suite).
**Reviewer + security passes, mandatory per this brief (real agent-identity lifecycle/access change).** Security: zero findings — the new insert is fully parameterized, `PerformedBy` is server-derived from JWT claims never client-supplied, no authorization logic touched by this diff, and the classified `409` is a disclosure *reduction* versus the previous leaked-driver-error `500`. **Feature — B-094 (2026-08-22), Audit entry detail view backend: `data_handling_designation` finally read back, `VerifyAuditChain` exercised bounded-to-one-entry — full frontend detail panel in `eami-ui` section below.** Task brief followed directly from the B-084 investigation's own findings (see `CONTEXT.md`): most of the value here was already free (parameters/policy_id/approval_id/prev_hash were already in `ListAudit`'s response, just never rendered), with exactly one genuine backend gap.
**The one real backend change:** `data_handling_designation` (B-078's `audit_log` column) was written by `eami-gateway`'s writer but never selected by `ListAudit`, never on `store.AuditEntry`, never on `AuditEntryResp` — confirmed directly by the investigation, not assumed. Added to the SELECT list (`audit.sql.go`), the model (`models.go`, nullable `pgtype.Text`), and the response type (`types.go`, `*string` with `omitempty` — never fabricates a value for rows that never had one set).
**`GET /v1/audit/verify` itself was NOT modified** — per the task brief's explicit boundary, this brief only calls it (bounded via `?to=<entry timestamp>`, no `?from`) the way the new frontend panel does, and adds test coverage proving that call pattern actually works against a real chain.
**Test coverage — real Postgres, `t.Cleanup`-only pool lifecycle per `CLAUDE.md`'s mandatory pattern:** new `audit_pg_test.go`, three tests. `TestAudit_RealDB_DataHandlingDesignation_RoundTrips` proves AC5 — a value written directly to `audit_log` (mirroring `eami-gateway`'s writer) round-trips through the real HTTP response, and a row with none set omits the field rather than fabricating one. `TestAudit_RealDB_VerifyChainToEntry_IntactChain_ReturnsValid` and `_TamperedRow_ReturnsInvalid` prove the backend half of AC3 against a real, hand-chained 2-3 row sequence (hash values computed with the exact same SHA-256 formula as `eami-gateway/internal/audit/writer.go`) — one genuinely intact, one genuinely tampered via a real `UPDATE` after insert. **A real bug found and fixed while writing these tests, not in shipped code:** the test's own `to=` query param used `time.RFC3339.Format(...)`, which truncates fractional seconds — since the real stored `timestamp` column carries full precision, this silently excluded the very row the test meant to bound to, producing `TotalRows=1` instead of `2` and masking the tampered row entirely from the walk. Fixed by using `time.RFC3339Nano` when building the test URL, and confirmed the real frontend hook (`useVerifyAuditChainToEntry`) never has this bug in the first place — it forwards `entry.timestamp` exactly as received from the JSON API, never reformats it. **Verified 2026-08-22: `go build`/`go vet` clean; full `go test ./... -count=1` clean across `eami-api`** (all three new tests pass, zero regressions).
**Reviewer pass, mandatory per this brief:** found and this session fixed a real gofmt misalignment introduced in `models.go`'s struct (new field's alignment didn't match its neighbors) — fixed precisely, without touching the rest of the file's pre-existing, unrelated CRLF-vs-LF diff noise the reviewer also flagged and correctly attributed to disk state predating this brief, not this diff.
**Security review, mandatory per this brief's own CRITICAL REQUIREMENT (independently re-derive the hash-labeling honesty claim, not just check it's present):** zero findings. The added column change touches only an already-parameterized, already org-scoped query; the new frontend policy/approval lookups are independently re-scoped server-side regardless of client input; the client-side SHA-256 self-consistency check uses only already-public data (the entry's own `hash`) and the caller's own session `org_id`, no secret material, no new trust boundary.

WHERE id=...` (simulating an attacker with DB write access) followed by the identical call confirmed `valid:false`, `first_broken_at` pointing at the exact tampered row. The client-side self-consistency formula was independently verified in Node.js against the real row's actual API response, byte-for-byte, before trusting it in the browser. **Bugfix — B-097 (2026-08-22), `GET /v1/finops/summary` 500 root-caused and fixed — a genuinely different bug from the already-fixed B-016.** The FinOps page had returned a 500 throughout the session, disclosed as "known, pre-existing" in every prior report that touched it but never actually diagnosed. B-016 (2026-08-08) had already fixed the nil-`s.queries` panic; that fix was confirmed still intact and irrelevant here.
**Real root cause, isolated by direct reproduction against the real running stack, not assumed:** `FinOpsSummary`'s `teamQ` did `SELECT COALESCE(ga.owner, 'unknown') AS team, ... GROUP BY team`. `token_usage` has its own real (schema-present, never-populated) `team TEXT` column (`schema/migrations-v2/000001_baseline.up.sql:414`) — and PostgreSQL's documented GROUP BY name-resolution rule prefers a matching **input column** over a same-named **output alias**, so `GROUP BY team` silently grouped by `tu.team` (always empty) instead of the alias, leaving `ga.owner` ungrouped and non-aggregated: `ERROR: column "ga.owner" must appear in the GROUP BY clause or be used in an aggregate function (SQLSTATE 42803)`. Reproduced live first (real login, real JWT, real request against the running container, real error captured), then isolated the exact clause in a standalone `psql` query before touching any code.
**Fix:** one-line change, `GROUP BY team` → `GROUP BY ga.owner` (`eami-api/internal/api/finops.go`) — valid because `COALESCE(ga.owner, 'unknown')` is a pure function of the now-grouped `ga.owner` column, so Postgres accepts it in the SELECT list without needing the literal expression repeated in GROUP BY. `agentQ`/`modelQ` already group by real columns directly and were never affected; `FinOpsTimeSeries` never references `team` at all. No schema change — `token_usage`'s write path is completely untouched, per this brief's explicit `MUST NOT MODIFY`. The misleading pre-existing comment above the query (claiming "token_usage does not have a team column") was corrected in the same pass, since it was the root of the original confusion.
**AC4 finding — UPDATED after a real live dispatch, per explicit user direction to actually test rather than stop at the code-level trace.** Initial code trace (see below) suggested the extraction logic was sound and untested-but-fine; a real live dispatch proved that conclusion incomplete and surfaced a **second, genuinely separate, live bug — logged as B-099, not fixed here (flagged, not silently expanded into this brief's scope, per explicit instruction).**
`token_usage` **is** being written by real dispatch activity — confirmed via direct `psql`: 25 real rows in the real Dev Org (`org_id=4142489b-83e8-452f-a035-0ce5a55e7988`), tied to genuine agent names from past live-verification sessions (`manual-test-agent` ×14, `b059-live-agent` ×9, `b075-verify-agent` ×2), not seed data. Every existing row has empty/zero `model`/`tokens_in`/`tokens_out`/`cost_usd`. Code trace: `extractTokenUsage` (`cmd/gateway/main.go:660-684`) only populates real values when the downstream response body's top level matches `{model, usage:{input_tokens,output_tokens}}`; every dispatch that had produced a row so far went through non-AI-provider `rest_api` test connectors (`postman-echo`, etc.), never returning that shape. Traced `aiprovider/claude.go`'s `Dispatch`: it returns Anthropic's raw, unmodified Messages API response body, genuinely shaped correctly at the top level, reaching `extractTokenUsage` undisturbed for an immediately-allowed call.
**The real live test, run after explicit user authorization to actually spend the (negligible, sub-cent) real API cost rather than stop at the code trace:** minted a real agent JWT, opened a real MCP SSE session, dispatched a real `tool_call` to the org's real `claude` connector (`claude-haiku-4-5-20251001`, `max_tokens:1`). The org has an active `escalate` policy on Claude calls, so this was held pending approval; approved it via the real `POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decide`. The resumed SSE message carried a **real, successful Anthropic response** (`id:"msg_011CeJG3rQs1tbFqHnsbM2sf"`, real `usage:{input_tokens:8,output_tokens:1}`) — proving the connector, credentials, and escalation-resume path all genuinely work end-to-end. **But no new `token_usage` row appeared afterward, and neither `eami-gateway` nor `eami-api`'s logs showed any write attempt at all** (not a logged failure — a code path never reached; confirmed via full container log inspection across the exact request window, and via `GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY`/`API_SERVICE_KEY` matching, ruling out an auth-mismatch explanation).
**Root cause of this second bug, traced precisely:** `extractTokenUsage`/`safeWriteTokenUsage` (`main.go:428-429`) has exactly one call site in the whole module (confirmed via repo-wide grep) — inside the `default: // policy.ActionAllow` switch branch. The `case policy.ActionEscalate:` branch (`main.go:304-375`) calls `approvalRouter.Hold(...)` and returns its result directly, **never falling through to the Allow branch's post-processing** — so token-usage recording is architecturally skipped for every escalated/approval-resumed call, regardless of whether the underlying dispatch succeeds. Since the org's real `claude` connector is unconditionally escalated by an active policy, this means **zero real AI-provider spend has ever been recorded in this org**, independent of B-097's fix.
**Correctly not fixed in this brief:** genuinely out of B-097's scope (a `GROUP BY` query bug), found while investigating a different question (AC4), and involves a real design decision (share a helper between the Allow/Escalate branches' now-duplicated post-dispatch steps vs. duplicate the token-usage call) that deserves its own scoped brief, not a rushed inline fix under a bugfix brief's banner. **Logged as B-099 (QUEUED, Medium-High severity) in `BACKLOG.md`**, flagged to the user before any fix was attempted, per explicit instruction.
**Test coverage:** new `finops_pg_test.go`, 3 real-Postgres integration tests (`t.Cleanup`-only pool lifecycle per `CLAUDE.md`'s mandatory pattern): `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_TeamBreakdown_RegressionAndDataCorrectness` reproduces the exact scenario that used to 500 (a `token_usage` row joined to a `gateway_agents` row with a real, non-null `owner`) and asserts numeric correctness across every breakdown (by-agent/by-team/by-model, grand totals) against known seeded values — not just "no error" (AC2); `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_OrgIsolation` proves a second org's rows never leak into the response; `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_NoDataInRange` proves an empty result set still returns a clean `200` with zeroed totals, not an error. **Verified 2026-08-22: `go build`/`go vet` clean; full `go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-api` module**, zero regressions in any pre-existing FinOps test.

AC3 (FinOps page loads end-to-end) is satisfied by construction — `useFinOps.ts`'s request shape was confirmed unchanged and already matching (`from`/`to` query params), so no frontend change was needed; the backend now returns exactly the schema the frontend already expects. **No browser screenshot taken** — same standing, disclosed limitation as every other `eami-ui`-touching brief this session (Chrome extension not installed). **Operational note:** `dev@example.com`'s password was reset to a known temporary value for this session's live logins (same disclosed precedent as B-046/B-058/B-054) — still set to that value; the user may want to reset it again.

**Interfaces — B-098 (2026-08-24), `api_keys` gains real agent-scoping and a working `expires_at`:** `CreateAPIKeyRequest`/`APIKeyResp` gain `agent_id`/`expires_at` (`types.go`) — `expires_at` existed in the schema since migration 002 but was never accepted at this layer; `agent_id` is new (see `schema/migrations-v2/000010` in the eami-gateway section above). `CreateAPIKey` (`auth.go`) validates `agent_id` against a real `gateway_agents` row scoped to the caller's own org (`GetAgent(ctx, id, uc.OrgID)`, never trusted as opaque) and — per this brief's own mandatory code-review finding — rejects binding to a non-`active` agent up front, rather than silently minting a key that could never authorize issuance. `expires_at` accepts RFC3339 or `YYYY-MM-DD` via a new `parseAPIKeyExpiresAt`, matching `finops.go`'s existing dual-format convention. `GetAPIKeyByHash` (`store/auth.sql.go`) now also filters `expires_at IS NULL OR expires_at > NOW()` — a second code-review finding: it previously only checked `revoked`, an inconsistency with eami-gateway's own new validator for the identical row (latent, since this query has zero production callers in `eami-api` today, but fixed for whichever caller reaches for it next).

**Interfaces — B-108 (2026-08-25), FinOps connector breakdown + two silently-broken metrics closed, all three found by the same-session Token Usage Optimization re-investigation:** `token_usage.tool_name` existed in the schema (baseline migration) but was never populated or queried anywhere. `by_tool` is new: `ac.Tool` is now threaded through `tokenUsagePayload` (`eami-gateway/cmd/gateway/main.go`, set unconditionally at construction — unlike `Model`/token counts, which live inside the possibly-unparseable downstream response body and stay zeroed when it doesn't parse) → `TokenUsageRequest` (`reports.go`, optional field, empty stays empty for any pre-B-108 caller) → `InsertTokenUsageParams`/`InsertTokenUsage` (`store/token_usage.sql.go`, empty encoded as SQL `NULL` via `pgtype.Text`, matching the nullable column). `finops.go` gains a `toolQ` query mirroring `modelQ` exactly, `COALESCE(tu.tool_name, 'unknown')` for an unresolved-tool dispatch (mirrors `by_team`'s identical `COALESCE(ga.owner, 'unknown')` precedent immediately above it) — a real, expected case, not an error.
**`avg_cost_per_outcome` (documented in `openapi.yaml` since before this brief, read by `FinOpsPage.tsx`, never actually computed) is now real** — investigated what the metric was meant to represent before deciding how to close the gap: each `token_usage` row already *is* one recorded dispatch outcome (exactly what `by_agent.request_count` counts per-agent), so `total_cost_usd / COUNT(*)` over the same period is the correct, already-well-defined value — one added `COUNT(*)` to the existing `totalQ` plus a guarded division (0, not NaN, for an empty period). Chosen over removing the field: computing it needed zero `openapi.yaml` changes (Architect-EAMI-owned, out of this session's boundary), where removing it would have.
**`by_team` (computed correctly since B-097, never rendered) now renders** — a "Spend by Team" table in `FinOpsPage.tsx`, same JSX pattern as the existing "Spend by Agent" table. Chosen over removing the backend field: the computation was already correct and already tested (`finops_pg_test.go`'s B-097 regression test); removing already-correct, already-tested work to avoid a small frontend addition was the wrong trade. A new "Spend by Connector" table renders `by_tool` the same way. `ToolSpend` isn't yet in `openapi.yaml` — disclosed, not silently edited, same B-098/`agent_id` precedent, logged as part of B-106 (already open for the identical class of gap).
**No schema migration** — `token_usage.tool_name` already existed, unused; no dispatch logic, B-102's hook mechanism, or `model_pricing` touched (all explicitly out of scope).

Two more rows seeded with distinct connectors/costs; a real admin login (`POST /v1/auth/login`, a freshly-created user with a known bcrypt hash for this verification) then a real `GET /v1/finops/summary` returned `total_cost_usd: 0.37`, `avg_cost_per_outcome: 0.12333...` (= 0.37/3, exactly right), `by_tool` correctly split $0.30/$0.07 across the two connectors, `by_team` correctly present — all cross-checked by hand against the seeded values. **Bugfix — B-111 (2026-08-26), FinOps cost calculation now accounts for Anthropic prompt-caching tiers, computed at query time from current rates:** `token_usage` gains 3 raw count columns (`cache_creation_5m_tokens`, `cache_creation_1h_tokens`, `cache_read_tokens`, `schema/migrations-v2/000011_cache_token_pricing.up.sql`), threaded through from `eami-gateway`'s corrected `extractTokenUsage` (see `BUILT.md`'s `eami-gateway` section) via `TokenUsageRequest`/`InsertTokenUsageParams`/`InsertTokenUsage` (`reports.go`, `store/token_usage.sql.go`). `model_pricing` gains 3 nullable rate columns matching the 3 new cache tiers, backfilled per-row for `claude-%` models from that row's own `cost_per_1k_in` (`* 1.25`/`* 2.0`/`* 0.1`) — never one shared multiplier, so a future distinct model generation (e.g. `claude-sonnet-5` alongside today's `claude-sonnet-4-6`) gets its own correctly-derived rates by construction, not a collapsed one.
**Deliberate design, per this brief's own explicit contract, not the write-time-frozen pattern the rest of this table uses:** cache cost is **never computed or stored** at write time — `IngestTokenUsage`'s existing base/output cost formula is completely untouched (the mechanism that makes "no regression to non-caching pricing" trivially true). Instead, all 5 `finops.go` queries (`totalQ`/`agentQ`/`teamQ`/`modelQ`/`toolQ`) gain an additive cache-cost term inside the same per-row `SUM(...)`, always computed fresh from **current** `model_pricing` rates (`COALESCE(mp.cost_per_1k_cache_*, 0)`) — so a rate correction re-prices every historical row the next time the query runs, proven live (see below), not just asserted.
**Two things investigated and found NOT to be real, rather than assumed true from the brief's own framing:** (1) a "Sonnet 5 vs. 4.6 rate collision" — `model_pricing` has no `claude-sonnet-5` row today, so there was no actual collision to fix, just a per-row-not-shared-multiplier backfill discipline to uphold, which the migration already does. (2) the brief's `MUST NOT MODIFY` list named two "separately logged" items (a `finops.go` fallback-rate collision, a concurrent test-helper race) that did not exist anywhere in `BACKLOG.md`/`CONTEXT.md`/`NOTES.md` at the time — logged fresh as **B-109**/**B-110** in a prior session turn, before this brief's build work began, rather than silently treated as already-real.
**Test coverage:** `finops_pg_test.go` gains 3 new real-Postgres tests plus 2 new helpers (`setModelPricing`, `insertUsageWithCache`): `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_CacheTokens_RecordedRawAndQueriedAtAllFiveTiers` (AC1 — raw counts verified via a direct pool query; AC2 — cost hand-computed across all 5 distinct tiers; AC5 — the same corrected total cross-checked against `ByAgent`/`ByModel`, not just the grand total), `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_NoCaching_PricesExactlyAsBefore` (AC3 — a model WITH cache rates configured but 0 cache tokens on the row prices identically to pre-B-111), `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_RateChangeThenRequery_ReflectsNewRate` (AC4 centerpiece — same already-recorded row, two queries either side of a live `model_pricing` UPDATE, cost provably changes between them). **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across both `eami-gateway` and `eami-api`** against a real Postgres — 16/16 FinOps tests pass (13 pre-existing + 3 new), zero regression to `by_agent`/`by_model`/`by_team`/`by_tool`/`avg_cost_per_outcome`; `schema/migrationtest`'s full suite (fresh/incremental parity, idempotent re-run, seeded-DB upgrade) passes with the new migration in place.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt and restarted with the fix before verification:** a real `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` dispatch with a genuine 1h `cache_control` recorded `cache_creation_1h_tokens: 4812` correctly via direct `psql` (AC1); a repeat dispatch of the identical cached content within the TTL window recorded a real cache **hit** instead (`cache_read_tokens: 4812`), independently confirming the read tier too, not just the write tier. A real `GET /v1/finops/summary` against this data returned `total_cost_usd: 0.00047896`, hand-verified exactly against each row's tier-specific rate (AC2). A real plain (no `cache_control`) dispatch priced at exactly the pre-B-111 base formula, `0.000032` (AC3). **AC4 proven live, not just in the Go test:** doubled the model's real `cost_per_1k_cache_read` rate via `psql`, re-queried the exact same already-recorded historical rows with zero new writes — total moved to `0.00086392`, exactly the hand-computed delta — then restored the original rate and confirmed the total reverted to `0.00047896` byte-for-byte. `by_agent`/`by_team`/`by_model`/`by_tool` all consistently reflected the same corrected total throughout every query (AC5). All live-dispatch approvals required real (sub-cent) Anthropic spend, explicitly user-approved before each one — an earlier same-session dispatch made against the pre-rebuild (pre-fix) binary was correctly identified and excluded from evidence once noticed, not silently counted. All fixtures cleaned up: API key deleted, the one stale pre-fix pending approval explicitly denied (its session no longer existed to receive a result), the agent suspended rather than hard-deleted (real episode/approval history from the live dispatches — `DeleteAgent`'s audit-trail guard correctly refused deletion), all 3 `token_usage` rows deleted manually (`token_usage` has no FK to `orgs`/`gateway_agents`, same pre-existing gap the B-108 entry above already documented).
**No `openapi.yaml` deviation this time** — unlike B-108, this brief added no new API response fields (cache cost folds into the existing `cost_usd`/`total_cost_usd`), so there's nothing new to disclose there.
**Dependencies:** none new. **Out of scope, as specified:** `model_pricing` admin CRUD (item 3), the B-110 fallback-rate collision, the B-109 test-helper race, any caching orchestration/management feature.

**Interfaces + bugfix — B-112 (2026-08-26), `model_pricing` admin CRUD + B-110's fallback-rate collision fixed:** new `model_pricing.go` + `store/model_pricing.sql.go` (`ModelPricingRow`, `ListModelPricing`/`CreateModelPricing`/`UpdateModelPricing`/`DeleteModelPricing`), mirroring B-098's `api_keys` CRUD as the structural template: `GET/POST/PATCH/DELETE /v1/admin/model-pricing[/{model}]`, exposing all 8 columns (base + B-111's 3 cache-rate tiers). `PATCH` uses the same `COALESCE($n, column)` partial-update convention as `agents.sql.go`'s `UpdateAgent`. Write routes gated **admin-only** in `router.go` (stricter than agents/policies/tools' admin+operator gating) — `model_pricing` has no `org_id` (confirmed genuinely global via schema + doc search, no per-org negotiated-rate concept anywhere), so a write here affects every org's cost reporting, not just the caller's own org.
**B-110's real mechanism, re-verified empirically (rolled-back real-Postgres reproduction) before building, not assumed from the brief's own description:** an unrecognized model's row was already kept in its own, correctly separate `by_model` bucket — never merged into a recognized model's. The real bug: its cost silently becomes exactly $0 (Postgres `SUM` ignores the `NULL` from `tokens × an unmatched rate`), under-reporting every aggregate that includes it, indistinguishable from a genuinely free dispatch. Fixed by adding `ModelSpend.PricingConfigured` (`bool_and(mp.model IS NOT NULL)` per `tu.model` group in `modelQ` — constant within a group, since the join key IS `tu.model`) and `TokenSpendSummary.UnrecognizedModelRequestCount` (`COUNT(*) FILTER (WHERE mp.model IS NULL)` in `totalQ`) — surfaces the undercount instead of hiding it. Neither B-111's cache-tier query-time mechanism nor the base/output frozen-`cost_usd` mechanism was touched.
**A real discrepancy caught during live verification, not silently resolved:** AC2 ("update pricing, historical rows re-price on next query, per B-111's design") only holds for cache-tier rates and for a base cost that was exactly $0/unrecognized at write time (stored as SQL `NULL`) — not for a normal already-priced row's base rate, which B-111 deliberately freezes at write time. Live-verified directly: updated a real model's `cost_per_1k_in` via the new PATCH endpoint, re-queried an already-recorded non-cache row — cost unchanged, exactly as the frozen mechanism predicts. Per explicit user decision: disclosed as-is (see `BACKLOG.md`'s B-112 entry for the precise corrected wording), B-111's design left untouched per this brief's own `MUST NOT MODIFY`; the open question of whether to add deliberate base-rate historical re-pricing is logged as **B-114**, not resolved here.
**A second real, disclosed-not-fixed gap found while investigating org-scoping:** any org's ordinary `admin` role can write to this global table, since EAMI has no platform-admin tier distinct from per-org roles — logged as **B-113**.
**Test coverage:** `finops_pg_test.go` gains `TestFinOpsSummary_Real_UnrecognizedModel_NeverMergesIntoRecognizedModelsCost` (AC3 centerpiece). New `model_pricing_pg_test.go`: full CRUD round trip, duplicate-model 409, negative-rate 400, admin-only-writes/operator-allowed-reads (AC4), new-model-then-real-ingestion-prices-correctly (AC1). **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across `eami-api`**, zero regressions. Real `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx vite build` both clean — Node/npm are now present on this machine (contradicting `CLAUDE.md`'s stale toolchain note; flagged, not fixed, out of this brief's file boundary).
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real rebuilt/restarted stack:** `GET /v1/admin/model-pricing` returned all 9 real seeded rows with cache columns correctly present/absent; a real `POST` new model + real ingestion via `POST /v1/internal/token-usage` + `GET /v1/finops/summary` priced it exactly (AC1); a real recognized+unrecognized-model pair via real ingestion showed the recognized model's cost exactly un-contaminated (`0.0028`) and the unrecognized model separately flagged (`cost_usd:0, pricing_configured:false, unrecognized_model_request_count:1`) (AC3). AC4 verified via the real-Postgres HTTP-level Go test rather than provisioning a new throwaway operator user in the shared dev database. The live `eami-ui` dev container confirmed serving the new Settings tab and FinOps warning banner. All live fixtures cleaned up, including unrelated orphaned test residue from this session's own iterative Go test runs (pre-existing `token_usage` no-FK gap, same as B-108).
**Dependencies:** B-098 (CRUD template), B-111 (cache-rate columns, query-time design). **Out of scope, as specified:** budget-based enforcement, model-tier routing, workflow-level cost attribution.

**Fix — B-090 (2026-08-26), `ReorderPolicies` batched into one statement, transaction wrapper removed entirely:** B-086's own mandatory code-review pass had flagged two related issues in its transaction-wrapped fix — a narrow concurrent-reorder deadlock risk (row locks held across N round trips) and N sequential single-row `UPDATE`s where one batched statement would do. Re-verified both still present, unchanged, before building.
**What shipped:** `eami-api/internal/store/policies.sql.go`'s `ReorderPolicies` rewritten as one `UPDATE policies AS p SET priority = v.priority FROM (SELECT unnest($1::uuid[]) AS id, unnest($2::int[]) AS priority) AS v WHERE p.id = v.id AND p.org_id = $3` — fixed 3-parameter shape regardless of `len(ids)`. **The explicit `tx.Begin/Rollback/Commit` wrapper was removed, not shrunk** — investigated empirically, not assumed: a real 2-item adjacent swap and a real 3-item rotation, each run directly against `psql` as bare single statements with no `BEGIN`, both landed correctly with zero spurious unique-violations. B-086's bug required N *separate* auto-committing statements for an intermediate half-renumbered state to trip the `DEFERRABLE INITIALLY DEFERRED` constraint; a single `UPDATE...FROM` reads all old values before writing any, so there's no intermediate state for the deferred check to ever see. Round trips: N+2 → 1.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both found real issues, all fixed:** security review confirmed the array-parameter binding and org-scoping (`WHERE p.org_id = $3`) sound, but caught a stale comment crediting a handler-side per-id org pre-check that (traced directly) doesn't exist on the production path (`s.queries != nil` branch calls `ReorderPolicies` with no per-id loop — that loop is mock-path-only) — fixed the comment to state the WHERE clause is the sole enforcement. Also caught that duplicate IDs in the request now hit Postgres's documented *arbitrary* pick between competing `UPDATE...FROM` source rows, where the old N-loop was deterministic last-write-wins — fixed by rejecting duplicate `policy_ids` with a 400 in the handler (`eami-api/internal/api/policies.go`) rather than accepting silent nondeterminism on an authorization-relevant ordering. Code review independently re-derived the transaction-removal reasoning from Postgres's autocommit/`DEFERRABLE` semantics (not just trusted the comment) and confirmed it sound, but caught an overclaim — an earlier comment draft said the fix "closes B-090's deadlock-window finding by construction, not just narrows it," which is false (two concurrent single-statement calls can still deadlock, `40P01`, which is exactly why the new concurrency test tolerates one) — fixed to say "narrowed," not "closed." Also caught sqlc-annotation drift (`ReorderPoliciesBatched` in the query doc vs. the real `ReorderPolicies` Go function name) that would silently break both call sites on a future `sqlc generate` — fixed by aligning the annotation.
**Deliberately not fixed here, logged fresh:** the accepted `40P01` deadlock surfaces as a raw-error-text 500 with no retry (**B-117**) — broader than this one-function scope, touches the app-wide `writeError(...,"internal_error", err.Error())` convention used throughout `eami-api`.
**Test coverage:** new `TestReorderPolicies_RealDB_ConcurrentOverlappingReorders_NoCorruption` (`policies_reorder_pg_test.go`) — 20 iterations of two goroutines concurrently reordering with an id list and its full reversal, asserting any error is either nil or a genuine `40P01` (accepted per this item's own severity note) and that `ListPolicies` always shows exactly N distinct priorities in `1..N`, zero duplicates. New `TestReorderPolicies_DuplicateID_BadRequest` (`policies_test.go`, mock-backed) for the dedup fix. **Verified 2026-08-26: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across all of `eami-api`** against a real Postgres, zero regressions — both pre-existing `policies_reorder_pg_test.go` tests re-run clean after the transaction removal.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real rebuilt/restarted stack:** the real 4-policy Dev Org list (2 pre-existing + 2 throwaway) reordered via a real `POST /v1/gateway/policies/reorder` swapping the last two — real 200, correct final priorities confirmed via direct `psql`, zero spurious unique-violation, the exact B-086 bug shape proven end-to-end through the real HTTP API. Re-run again post-rebuild after the security/code-review fixes with the same result. The new duplicate-ID rejection live-verified with a real request returning a real 400. All fixtures cleaned up afterward.
**`go test -race` still unavailable in this environment** — no C compiler (`gcc`/`cc`/`clang` all absent), `CGO_ENABLED=0`; unchanged from B-100's disclosure, re-confirmed rather than assumed still true.
**Dependencies:** B-086 (the fix this hardens). New discovered work: **B-117**.

**Fix — B-117 (2026-08-27), `ReorderPolicies`'s accepted `40P01` deadlock is now retried instead of surfacing as a raw 500:** three new unexported additions to `policies.go`, wired only into the production (`s.queries != nil`) path: `isReorderDeadlock(err)` (detects `*pgconn.PgError{Code:"40P01"}` via `errors.As`, mirroring the existing test-only `isDeadlockError` in `policies_reorder_pg_test.go`), `reorderPoliciesExecFunc` (a package-level `var` test seam wrapping the real `q.ReorderPolicies` call, mirroring `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s `tokenUsageWriteFunc` convention in the sister module), and `reorderPoliciesWithRetry` (bounded at 3 total attempts, 20ms fixed backoff — retries only on a detected `40P01`; any other error, including a genuine final-state priority collision, surfaces immediately unretried). `storeIface`/mock test path deliberately left un-retried (unreachable in production; `cmd/api/main.go` always constructs with non-nil `queries`).
**Explicitly not fixed, by design, matching the original B-117 backlog entry's own scope decision:** the raw-driver-error-text-in-response leak (an exhausted retry, or any other error, still returns `err.Error()` verbatim) — a separate, app-wide `writeError` hygiene concern the original fix sketch already scoped out of a one-endpoint fix. Only the *frequency* of hitting that pre-existing leak changed.
**Mandatory reviewer + security passes, both ran (per explicit user correction — this brief's own STANDING RULES omitted them, a gap in how the brief was written, not a deliberate exception), both found real issues, all fixed:**
- **Code review + security review, independently:** an in-code comment overstated the fix as closing the raw-error-text leak. Corrected to state plainly it does not.
- **Code review (Low):** `reorderPoliciesMaxAttempts <= 0` would silently return `nil` (success) without the retry loop ever executing — not reachable today (positive compile-time constant), but fixed with an explicit guard returning an error instead, matching this codebase's own established fail-closed-on-non-positive-config precedent (B-070/B-118's `rateLimiter.Allow`).
- **Code review + security review, independently (Low):** the fixed 20ms backoff used a plain `time.Sleep`, ignoring `ctx` cancellation. Fixed with a `select` on `ctx.Done()`/`time.After`.
- **Security review (Low):** the retry's correctness silently depends on `store.ReorderPolicies` being a bare autocommit statement (true today, per B-090's own design, confirmed unchanged) — a future change wrapping it in an explicit transaction would make every retry attempt return a misleading `25P02` instead of the real `40P01`. Documented explicitly in `reorderPoliciesWithRetry`'s doc comment as a constraint, not fixed structurally (no transaction exists to guard against today).
- **Code review (Low, test quality) — a real gap, not a nitpick:** the first draft's "unrecoverable deadlock" test simulated a deadlock on every attempt including the last, so no attempt ever reached real Postgres and its "no partial state left behind" assertion was vacuously true — nothing could have corrupted state that was never touched. Rewritten: 2 simulated deadlocks + a REAL final attempt against real Postgres hitting a genuine final-state priority collision (mirroring `TestReorderPolicies_RealDB_GenuineCollision_RollsBackCleanly`'s exact shape) — now a real, non-vacuous proof.
- **Security review, adversarially traced end to end, all clean:** retry inputs (`uc.OrgID`, `req.Order`) confirmed byte-identical on every attempt, never mutated between retries; `reorderPoliciesExecFunc` confirmed zero runtime/request-reachable attack surface via repo-wide grep (compile-time test seam only, never assigned outside `_test.go`); the 20ms×2 backoff holds no DB connection or lock (`time.Sleep` occupies only the caller's own HTTP goroutine), so no meaningful new DoS surface versus the pre-fix single-attempt-then-fail behavior; `40P01` confirmed narrow enough (Postgres's own deadlock detector only) to not risk masking a different, more serious error; B-090's duplicate-ID rejection and org-scoping (`uc.OrgID`, never client input) both confirmed unaffected — the retry wrapper sits strictly inside the existing `s.queries != nil` block, after both checks.
- **Security review (Medium), a real pre-existing production gap found while looking for B-115's production analogue, NOT fixed here (outside this brief's granted file scope — `dispatcher.go`, not `policies.go`):** `eami-gateway/cmd/gateway/dispatcher.go` silently discards `auditWriter.Write` errors on the `denied`/`escalated` branches, unlike the `allowed` branch, which logs a failure. Logged fresh as **B-121**, flagged by explicit user direction as the highest-priority of this session's 3 new findings — a real audit-trail reliability gap on the security-critical path, not test-only like B-122/B-123.
**Test coverage:** new `policies_reorder_retry_test.go` (internal `package api`, since the 3 new symbols are unexported) — 4 pure unit tests for `isReorderDeadlock` (mirroring `agents_internal_test.go`'s `TestIsUniqueViolation_*` pattern), 3 pure control-flow tests for `reorderPoliciesWithRetry` via the `reorderPoliciesExecFunc` override seam (persistent-deadlock-exhausts-then-fails with exact attempt-count assertion, non-deadlock-not-retried, success-on-first-attempt), 2 real-Postgres tests (simulated-deadlock-then-real-success, verified via `q.ListPolicies`; 2 simulated deadlocks then a real terminal collision proving no partial state), 1 constants sanity check. B-090's own `TestReorderPolicies_RealDB_ConcurrentOverlappingReorders_NoCorruption` re-run byte-identical — confirmed unaffected (it calls `q.ReorderPolicies` directly, never the handler or the retry wrapper; `git diff` on both `policies.sql.go` and `policies_reorder_pg_test.go` empty). **Verified 2026-08-27: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test -count=1 ./...` clean across the entire `eami-api` module** against a real Postgres, zero regressions.
**Live-verified against the real rebuilt/restarted `eami-api` container:** a real admin user seeded and logged in via the real `POST /v1/auth/login`, 5 real policies created via the real `POST /v1/gateway/policies`, then 30 iterations of 2 genuinely concurrent overlapping `POST /v1/gateway/policies/reorder` HTTP requests (60 real requests total, scaled up from B-090's own 20-iteration precedent) — zero non-200 responses across all 60, final DB state confirmed a valid, uncorrupted permutation (priorities 1-5, each exactly once) via direct `psql`. All fixtures (policies, user, org) cleaned up afterward, confirmed 0 remaining.
**Dependencies:** B-090 (found this while reviewing). New discovered work: **B-121** (dispatcher.go audit-write-error swallowing).

## eami-ui
**Purpose:** React SPA — Dashboard, Discover, Gateway (Agents/Policies/Tools/Nodes), Approvals, FinOps, Memory, Paste Detection, Audit, Alerts, Settings.
**Status:** STABLE (all 14 pages are real implementations, 105–707 lines each; no bare placeholders remain).
**Key files:** `src/pages/{auth,dashboard,discover,finops,gateway,ops,settings}/*.tsx`; `src/hooks/*.ts` (15 resource hooks); `src/api/client.ts` (generated-client wrapper + documented `apiFetch()` escape hatch).
**Interfaces:** consumes `eami-api` exclusively via the OpenAPI-generated client (`npm run generate-client` → `src/api/schema.ts`, gitignored, build-time artifact — not present in a fresh checkout until generated).
**Data owned:** none (client only); Zustand for local UI/auth state.
**Test coverage:** **0** automated (no vitest/jest/playwright config exists anywhere in the repo, despite `BOUNDARIES.md` assigning `vitest.config.ts` + `playwright/` to QA-EAMI). `tsc --noEmit` itself still can't run directly (no Node/npm on this machine) — **but `npm run build` (`tsc && vite build`) can be, and was, run for real via `docker build --target builder -f eami-ui/Dockerfile .` from the repo root (B-024, 2026-07-24)** — this builds the `builder` stage of `eami-ui/Dockerfile`, which does `npm ci && npm run generate-client && npm run build` inside a `node:20-alpine` container, so `schema.ts` gets generated from `api/openapi.yaml` first (solving the "requires generate-client to run first" blocker noted below) and `tsc` runs for real. **This is a reusable verification path for any future eami-ui change on this machine** — no live `docker compose up`/browser click-through was performed for B-024 specifically (out of proportion for a narrow badge/error-parsing change already type-checked for real), but the `docker build --target builder` path itself is a genuine typecheck+build, not a manual read-through.
**Known limitations:**
- `SettingsPage.tsx:494` — email notification channel is explicitly disabled ("Coming soon — email dispatch is stubbed"); Slack/webhook channel is live.
- `MemoryPage.tsx` fetches directly via `apiFetch()` rather than a dedicated `useMemory.ts` hook — inconsistent with every other page's hook-per-resource pattern (all other 12 pages have one). Not broken, just a convention drift.
- `useAgents.ts:84,98` use raw `fetch()` for agent-config GET/PUT — likely a legitimate use of the documented "endpoint not yet in OpenAPI spec" escape hatch, not confirmed against the spec this session.
- `@ts-expect-error` count is 0 (TASK-049 fully closed).
