**Interfaces — B-024 (2026-07-24), `ToolsPage.tsx` reads the real `TestTool` result:** `handleTest` previously only checked whether the `POST /v1/gateway/tools/{toolId}/test` call threw — since B-023 made that endpoint always resolve with HTTP 200 and put the real result in the body (`{success, latency_ms, error}`), the button always showed green "OK" regardless of actual reachability. Fixed: `handleTest` now reads `result.success`, and on failure parses B-023's `"<reason>: <detail>"` `error` string (`classifyTestError`, splits on the first `:`) into one of `auth-failed`/`unreachable`/`misconfigured`/a generic `failed` fallback for anything unrecognized. A new `TEST_STATE_CONFIG` map gives each state a distinct badge (green `success`, amber `auth-failed`, red `unreachable`, gray `misconfigured` via a new `HelpCircle` icon, red `failed`) reusing the same color language as the page's existing `StatusBadge` (connected/degraded/disconnected) rather than inventing new colors. The full backend message is surfaced via the button's `title` tooltip (e.g. hovering a failed test shows "auth-failed: remote returned HTTP 401"). Auto-clear delay extended from 4s to 6s since there's now a specific reason worth reading. Scope was strictly `ToolsPage.tsx`'s `handleTest` + its status-display button — `useTools.ts`, `AddToolPanel`, delete/list flows, and every other page are untouched (confirmed via `git diff --stat`, one file changed).

**Interfaces — B-038 (2026-08-03), `PasteEventsPage.tsx` — first admin view over captured paste-detection events (B2):** new page at `/paste-events`, new sidebar entry under the `Operations` group (`Navigation.tsx`, `Copy` icon — already used elsewhere in this codebase, so guaranteed present in the installed `lucide-react` version without needing `npm install` to confirm). **Placement decided against Discover, with reasoning recorded rather than just picked:** `DiscoverPage.tsx` is a per-endpoint asset inventory (one row per machine, drill into a detail drawer); paste events are an org-wide time-series event log, structurally identical to `AuditPage.tsx`/`FinOpsPage.tsx` (filter bar + paginated table + a `time_bucket`-driven chart), not to Discover's shape — so this is a new sidebar item, not nested under Discover.
**UI:** filter bar (domain select from the same bundled 6-domain list as `eami-api`'s `KnownPasteDestinations`/the browser extension's `domains.js`, from/to date range defaulting to the last 30 days) drives both a stacked `recharts` `BarChart` (counts by domain per day — real data straight from `PasteEventsTimeSeries`, no client-side estimation needed, unlike `FinOpsPage.tsx`'s per-model proportional-distribution workaround) and a paginated table (timestamp, domain, length, a click-to-copy truncated hash cell mirroring `AuditPage.tsx`'s `HashCell`, OS user). Empty state is deliberately generic ("No paste events detected yet — events appear here once the browser extension is installed...") with **no mention of B-035 or any internal wiring gap** — that context belongs in `BACKLOG.md`/this file, not in production UI copy shown to an admin.
**New hook, `src/hooks/usePasteEvents.ts`:** `usePasteEvents`/`usePasteEventsTimeSeries`, both via `apiFetch` (not the generated typed `api.GET`, since these two routes aren't in `api/openapi.yaml` yet — see B-033's widened scope) — same escape-hatch convention `MemoryPage.tsx` used for its episode hooks before B-002 Brief 3 documented those routes.
**Verification:** real `tsc && vite build` via the `docker build --target builder -f eami-ui/Dockerfile .` path B-024 established (Node/npm still absent locally) — clean, zero type errors, confirming both new files and the `Navigation.tsx`/`router.tsx` edits compile correctly against the rest of the app. The already-running dev container (bind-mounted, Vite) served the new page/route with no compile error logged after a direct request. **No live interactive browser click-through performed** — same standing limitation as B1 (no safe browser-automation tool in this environment; see the elevated-shell/`taskkill` incident logged under B-036/`CONTEXT.md`) — manual verification steps below cover what a human should confirm.
**Manual verification steps (not yet run by a human as of this entry):** 1) log in, confirm "Paste Detection" appears in the sidebar under Operations and navigates to `/paste-events`; 2) confirm the domain filter, date range, and pagination controls all actually refetch (network tab shows a new `GET /v1/paste-events`/`.../timeseries` per change); 3) ~~confirm the chart renders with no data today for the real `Dev Org`~~ — **B-035 shipped 2026-08-04: real paste events now flow into this table, so this page should show live data for any org with actual browser-extension activity** — re-verify the chart/table populate correctly with real rows (zero code changes were needed here, as intended); 4) if the buffer is still empty, confirm that's because no real paste events have occurred yet, not because the pipeline is broken.
**Known limitations:** `api/openapi.yaml` gap noted above (B-033); no export/alerting integration, explicitly out of scope per this task's brief. ~~empty for the real `Dev Org` until B-035 ships~~ — **B-035 done, see `eami-api` section.**

**Interfaces — B-045 (2026-08-06), `ToolsPage.tsx` gains an Edit flow:** full writeup in `eami-api` section above (backend + credential-handling detail); this entry covers the frontend-specific pieces. New `EditToolPanel` (near-identical structure to the existing `AddToolPanel`, pre-filled from the already-in-hand `useTools()` list row — no new GET endpoint needed). `type`/`auth_type` render read-only with an explanatory note (not editable, matching the backend's own scope decision). The credential input always starts blank (the current value is never returned by any API response, by design) with "leave blank to keep the current value" placeholder copy — submitted only if the admin actually types something, so `credentials` is entirely omitted from the PATCH body otherwise, never sent as an explicit `{}`/`null`. New `useUpdateTool()` hook in `useTools.ts`, and a small generic extension to `apiFetch()` (`src/api/client.ts`): optional `{method, body}` params (previously GET-only), used here because the generated OpenAPI client has no typed call for the new undocumented `PATCH` route — confirmed backward compatible with `apiFetch`'s only two existing callers (`MemoryPage.tsx`, `usePasteEvents.ts`, both call it with no second argument, unaffected by the new optional param). **Verified:** real `tsc && vite build` via the established `docker build --target builder` path, clean, run twice (before and after a code-review-caught fix to the `mcp_args` whitespace-handling — see `eami-api` section for detail). **No live interactive browser click-through performed** — same standing limitation as other recent frontend work in this environment; the PATCH flow itself was live-verified end-to-end via direct API calls against the real deployed stack (see `eami-api` section's B-045 entry) rather than through a browser.

**Interfaces — B-046 (2026-08-06), `ToolsPage.tsx` gains an Action mappings editor:** new `ActionPathsEditor` component (dynamic add/remove rows of action name / HTTP method / path), rendered in both `AddToolPanel` and `EditToolPanel`, `rest_api`-only (hidden entirely for `mcp`/`database` tools, matching the existing `base_url` field's own conditional-render pattern). `rowsToActionPaths` converts the row array to the `{action: {path, method}}` shape the API expects, silently dropping any row with a blank action name or path (an in-progress blank row shouldn't become a stored mapping to `""`) — duplicate action names resolve last-row-wins, matching the backend's own documented behavior for the same case.
**A real TypeScript typing problem, solved without touching the generated schema:** `Tool`/`ToolCreate` are frozen types generated from `api/openapi.yaml` (Architect-EAMI-owned, and `action_paths` isn't documented there — same undocumented-route situation B-045's `ToolUpdate` already established a precedent for). Solved with local intersection types in `useTools.ts`: `ToolWithActions = Tool & {action_paths?: ...}` (for reading the field back out of a list/edit response) and `ToolCreateWithActions = ToolCreate & {action_paths?: ...}` (for the create body) — a wider type is structurally assignable wherever the narrower generated type is expected, so `api.POST`/`api.GET`'s existing typed calls still compile unchanged, and the extra field survives at runtime (`JSON.stringify` doesn't know or care about TS's static type narrowing). `ToolUpdate` (already local, from B-045) simply gained an `action_paths?` field directly, no intersection needed there.
**Edit-panel prefill, submit semantics matching `mcp_args`' established precedent:** `EditToolPanel`'s row state initializes from `tool.action_paths` (present on the list response since `eami-api`'s `toolToResp` now includes it). On submit, `action_paths` is **always** sent from current row state (never diffed against the original) when the tool is `rest_api` — the same "reflect current form state, not a diff" convention every other field in this panel already uses — which is also how an admin explicitly clears all mappings: empty the rows, submit, the resulting `{}` is the backend's documented "clear" signal.
**Reviewer + security subagent passes:** both automated subagent passes failed on a platform session-limit error (see `eami-gateway` section) — the frontend-specific surface (the intersection-type trick, row-editor logic) was covered by this session's own substituted manual review; no exploitable surface here regardless, since all real validation/security enforcement happens server-side (`eami-api`'s `validateActionPaths`, `eami-gateway`'s dispatch-time guards) — this component only shapes what gets submitted, never trusts what it receives.
**Verification:** real `tsc && vite build` via the established `docker build --target builder` path — clean, zero type errors, confirming the intersection-type approach actually compiles against the real generated schema (not just plausible in isolation). **No live interactive browser click-through performed** — same standing limitation as B-045/other recent frontend work in this environment; the Action-mappings UI's actual request/response shape was proven correct indirectly, via the live end-to-end API+gateway verification in the `eami-gateway`/`eami-api` sections above (same JSON shape the UI constructs and consumes), not through a browser session.
**Known limitations:** no client-side warning for duplicate action names entered in the editor (silently last-row-wins on submit, matching the backend); no path-format hint/validation in the UI itself before submit (a full-URL paste-in is caught server-side with a clear 400, surfaced via the existing generic "Failed to add/update tool" toast — not a field-level inline error, consistent with every other validation failure in this form today).

**Interfaces — B-055 (2026-08-10), `SetupWizardPage.tsx` — the first-boot setup wizard (full backend design in `eami-api` section above):** new top-level `/setup` route (`router.tsx`, outside `AppShell` like `/login` — no auth guard applies, correctly, since no user exists yet). On mount, calls `GET /v1/setup/status`; an already-configured appliance redirects straight to `/login` via `<Navigate replace>` before rendering any form (closes the "wizard is unreachable after setup" requirement). Otherwise a two-step form matching `LoginPage.tsx`'s existing structure/Tailwind conventions and `react-hook-form`+`zod` pattern: step 1 is a single setup-token field, checked against the new read-only `POST /v1/setup/token/validate` (does not consume the token); step 2 (org name, admin email, admin password + confirm) only renders after step 1 succeeds, and submits everything together to `POST /v1/setup/bootstrap`. Success shows a confirmation with a link to `/login` — deliberately no auto-login, since AC2's "verified by actually logging in" means a real subsequent login, not an implicit session.
**A real bug caught in this session's own manual re-read, before the automated code-review pass even ran:** `onSubmitBootstrap`'s original catch block set the failure message on `bootstrapForm`'s error state, then immediately called `setStage('token')` — which unmounts the form that error was set on, so the admin would silently bounce back to the token screen with no visible reason why. Fixed by setting it on `tokenForm` instead when the failure really is token-related.
**A second issue, found by the code-review pass, fixed alongside the first:** *every* bootstrap failure forced the admin back to re-enter the 64-character console token, even a merely fixable input error — needlessly punishing, and it interacted badly with a real server/client validation mismatch also found (see `eami-api` section: the server's email regex was stricter than this page's zod `.email()` check, so `admin@localhost` was accepted here but 400'd on the server). Now `apiFetch` throws a new status-carrying `ApiFetchError` (`client.ts`), and `onSubmitBootstrap` only returns to the token gate for a `401` (dead token) or `409` (appliance configured by someone else) — every other failure keeps the admin on the form with their already-typed org/admin details intact, and shows the error there instead.
**`apiFetch` (`src/api/client.ts`) gained error-body surfacing (`ApiFetchError extends Error`, carries `status` alongside `message`):** previously threw a generic `HTTP ${status}`; now best-effort-parses the server's `{code, message}` body (`ErrorResponse`, matches every `eami-api` handler's `writeError` convention). Backward compatible — falls back to the old message shape for non-JSON bodies, still `instanceof Error`, and every existing `apiFetch` caller (`MemoryPage.tsx`, `usePasteEvents.ts`, `useTools.ts`) only ever checked `catch()` truthiness, never the exact string or type.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` (`tsc && vite build`), both clean — run again after every fix above, not just once before review. Node/npm were newly installed this session (previously absent, per `CLAUDE.md`'s toolchain note). Live-verified against a real isolated stack, twice (before and after the fixes) — see the `eami-api` section's B-055 entry for the full end-to-end proof (token → validate → bootstrap → real login, second pass using a dot-less-domain email to prove the validation-mismatch fix); the served `/setup` route and its resolved `SetupWizardPage` component were confirmed directly over HTTP against the real running `eami-ui` dev server. **No interactive browser click-through** — browser automation was unavailable this session (declined Claude-in-Chrome install); disclosed, not glossed over.

**Interfaces — B-058 (2026-08-12), `WorkflowsPage.tsx` — new page for Multi-Hop Workflows Brief 1 (full backend + cross-org-guard design in `eami-api` section above; this entry covers the frontend-specific pieces):** new route `/gateway/workflows`, new sidebar entry (`Navigation.tsx`, `Workflow` icon from `lucide-react`, positioned after Tools within the Gateway group). Structure mirrors `ToolsPage.tsx` exactly: table + slide-out `AddWorkflowPanel`/`EditWorkflowPanel` drawers + `ConfirmDialog` for delete. New `StepsEditor` component reuses `ActionPathsEditor`'s reorderable-rows shape (`ToolsPage.tsx`) — add/remove rows — extended with explicit up/down move buttons, since a workflow step's order is load-bearing (unlike `action_paths`' rows, which have no inherent order). Each step row is a `<select>` populated from the existing `useTools()` hook (already fetches this org's connectors — no new data source needed) plus an action-name text input.
**Dangling-connector UX:** a step whose `gateway_tool_id` comes back empty from the API (its connector was deleted, `ON DELETE SET NULL`) renders with a red warning icon and a "connector removed -- select a new one" placeholder in the dropdown, rather than silently defaulting to a blank/wrong selection.
**New `useWorkflows.ts` hook** — mirrors `useTools.ts`'s exact shape (`useWorkflows`/`useWorkflow(id)`/`useCreateWorkflow`/`useUpdateWorkflow`/`useDeleteWorkflow`), all via `apiFetch()` (routes undocumented in `openapi.yaml`, same convention as `useUpdateTool`), query key `['workflows']` invalidated wholesale on mutation.
**A real bug found by the mandatory code-review pass, fixed:** the step-row-to-request-body conversion (`rowsToSteps`) originally silently filtered out any incomplete row — including a row explicitly flagged as having a deleted connector, i.e. exactly the row an admin is supposed to be able to see and fix per the dangling-connector UX above. Editing an unrelated field (renaming the workflow, reordering a different step) without touching that broken row would silently delete it on submit instead. Fixed: renamed to `validateAndConvertRows`, now blocks submission with a clear "Step N is incomplete -- select a connector and an action, or remove it" error instead of ever silently dropping a row.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, clean, run twice (before and after the code-review fix). Live-verified against the real running stack: created/edited/deleted a real workflow via direct API calls exercising the same handler code the page's `apiFetch()` calls trigger (see `eami-api` section's B-058 entry for the full transcript); confirmed via `curl` that `GET /gateway/workflows` resolves (`200`) against the real running Vite dev server, and that the dev server's hot-module-reload picked up the new page/route with no compile error logged. **No interactive browser click-through performed** — same standing limitation as every other recent `eami-ui` brief in this environment (browser automation unavailable/declined); the actual request/response shapes the page constructs and consumes were proven correct via the direct API verification instead, not through a browser session.
**Known limitations:** no automated frontend test exists for `validateAndConvertRows`/`StepsEditor` (matches this repo's pre-existing 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline, `BACKLOG.md` B-006, not a new gap); `AddWorkflowPanel`/`EditWorkflowPanel` are near-duplicate components, matching `AddToolPanel`/`EditToolPanel`'s own pre-existing precedent (disclosed code-review finding, not fixed, consistent with existing convention).

**Interfaces — B-060 (2026-08-12), `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s step "action" field becomes a real picker instead of free text.** Explicitly adjacent to Thread B (feeds its UX), not itself part of Thread B's epic — per the MCP Discovery investigation's own distinction (D8) between the workflow-chaining epic and MCP-protocol/connector-definition work that happens to feed it. Only file changed. `StepsEditor`'s `tools` was retyped from `any` to `ToolWithActions[]` (mirrors `ToolsPage.tsx`'s own identical pattern), giving real typing for `action_paths` (B-046) — already fetched by the existing `useTools()` call, no new endpoint or hook needed, confirming D8's own finding directly before building against it. Per row: if the selected connector's `action_paths` has ≥1 key, a real `<select>` of those action names replaces the `<input>`; a connector with none (`ai_provider`/`mcp`/`database`, or a `rest_api` tool with no mappings yet) falls back to free text completely unchanged, matching `action_paths`' own existing "unmapped falls back to base_url" convention with zero special-casing. A saved action no longer among the connector's current `action_paths` keys is appended as an extra dropdown option rather than dropped, so loading a workflow never silently mutates saved data. The connector `<select>`'s `onChange` now unconditionally resets `action: ''` in the same state update as the connector change — one unbranched code path, so a stale action from a previously selected connector can never survive a switch.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, clean. **Live-verified against the real running stack** (no interactive browser click-through — same standing limitation this environment has carried through every `eami-ui` brief, disclosed, and this machine's documented Chrome/Edge automation incident risk makes that the correct call again, not a shortcut): the real `b059-live-verify` workflow's two steps were switched to `b046-live-verify` (real `action_paths`: `echo_get`/`echo_post`, confirmed via `GET /v1/gateway/tools`) and `claude` (`ai_provider`, no `action_paths`) via real `PATCH`/`GET` calls exercising the identical save-and-reload data contract `handleSubmit`→`update.mutateAsync`→`useWorkflow` goes through — a dropdown-selected value (`echo_get`) and a free-text value (`messages`) persisted and reloaded byte-identical, proving the backend can't and doesn't distinguish which control produced the value. The connector-switch reset was verified by direct inspection of the committed, single unconditional `onChange` handler rather than a browser trace, disclosed as such.
**Known limitations:** same 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline as the rest of `eami-ui` (`BACKLOG.md` B-006); no live browser click-through, per the standing limitation above.

**Fix — B-062 (2026-08-12), Vite dev server was serving stale code after edits (bind-mount watch failure), reported live against B-060's dropdown:** user reported the `Workflows` step editor's action field for `b046-live-verify` (a connector with real `action_paths`) was still rendering as plain text, not B-060's dropdown, and asked to rule out a stale Docker image first (same class as B-035/B-055) before assuming a code bug. **Investigation:** the running `eami-ui` image genuinely *was* stale (built ~6 hours before B-060's commit) — but `docker-compose.yml` bind-mounts `./eami-ui/src:/app/src` for hot reload, and `md5sum` confirmed the container's `WorkflowsPage.tsx` was already byte-identical to the committed B-060 code *before* any rebuild — so image staleness could not actually explain this symptom. Fetching the Vite dev server's actually-served module directly (`curl http://127.0.0.1:5173/src/pages/gateway/WorkflowsPage.tsx`) proved the real cause: the served module was pre-B-060 code (no dropdown logic at all), despite the on-disk file being current. **Root cause:** Vite's default `chokidar` FS-event watcher doesn't reliably see writes made from the Windows host into this container's bind-mounted `src/` (Docker Desktop's host↔Linux filesystem bridge doesn't propagate inotify events across that boundary on Windows) — so the dev server's module cache never invalidated after B-060 was written, and kept serving the old build indefinitely until the process itself restarted.
**Fix:** `eami-ui/vite.config.ts` — `server.watch.usePolling: true` (300ms interval), so Vite periodically re-stats watched files instead of relying on FS events. Required an image rebuild (`vite.config.ts` isn't inside the bind-mounted path, unlike `.tsx` source edits).
**Verified live:** restarting the container alone (before the fix) immediately started serving B-060's real code, confirming the diagnosis. After rebuilding with the fix, made a real trivial edit to `WorkflowsPage.tsx` and confirmed via direct curl to the dev server that it was picked up and served within ~2s **with no container restart** — proving the fix closes the recurrence risk, not just this one instance. Edit reverted afterward, confirmed zero residual diff. `npm run type-check` clean.
**Known limitation:** polling adds a small constant CPU/disk-stat cost, acceptable for a local dev-only server (`target: development`; production builds don't run a dev server or watch files at all).

**Interfaces — B-064 (2026-08-13), `WorkflowsPage.tsx` gains a real extraction-expression UI:** B-063 built the backend mechanism for a step's parameter to be extracted from a prior step's real recorded result, but shipped with zero UI. Two findings, re-verified directly against current code before building, changed what "minimal but real" meant: (1) no static-value step UI existed at all (`grep` confirmed zero "params" references anywhere in this file) — B-059's `PUT/GET .../workflow-steps/{stepId}/params` had only ever been driven by direct API calls, so this brief's real scope also had to include the first-ever static-param editor, a precondition for "static vs. extracted" to be a meaningful per-param choice; (2) no path exists today for fetching a prior step's last real run result (`workflow_run_steps`/`workflow_runs` are gateway-owned tables with zero references anywhere in `eami-api`, and `eami-ui` has no network path to `eami-gateway` at all — `vite.config.ts`'s only proxy target is `eami-api`, `docker-compose.yml`'s `VITE_GATEWAY_URL` is dead config, never referenced in any frontend source file). **Decision, confirmed with the user before building:** always use a plain-text/dropdown fallback, never attempt a live sample/preview — doesn't cross this brief's file scope (the safe choice, not an expansion); logged as a natural future brief, likely worth pairing with a runs-history view.
**`useWorkflows.ts`:** `WorkflowStep` gained `id`/`input_mapping`; Create/Update step-input shapes gained optional `id`/`input_mapping` (purely additive, matches B-063's own additive backend contract). New `useWorkflowStepParams`/`useSetWorkflowStepParams`, B-059's endpoint's first-ever UI consumer.
**`StepRow` gained `localId`** (client-generated `crypto.randomUUID()`, or the step's own real id once loaded) — the frontend-local analog of the exact "reference by stable id, not array position" problem B-063 already solved server-side, needed because a mid-edit-session reorder must not misdirect a reference either. `ParamRow` unifies static and extracted params into one per-step list with a mode toggle; an extract row's source `<select>` is built from `rows.slice(0, i).filter(r => r.id)` (label `"Step N: <connector> / <action>"`, never a raw UUID) — **eligibility requires both an earlier array position AND a real, already-persisted backend id**, since a step just added in the current session has no id yet for a later step to reference (real, disclosed constraint — AC1's actual save-then-reopen flow has zero friction from it).
**AC3, reorder/removal invalidation:** every row/param mutation routes through a single `commit()` that always re-runs new `revalidateExtractionRefs` — an extract row whose source is no longer earlier-and-real-id'd gets flagged `invalid` (visible red border + message), **never silently cleared or left looking valid**, mirroring the existing `toolDeleted` step-level pattern and `validateAndConvertRows`' established "block with a clear message, never silently drop" precedent, now extended to param rows.
**Static params solved with zero "save, reopen" friction** (a design choice, not in the plan's first draft): `saveStaticParams` fires B-059's PUT for every step with static params using the ids the SAME save's response just returned — so a brand-new step's own static params can be set in the exact submit that creates it. Only the extraction-*source* eligibility constraint above remains (can't extract from a sibling created in the identical submit, since `input_mapping` must be embedded in the request before the response is known) — minor, disclosed, blocks no stated AC.
**`EditWorkflowPanel`'s row-init:** extract rows seed synchronously from the loaded workflow's `input_mapping` (already embedded); static rows seed via `useQueries` (TanStack Query's multi-query primitive, one per already-real-id'd step) — `rows` only initializes once the workflow *and* every step's static params have loaded.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean, run locally — Node/npm confirmed actually installed on this machine now, superseding `CLAUDE.md`'s prior "not installed" toolchain note. No dedicated reviewer/security subagent pass — this brief's own standing rules didn't mandate one (frontend-only, zero backend/SQL/extraction-logic surface touched, matching B-060's identical no-review precedent for a comparably-scoped brief) — diff self-reviewed directly before commit.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt fresh, served-module content confirmed via direct `curl` before proceeding (the B-062 lesson — never assume a rebuild landed):** browser automation remains unavailable in this environment (same standing limitation every prior `eami-ui` brief has disclosed) — "configured entirely through the UI" was verified by sending the exact request sequence and body shapes the UI's own code generates for a real simulated admin flow, not literal clicks: add step 1 with no params → reopen → add a static param on step 1 + a new step 2 extracting from step 1 → submit → reload. **AC1**: full round trip confirmed, step 1's static param and step 2's `input_mapping` (referencing step 1's real, preserved id) both reloaded byte-identical. **AC4**: the resulting workflow, run for real via a real agent JWT, genuinely dispatched step 2 carrying the value extracted from step 1's real recorded response — traced through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` column, the same evidence B-063 used. **AC2/AC3** verified by direct code trace, disclosed as such (matches B-060's own identical precedent for its connector-switch-reset claim) — AC3 in particular is a pure client-side state transition with no network round-trip to observe live at all. Test workflow deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Known limitations:** no live sample/preview of a prior step's real result (finding 2 above — needs a new backend read endpoint, a natural separate future brief); same 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline as the rest of `eami-ui` (`BACKLOG.md` B-006); no live browser click-through, per the standing limitation above.

**Interfaces — B-065 (2026-08-13), `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s step editor restyled into Freshservice-pattern cards:** presentation only — `useWorkflows.ts` confirmed zero diff (`git diff --stat`), no backend/logic touched. Confirmed before building: no graph-canvas library needed, a vertical stack of step cards with a connecting chevron reads as a clear sequence without node/port machinery, matching the standing v1-linear-only scope.
**The one real structural change:** a step's connector/action pickers and its full B-064 parameter editor, previously inline in one form row (B-064's own accordion), moved into a new, second, layered slide-out panel (`StepConfigPanel` — higher z-index + its own backdrop over the workflow panel), opened via a "Configure" button on each step's summary card. Every underlying handler (`moveRow`, `removeRow`, `validateAndConvertRows`, `revalidateExtractionRefs`, `updateParam`/`addParam`/`removeParam`, `saveStaticParams`, `commit`) is reused completely unchanged; `StepConfigPanel` holds zero state/validation logic of its own, purely a view over the same shared `rows` state.
**New `summarizeParams`** drives each card's always-accurate one-line summary (AC2), computed from already-loaded local state: "No parameters configured", a specific "Extracting `x` from Step N" sentence for a single extraction, or a count otherwise. Existing `toolDeleted`/invalid-extraction warnings surface directly on the card (red border + message), not hidden behind Configure.
**Status prominence + Activate action (AC4), a genuine small new capability, not just a restyle:** `EditWorkflowPanel` gained a prominent status badge plus a real "Activate" button when `draft`, firing an immediate standalone `PATCH {status: 'active'}` that touches nothing else — flipping status no longer requires a full steps save. The existing status dropdown is untouched and still works.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. No dedicated reviewer/security pass, matching B-064's own precedent (diff confined to `WorkflowsPage.tsx`, no backend/SQL/extraction-logic surface touched).
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack, rebuilt fresh, served-module content confirmed via direct `curl` before proceeding:** browser automation still unavailable in this environment (same standing limitation) — verification reused B-064's exact request-sequence-equivalence method: create with step 1 (draft) → reopen → configure step 1's static param + add step 2 extracting from step 1 (the new panels' equivalent request shapes) → save → click Activate → reload. **Confirmed live:** step 1's id/static param and step 2's `input_mapping` all persisted correctly on reload; the standalone Activate PATCH flipped `status` to `active` while leaving both steps byte-identical (proving the status-only design); the resulting workflow, run for real via a real agent JWT, genuinely dispatched step 2 carrying the value extracted from step 1's real recorded response, traced through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` column, the same evidence B-063/B-064 used. **Disclosed, not overclaimed:** this proves the data/API side is unaffected by the redesign but cannot itself observe the new card layout rendering — the dev-server-served-module `curl` check (confirming `StepConfigPanel`/`summarizeParams` are the code actually served) is the closest available proof short of a rendered-pixel check. Test workflow deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Known limitations:** same as B-064 (no live sample/preview, 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline, no live browser click-through); drag-to-reorder not built (up/down arrows preserved, restyled — explicitly a nice-to-have, not required, per the brief).

**Interfaces — B-066 (2026-08-13), Workflow Canvas Brief 1: `@xyflow/react` integration + read-only rendering, a new epic distinct from Thread B:** proves the library integrates cleanly and renders a real workflow's steps as ordered canvas nodes, before any editing/interactivity. B-065's card UI remains the only editor. Corrected a stated premise before building on it: this project's Tailwind *does* have a real JIT compiler (`postcss.config.js`/`tailwind.config.ts` confirmed) — doesn't change the outcome (React Flow ships its own precompiled CSS regardless) but worth verifying rather than assuming.
**`WorkflowsPage.tsx`, purely additive (19 lines, confirmed via `git diff --stat`):** `ParamRow`/`StepRow`/`summarizeParams` gained `export` — zero logic touched, every existing component byte-for-byte untouched. One new `Waypoints` icon link per table row to a new route.
**New `WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx`:** reuses `EditWorkflowPanel`'s exact data-fetching pattern (`useWorkflow` + per-step static-params `useQueries`) and the *imported* `summarizeParams` (not reimplemented) to drive a new `WorkflowStepNode` custom node styled identically to B-065's cards. Deterministic vertical stack from `step_order`, no position persisted. Explicitly, structurally read-only (`nodesDraggable`/`nodesConnectable`/`elementsSelectable` all `false`, every `Handle` `isConnectable={false}`) — no code path can mutate anything.
**Real, measured bundle-weight data:** `npm run build` before/after — JS 933.80 kB → 1,110.92 kB minified (256.72 kB → 313.67 kB gzipped, **+~57 kB gzipped**), CSS +~16 kB.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean first try — re-confirms the investigation's library choice against actual integration. No dedicated reviewer/security pass (presentation-only, no backend/SQL surface).
**Live-verified, honestly split between checkable and not, per this brief's own kickoff-line instruction:** rebuilt/redeployed (a real image rebuild was required — new `package.json` dependency, `node_modules` isn't bind-mounted); served-module `curl` confirmed the new code is real and wired; real API calls confirmed correct data for an existing 2-step workflow (`b063-live-verify`, including its real `input_mapping`) and a temporary real 5-step workflow made specifically for AC4's "many steps" case (deleted afterward); a real spot-check `PATCH` confirmed zero regression to B-065's edit flow. **AC4's "zero steps" case is defensively coded (`EmptyState`) but not exercisable live** — `validateWorkflowSteps` has required ≥1 step since B-058, so a truly empty workflow isn't reachable through normal API use; disclosed, not glossed over. **AC1/AC2 are genuinely visual claims ("renders correctly," "no visual/CSS conflict... side by side") this environment's standing no-browser-automation limitation cannot verify directly** — data and served-code correctness are both confirmed live, the actual rendered pixels were not seen by this session; stated plainly rather than overclaimed, the user is invited to look themselves.
**Known limitations:** no editing/interactivity (Brief 2+); no connection-validation logic yet (nothing editable, nothing to validate); layout not persisted; cards remain the primary/only editor.

**Interfaces — B-067 (2026-08-13), Workflow Canvas Brief 2: interactivity + draw-time connection validation:** click a node to configure it (`StepConfigPanel`, B-065, reused verbatim, zero changes to its own code), add/remove nodes and draw connections locally, real draw-time validation (one-in/one-out + cycle rejection) per the investigation's A.2 finding. Explicitly not the save-time full-graph validation layer (Brief 3) — the investigation was explicit draw-time alone is not sufficient, and this brief never lets a structurally-broken graph reach the backend by simply never persisting structure at all yet.
**A critical finding, re-verified against actual current code before building:** the brief's own assumption that clicking a node "already has real, working persistence" turned out false on inspection — `StepConfigPanel`'s param mutators only ever updated local state in every existing caller; the real network write only ever fired from the *enclosing panel's* main Save submit, which the canvas page doesn't have. **Consequent design fork, resolved and disclosed:** static params have their own independent endpoint (safe to save immediately) — `input_mapping` (extraction) does not, it can only ever be written as part of a full workflow PATCH that also carries structure, which this brief is forbidden from calling. Resolution: static edits persist for real on config-panel close; extraction edits stay local-only, surfaced via the same unsaved-changes banner as structural edits.
**`WorkflowsPage.tsx`:** two more additive `export`s (`StepConfigPanel`, `revalidateExtractionRefs`) — 2 lines changed total, confirmed via `git diff --stat`.
**`WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx` becomes stateful:** `closeConfigPanel` compares a step's static-params snapshot before/after the panel was open, firing a real PUT only if it actually changed. `addNode`/`removeNode` mutate only local state (grep-confirmed zero `apiFetch`/`mutateAsync` calls). `isValidConnection` implements React Flow's own documented pattern exactly (self-loop/one-out/one-in/`getOutgoers`-based cycle rejection), each with a specific toast, not a silent no-op.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. No dedicated reviewer/security pass (presentation-only, no backend/SQL surface).
**Live-verified, per this brief's own stated method:** served-module `curl` confirmed the real validation/persistence-split code is deployed. **AC1**: a real static-param PUT against the real `b063-live-verify` workflow persisted (confirmed via `GET`) with the workflow's own `updated_at` completely unaffected (proving the write never touches `workflows`/`workflow_steps`) — reverted afterward. **AC2/AC3**: verified by direct code inspection of the four validation checks plus confirming that's the exact code served — no test suite exists to run automatically, and a literal draw-and-reject browser interaction remains unavailable in this environment. **AC4**: verified by direct code inspection — the three structural-mutation functions grep-confirmed to contain zero backend-call sites, a precise structural proof, not a substitute for watching live network traffic. **AC5**: `git diff --stat` confirms exactly 2 lines changed in `WorkflowsPage.tsx`.
**Known limitations:** no structural persistence yet (Brief 3); no save-time full-graph validation yet (Brief 3 — this brief's draw-time layer alone is not sufficient, by the investigation's own explicit finding); cards remain the only way to persist structure or extraction config.

**Interfaces — B-068 (2026-08-13), Workflow Canvas Brief 3: save-time validation, real structural persistence — closes the epic's own two-layer requirement:** draw-time (B-067) alone can't catch a deletion leaving a broken graph; this brief adds the save-time full-graph validation backstop, then wires the canvas to the same `UpdateWorkflow` PATCH `EditWorkflowPanel`'s cards already use, unmodified. First brief in this epic where the canvas actually writes to the backend.
**`WorkflowsPage.tsx`:** two more additive `export`s (`validateAndConvertRows`, `saveStaticParams`) — confirmed via `git diff --stat`, zero logic touched.
**New `validateGraph(rows, edges)`:** re-derives graph validity fresh from current state, trusting nothing from draw time — requires exactly one start (zero incoming), one end (zero outgoing), no node with more than one edge in or out, and a walk from the start visiting every node exactly once (the only shape the backend's flat, ordered `steps` array can represent). Every rejection names the specific offending step(s) by number, per CONTEXT.md's Workflow Canvas ease-of-use principle — never a generic "invalid graph."
**`handleSave`:** `validateGraph` → reorder `rows` to the derived order → `revalidateExtractionRefs` on the *reordered* array (order changes what counts as "earlier") → the imported `validateAndConvertRows` → any error surfaces as a persistent `saveError` banner (distinct from the transient toast, never auto-clears), save aborted before any network call → on success, the real `mutateAsync` PATCH, then `saveStaticParams`, then local state rebuilt from the confirmed persisted response. The extraction-persistence gap B-067 left open closes itself here with zero new logic: once the graph is correctly ordered, the existing `validateAndConvertRows` builds `input_mapping` from the reordered rows exactly as it always has.
**A real defensive gap found and closed during the mandatory reviewer pass:** the original reachability check (`order.length < rows.length`) only guarded under-coverage — an edge targeting an id not present in `rows` (not reachable via any current UI path, but not structurally prevented by the function's own types either) could let a corrupted `order` slip through as `error: null`, crashing `handleSave` outside its `try/catch`. Hardened: the walk now only ever advances onto a real row id, and the length check is `!==` (impossible to overshoot, not just unlikely to).
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean.
**Reviewer + security subagent pass, mandatory per this brief's own standing rules (unlike B-064–067):** `validateGraph` independently re-traced against every malformed/valid topology in scope, correct except the phantom-edge gap above (now fixed); confirmed no path reaches `mutateAsync` without a validated order first; confirmed `eami-api/internal/api/workflows.go` genuinely unmodified. Traced the real trust boundary in code: the backend deliberately doesn't validate `input_mapping.from_step` ordering/existence at write time, but this is safe by construction — `eami-gateway`'s `resolveParams` is scoped per-run to only that run's already-completed steps, so a forward or cross-org reference simply isn't found and fails the run cleanly rather than resolving to anything. Cross-org step-id adoption independently confirmed impossible via `resolveStepIDs`'s org-scoped existing-steps map. No vulnerability found.
**Live-verified end-to-end, rebuilt fresh, served-module `curl` confirmed, at B-063's original full rigor standard (not the lighter substitute used since B-064):** created a real throwaway 2-step workflow, sent the exact request sequence `handleSave` generates for a real canvas save. **AC1/AC2**: confirmed byte-identical on reload; `validateGraph` extracted verbatim from the served module and *executed* (not just read) against six concrete topologies (valid chain, valid chain in reverse array order, the brief's own "deleted connecting edge" example, an over-connected node, a pure cycle, two competing starts) — every rejection named the correct step(s), every acceptance produced the correct order. Backend-side defense in depth confirmed directly: a structurally incomplete step submitted straight to the PATCH endpoint got a real `400`, stored workflow unaffected. **AC3, full B-063-standard proof**: real extraction configured, saved, `input_mapping` confirmed persisted via `GET`, workflow run for real via a real agent JWT, extracted value traced through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` column in Postgres — step 1's real dispatched value reappeared as step 2's real dispatched param, genuinely extracted at run time. **AC4**: `EditWorkflowPanel`'s and `WorkflowCanvasPage`'s row-init blocks confirmed byte-identical in construction from the same `GetWorkflow` response. **AC5**: `git diff --stat` confirms `WorkflowsPage.tsx` stays to the two new `export`s. Test workflow, its runs, and scratch token files all deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
**Known limitations:** cards remain the primary editor (full cutover not yet scoped); no free node dragging/position persistence; still linear-chain-only, matching Thread B's own current backend scope (no branching).

**Epic discontinued — B-069 (2026-08-13), Workflow Canvas removed, code and this entry both kept as history:** real hands-on browser testing (the first time any human, rather than this environment's code-inspection/structural-proof substitute, actually clicked through the canvas) found multiple genuinely broken interactions the B-066/067/068 entries above did not catch — edge/node keyboard-deletion (`onDelete`/`onNodesDelete`/`onEdgesDelete` were never wired to `<ReactFlow>` at all, and `deleteKeyCode` defaulted to `'Backspace'`-only, missing Windows' separate `Delete` key) and step-configuration dropdowns not responding when opened from the canvas (root cause never found despite exhaustive tracing against the actual installed `@xyflow/react`/`@xyflow/system` source, byte-identical prop-wiring confirmed against the proven-working card editor). `WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx` deleted, its route removed, `@xyflow/react` uninstalled for real (bundle size confirmed to return to the exact pre-B-066 baseline: 933.80 kB / 256.72 kB gzipped), the 7 additive `export`s B-066/067/068 added to `WorkflowsPage.tsx` reverted to file-private (none of the underlying functions/types deleted — all still power the card editor). B-065's card editor was never broken, never depended on the canvas, and is the sole editor again — re-verified end-to-end (create, static param, real extraction, save, real run, `workflow_run_steps.result` trace) with zero regression. Full reasoning also recorded in `BACKLOG.md`'s Workflow Canvas epic header. The "Workflow Canvas ease-of-use principle" in `CONTEXT.md` is left in place for any future decision to revisit a visual canvas.

**Fix — B-080 (2026-08-21), `NodesPage.tsx` no longer claims live "Serf mesh" telemetry it doesn't have — a systematic UI-to-backend interactivity audit ("Dead Clicks Audit," triggered by a user-reported dead click on the Tools page, later logged as B-079) found the Nodes page's subtitle ("Serf mesh cluster -- refreshes every 15 s"), empty state, and delete-confirm copy all implied live multi-node infrastructure telemetry that nothing in the codebase produces.** Re-verified directly before touching anything, not assumed from the audit alone: `eami-api/internal/store/nodes.sql.go` has only `ListNodes`/`DeleteNode` (SELECT/DELETE) — zero INSERT/UPSERT anywhere; a repo-wide grep for `Serf`/`serf` across `eami-gateway` and `eami-api` returns zero hits (not partially built, entirely absent); the only writer to `gateway_nodes` in the whole repo is `scripts/seed-db.sh:96`'s static demo `INSERT`. Independently corroborated by the already-open **B-043**, which found the identical false "broadcast to all nodes via Serf" claim in `schema.sql:168`'s comment for a *different* feature (JWT-revocation propagation) — this codebase has a pattern of aspirational Serf claims with zero implementation behind them, not a one-off. One correction to the audit's own finding: `useNodes.ts` does have a real `refetchInterval: 15_000` — the page genuinely re-polls `GET /v1/gateway/nodes` every 15s, so "refreshes every 15s" is mechanically true; what's false is "Serf mesh cluster," which implies that repolling surfaces live infrastructure state, when it can only ever return the same static seed rows today.
**Framing decision, confirmed with the user before building:** honest-but-functional, not a "coming soon" empty state. Reasoning: (1) this brief's own file-scope restriction (copy-only, no backend/schema changes) makes a coming-soon state's implied condition — clearing seed data from customer-facing environments — impossible to satisfy here, and moot anyway, since `CONTEXT.md` confirms no customer-facing environment exists yet; (2) the Refresh/Delete plumbing is genuinely real and correctly wired to a real table (confirmed live below) — hiding the whole page behind an empty state would misrepresent the working half downward, trading one dishonest claim for a different one.
**Scope, copy-only, three strings changed in `NodesPage.tsx`:** the `PageHeader` subtitle now reads "Nodes registered in gateway_nodes -- no gateway process registers itself automatically yet"; the empty-state description ("Start an eami-gateway instance to register it in the Serf cluster," which implied the current `eami-gateway` binary already self-registers — it doesn't) now reads "No gateway nodes are registered. Automatic self-registration isn't built yet -- rows here are added manually."; the delete `ConfirmDialog` description (previously implied real failover behavior — "Traffic will failover to remaining nodes") now reads "This deletes the row from gateway_nodes. There is no automatic traffic failover -- the node process itself will not be stopped." No component logic, hooks, or backend code touched — confirmed via `git diff --stat`, one file, three string literals.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. **Live-verified against the real running stack, served-module `curl`'d first per the B-062 lesson** (never assume an edit landed without checking): confirmed zero remaining `Serf` occurrences in the actually-served module, all three new strings present verbatim. Then exercised the real plumbing this brief left untouched, to prove no regression: logged in via a real admin JWT, `GET /v1/gateway/nodes` confirmed real (200, real query); inserted one throwaway test row directly via `psql` (data only, no schema/code change) to have something to delete; `GET` confirmed the row visible, `DELETE /v1/gateway/nodes/{id}` returned a real `204`, and a follow-up `GET` confirmed the row gone. Test row was the only state touched, cleaned up as part of the same verification.
**Known limitation, disclosed, out of scope per the brief:** the page still shows whatever real rows exist in `gateway_nodes` — today, none for the real `Dev Org` (the seed-data row lives only in `scripts/seed-db.sh`'s demo data, not inserted by default) — with no code path anywhere that registers a real gateway node. Building that (real self-registration/heartbeat, real Serf gossip or an equivalent) is separate future work, tied to the already-open **B-043**, not this brief's job.

**Fix — B-086 (2026-08-22), `PoliciesPage.tsx`'s decorative drag-handle icon replaced with a real, working reorder control — full backend transaction fix in `eami-api` section above (this entry covers the frontend-specific pieces).** The Dead Clicks Audit found the `GripVertical` icon (`:341-343`) implied drag-to-reorder but was wired to nothing.
**A real bug found before the UI wiring could even be proven, not assumed from the audit's "working, tested endpoint" description:** `useReorderPolicies()` (`usePolicies.ts`) sent `{order: [...]}`, but the real handler expects `{policy_ids: [...]}` — proven live, the real running endpoint 400'd on the hook's exact body. Root cause: `api/openapi.yaml` itself documents `order`, not `policy_ids` — the hook, built against the generated typed client, had faithfully followed a spec that doesn't match the real implementation (spec correction logged separately, `openapi.yaml` is Architect-EAMI-owned, see `BACKLOG.md` B-089). **Fixed:** `useReorderPolicies()` now calls `apiFetch()` directly with the real `policy_ids` key instead of the generated `api.POST` client, since the generated types would keep producing the same broken body until the spec itself is corrected.
**Design choice, confirmed with the user before building: up/down move buttons, not drag-and-drop.** No drag-and-drop library exists anywhere in `eami-ui/src` (confirmed by the audit and re-confirmed here), and this codebase already has a real, working precedent for exactly this reordering problem without one — `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s `StepsEditor` (B-065). Adding a DnD library was explicitly avoided as new, risky surface area, consistent with this codebase's own B-069 history (a real DnD library shipped, then fully reverted after live testing found broken interactions no code review had caught).
**`PoliciesPage.tsx`:** `GripVertical` replaced with `ChevronUp`/`ChevronDown` buttons per row, boundary-disabled (top row can't move up, bottom can't move down) and disabled while a reorder is in flight. `movePolicy` always submits the **full** org policy-id list in the new order (not just the two swapped ids) — required, since the endpoint renumbers priority sequentially from array position for every id it's given; a partial submission would leave untouched policies' priorities stale and risking collisions. A small error banner surfaces if a reorder mutation fails.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. Live-verified against the real running stack, served-module `curl`'d first (the B-062 lesson): confirmed the fixed `policy_ids` key and the `movePolicy`/`ChevronUp`/`ChevronDown` wiring are genuinely what's served. **The full AC2 enforcement-outcome proof — real reorder flips a real policy decision — is documented in the `eami-api` section above**, since it exercises the fixed hook's exact real request shape end-to-end through the real gateway, not just the UI in isolation (no browser-automation tool available in this environment, the same standing, consistently-disclosed limitation as every other `eami-ui` brief).
**Dependencies:** none for this fix. **B-089**/**B-090** are new, separate, low-priority follow-ups discovered during this brief — not blocking.

**Fix — B-088 (2026-08-22), Approvals "All" tab pagination can actually reach page 2+.** `ApprovalsPage.tsx:212`'s `const [allPage] = useState(1)` never destructured a setter, so `useApprovals({ page: allPage, per_page: 25 })` was permanently pinned to page 1.
**A correction to the Dead Clicks Audit's own characterization, found by re-reading `DataTable.tsx` before fixing, not assumed:** the bug wasn't just "the setter is missing" — `DataTable` computes its own pager visibility purely from `Math.ceil(data.length / pageSize)`, built for client-side pagination over an already-fully-loaded array. `allData` was already server-paginated down to at most 25 rows before `DataTable` ever saw it, so its internal `totalPages` was always exactly `1` and its `{totalPages > 1 && ...}` pager never rendered at all — a structural incompatibility between server-side and client-side pagination assumptions, not a working control that happened to be pinned.
**Resolution:** `allPage` now has a real setter (`setAllPage`). `DataTable`'s own pager is left unused for this tab (it's the wrong tool for server-paginated data, and `DataTable.tsx` was out of this brief's `MAY MODIFY` scope regardless) — a new, separate Previous/Next control was added directly in `ApprovalsPage.tsx` below the table, driven by the server's real `meta.total` (the same field already powering the Pending tab's count badge), matching this codebase's own established pattern for server-paginated pages (Audit/Paste Detection already build their own external pager for the same reason).
**Live-verified against the real running stack — the brief's own required proof (real records on page 2, not just that state changes):** seeded 25 throwaway `approval_requests` rows via `psql` (data only, no schema/backend change) with timestamps newer than the org's 7 real pre-existing approvals. Confirmed via the real `GET /v1/approvals`: `meta.total=32`, page 1 returns exactly the 25 seeded rows, page 2 returns exactly the 7 real original approvals (2 `claude`, 5 `manual_verify_tool`) — genuinely different, real records. Served-module `curl`'d first (the B-062 lesson) confirming `setAllPage` and the new pagination block are exactly what's deployed. All 25 seeded rows deleted afterward, confirmed back to the original 7. Pending tab's query (`useApprovals({status:'pending'})`) is completely independent of `allPage` by construction — confirmed via direct code read, no regression possible from this change.
**Test coverage:** none added — matches this repo's 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline (`BACKLOG.md` B-006), consistent with prior `eami-ui` briefs' precedent; verification is the live proof above.
**Dependencies:** none.

**Fix — B-081/082/083 (2026-08-22), row-click affordance standardized across Agents/Policies/Workflows.** Three pages had the exact same bug the Dead Clicks Audit first found on Tools (B-079): a table `<tr>` with `hover:bg-gray-50` implying clickability, no `onClick` behind it. Fixed together in one brief rather than three separate patches, standardizing all three onto the same correct pattern instead.
**Re-verified `DataTable.tsx`'s exact mechanism before treating it as the reference, not assumed:** `onClick={onRowClick ? () => onRowClick(row) : undefined}` paired with `className={onRowClick ? 'cursor-pointer hover:bg-gray-50' : ''}` — hover *and* cursor styling are applied together, only when a real handler exists. The bug on all three pages wasn't just a missing `onClick` — the rows were also missing `cursor-pointer`, half of the correct affordance pair.
**Per-page destination, confirmed against each page's existing edit-panel trigger, not assumed uniform:** Agents' row now opens the same `ConfigPanel` its "Configure" button already opened (`setConfigAgent(agent)`); Policies' row now opens the same edit panel its pencil icon already opened (`setPanel({mode:'edit', policy})`); Workflows' row now opens the same `EditWorkflowPanel` its pencil icon already opened (`setEditTargetId(wf.id)`). All three match the task brief's stated ACs exactly.
**Icon-vs-row-click double-fire, closed on every in-row button:** Agents' Configure (1 button); Policies' move-up/move-down (added by B-086, same session) plus pencil/trash (4 buttons); Workflows' pencil/trash (2 buttons) — each `onClick` now calls `e.stopPropagation()` before its own action, so a click on any icon fires only that icon's handler, never also the row's.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. `git diff --stat` confirms the change is scoped to exactly the three pages' row rendering (22 insertions/10 deletions total across the three files) — no panel, hook, or backend code touched. **Live-verified via served-module `curl` (the B-062 lesson) on all three pages**, confirming the real `cursor-pointer hover:bg-gray-50` + row `onClick`, and every icon button's `e.stopPropagation()` call, are genuinely what's deployed — counts matched exactly (1/4/2 `stopPropagation` calls on Agents/Policies/Workflows respectively). `stopPropagation()` correctly preventing bubbling to an ancestor's `onClick` is standard, deterministic DOM/React behavior, not something that needs a literal click to confirm beyond confirming the code itself is deployed — disclosed as such rather than overclaimed; no browser-automation tool is available in this environment, the same standing limitation as every other `eami-ui` brief in this codebase's history.
**Test coverage:** none added — matches this repo's 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline (`BACKLOG.md` B-006).
**Dependencies:** none. **B-084** (Audit — needs a real detail view built first) and **B-085** (Paste Detection — recommends removing the affordance instead of wiring a click) were explicitly out of scope, separate briefs, per the task brief's own OUT OF SCOPE section.

**Fix — B-085 (2026-08-22), Paste Detection's decorative row hover affordance removed rather than wired to a click.** Same bug class as B-079/081/082/083, but this page's own fix is the opposite: the Dead Clicks Audit recommended removing the misleading `hover:bg-gray-50`, not adding an `onClick`, since the page is deliberately read-only by design.
**Re-verified before fixing, not assumed from the audit's own recommendation alone:** traced the real data model end to end, not just the frontend `PasteEvent` type. The real backend response struct (`eami-api/internal/api/types.go`'s `PasteEventResp`) has exactly the six fields already rendered as table columns — `id`, `destination_domain`, `occurred_at`, `content_length`, `content_hash`, `os_username`. The `paste_events` schema's two additional columns (`org_id`, `source_endpoint_id`) are never exposed by the read handler at all. `PasteEventResp`'s own doc comment states directly: "there is no raw-content column anywhere upstream of this struct, so it cannot expose pasted text even by accident." **Confirmed conclusively: there is nothing a row click could reveal that isn't already visible in the row** — "remove," not "build a detail view," is the correct fix, matching the task brief's own hypothesis exactly.
**Resolution:** one-line change in `PasteEventsPage.tsx` — `<tr key={e.id} className="hover:bg-gray-50">` → `<tr key={e.id}>`. No `onClick`, no `cursor-pointer` added. `HashCell`'s copy button, the domain/date filters, Reset, and pagination are completely untouched by the diff.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. `git diff --stat` confirms exactly 1 file, 1 line changed. **Live-verified via served-module `curl` (the B-062 lesson):** confirmed zero `hover:bg-gray-50` remains anywhere in the served module, the row `<tr>` renders with no `className` at all, and `HashCell`/`handleCopy` are byte-identical to before, still correctly wired to `navigator.clipboard.writeText`. The real `GET /v1/paste-events` endpoint (backing filters/pagination) confirmed live and unaffected via a direct call.
**Test coverage:** none added — matches this repo's 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline (`BACKLOG.md` B-006).
**Dependencies:** none.

**Feature — B-087 (2026-08-22), `AgentsPage.tsx` gains real create/suspend/delete UI — full backend + B-077 fix + false-premise investigation in `eami-api` section above (this entry covers the frontend-specific pieces).** Previously the only reachable mutation was Configure; `useCreateAgent`/`useUpdateAgent`/`useDeleteAgent` all existed, wired to real endpoints, called by nothing.
**New `AddAgentPanel`:** react-hook-form + zod, structurally matching `ConfigPanel`'s existing slide-out shape exactly (fields: name, model, owner, declared scope, risk tier, token TTL). `risk_tier` deliberately offers only `low`/`medium`/`high` — matching `api/openapi.yaml`'s documented `AgentCreate` enum exactly, even though the real backend's `validRiskTiers` also silently accepts `critical` (a minor, disclosed pre-existing spec/implementation gap, not touched — this form only ever sends values the documented contract actually promises).
**Main table gains per-row Suspend/Reactivate and Delete,** both `e.stopPropagation()`-guarded against B-081's already-shipped row-click (`setConfigAgent`) so neither double-fires it. Delete opens the existing `ConfirmDialog` pattern; a page-level dismissible error banner (`actionError`) surfaces the real backend message on any failed lifecycle action — most importantly B-077's new `409`'s actionable "suspend it instead" text, not a generic failure string.
**A real bug found by the mandatory code-review pass, fixed before shipping:** the blocked-delete error was being set correctly in state but was structurally invisible — `ConfirmDialog` renders as a `fixed inset-0 z-50` full-screen overlay, and the error banner lived in normal page flow underneath it; since a failed delete deliberately keeps the dialog open (the whole point is the admin needs to see why), the message was completely hidden the one time it mattered most. **Fixed** by rendering the error inside `ConfirmDialog`'s own `children` slot instead (an already-existing prop on the shared component, no change to the component itself needed) — confirmed via served-module `curl` that the fix is genuinely deployed.
**Two more findings from the same review pass, disclosed rather than side-patched:** `ConfirmDialog.tsx` declares an `isLoading` prop its own implementation never uses — the Confirm button never actually disables mid-request, a pre-existing gap affecting every page already passing this prop (Policies/Workflows/Approvals, now Agents too), logged separately as **B-091** since fixing a shared component is a different blast radius than this brief's stated `AgentsPage.tsx`-only scope. Separately, instantiating one shared `useUpdateAgent()`/`useDeleteAgent()` per page (not per row) means `isPending` briefly disables every row's Suspend button while any single row's request is in flight — matches this codebase's own existing, already-accepted precedent (`PoliciesPage`'s `reorder.isPending`, B-086), not a new pattern, not fixed.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean. Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack — the full AC1–AC5 proof (real create/suspend/blocked-delete/clean-delete/lifecycle-event/no-regression sequence) is documented in the `eami-api` section above, since it exercises this UI's exact real request shapes through the real gateway and database, not the component in isolation. Served-module `curl` confirmed the error-visibility fix and the `stopPropagation` wiring are genuinely deployed (no browser-automation tool available in this environment, the same standing, disclosed limitation as every other `eami-ui` brief in this codebase's history).
**Dependencies:** **B-077**, closed together. **B-091** separate, not blocking.

**Feature — B-094 (2026-08-22), Audit Log gets a real detail view, closing B-084 with an honest, two-tier hash-chain verification indicator — backend field addition in `eami-api` section above.** Followed directly from the B-084 investigation: most fields were already sent by `GET /v1/audit` and simply never rendered.
**New `AuditEntryDetailPanel.tsx`:** slide-out panel (same shell classes as `ToolsPage.tsx`'s `EditToolPanel`, for visual consistency), opened by a real `onClick`/`cursor-pointer` on the Audit table's `<tr>` — the row-click B-081/082/083 correctly left unwired pending exactly this detail view. `HashCell`'s existing copy button gets `e.stopPropagation()`, matching every other in-row-button guard this codebase already established.
**Shows, all with real data:** full pretty-printed `parameters` (or an explicit "No parameters recorded"), full untruncated `hash`/`prev_hash` each with their own copy-to-clipboard button, raw `agent_id`/`policy_id`/`approval_id`, `data_handling_designation` when present (reusing `ToolsPage.tsx`'s exact three label strings for consistency), and policy/approval resolved to a human-readable name/description via live `GetPolicy`/`GetApproval` calls made lazily when the panel opens (not pre-fetched into the list) — degrading to an explicit "Policy no longer exists" / "Approval no longer exists" on a real 404, distinguished from a generic fetch failure.
**A real spec gap found while wiring the policy/approval lookups:** `api/openapi.yaml` documents only a `200` response for both `GET /v1/gateway/policies/{policyId}` and `GET /v1/approvals/{approvalId}` — no 404 schema at all — which collapses openapi-fetch's generated error/response types to `never` for these two operations, making the typed `api` client structurally unable to detect a 404. Used `apiFetch` (the existing undocumented/spec-mismatch escape hatch, same category of workaround as `useReorderPolicies`'s own precedent) instead, reading `ApiFetchError.status` directly.
**The CRITICAL REQUIREMENT — two hash-chain claims, deliberately never blurred:** an always-on **"Self-consistent (this entry only)"** indicator, explicitly worded to state it does *not* confirm the rest of the chain, computed entirely client-side via `crypto.subtle.digest` against the entry's own already-public fields (`org_id` isn't in the API response, but `ListAudit` is always scoped to the caller's own org, so the session's own known `org_id` from `useAuthStore` is exact, not a guess) — zero backend call, zero new endpoint. Separately, in its own visually distinct bordered "Full Chain Verification" section, a **"Verify chain to this entry"** button that calls the real, already-shipped `GET /v1/audit/verify?to=<entry timestamp>` and renders only the real returned result (`"Chain verified -- genesis through this entry (N rows checked)"` or the backend's own broken-chain message) — never a simulated outcome, never rendered before the button is clicked. The word "Verified" alone never appears on the self-consistency element.
**Known limitation, documented in code rather than silently accepted:** `VerifyAuditChain`'s boundary is `timestamp <= $to`, not an exact row match — a timestamp tie under concurrent writes could in principle include a row written after the target entry. `entry.timestamp` is forwarded to the backend exactly as received (full precision, never reformatted) to make this as tight as the endpoint allows; eliminating the tie case entirely would require changing `VerifyAuditChain` itself, explicitly out of this brief's scope.
**A real bug found and fixed by the mandatory code-review pass:** without a React `key`, switching directly from one audit row to another while the panel was open reused the same component instance, so `useVerifyAuditChainToEntry`'s mutation state (a stale "Chain verified" result) could persist onto a completely different entry's fields — the exact class of mistake the honest-labeling requirement exists to prevent. Fixed with `key={selected.id}` on the panel, forcing a full remount on every row change. Two smaller findings from the same pass also fixed: an unhandled promise rejection on a denied clipboard write (added `.catch`), and a gofmt struct-alignment slip in the backend's own new field (fixed precisely, without touching the file's pre-existing unrelated CRLF diff noise).
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build`, both clean, after every fix. Served-module `curl` (the B-062 lesson) confirmed the dev server was serving current source throughout, including after the code-review fixes.
**Live-verified end-to-end against the real running stack** — documented in full in the `eami-api` section above, since it's backend behavior this UI's exact real request shapes exercise (the `data_handling_designation` round-trip, the intact-chain and tampered-chain `VerifyAuditChain` calls, and an independent Node.js re-derivation of the exact client-side self-consistency hash formula against a real row's real API response, confirmed byte-for-byte before trusting it in the browser). **The panel's own rendering and the visual separation between the two hash-chain claims were not confirmed via an actual browser screenshot this session** — no browser-automation tool was available (the user declined installing the Chrome extension) and the user chose to perform that visual check personally rather than wait; every backend behavior the panel's labels describe was independently proven live as above.
**Test coverage:** none added directly to this file — matches this repo's 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline (`BACKLOG.md` B-006); the real-Postgres backend tests in the `eami-api` section above cover the data this panel renders.
**Dependencies:** none. **B-092** (cross-page deep-linking) and **B-093** (`workflow_run_id` linkage), both already logged from the B-084 investigation, remain separate, unscoped follow-ups — correctly not folded into this brief.

**Feature — B-096 (2026-08-22), a real centralized branding mechanism (logo/name/theme), shipped with rheoARC as its first instance.** Preceded by a from-scratch investigation this same session (no code changes) inventorying every EAMI branding surface across the whole repo — logo/theme/name in `eami-ui`, the appliance boot banner, the browser extension manifest, and the agent installers' product identifiers (`Product.wxs`, the macOS `plist`, the Linux `nfpm.yaml`) — to scope this brief correctly against everything *not* touched.
**Mechanism chosen, explicitly not the alternative:** a build-time TS config (`eami-ui/src/branding/config.ts`), not a runtime/admin-editable DB-backed version with a Settings UI upload flow — flagged to the user up front as the smaller of two real options; the runtime version would need a schema table, an upload endpoint + file storage, a server-side re-implementation of color extraction, and a new Settings page, all explicitly out of this brief's scope.
**Real, repeatable color extraction, not eyeballed:** new `eami-ui/scripts/generate-theme.mjs` (devDependencies `colorthief` + `sharp`, build-time only, never shipped to the browser bundle) runs `colorthief.getSwatches()` (MMCQ quantization, `ignoreWhite:true`) against the trimmed source logo (`Logo/rheo logo name.png`, copied+trimmed+resized to `eami-ui/src/branding/source/rheoarc-logo.png` — the raw asset was a 2816x1536 PNG that was ~90% blank canvas). The lowest-lightness real swatch (the bold navy "ARC" glyph) anchors `brand-500`, pinned to the exact extracted hex; `brand-50/100/600/700/900` are computed from that anchor by holding its OKLCH hue+chroma constant and moving only lightness (chroma tapered near the extremes to stay in sRGB gamut, using Bjorn Ottosson's OKLab conversion math, implemented directly in the script — no extra dependency for that part). The highest-chroma swatch *other than* the navy anchor (the teal "rheo" glyph) is extracted and kept as `rawSwatches.teal` for future reference, not wired into the ramp — an earlier version of the script picked this by lightness instead of chroma and wrongly grabbed a pale PNG anti-aliasing artifact near the glyph edges instead of the real teal ink; caught before shipping and fixed to select by chroma. Output: `eami-ui/src/branding/theme.generated.ts` (the generated brand scale, header-documents the exact process and is regenerated via `npm run generate-branding-theme`) and `eami-ui/public/favicon.svg` (colors inlined from the same run, so it can't drift out of sync with the generated theme file).
**`tailwind.config.ts` is now the OUTPUT of this mechanism, not the source:** its hardcoded 6-value hex map is replaced with `import { brand } from './src/branding/theme.generated'`; confirmed via the compiled CSS itself (`dist/assets/index-*.css`) that the real extracted hexes (`#133f6d`, `#002c58`, etc.) landed in the shipped `.bg-brand-*`/`.text-brand-*` utility classes.
**A real, honest gap, flagged rather than papered over:** the source logo is a pure wordmark ("rheo" + "ARC" typography, no separate icon glyph anywhere in the asset) — there is no icon-only mark to derive for a small/collapsed context. `branding/config.ts`'s `logo.mark` slot and `Logo.tsx`'s `variant="mark"` are wired up and render (a generated rounded-square monogram in the real extracted brand colors), but this is explicitly documented in both files as a placeholder, not a real designed mark — needs a proper icon-only asset from design later. Separately confirmed directly: this codebase has no existing collapsed/icon-only sidebar rail state (`uiStore.sidebarOpen` is a binary show/hide, not a rail) — not invented as part of this brief. "Large" and "small" were proven against the two size contexts that actually exist today: the Sidebar header logo and the browser-tab favicon.
**The EAMI/rheoARC text-duplication tension, raised for approval before building, not silently resolved either way:** the wordmark image itself renders "rheoARC" as pixels, while `branding.displayName` stays `'EAMI'` per this brief's explicit scope (the name change is the separate, deferred full-rebrand epic). Showing the logo image next to a literal "EAMI" text label would read as two different product names in the same UI element. User-approved resolution: drop the redundant adjacent name text where the logo *is* the name (`Sidebar.tsx` — was `<Shield>` + a literal "EAMI" span, now just `<Logo variant="full">`), but leave standalone name-copy sentences elsewhere untouched. Applied identically to `LoginPage.tsx` per an explicit mid-session scope addition from the user: `Shield` icon replaced with `<Logo variant="full">`, but "Sign in to EAMI" heading text intentionally left unchanged (user's explicit choice between two offered options) — the same logo/text visual overlap exists there by design, not an oversight.
**`document.title` is genuinely config-driven, not just a static string:** `main.tsx` sets it at runtime from `branding.displayName` (`` `${branding.displayName} — Enterprise AI Governance` ``, the descriptive suffix is unrelated to branding and stays a literal); `index.html`'s static `<title>` is an unchanged pre-JS fallback.
**AC5 proven concretely, not just asserted:** temporarily changed `branding.displayName` to a distinct sentinel string, ran a real `npm run build`, and grepped the emitted `dist/assets/*.js` to confirm the sentinel reached the shipped bundle with zero component files touched; reverted, rebuilt, confirmed the sentinel is gone and `"EAMI"` is back. Same live-build technique (not just visual inspection) used to confirm the generated theme reached the compiled CSS.
**Verified:** real `npm run type-check` and `npm run build`, both clean, after every change including the mid-session LoginPage addition. **Visual/browser confirmation of the rendered logo and favicon was not done this session** — the user declined installing the Chrome extension when offered; the dev server was started and manually curl-verified to serve the correct HTML/favicon/bundle, but no screenshot was taken. Flagged explicitly rather than claimed.
**Test coverage:** none added — matches this repo's 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline (`BACKLOG.md` B-006); `generate-theme.mjs` is a build-time script, not application code under test in this codebase's existing convention.
**Out of scope, explicitly, not silently skipped:** any actual display-name change (still "EAMI"); a runtime/admin-editable branding UI; agent installers, appliance boot banner, browser extension manifest (all three confirmed untouched — `git diff --stat` covers exactly `eami-ui/`).
**Dependencies:** none.

**Interfaces — B-104 (2026-08-24), `AgentsPage.tsx`/`PoliciesPage.tsx`/`WorkflowsPage.tsx` migrated onto the shared `DataTable` component, closing the root cause behind B-081/082/083's bug class.** `DataTable.tsx` already correctly ties hover/cursor styling to whether a real `onRowClick` prop is passed; the three pages hand-rolled their own `<table>`/`<tr>` instead of using it. `DataTable.tsx` itself was **not** modified — investigated and confirmed unnecessary.
