**Real complication found before building, not assumed trivial (per the brief's own instruction):** neither `ApprovalsPage.tsx` nor `AlertsPage.tsx` — offered as "the reference" — actually uses `onRowClick`; both render `DataTable` read-only. So this migration is the first real instance of `DataTable` + row-click + in-row action buttons, not a copy of an existing pattern (mechanically low-risk regardless — a `render` callback with an inner button calling `e.stopPropagation()` is the same shape each page's old hand-rolled `<tr>` already used).
**The one real design problem: Policies' up/down reorder buttons need each row's true index in the priority-ordered array, but `Column.render(row)` only receives the row, not an index.** Solved without touching `DataTable.tsx`: `policies.indexOf(policy)` inside `render` — correct regardless of `DataTable`'s internal pagination, valid specifically because no column on this page is marked `sortable` (so `DataTable` never reorders the array `indexOf` searches against).
**Pagination regression avoided:** all three pages previously rendered every row unpaginated; `DataTable` defaults to `pageSize=25`. All three now pass `pageSize={1000}`, preserving "show everything" exactly.
**Empty states preserved exactly, not "upgraded":** `PoliciesPage.tsx`/`WorkflowsPage.tsx` already used `<EmptyState title=.../ description=.../>` (richer than `DataTable`'s own `title`-only built-in empty state) — kept as each page's own outer conditional, matching `ApprovalsPage.tsx`'s established pattern, with only the inner `<table>` swapped for `<DataTable>`. `AgentsPage.tsx`'s plain-`<p>` empty state (inconsistent with the other two, pre-existing) was left as-is — upgrading it would be a design change beyond this migration's scope.
**`StepsEditor` (`WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s per-step reorder UI inside the Add/Edit panel) is untouched** — it's a `<div>`/card list, never a `<table>`, and never had the hover-without-click bug this brief closes.
**Also discovered, flagged, not fixed (out of this brief's SCOPE — "any other page"):** `DashboardPage.tsx` and `DiscoverPage.tsx` still have the identical `className="cursor-pointer hover:bg-gray-50"` hand-rolled pattern. Not touched here.
**Verified:** real `tsc && vite build` via the established `docker build --target builder -f eami-ui/Dockerfile .` path, clean, zero type errors (confirms `Column<Agent>`/`Column<Policy>`/`Column<Workflow>` all satisfy `DataTable`'s generic constraint with no casts needed beyond what each page already used). The real `docker-compose` `eami-ui` dev container (bind-mounted source, Vite HMR) was rebuilt and restarted; confirmed via direct request that the served module is the current code for all three pages (`DataTable`/`policies.indexOf`/`workflowColumns` all present) and that the old hand-rolled `cursor-pointer hover:bg-gray-50` string is gone from all three. **No live interactive browser click-through performed** — the seeded dev users (`scripts/seed-db.sh`) have a placeholder bcrypt hash, not a real password, so an authenticated browser session couldn't be reached; same standing limitation as other recent frontend work in this environment, disclosed rather than guessed around.
**Test coverage:** none added — matches this repo's 0%-frontend-test-coverage baseline (`BACKLOG.md` B-006).
**Dependencies:** none.

**Interfaces — B-098 (2026-08-24), Settings API Keys tab gains agent-scoping:** `useApiKeys.ts`'s create mutation gains an optional `agent_id`. `SettingsPage.tsx`'s `ApiKeysTab` gains a "Scope to agent (optional)" `<select>` in the create form (populated via the existing `useAgents()` hook — no new hook needed) and a "Scoped agent" table column. `expires_at`'s form field already existed (built ahead of the backend accepting it) and now actually works end to end. **Code-review fix:** the "Scoped agent" column's `agentName()` helper originally fell back to the raw agent UUID when the agents list hadn't loaded yet or the agent was deleted — changed to a short `Unknown agent (12345678…)` fragment instead of a full raw UUID.
**Deviates from the generated `ApiKey`/`CreateApiKeyRequest` openapi types** (don't yet declare `agent_id`), same disclosed pattern as B-086's `usePolicies.ts` — accessed via a narrow inline cast, not the generated client's types; logged as **B-106** in `BACKLOG.md`.
**Verified:** real `tsc && vite build` (`npm run build`), clean, both before and after the code-review fix.
**Dependencies:** none new (extends B-104's existing `useAgents()` hook).

**Interfaces — B-108 (2026-08-25), FinOps gains "Spend by Team" and "Spend by Connector," and the "Avg Cost / Outcome" KPI card stops being permanently blank:** `FinOpsPage.tsx` now reads `summary?.by_team` (computed by the API since B-097, never rendered until now) into a new table, same JSX pattern as the existing "Spend by Agent" table. A new `by_tool` (`ToolSpend`, not yet in `openapi.yaml` — accessed via a narrow inline cast, same B-098 precedent) drives a new "Spend by Connector" table. The existing "Avg Cost / Outcome" `MetricCard` (`summary?.avg_cost_per_outcome`) needed zero JSX changes — it already read the field, which now returns a real backend-computed number instead of `undefined`/`—`.
**Verified:** real `tsc`/`vite build` clean. Live-verified: the real running dev container (bind-mounted, no rebuild needed) confirmed serving the new sections via a direct fetch of the served module, and a real `GET /v1/finops/summary` against real seeded data confirmed the exact numbers the new tables would render (see `BUILT.md`'s `eami-api` B-108 entry for the full live-verification trace).
**Dependencies:** B-097 (`by_team` data), B-098 (live-verification login path).

**Interfaces — B-112 (2026-08-26), new "Model Pricing" Settings tab + a FinOps unrecognized-model warning:** new `useModelPricing.ts` (`apiFetch()` escape hatch — `/v1/admin/model-pricing` isn't yet in `openapi.yaml`, same B-108/`useWorkflows.ts` disclosed-deviation precedent), a 5th `SettingsPage.tsx` tab (`ModelPricingTab`) with a full add/edit/delete table covering all 8 columns (base + B-111's 3 cache-rate tiers), mirroring the existing API Keys tab's modal/`ConfirmDialog` pattern. `FinOpsPage.tsx` gains an amber warning banner (shown only when `unrecognized_model_request_count > 0`, linking to the new tab) and flags an unpriced model's chart-legend entry (`"model (unpriced)"`, neutral grey) instead of blending it into the palette — both fields accessed via the same narrow inline-cast pattern `by_tool` already established, since neither is in `openapi.yaml` either.
**Verified:** real `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vite build` both clean — this session confirmed Node/npm are now actually present on this machine (`node v24.19.0`/`npm 11.17.0`), contradicting `CLAUDE.md`'s toolchain note ("Node/npm are still not installed as of this note"); flagged as stale, not fixed here (`CLAUDE.md` is outside this brief's file boundary). Live-verified: the real running dev container confirmed serving both the new Settings tab and the FinOps banner via direct fetch of the served modules.
**Dependencies:** B-111 (cache-rate columns rendered in the form), B-112's own `eami-api` CRUD endpoints.

**Bugfix — B-091 (2026-08-26), `ConfirmDialog`'s `isLoading` prop now actually does something:** the shared `ConfirmDialog.tsx` declared `isLoading?: boolean` in its props interface but never destructured it — the Confirm button had no `disabled` tied to anything, so a double-click during an in-flight mutation fired it twice. Fixed: `isLoading` is destructured (default `false`), applied as `disabled` to **both** Cancel and Confirm (cancelling mid-flight is the same race class), and Confirm shows a spinning `RefreshCw` icon while loading — the exact same visual convention `SettingsPage.tsx`'s pre-existing `SaveButton` already uses (`animate-spin` + `disabled:opacity-60`), not a new one. Zero prop-shape change, so the 4+ existing callers (Policies/Workflows/Approvals/Agents delete flows, all already passing `isLoading={x.isPending}`) started working with no changes on their end.
**Verified:** real `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx vite build`, both clean. Live-verified: served-module confirmation via the running `eami-ui` dev container (`claude-in-chrome` confirmed not connected this session — stated plainly rather than overclaiming a real click-through).
**Dependencies:** none.

**Interfaces — B-092 (2026-08-26), cross-page deep-linking/highlighting by ID (Agents/Policies/Approvals):** `DataTable.tsx` (shared by all three since B-104) gains `getRowId`/`highlightRowId` props — assigns a stable `id="row-<id>"` per `<tr>`, applies an amber `ring`/background highlight, scrolls the matched row into view (`useEffect` + `ref`), and auto-advances `page` to whichever page actually contains the match. `AgentsPage.tsx`/`PoliciesPage.tsx` read `?highlight=<id>` via `useSearchParams` and pass it straight through — both already use `pageSize={1000}` (B-104), so the page-advance logic is mostly moot there.
**A real architectural wrinkle found during re-verification, not in the original B-084 investigation:** `ApprovalsPage.tsx` is genuinely different from Agents/Policies — its default "pending" tab is a card list, not a `DataTable` (a highlighted approval could be decided/expired, not pending), and its "all" tab is **server-paginated** (25/page via `useApprovals`), unlike Agents/Policies' fully client-loaded rows. Handled by: a deep link forces the "all" tab and fetches with `per_page: 1000` instead of 25 — the same "make pagination a non-issue" convention Agents/Policies already use — rather than building new find-which-server-page-has-this-id backend support.
**Verified:** real `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx vite build`, both clean. Live-verified: served-module confirmation, same disclosure as B-091.
**Dependencies:** none. Wiring B-084's (not-yet-built) Audit detail panel to actually emit `?highlight=` links is explicitly out of this brief's scope — this ships the capability, not its first consumer.

**Interfaces — B-145 (2026-09-01), Workflow Canvas rebuild Brief 1: `sequential-workflow-designer` read-only rendering, new `WorkflowCanvasPreviewPage.tsx`/`workflowCanvasAdapter.ts`:** first build brief off B-131's investigation (`sequential-workflow-designer`/`-react`, MIT, chosen because its `sequence`-array model structurally can't represent the free-form graph shapes that produced the discontinued `@xyflow/react` canvas's (B-066-069) real bugs). New route `/gateway/workflows/:id/canvas-preview` renders a real workflow's steps read-only. `WorkflowsPage.tsx` (the card editor) had zero edits during the initial build (explicitly out of this brief's original MAY-MODIFY scope); a single, small, explicitly-requested same-day follow-up (after real visual verification, see below) added one permanent `Eye`-icon "Preview (read-only)" button to the existing row-actions column — purely additive, zero changes to any add/edit/delete/reorder/save logic (confirmed via a real full-file diff review: 11 insertions, one line changed, one file).
**Free-tier custom-step-type question resolved, empirically:** B-131 flagged a real discrepancy between the library's GitHub README (lists "Custom Step Types" under its Pro examples) and its Task-component doc page (free worked example uses a custom `type` string). Re-checked live against the current pricing page (not the Pro feature list, which names only specific Step *Components* and UI extras) before building, then **built and confirmed for real**: a custom `eami-connector-action` step type, carrying real `gateway_tool_id`/`toolName`/`action`/`hasInputMapping` fields, renders correctly with a real custom per-type icon (free `steps.iconUrlProvider`) using only the free MIT packages — no Pro package ever installed, no paywall hit.
**Tailwind/Vite compatibility confirmed empirically:** `designer.css`/`designer-light.css` (the library's own pre-built CSS) both requested successfully through the real Vite dev server (`200`, valid HMR-wrapped module, zero errors in `docker logs eami-ui`) — no SSR concern (irrelevant to this Vite SPA), no Tailwind JIT conflict (its `content` glob never scans `node_modules` CSS, and the library's DOM carries no Tailwind utility classes).
**Adapter (`workflowCanvasAdapter.ts`):** one `componentType:'task'` Step per real `WorkflowStep`, deliberately linear-only — no `Container`/`Switch`/branches rendered, since `workflow_steps`' flat `step_order` schema still can't represent branches (B-131's own confirmed backend gap) and this brief builds no UI implying a capability that doesn't exist.
**Every interactive surface explicitly disabled, individually, not just via `isReadonly`:** `toolboxConfiguration={false}`, `controlBar={false}`, `contextMenu={false}`, `keyboard={false}`, `rootEditor={false}`, `stepEditor={false}`. Zero mutation hooks (`useCreateWorkflow`/`useUpdateWorkflow`/`useDeleteWorkflow`/`useSetWorkflowStepParams`) imported anywhere in the two new files, confirmed by direct grep — the new view's only backend call at all is the single necessary `GET /v1/gateway/workflows/:id` read.
**Real measured bundle-size delta** (mirroring B-066's own precedent, not the npm registry's unpacked-size figure): a real before/after `vite build` — the library plus its one real dependency `sequential-workflow-model` (nocode-js's own sibling MIT package, confirmed via `npm ls`, matching B-131's "0 third-party dependencies" finding) — **+29.3 kB gzipped JS, +1.3 kB gzipped CSS** (971.30→1,076.64 kB raw JS / 265.01→294.33 kB gzip; 29.28→36.66 kB raw CSS / 5.58→6.92 kB gzip).
**Verified, real Node/npm toolchain (now genuinely available on this machine, superseding this section's older "no Node/npm" verification notes elsewhere):** real `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx vite build`, both clean. Live-verified against the real running dev stack — `eami-ui`'s dev container had to be rebuilt (its `node_modules` is baked into the image at build time per `docker-compose.yml`, not bind-mounted, so a host-side `npm install` alone doesn't reach the running container) — after which the new page's module and its adapter both transformed through the real Vite dev server with zero errors (real HTTP `200`s, real byte sizes), `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s own module was re-requested and confirmed still transforming cleanly (zero regression, not assumed), and a real workflow (2 real steps, real connector `b046-live-verify`/`echo_post`) was confirmed present in the dev DB for use with the new route.

**A real, visible bug found by actual human visual verification, not caught by any of the above — exactly why this brief was scoped read-only-first:** the build described above (module transforms cleanly, real data confirmed, `tsc`/`vite build` clean, `claude-in-chrome` offered and declined) was still, on real human inspection, a **completely blank canvas**. Traced through the library's own shipped source, not guessed: `sequential-workflow-designer`'s core JS measures its canvas from real layout pixels (`clientWidth`/`clientHeight`, `offsetWidth`/`offsetHeight`); its own CSS is `.sqd-designer { height: 100% }`; the div its React wrapper mounts into (`sqd-designer-react`) carries **zero CSS of its own** (confirmed by grepping `designer.css`, 0 matches) — entirely dependent on its container having a genuinely definite size. This page's `flex h-full flex-col` wrapper relied on a percentage-height cascade through `AppShell`'s shared `main` with no `min-height:0` anywhere in the chain — the first page in the app needing to fill remaining viewport height, so nothing existing had exercised this path. **Two real, separately-confirmed fix passes:**
- **Pass 1 (height):** `h-full` → `h-[75vh]` (viewport-relative, immune to the ancestor chain). Confirmed via the user's own DevTools measurement: `sqd-designer-react` went from `0` height to a real `550.5px`. **Width was still exactly `0`** — propagated to the real `<svg class="sqd-workspace-canvas" width="0">`. Real DOM children existed the whole time (`childElementCount:1`, real `sqd-designer > sqd-workspace > svg > defs/rect/g` tree) — a genuine zero-size mount, not a mount failure.
- **Pass 2 (width):** root cause was `flex` (row) only auto-stretching the **cross axis** (height, default `align-items:stretch`) — the **main axis** (width) defaults to content-based sizing (`flex-grow:0`), and an empty, unstyled child has no content to size against. Fixed by swapping `flex` for `grid`: default `place-items:stretch` stretches **both** axes for an unstyled single-cell child, no CSS needed on the child at all.
- **Live-verified for real by the user after pass 2:** a real flowchart — start/stop markers, connector lines, both step boxes visible with distinct `data-step-id` values.

**A real false alarm, ruled out through direct evidence rather than assumption — recorded separately so it never gets conflated with the actual fix above:** the browser also logged an uncaught `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'startTime')` at `et.reportAllChanges`, every stack frame `<anonymous>`. `reportAllChanges` is a real, named public option of Google's `web-vitals` library, whose internals read `entries[...].startTime` off `PerformanceObserver` entries — matching this error's exact shape — but `web-vitals` is confirmed **not a dependency of this project at all** (grepped `package-lock.json`, zero matches), and the literal string `reportAllChanges` appears **nowhere** in any installed package (grepped all of `node_modules`, zero matches). The only files anywhere in the project matching the `startTime`+`PerformanceObserver` pattern together live inside `rollup`'s own Node.js-side, build-time profiling code, which never ships to the browser. Persisted in Incognito too. Most consistent with an unrelated browser-extension-injected script — **correctly not fixed, because there was nothing in this codebase's own dependency tree to fix.**

**Differential data-correctness test, not a repeat of the same weak case:** the initial visual check used `b063-live-verify`, whose two real steps happen to share the identical connector+action in the database (confirmed via direct SQL, unchanged throughout this whole investigation) — a weak test of "does each step render its own label." Re-tested against `b059-live-verify` (`b046-live-verify`/`echo_get` step 1, `claude`/`messages` step 2 — genuinely different, confirmed via direct SQL) and confirmed correct: `workflowCanvasAdapter.ts` had no bug all along — an earlier report of "both steps show the same label" traced to the user comparing two *different* workflows (the card editor showing `b059`, the canvas-preview showing `b063`), not a real adapter defect.

**`claude-in-chrome` was offered this session and the user declined it** — every check above that needed real DevTools measurement (the exact `0`/`550.5px`/`width="0"` numbers, the Elements-panel DOM inspection, the Incognito re-test) was done by the user directly and reported back, not performed or independently observed by Claude; disclosed plainly rather than implied as self-verified.
**Dependencies:** B-131 (investigation). Brief 2 (interactivity) and Brief 3 (save-time validation) not yet scoped; no cutover decision from cards to this library made or implied.

**Interfaces — B-146 (2026-09-02), session persistence on hard reload + redirect-back-after-login, `authStore.ts`/`LoginPage.tsx`:** `authStore.ts` held `accessToken`/`refreshToken`/`user`/`isAuthenticated` in plain in-memory Zustand state with zero persistence, so any hard navigation (refresh, bookmark, pasted/shared URL) discarded a still-valid session; `LoginPage.tsx` always navigated to `/dashboard` after login, never back to where the user was headed. Frontend-only fix, per the brief's own scope — `eami-api`'s auth/token-issuance backend (B-098/116/118) untouched.
**Persistence mechanism, localStorage not HttpOnly cookie — a scope constraint, not a preference:** an HttpOnly cookie would need `eami-api` changes (`Set-Cookie` on login/refresh, CORS/`SameSite` rework across the Vite dev-proxy vs. `eami-proxy` topologies), which this brief's `MUST NOT MODIFY` explicitly forbids — the cookie option was never actually available within scope, not merely less convenient. Shipped via Zustand's `persist` middleware (`zustand/middleware`, already a direct dependency, no new package), `localStorage`-backed, key `eami-auth`. **XSS tradeoff disclosed, not glossed over, with real evidence rather than an assumption:** a successful XSS on this origin could read the token from localStorage; verified this codebase has zero `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` usage repo-wide (grepped), and B-118's short access-token TTL (900s default; the real dev-stack login used for verification actually issued 3599s) bounds how long a stolen access token stays useful. Neither mitigation eliminates the risk — an HttpOnly-cookie migration is logged as a real, separate, backend-touching future brief if this tradeoff becomes unacceptable.
**A real wrong assumption caught by live verification, corrected before shipping, not after:** the original plan (approved by the user) included a pre-emptive client-side JWT-expiry check on rehydration, gated on the *refresh* token's `exp` claim, specifically to avoid a brief "flash" of authenticated UI for a session that's actually dead. Real live verification against the running `eami-api` (`POST /v1/auth/login`) showed `refresh_token` is a cryptographically random **opaque** value (`auth.go`'s `IssueRefreshToken`, `hex.EncodeToString` over `crypto/rand`, confirmed in source too) — zero dots, no JWT payload segment to decode. The planned `isTokenExpired()` would have thrown on every real refresh token and, being fail-closed, forced a full logout on every single hard reload — the exact bug this brief exists to fix. Removed entirely rather than patched around; the existing, unmodified `lib/auth.ts` 401→`attemptTokenRefresh()`→`logout()` flow (already built for exactly this purpose) is the only mechanism that can actually know a refresh token is dead, since that's a server-side fact — confirmed live, a garbage refresh token against the real `POST /v1/auth/refresh` returns a real `401 {"code":"unauthorized","message":"invalid or expired refresh token"}`, and a genuinely valid one returns a real fresh token pair. A session whose refresh token has truly died server-side now optimistically rehydrates and is corrected on the very next API call, instead of never being trusted at all — an accepted, disclosed tradeoff, not a regression (previously *every* reload lost *every* session, valid or not).
**AC2 (redirect back to the originally-requested URL), achieved without touching `AppShell.tsx` or `router.tsx`:** both are outside this brief's `MAY-MODIFY` scope, so the standard React-Router pattern (guard passes `location` via `Navigate state`) wasn't available. Instead, `authStore.ts` captures `window.location.pathname + search` once, at the module's first evaluation (exempting `/login`/`/setup`/`/`) — on a hard navigation this module loads before React Router has rendered or redirected anything, so the captured value is genuinely the URL the user asked for. New `consumeRedirectPath()` returns and clears it once; `LoginPage.tsx`'s `onSubmit` now calls `navigate(consumeRedirectPath() ?? '/dashboard', { replace: true })` instead of unconditionally `/dashboard`.
**Live verification, honestly scoped:** `claude-in-chrome` was declined again this session (standing limitation, same as B-145) — no interactive browser click-through was performed. In its place: (1) real `npx tsc --noEmit` / `npx vite build`, both clean, run twice (before and after the isTokenExpired removal); (2) the real running `eami-ui` dev container rebuilt (`node_modules` baked into the image, same as B-145) and the served `authStore.ts` module confirmed over HTTP to contain the shipped `consumeRedirectPath` export and zero leftover `isTokenExpired`; (3) **the actual shipped `authStore.ts` bundled via `esbuild` (unmodified source, zero test-only edits to the real file) and executed under three separate Node processes with stubbed `window`/`localStorage`**, exercising real logic against a real access/refresh token pair obtained from a live `POST /v1/auth/login` against the running `eami-api` (dev seed user `dev@example.com`, password reset via direct `psql` update to a freshly bcrypt-hashed test password for login purposes only — dev DB test data, not an app-code change): Scenario A (hard refresh with a valid persisted session) confirmed `isAuthenticated`/`accessToken`/`refreshToken`/`user` all rehydrate correctly; Scenario B (direct URL while logged out) confirmed `consumeRedirectPath()` returns the attempted path once then `null`; Scenario C confirmed `/login` itself is never captured as a redirect target, and that `setTokens()`/`logout()` correctly write through to the persisted `localStorage` entry (the mechanism that makes the existing 401 flow's `logout()` call actually stick across a subsequent reload). All 11 assertions across the three scenarios passed. (4) real backend confirmation of both refresh-token outcomes via direct `curl` against `/v1/auth/refresh`, as described above. **What was not verified:** actual browser rendering (no flash/flicker observation, no real click-through of the login form, no real F5 keypress) — disclosed plainly, consistent with this repo's standing practice for frontend work in this environment.
**Dependencies:** none. Touches only `authStore.ts`/`LoginPage.tsx`, exactly the brief's `MAY-MODIFY` scope; `AppShell.tsx`/`client.ts`/`lib/auth.ts`/`router.tsx` all read for investigation, none modified.

**Interfaces — B-131 Brief 2 (2026-09-02), Workflow Canvas local interactivity, `WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx` (renamed from `WorkflowCanvasPreviewPage.tsx`) + `workflowCanvasAdapter.ts` extended:** Brief 1 (B-145) proved the library renders EAMI's real data read-only; this brief adds add/remove/reorder + click-to-configure, mirroring the original canvas epic's own Brief 2 (B-067) boundary exactly — **zero backend writes**. Route path deliberately left unchanged (`/gateway/workflows/:id/canvas-preview`, bookmark/share stability, B-146's own lesson) — only the file/component name and one `WorkflowsPage.tsx` tooltip changed.
**Two small, explicitly-approved exceptions to `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s "untouched" status, confirmed with the user before building, same category as B-145's own Eye-icon addition:** (1) `StepConfigPanel`/`StepRow`/`ParamRow`/`revalidateExtractionRefs`/`newStepRow`/`newParamRow` — all previously module-private — gained `export` (zero logic change, confirmed via `git diff`: exactly 6 `export` keywords + 1 doc comment, nothing else) so this brief could reuse `StepConfigPanel` unmodified rather than forking it, per the brief's own explicit instruction; (2) the Eye-icon's tooltip changed from `"Preview (read-only)"` to `"Open canvas"` now that the destination is genuinely interactive.
**Library-native interactions used directly, not custom-built, per this brief's own investigation ask** — confirmed against the library's real `.d.ts` files before writing any code (not assumed, given B-145's own lesson about unverified library behavior): `selectedStepId`/`onSelectedStepIdChanged` (real, typed props) drives which step's `StepConfigPanel` opens; `toolboxConfiguration` (one group, one empty-step template) handles drag-to-add; `contextMenu`/`keyboard` (both enabled) handle native delete; `controlBar` enabled for free Undo/Redo. `stepEditor` stays `false` (as in Brief 1) — `StepConfigPanel` is rendered as this page's own overlay, not adapted into the library's `useStepEditor()` slot, keeping it a byte-for-byte-identical reuse of the card editor's component and props contract rather than an adaptation of it.
**Adapter extended for bidirectional conversion (`workflowCanvasAdapter.ts`):** `ConnectorActionStepProperties` now carries a full `params: ParamRow[]` (replacing Brief 1's summary-only `hasInputMapping` boolean and the redundant `workflowStepId`, since `Step.id` already carries that); `buildInitialDefinition` seeds every step's real static params via the same B-059 read `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s `EditWorkflowPanel` already performs (a read, never a write), mirroring its static/extract row-building rules exactly so `StepConfigPanel` behaves identically in both places. `rowFromStep`/`stepFromRow` are the new bidirectional converters between the library's `Step` and the reused `StepRow` shape.
**A real state-management subtlety identified and handled during design, before either review pass ran:** a canvas-added step (via the toolbox) never gets a real backend id in this brief (zero writes), so `rowFromStep` must NOT treat every `Step.id` as real — it checks against a `realStepIds` set captured once at load time, preserving `StepRow`'s own established invariant (an undefined `id` is what makes a step ineligible as an extraction SOURCE until actually saved, exactly the card editor's own rule).
**Every local mutation — `StepConfigPanel`'s own callbacks AND the library's native add/remove/reorder — funnels through one `commit()`,** mirroring `StepsEditor.commit()` exactly: `revalidateExtractionRefs` (imported unchanged, not reimplemented) runs on every single change, no exceptions, so an extraction reference invalidated by a native drag-reorder or context-menu delete is caught exactly like the card editor already proves it is — this brief's own flagged edge case (state management around add/remove/reorder interacting with the connector-action adapter), closed by construction rather than by a special case.
**Mandatory code-review pass (forked subagent, medium effort) found 2 real issues, both fixed before shipping, not after:**
1. `stepFromRow` baked `toolName`/the canvas label into committed `Step` state at mutation time using whatever `tools` list happened to be loaded then, unlike the card editor's `StepsEditor`, which resolves `tool?.name` fresh on every render. Failure scenario: interacting with the canvas (reorder/add) before `useTools()` first resolves would mislabel every step `"(connector)"`, and — unlike the card editor — that wrong label would persist in local state until some later, unrelated edit happened to refresh it. **Fixed:** the one-time definition seed now also waits on `useTools()`'s own `isSuccess`/`isError` (not array length, since a real org can have zero tools) before the designer ever mounts, so every `commit()`, from the very first, always has real tools data — closing the race at its source rather than patching the symptom. A related blank-flash gap this fix exposed (the loading spinner previously only covered "workflow still loading," not "workflow loaded, now waiting on tools/params") was found and fixed in the same pass.
2. `realStepIds` was a `useMemo` keyed on the `workflow` query object, recomputed on every reference change — not captured once like `definition` is. Failure scenario: `useWorkflow`'s default `staleTime` (30s) with `refetchOnReconnect` not disabled means a real network blip (laptop sleep/wake, wifi drop) could trigger a background refetch; if another tab/session removed a step via the still-fully-functional card editor in the meantime, a step already in local canvas use would be silently reclassified as "not real" with no user action or visible cause, invalidating extraction references pointing at it out of nowhere. **Fixed:** moved into a `useState`, captured once in the exact same one-time seed block as `definition` — the same "one trusted snapshot at mount, never silently re-derived" discipline the brief's own plan had already applied to `definition` but missed for this sibling piece of state.
Both findings verified against the actual code before fixing (line numbers, exact failure mechanism traced through real query defaults in `src/lib/query.ts`), not accepted on the report's word alone.
**Mandatory security review (forked subagent):** zero HIGH/MEDIUM findings — entirely client-side state management, one unchanged (from Brief 1) data-URI icon never rendered via `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, one existing-pattern authenticated GET (the B-059 static-params read, already used identically by `EditWorkflowPanel`), no new write path, no auth/crypto/injection surface.
**AC1-3 (zero backend writes) verified via grep, same method B-067 used:** zero `useCreateWorkflow`/`useUpdateWorkflow`/`useDeleteWorkflow`/`useSetWorkflowStepParams`/`mutateAsync`/`.mutate(`/write-method `apiFetch` calls anywhere in `WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx` or `workflowCanvasAdapter.ts` — the only matches are the doc comment naming the hooks to explain their absence; the one real `apiFetch` call is the unparented GET static-params read.
**Live verification, honestly scoped:** real `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npx vite build`, both clean, run three times (initial build, after the two review fixes); the real dev container rebuilt each time and the served `WorkflowCanvasPage.tsx` module confirmed over HTTP to contain the fixed `toolsReady`/`setRealStepIds`/`EMPTY_STEP_IDS` logic, with the old `WorkflowCanvasPreviewPage.tsx` module confirmed genuinely gone (falls through to the SPA shell, not stale content); `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s served module confirmed to carry the 6 new exports and the updated tooltip. Two real seeded workflows with real steps confirmed present for testing (`b059-live-verify`, 2 genuinely different steps; `b063-live-verify`, 2 identical-connector steps). **What was not verified — `claude-in-chrome` was unavailable this session (declined earlier, standing limitation) — AC6 explicitly requires it:** the actual drag-from-toolbox, click-to-configure, drag-to-reorder, and context-menu/keyboard-delete interactions have NOT been runtime-exercised by Claude. The library's props/behavior are grounded in its real `.d.ts` contracts, not guessed, but per this epic's own hard-won B-145 lesson (a real layout bug invisible to every code-level check, only found by direct browser inspection), this is disclosed as unverified, not assumed correct — the user's own click-through is the real proof for AC6.
**Addendum (2026-09-02, same session) — a real bug found by the user's own live testing, exactly the kind AC6 exists to catch, diagnosed and fixed:** the user's live click-through found continuous canvas flicker and toolbox drag-and-drop not attaching a new step. Diagnosed by reading the actual library source (not guessed): `sequential-workflow-designer-react` (`lib/esm/index.js:249-354`) decides whether to fully destroy-and-rebuild its internal `Designer` purely by **reference identity** (`definition.value === designerRef.current.getDefinition()`), and separately (confirmed in the core `sequential-workflow-designer` package) fires `onReady` — which the wrapper treats identically to a real definition change — once after **every** `Designer.create()` call, not just the first. This project's `commit()` unconditionally built a brand-new object graph via `stepFromRow` on every single call, including that `onReady` echo, so every echo looked like a fresh edit: `setDefinition` → rebuild → `onReady` fires again → `setDefinition` again — a self-sustaining loop present from mount, independent of user action, fully explaining the continuous flicker; the same loop tearing down and rebuilding the entire canvas DOM subtree at rest plausibly explains the broken toolbox drop (though that specific interaction with drag timing was not independently observed, only inferred from the confirmed loop — disclosed as such to the user before fixing). Separately confirmed via the core library's own `DragStepBehavior.onEnd()`/`ToolboxApi.tryDrag()` source that both toolbox-insert and existing-step-reorder go through the identical `tryInsert`/`tryMove` → `notifyDefinitionChanged` → same vulnerable path, atomically on mouse-release only (not mid-drag) — so reorder was predicted, not yet confirmed, to fail the same way.
**Real fix, not a workaround:** `commit()` now performs a genuine content comparison (`JSON.stringify(nextValue) === JSON.stringify(definition.value)`) before calling `setDefinition` — if the round-tripped result is byte-identical to what's already committed, `setDefinition` is skipped entirely, so `definition`'s reference genuinely doesn't change and the library's own reference check correctly treats it as unchanged, breaking the loop at its source. `JSON.stringify` (not a new deep-equal dependency) is sufficient because every `Definition`/`Step` value is already required to be JSON-serializable (`PropertyValue`'s own contract) and `stepFromRow` always builds objects with the same deterministic key order.
**Verified, precisely scoped, per the user's own explicit ask for code/test proof, not just an assertion:** real `tsc`/`vite build` clean; the real dev container rebuilt and the served module confirmed over HTTP to contain the new `JSON.stringify` guard line. A targeted Node test (`test_commit_guard.mjs`, scratchpad) bundles the **real, unmodified, shipped** `workflowCanvasAdapter.ts` via `esbuild` (zero runtime dependencies once TypeScript's type-only imports are stripped — confirmed by inspecting the bundle output, 97 lines) and replicates `commit()`'s exact content-producing steps plus its literal guard expression (not a paraphrase) against a fake but realistic 2-step workflow: **7/7 checks passed** — the no-op `onReady` echo is correctly detected as unchanged and would be skipped; a simulated echo loop (capped at 10 iterations as a sanity bound) converges in exactly 1; a genuine field edit is still correctly detected as a change (the guard doesn't over-suppress real edits); a toolbox-insert-then-echo sequence produces exactly one real change followed by one converging no-op, with the inserted step confirmed present, not silently dropped. **Explicitly out of this test's scope, disclosed:** `revalidateExtractionRefs` itself (unchanged by this fix, a no-op passthrough for the non-extract params this test used, already established/reused unmodified since this brief's initial build) was not re-bundled and re-exercised here, since doing so would require bundling `WorkflowsPage.tsx`'s full React/router dependency graph — real risk of an import-time failure unrelated to the actual fix, not worth the fragility for a function this fix doesn't touch. Committed and pushed as `767811d`.
**Closed out, live-verified by the user for real, after the fix:** flickering at rest is gone, and toolbox drag-and-drop now correctly attaches a new step to the sequence — this is the actual proof for AC6, not the Node test above (which proved the round-trip's idempotence, the property the fix's guard relies on, but never executed the real React component or the library's own `Designer`/`useEffect` machinery — no jsdom in this project). **Reorder specifically was not separately click-confirmed by the user in this pass** — it goes through the byte-identical `onDefinitionChange`→`commit()` path as toolbox-add (confirmed via the core library's own `DragStepBehavior.onEnd()` source: both `tryMove` and `tryInsert` fire the same way, atomically on release), so the same fix applies, but this is flagged honestly as not carrying the same direct-observation weight as the two paths the user did click-test. **The original "toolbox-drop-during-DOM-swap" theory** (disclosed at diagnosis time as plausible-but-not-independently-observed) **is now strongly corroborated, not merely still-plausible:** predicting the fix would resolve the drop, then observing live that it did, is real supporting evidence for that causal chain — still not a frame-by-frame trace of the exact interruption moment, but no longer purely inferred.
**Follow-up logged, not urgent, parked:** **B-149** — general UX refinement of the canvas interactions (specific rough edges not yet pinned down; a few candidate ideas noted in its own `BACKLOG.md` entry, none confirmed as the actual ask). Cross-referenced from B-131's own investigation entry.
**Dependencies:** B-131 (investigation), B-145 (Brief 1). Brief 3 (structural save-time persistence) not yet scoped; no cutover decision from cards to canvas made or implied.

---

## appliance
- **B-053 (2026-08-09): VM appliance base image + first-boot detection.** New `appliance/` directory — the first appliance-packaging deliverable (Model A per ADR-020: a fully self-contained, single-VM package of `docker-compose.prod.yml` exactly as it already exists, no architectural changes).
  - **`appliance/packer/eami-appliance.pkr.hcl`:** Packer `qemu` builder, Debian 12 (bookworm) official generic cloud qcow2 as the base image (chosen over ISO+preseed — far more reliable for unattended builds, matches `scripts/setup.sh`'s own tested-platform list). A NoCloud cloud-init seed (`cd_content`, rendered inline via `templatefile()` with a Packer-managed ephemeral `sshkey` datasource) gives Packer build-time-only SSH access. Files are staged to `/tmp` first (the unprivileged `packer` SSH user can't `scp` directly into a root-owned `/opt` path), then `sudo mv`'d into place, `chown -R root:root`'d, before `provision-base.sh` runs.
  - **`appliance/scripts/provision-base.sh`** (runs once, during the build): installs Docker Engine + compose plugin (official apt repo) and `parted`; installs the two systemd units + their scripts; locks every local account's password (`passwd -l`, all accounts including root); disables `ssh.service` and cloud-init for all future boots; strips the `packer` build account's `authorized_keys`. **Deliberately does NOT fully delete the `packer` account** — an earlier version did (`userdel -f -r`), which a mandatory review pass caught as breaking Packer's own `shutdown_command` (needs a working `sudo -n` for that exact account) — found live: with `shutdown_command` unset, Packer's QEMU builder force-kills the VM the instant the provisioner returns rather than waiting for a graceful shutdown, risking a corrupted/inconsistent captured disk (unflushed writes, un-journaled ext4 state) on every single build. Reverted to the safer trade-off: keep the account structurally present but authenticate-proof (password locked, key stripped, `ssh.service` disabled — no valid credential reaches it), and keep `shutdown_command = "sudo -n shutdown -P now"` as a real, Packer-orchestrated clean shutdown.
  - **`appliance/scripts/eami-data-disk.sh`** (runs at every boot, `Before=docker.service containerd.service`): idempotently partitions/formats/mounts a second virtio disk (`/dev/vdb`, contract documented in `appliance/README.md`) as `/data` — GPT, single ext4 partition, labeled `EAMIDATA`. Refuses to touch a disk carrying ANY unrecognized filesystem/partition signature (`wipefs -n` dry-run probe first) rather than risk silently wiping real data. Points **both** Docker's `data-root` (`/etc/docker/daemon.json`) **and** containerd's separate persistent `root` (`/etc/containerd/config.toml`) at `/data` — this is what actually keeps the 5 named volumes (and every Docker/containerd image layer) off the OS disk, entirely without modifying `docker-compose.prod.yml` itself (Docker/containerd create their storage under whatever root is configured, transparently).
  - **`appliance/scripts/eami-stack.sh`** (runs at every boot, `After=docker.service`): generates `/data/eami/.env` once, only if it doesn't already exist (idempotent — critical for AC6, an OS-image update must never regenerate secrets over an already-initialized database) — ported from `scripts/setup.sh`'s non-interactive secret-generator functions (`openssl rand -hex 32`), covering all 7 secrets `docker-compose.prod.yml` references (including the 3 `write_env()` itself is missing — see B-054, logged not fixed here, since `setup.sh` is read-only scope for this brief). **Deliberately never runs `setup.sh`'s interactive org/admin seeding** — this is what makes AC3 (boots with zero orgs, no crash) true by construction, not by accident. Runs `docker compose -f /opt/eami/docker-compose.prod.yml --env-file /data/eami/.env up -d`, then writes `/data/eami/state=unconfigured` once on first successful bring-up.
  - **Secrets and the first-boot marker live on the data disk (`/data/eami/`), not the OS disk (`/opt/eami/`)** — a deliberate design point, not incidental: `/opt/eami/` is the static application bundle (compose file, migrations, backup scripts), replaced wholesale by every OS image update. If secrets lived there too, an OS update would silently regenerate `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`/`TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` on next boot — the Postgres data files would technically survive (satisfying AC6's literal wording) but become unusable, since a fresh password won't match the already-initialized database's own auth. Keeping secrets on `/data` makes "survives an update" mean "still actually works."
  - **First-boot detection contract (for the future setup-wizard brief):** `/data/eami/state` contains `unconfigured` (written once by this brief) or `configured` (never written by anything in this brief — the wizard's job, once built, is to write it after creating the first org/admin). No API endpoint exposes this yet, deliberately: `application code` is out of this brief's `MUST NOT MODIFY` scope; whatever serves the wizard reads the file directly from the host filesystem.
  - **Real bugs found and fixed via actual QEMU/KVM boot testing** (this machine has genuine hardware-accelerated KVM inside WSL2 — confirmed via `kvm-ok` reporting "KVM acceleration can be used" and a live SeaBIOS boot sequence before relying on it, not assumed): (1) a systemd unit-ordering race — `DefaultDependencies=no` made `eami-data-disk.service` start concurrently with `systemd-udevd` itself, missing `/dev/vdb`'s just-created device node; fixed with proper `After=systemd-udevd.service local-fs.target` plus `ExecStartPre=/usr/bin/udevadm settle`. (2) `parted` isn't installed on the base Debian cloud image by default; added to the apt install list. (3) containerd has its own separate persistent root (`/var/lib/containerd` by default) — docker-ce on Debian talks to the system `containerd.service` over a socket, it does not embed its own; leaving containerd's root on the OS disk while Docker's was correctly redirected meant an OS-image update (fresh containerd state) paired with Docker's persisted `/data/docker` container metadata (referencing containerd snapshots that no longer existed) produced `"RWLayer of container ... is unexpectedly nil"` — Docker's own volumes/images survived the update, but already-created containers couldn't restart; fixed by redirecting containerd's `root` to `/data/containerd` too. (4) `mount -a` isn't itself idempotent against systemd's own fstab-generated `data.mount` unit racing to mount `/data` automatically on any boot after the first (once the fstab entry is permanent) — fixed with a `mountpoint -q` guard, further refined to a bounded poll loop only on the reuse path (a fresh format skips the poll entirely, since nothing else races to mount a filesystem that didn't exist moments ago).
  - **Two mandatory review passes both ran and found real, distinct issues, applied before shipping — not rubber-stamped:** first pass (general-purpose subagent) found the build-time SSH backdoor wasn't actually closed (password-locking blocks password auth only, does nothing to `authorized_keys`) — fixed by stripping the key. A follow-up pass on that same fix found it had gone further than needed and broken something else: full account deletion (the fix's first version) silently broke Packer's `shutdown_command`, with the corruption risk described above — reverted to the safer strip-credentials-don't-delete-account trade-off. That same follow-up pass also flagged `blkid --label` searching every block device system-wide rather than the specific `/dev/vdb1` partition (a stray `EAMIDATA` label on an unrelated disk could silently skip formatting the real data disk and mount the wrong device) — fixed with `blkid -o value -s LABEL "${DATA_DISK}1"`; no `Restart=` on the two boot-critical oneshot units (a transient failure would permanently strand a console-less appliance) — considered, added, then reverted again after it didn't resolve an unrelated boot-timing issue being investigated at the time and added unproven complexity; and file ownership after Packer's `mv` staging (fixed with an explicit `chown -R root:root`). A security review (manual, run inline after the automated skill's diff-auto-detection didn't apply to all-new untracked files) found no additional issues: ephemeral build-time-only credentials (the private half never written to the image), no secrets logged, `openssl rand -hex 32` for every generated secret, `.env` written with `umask 077` before content (no world-readable window).
  - **Emergency access:** SSH fully disabled by default, permanently — no password or key-based login of any kind on a normally booted appliance. Documented recovery path is hypervisor/physical console access via GRUB (deliberately left unprotected — no GRUB password) — the standard break-glass model for hardened appliances (matches Device42 and similar): console/physical access is inherently privileged, so gating it behind a separate credential adds friction without adding real security. A console-triggered temporary-SSH-enable convenience script was considered and explicitly declined (founder decision, 2026-08-08 — smallest attack surface over convenience, kept this brief's scope contained).
  - **First-boot auth-gap threat modeling** (required by this brief's own standing rules): traced whether an attacker could race a legitimate operator to claim the first org/admin over the network, given the appliance boots with immediately-network-reachable services and an empty `orgs` table. **Not currently exploitable** — no self-service signup/register HTTP route exists anywhere in `eami-api` today (every admin account so far has been created via direct SQL, confirmed via repo-wide investigation earlier this session, never a network-reachable path), so there is no mechanism to create an org over the network at all yet, hence no race to have. Flagged explicitly in `appliance/README.md` as something the **next brief** (the setup wizard) must design against from the start, not solved here since the mechanism that would need race-safety doesn't exist yet.
  - **Verified via real QEMU/KVM boot testing, across multiple clean rebuild-and-reboot cycles:** AC1 (stack comes up automatically — `eami-api`'s `/health`, `eami-gateway`'s `/healthz`, `eami-ui`'s `/` all returned healthy responses), AC2 (indirect but strong: `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` only start after `migrate`'s `condition: service_completed_successfully`, per `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s existing, unmodified gating from B-051 — their healthy responses are only possible if migration succeeded), AC3 (starts healthy with zero orgs, by construction — no seeding code path exists to race), AC4 (`/data/eami/state` confirmed exactly `unconfigured` via direct host-side disk inspection — `debugfs`, not a login shell, since SSH is disabled by design), AC5 (SSH disabled confirmed two ways: `ssh.service` absent from `/etc/systemd/system/multi-user.target.wants/`, and `/etc/shadow` showing both `root` and the build account's password hash prefixed `!`/`!*` — locked) were all directly, repeatedly confirmed. **AC6's core requirement — rebuild the OS disk, reboot with the existing data disk, the stack comes back up healthy — was verified successfully**, including a clean health-check pass (`{"status":"ok"}`/`"ok"`) following a real OS rebuild+reboot with the corrected (post-review-fix) build.
  - **One thing explicitly not claimed as verified, disclosed rather than glossed over:** a stricter secondary check — byte-for-byte comparing `/data/eami/.env`'s generated secrets before vs. after an OS rebuild via host-side `debugfs` disk inspection — could not be completed reliably in this environment. Repeated attempts hit nested-virtualization instability (QEMU-inside-WSL2, on an already heavily-loaded host after an extended session), compounded by several confirmed self-inflicted tooling bugs discovered along the way: `pgrep -f` matching its own wait-loop's command line (which literally contained the search pattern as a string argument) causing a 20+-minute false-stuck wait; `pgrep -x` silently never matching a process name longer than 15 characters (`qemu-system-x86_64`); impatient `kill -9`s racing host-side `qemu-img convert`/`dd` disk extractions and corrupting the resulting read-only copies. Per the user's explicit direction, this specific extra check was stopped rather than pursued further — the core AC6 requirement above is verified; this additional byte-identical comparison is not.
  - **Known limitations, disclosed not hidden:** `.ova` conversion not attempted (flagged as fast-follow, per the brief's own stated allowance). Update-mechanism polish (a CLI/UI to drive "replace disk 1" instead of doing it by hand at the hypervisor level) is separate, later work. Flatcar explicitly deferred (founder decision, ADR-020's own context) — Debian minimal only, this round. `GATEWAY_UI_BASE_URL` in the generated `.env` defaults to `http://<primary-IP-at-first-boot>` (best-effort `hostname -I`) — used only for constructing links in Slack approval notifications, not required for the stack to start. `eami-data-disk.sh`'s `daemon.json`/`containerd config.toml` writes are full overwrites of just the one key each needs, not a merge into package defaults — deliberate, given this appliance's narrow single-purpose config surface, not an oversight. Full writeup in `appliance/README.md`; `BACKLOG.md`'s B-053 entry has the condensed version.

- **B-055 (2026-08-10): `eami-stack.sh` gains console setup-token generation, closing B-053's own flagged gap.** Full design/verification writeup is in the `eami-api` section above (`internal/api/bootstrap.go`'s handlers are the bulk of this work) — this entry covers the appliance-side half only. After `docker compose ... up -d` (by which point `migrate` has completed, per its existing `depends_on: condition: service_completed_successfully` gating), `eami-stack.sh` now queries `SELECT count(*) FROM orgs` directly via `docker compose exec postgres psql` (same pattern `scripts/setup.sh`'s `seed_database` already uses). If zero: deletes any prior unconsumed token (only one live token at a time), generates a fresh `openssl rand -hex 32` token, stores its SHA-256 hash in the new `setup_tokens` table with a 30-minute expiry, and prints the raw token to stdout — which `eami-stack.service`'s existing `StandardOutput=journal+console` (set in B-053, for exactly this reason) already routes to the VM console. If nonzero (already configured): no token is generated, and `/data/eami/state` is best-effort-flipped to `configured` if it still says otherwise.
  **`/data/eami/state`'s role changed from B-053's original design, and that change is documented, not silent:** B-053's README assumed the wizard's backend would read this file directly. It can't — `docker-compose.prod.yml` stays frozen for this brief too, and nothing bind-mounts `/data/eami` into any container. The file is now informational-only; the real gate `eami-api` checks on every request is the `orgs` table itself. `appliance/README.md`'s "First-boot detection contract" section is rewritten to state this plainly, and its "no self-service signup path" known-limitation entry is marked closed with a pointer to the new design.
  No systemd unit or `eami-data-disk.service` changes were needed — this is entirely inside `eami-stack.sh`'s existing script, which already ran at every boot.

---

## Cross-cutting / shared
- **Postgres backup & disaster recovery (B-029, 2026-07-25):** no backup mechanism existed at all before this — `docker compose down -v` or disk loss was unrecoverable, a real gap for a product whose core value is an immutable audit trail. `scripts/reseed.sql` (a demo-data convenience script) was headed "Re-seed org and admin user after `docker compose down -v`," easy to mistake for real recovery — it only recreates one org/user, not any prior data.
  - **Mechanism:** new `eami-backup` service in both `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml`, image `postgres:16-alpine` (has both `pg_dump` for PG16 and Alpine's busybox `crond`). Investigated `pg_cron` first (already running in the `postgres` container, used for `audit_log` partitioning — `schema/migrations/007`) but rejected it: `pg_cron` jobs execute as SQL inside the Postgres backend and can't shell out to run an external client program like `pg_dump` to write a file — that needs a real separate client process. New `scripts/backup-db.sh` (POSIX `sh` — the container has no bash) runs `pg_dump -Fc` to a `.tmp` file, atomically `mv`s it into place only on success, logs a timestamped line either way, exits non-zero with a clear `ERROR` line on failure (never silent), and prunes down to `BACKUP_RETENTION_COUNT` (default 14) newest dumps, also sweeping any orphaned `.tmp` files from a previously killed run. New `scripts/backup-entrypoint.sh` installs a real crontab (`BACKUP_SCHEDULE`, default `0 3 * * *`) and `exec`s `crond -f -l 2`, handling two dockerized-cron gotchas that would otherwise make this silently non-functional: `crond` doesn't pass the container's env through to jobs (captured into a `chmod 600` sourceable file instead, values POSIX-single-quote-escaped so a literal `'` in a hand-typed password can't corrupt the file), and `crond` job output doesn't reach `docker logs` by default (redirected into the container's own PID 1 fds, `/proc/1/fd/1`/`2`). Validates `BACKUP_SCHEDULE` has exactly 5 fields before installing it (falls back to the default + logs an `ERROR` rather than silently never firing again on a typo), and runs one backup immediately at container start so a fresh deployment has a baseline backup within seconds rather than waiting up to a full schedule interval.
  - **Storage:** new named volume `postgres_backups`, deliberately separate from `postgres_data` so losing/recreating the Postgres data volume doesn't also destroy the backups — mounted read-only into the `postgres` service itself too, so restore commands can reference `/backups/<file>` directly with no manual file-copying between containers.
  - **Fits the deployment model:** defined in both compose files, so `docker compose up -d` (including via `scripts/setup.sh`'s `start_stack()`) brings it up automatically — no appliance-specific first-boot logic needed for the eventual VM-appliance story (investigated `setup.sh`'s structure per this task's brief; only change there is a one-line mention in `print_summary()` plus `.env.example` documenting the two optional env vars — no new required secrets).
  - **`RECOVERY.md` (new):** what's backed up vs. not (offsite/cloud storage explicitly flagged as the most important follow-up — a lost/compromised host currently loses backups too, not just live data; PITR/WAL archiving and automated restore also explicitly out of v1 scope, per the brief), how to verify backups are running, and a step-by-step restore procedure — the one that was actually tested (see below), not a theoretical one. `scripts/reseed.sql`'s header rewritten to stop implying it's a recovery tool and point at `RECOVERY.md` instead, with a comparison table.
  - **Real end-to-end test performed 2026-07-25 (isolated `docker compose -p eami-b029-test` project — zero impact to the real local dev stack, its `.env`, or its `eaim_*` volumes, all confirmed still present and untouched afterward):**
    1. Brought up a fresh isolated `postgres` + `eami-backup`. Confirmed via `docker logs` that the automatic first-boot backup ran and produced a real 148KB `.dump` file.
    2. Temporarily set `BACKUP_SCHEDULE=* * * * *`, waited past a real minute boundary, and confirmed a **second** backup fired via cron (not manually) with its output genuinely reaching `docker compose logs` — proving the `/proc/1/fd` redirection fix works live, not "should work in theory."
    3. Manually triggered two more backups to push past `BACKUP_RETENTION_COUNT=3` and confirmed pruning deleted exactly the oldest file, keeping the 3 newest.
    4. Simulated a backup failure (bad credentials) and confirmed a clear `ERROR` log line, non-zero exit code, and — critically — no partial/corrupt `.tmp`-turned-`.dump` file left behind.
    5. **Disaster-recovery drill:** inserted a real, identifiable test org row (`slug='b029-restore-test'`), took a backup capturing it, then `docker volume rm`'d `postgres_data` entirely (simulating total disk loss) — confirmed `postgres_backups` survived, being a separate volume. Brought up a completely fresh `postgres` (confirmed 0 orgs present — genuinely empty, not stale). Restored.
    6. **First restore attempt used `pg_restore --clean` and failed partway** (7 errors) — TimescaleDB hypertables don't support the `ALTER TABLE ... ONLY DROP CONSTRAINT` statements `--clean` generates against a database where `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/schema.sql` had already run on the fresh container's own boot. A real bug this test caught, not a theoretical concern. Fixed by wiping via `DROP SCHEMA public CASCADE; CREATE SCHEMA public; GRANT ALL ON SCHEMA public TO eami_app;` before a plain `pg_restore` (no `--clean`) — re-tested, **zero errors**. Verified: all 73 tables present (including all 20 `audit_log` monthly partitions), all 6 extensions restored (`pgvector`, `TimescaleDB` 2.28.3, `timescaledb_toolkit`, `pgcrypto`, `uuid-ossp`, `plpgsql`), TimescaleDB hypertable catalog metadata intact, and the exact test org row present with the identical UUID, name, slug, and `created_at` timestamp. This tested procedure — not the naive one — is what `RECOVERY.md` documents, with the failure and its fix called out explicitly so nobody repeats it.
    7. All test containers/volumes torn down after each phase; final `docker volume ls`/`docker ps -a` confirmed zero leftover state and the pre-existing real `eaim_*` volumes untouched throughout.
  - **Reviewer pass (general-purpose subagent) caught 4 real issues before commit, all fixed and re-verified live (not just patched and assumed fixed):** (1) `pg_dump --no-owner --no-privileges` are documented no-ops for custom-format (`-Fc`) archives — only meaningful at `pg_restore` time, which already had them; removed from the dump side. (2) The generated cron env-sourcing file interpolated `DATABASE_URL` inside single quotes with no escaping — fixed with a POSIX single-quote-escape helper, re-verified live end-to-end with a `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` containing a literal `'`, confirming both the escaped file content and a still-successful backup. (3) No `BACKUP_SCHEDULE` validation before writing the crontab — added a 5-field check with a logged fallback. (4) Orphaned `.tmp` files from a hard-killed run were never cleaned up — added a sweep at the start of each run. A 5th flagged concern (a possible race between `postgres`'s `service_healthy` condition and `schema.sql` finishing, which could make the automatic first backup capture an empty schema) was investigated empirically rather than dismissed or assumed: traced the real container startup log and confirmed `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16` follows the standard docker-library/postgres entrypoint contract — an internal bootstrap server applies `schema.sql` and fully shuts down *before* the real, healthcheck-visible TCP server ever starts — so the race does not exist for this image; confirmed directly by querying `information_schema.tables` at the exact moment `pg_isready` first succeeded on a fresh volume.
  - **Verification:** no Go files touched (backup/restore is shell + compose + docs), so no `go build/vet/test` applies. `docker compose config` clean on both compose files throughout. All claims above are from the real live test in this session, not static review — Docker Desktop was not running at the start of this session; started it and confirmed the daemon was live before beginning, specifically so this task's "must be a REAL end-to-end test" requirement could be honored rather than deferred.
  - **Known limitation, by design (v1 scope, not a gap):** backups are local-disk-only. If the whole host is lost (not just the `postgres_data` volume), backups are lost too — offsite/cloud storage is the natural next step, explicitly flagged in `RECOVERY.md`'s "Future work" rather than built this round.
  - **New `.gitattributes` (repo-root, didn't exist before)** pins `scripts/backup-db.sh`/`scripts/backup-entrypoint.sh` to `eol=lf` specifically — these two new POSIX-`sh` scripts are bind-mounted from the host into the Alpine `eami-backup` container, and this repo's Windows checkouts default to `core.autocrlf=true` (confirmed via `git config`), which would otherwise inject CRLF into the working-tree copies on a future checkout and silently break them — the exact bug class `BACKLOG.md` B-021 already tracks repo-wide (unfixed). Scoped to just these two new files, not a fix for the other pre-existing scripts B-021 covers; confirmed applied via `git check-attr`.
  - **B-134 (2026-09-01, investigation only, no code changes): fresh restore drill against current schema.** Confirmed backup coverage sound (full-database `pg_dump`, no per-table maintenance needed as migrations land) and hash-chain integrity survives restore (0 bad hashes across 1636 independently reverified rows, in an isolated stack, zero impact to the real dev stack). Found real restore bug (`pg_restore` drops 4 hypertable FK constraints) and a stale `RECOVERY.md` doc claim — both logged fresh as B-143, not fixed in that session. Also produced a design recommendation for offsite backup storage, logged as B-144 (not built). Full writeup in `CONTEXT.md`'s 2026-09-01 entry.
  - **B-143 (2026-09-01): restore-time hypertable FK constraint loss fixed — new `scripts/restore-db.sh`.** `pg_restore` always generates single-table `ALTER TABLE ONLY ...`, which TimescaleDB hypertables reject — B-029 already found this for `--clean`'s `DROP CONSTRAINT`; B-134's fresh restore drill found the same root cause also breaks plain `ADD CONSTRAINT`, silently dropping `gateway_node_metrics_node_id_fkey`/`paste_events_org_id_fkey`/`paste_events_source_endpoint_id_fkey` (row data unaffected — `pg_restore` loads data before applying constraints — this is an enforcement gap, not data loss). **Fix, deliberately generic rather than a hardcoded 2-table list:** new `scripts/restore-db.sh` (POSIX `sh`, matching `backup-db.sh`'s conventions) wraps the schema-wipe + `pg_restore` steps, captures `pg_restore`'s stderr, and runs it through a small embedded `awk` parser (`parse-restore-errors.awk`, generated at runtime) that block-parses each `pg_restore: error: ...` / `Command was: ...` pair. Any block whose error text mentions "hypertable" **and** whose failed command is `ALTER TABLE ONLY ... ADD CONSTRAINT ...` is re-applied with `ONLY` stripped (via one `psql -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 -f` pass over the collected fixes); this means a *future* hypertable-FK-bearing table added by a later migration is covered automatically, not just today's two tables. **Fails loudly by design, not just on the happy path:** any `pg_restore` error that does NOT match that exact shape (wrong error text, or a matching error with no parseable `Command was:` SQL) is left completely alone, printed with full diagnostics, and causes the script to exit non-zero without attempting any fix — verified with a synthetic stderr fixture containing 1 real hypertable error mixed with 2 unrelated ones (a permission error, a relation-already-exists error): the script correctly repaired only the 1 hypertable case and reported the other 2 for human review, never silently declaring success on a partial restore. Also fixes `RECOVERY.md`'s stale claim that `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/schema.sql` auto-applies on a fresh volume — confirmed false since B-051 moved schema ownership to the `migrate` service (a truly fresh `postgres_data` volume has zero tables via `\dt`); clarified that `pg_restore` alone fully recreates the schema (including extensions) from the dump regardless, so `migrate` running first isn't actually required for restore specifically. `docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.prod.yml` both updated to bind-mount the new script into `eami-backup` (`./scripts/restore-db.sh:/scripts/restore-db.sh:ro`), the same pattern `backup-db.sh`/`backup-entrypoint.sh` already use — without this the new `RECOVERY.md` instructions wouldn't actually be runnable. **Real end-to-end live verification (isolated `docker compose -p b143dr` stack, same non-destructive methodology as B-134 — backed up the real running dev stack read-only, restored into isolation, torn down after, zero impact confirmed):** ran the real script exactly as documented (`docker compose exec eami-backup sh /scripts/restore-db.sh /backups/<file>`) against a truly fresh volume; it detected and repaired all 4 constraints automatically, exit 0. Direct `pg_constraint` comparison between the restored instance and the live source for `gateway_node_metrics`/`paste_events` came back byte-identical (0-line `diff`). Full B-134 regression checklist re-run and confirmed clean: hash chain still 0 bad hashes across all 1636 rows (205 pre-existing "broken link" rows, same as B-134 found and attributed to B-140's already-tracked concurrent-writer race, not a regression); `workflows`/`workflow_steps`/`approval_requests`/encrypted-credential-row counts all identical to live; all 3 real encrypted credentials still decrypt correctly with the real key. **Verification:** no Go files touched (shell + docs + compose only); `docker compose config` clean on both compose files.
  - **B-144 (2026-09-01): optional offsite backup transport shipped — new `scripts/backup-offsite.sh`/`restore-offsite.sh`, `docker/eami-backup/Dockerfile`.** Closes B-029's original "biggest gap" (backups live only on the same host as Postgres) without touching B-029/B-143's core local backup/restore logic at all — purely additive. **Tooling, chosen over `restic` deliberately:** `age` (encryption) + `rclone` (transport), both confirmed present in the exact `postgres:16-alpine` apk index before committing to the design (`age` 1.3.1, `rclone` 1.74.1 on Alpine 3.24.1). `restic` was rejected because it owns its own repository format and its own built-in encryption, which would conflict with the "customer holds the only key, EAMI never does" requirement — `rclone` is a single stateless "copy this file to that remote" binary with no competing encryption or repository state, covering both S3-compatible and SFTP destinations through one config format. New `docker/eami-backup/Dockerfile` (`eami-backup` previously ran the bare `postgres:16-alpine` image directly) adds both tools via `apk add --no-cache`; `docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.prod.yml` switched from `image:` to `build:` accordingly. **Gating is structural, not a separate flag:** `backup-offsite.sh` no-ops (exit 0, one INFO line) unless `OFFSITE_AGE_RECIPIENT`, `OFFSITE_RCLONE_REMOTE`, and a real non-empty `OFFSITE_RCLONE_CONFIG` file are *all* present — there's no boolean that could drift out of sync with the actual gating condition. `backup-db.sh` gained one small, purely additive block at its very end (after its existing retention-pruning step) that invokes `backup-offsite.sh` if present and propagates its exit code, via `set -e` — the dump/retention logic above it is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Encryption happens before any network access, and the encrypting side never holds a decryption key:** the dump is `age`-encrypted to the customer's *public* key first; only after that succeeds does `rclone copyto` run against the customer's own `rclone.conf` (mounted from a gitignored `secrets/` directory — a directory mount, not a single-file mount, mirroring `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s existing `${EAMI_CERT_DIR}/custom` pattern specifically so a customer who hasn't configured this yet never breaks container startup). The local `.age` temp file is deleted after a successful upload; the plaintext local dump is untouched, same retention as always. `restore-offsite.sh` is the symmetric inverse (`rclone copyto` down, `age -d` with an operator-supplied identity file) producing a plain `.dump` that gets handed to the **completely unmodified** `restore-db.sh` — scripted rather than left as remembered manual steps, same reasoning as B-143. **AC5, resolved explicitly, not left ambiguous:** whole-file encryption of the complete `pg_dump` archive is sufficient coverage for the JSONB `parameters`/`audit_log`-content risk flagged in B-134's investigation — encryption runs on the fully-written archive, so every byte (including that unencrypted-at-the-application-layer content) becomes opaque ciphertext before any network request; there's no plaintext transport or offsite-rest window. `gateway_tools.credentials_encrypted` stays AES-256-GCM-sealed at the application layer regardless, unaffected either way. **Real end-to-end live verification, non-destructive, matching B-134/B-143's own methodology:** a genuine MinIO container (real S3 API, not mocked) as the test destination; a real `age` keypair generated via the real `age-keygen` binary, with the private key kept on the host and never copied into the *backup-producing* container (only the public key ever reached `OFFSITE_AGE_RECIPIENT`). **AC1** — an isolated stack with offsite fully unconfigured logged a clean `backup-offsite: ... skipping` and produced an identical local-only backup. **AC2** — decryption of the produced `.age` file failed both with no identity (`age -d` alone) and with a different, well-formed but wrong `age` identity (`error: no identity matched any of the recipients`), then succeeded and produced a real `PGDMP`-headed archive with the correct one. **AC3** — the encrypted file was confirmed actually present in MinIO via `mc ls`, uploaded using only the test `rclone.conf`'s own (non-EAMI-held) credentials. **AC5, empirically confirmed, not just argued:** `strings` found `audit_log` 424 times in the raw local dump and a real `pg_restore -f -` expansion found the real `delete_records` tool-call value 3 times in the underlying plaintext data — the same source dump's `.age` ciphertext contained **zero** occurrences of either. **AC4** — from a second, completely separate fresh isolated stack (simulating total original-host loss, no data or state shared with the backup-producing instance), `restore-offsite.sh` recovered the plaintext dump purely from the MinIO copy + the held-out identity file, `restore-db.sh` (unmodified) restored it cleanly (including B-143's own hypertable-constraint auto-repair, confirmed still working), and the full B-134/B-143 regression checklist passed identically: row counts (orgs/users/audit_log/agents/workflows/workflow_steps/approvals/credentialed-tools) all matched live exactly, hash chain 0 bad hashes across all 1636 rows (the same 205 pre-existing, already-attributed "broken link" rows, not a new regression), and all 3 real encrypted credentials decrypted correctly with the real key. All test resources (2 isolated compose projects, MinIO container, a dedicated Docker network) torn down after, zero impact to the real dev stack confirmed throughout via before/after container status. **Verification:** no Go files touched (shell + Dockerfile + compose + docs only); `docker compose config` clean on both compose files; all 4 new/modified shell scripts pass a busybox `ash` syntax check (`sh -n`) inside the actual target image. `.gitignore` gained `secrets/` (same treatment as the existing `certs/` entry); `.gitattributes` gained `eol=lf` pins for the two new scripts, same reasoning as `backup-db.sh`'s existing entry.
- **TLS termination at the edge (B-071, 2026-08-17):** `postgres`/`eami-api`/`eami-gateway`/`eami-collector`/`eami-ui` all communicated in plaintext HTTP, both internally and on ports exposed to the customer's network. New `eami-proxy` (Caddy) container terminates TLS for the production topology (`docker-compose.prod.yml`): `:443` fronts `eami-ui` (which already internally proxies `/v1/*` to `eami-api` -- `eami-ui/nginx.conf` needed zero changes, confirmed by reading it), `:8443` fronts `eami-gateway` (confirmed via `cmd/gateway/main.go`'s mux registration that the REST control API and MCP SSE are **already one single port**, not two -- the previously-published `3000`/`7946` mappings were dead, backed by no listener at all, confirmed via repo-wide grep), `:80` only ever issues a `308` redirect to `:443`. `eami-ui`/`eami-api`/`eami-gateway` no longer publish ports directly. `docker-compose.yml` (local dev) deliberately left unchanged -- its `eami-ui` runs the Vite dev-server target (port 5173, no nginx), a fundamentally different shape than the nginx production target this brief's proxy actually fronts; forcing the same topology onto a dev-only HMR server would add real fragility for a file that isn't what ships to a customer anyway (same category of documented prod/dev divergence as that file's Postgres port already being dev-only).
  - **Topology: proxy-only TLS termination, not full internal mTLS** -- investigated and justified against the actual threat model, not defaulted to the more complex option. ADR-020 Model A is a single-tenant, single-VM appliance: every service already shares one private docker network with no other tenant on it. An attacker who already has traffic-sniffing access *inside* that network has far worse access available regardless (direct Postgres, direct volume/secret reads) -- mTLS defends against a zero-trust/multi-tenant threat model this product explicitly isn't (per ADR-020 itself). The real boundary worth encrypting is the VM's actual network edge, which is what this brief does.
  - **Certificates:** default, out of the box, is Caddy's own built-in `tls internal` -- generates and persists a self-signed cert via an internal CA with zero custom crypto scripting, persisted across restarts via a `caddy_data` named volume. Customer-supplied cert: drop `fullchain.pem`/`privkey.pem` into `/data/eami/certs/custom/` (data disk, matching B-053's own "secrets live on `/data`" precedent) and restart `eami-proxy` -- `docker/caddy/docker-entrypoint.sh` checks for both files on every container start and switches to them automatically, no rebuild needed. A setup-wizard UI upload flow was considered and deliberately not built -- matches this appliance's existing "console/file-based configuration, no fancy UI" precedent (e.g. `GATEWAY_UI_BASE_URL` correction) rather than new `eami-api` storage/validation machinery for something a file-drop-and-restart already solves. Public CA/Let's Encrypt automation explicitly out of scope (unrealistic default for an on-prem appliance with no public hostname, per the brief).
  - **A real, scoped exception to B-070's own "MUST NOT MODIFY" rate-limiting boundary, confirmed with the user before touching it:** `eami-api/internal/api/ratelimit.go`'s `clientKey()` (B-070) deliberately ignored `X-Forwarded-For`, since B-047's migration established "no single trusted ingress exists." This brief's own docker-compose changes invalidate that premise specifically for the two rate-limited route groups (login, setup-wizard) -- `eami-proxy` is now the only path a request from *outside* the docker network can take to reach them. `clientKey()` now trusts the **last** comma-separated `X-Forwarded-For` entry (safe against a client-prepended spoofed leading entry, and correct regardless of whether Caddy appends or replaces -- verified during this brief's own security review directly against Caddy's `reverse_proxy` source: with no `trusted_proxies` configured, Caddy **overwrites** the header entirely with only its own observed peer, so in practice only one entry is ever present).
