The global `middleware.ClientIPFromRemoteAddr` (`router.go`, used by every other consumer) is completely untouched -- this is a narrowly-scoped exception confined to `clientKey()` alone, not a reversion of B-047's fix.
  - **A real gap in that first fix, found by this brief's own mandatory security review and closed before shipping:** closing `eami-api`'s published port only blocks access from *outside* the host -- `docker-compose.prod.yml` has no network segmentation, so any *other* container on the same compose network (e.g. `eami-collector`, which has its own real external-facing attack surface via endpoint-agent report ingestion) could still reach `eami-api:8081` directly by service name and send an arbitrary `X-Forwarded-For`, defeating login/setup-wizard rate limiting via an unrelated compromise. Closed with `trustedProxyPeer` (new package var, overridable in tests): `X-Forwarded-For` is trusted only when the request's actual TCP peer resolves via live DNS lookup to `eami-proxy`'s own address; any other caller falls back to the strict `RemoteAddr`-based behavior, unable to spoof anything. Ordered so the DNS lookup only ever runs when `X-Forwarded-For` is actually present (an early version checked trust unconditionally on every request, adding a real, unintended multi-second-per-request slowdown to the full test suite once discovered -- fixed before it reached any live traffic).
  - **Two more real findings from the same mandatory review, both fixed:** (1) the generated `:80` redirect block originally echoed the client-supplied `Host` header verbatim into the `Location` (`redir https://{host}{uri}`) -- a genuine open-redirect/phishing primitive (a request with a forged `Host` gets redirected to an attacker-chosen domain). Fixed with a fixed `EAMI_PUBLIC_HOST` value (sourced from `eami-stack.sh`'s already-correct host-level `hostname -I` detection, the same value `GATEWAY_UI_BASE_URL` uses -- never derived from client input or auto-detected inside the container, which would only see its own internal docker-network address). (2) `tls internal` applied to a bare-port site address (`:443`, no host) gives Caddy's internal issuer no fixed identifier to bind the self-signed cert's SAN to -- ambiguous for the common case of a browser connecting to a raw IP with no SNI (RFC 6066). Fixed by qualifying the site address with `EAMI_PUBLIC_HOST` specifically in the self-signed branch (kept bare for the customer-cert branch, where a real cert may cover a different DNS name than the detected IP and TLS should accept any host/SNI regardless).
  - **A real upgrade-survival gap, found by the same review and closed:** `EAMI_CERT_DIR`/`EAMI_PUBLIC_HOST` were originally only written into `eami-stack.sh`'s generated `.env` on first boot -- an appliance already provisioned *before* this brief shipped keeps its existing `.env` untouched on a later OS-image update (the exact "AC6 survives an update" scenario this script's own header comment exists to protect), silently breaking the documented customer-cert path (falls back to a path relative to `/opt/eami`, the OS disk, wiped on every update) with no error. Fixed with an idempotent, append-only backfill in the "reusing existing secrets" branch, not just the fresh-boot branch.
  - **Test coverage:** `eami-api/internal/api/ratelimit_clientkey_test.go` -- 7 white-box tests: single/multiple/whitespace `X-Forwarded-For` entries, `RemoteAddr` fallback when absent, two different values producing two different keys (the actual functional payoff), an untrusted-peer regression test (spoofed header from a non-`eami-proxy` peer is completely ignored), and a real (not mocked) `defaultTrustedProxyPeer` call proving the fail-closed behavior against an unresolvable name. **Verified 2026-08-17: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` clean across the full `eami-api` module**, zero regressions in any pre-existing test (including `router_realip_test.go`, left completely untouched since it tests the separate, unmodified global middleware).
  - **Live-verified end-to-end against a real, isolated, fully-built (not stale-pinned-image) instantiation of `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s new topology** (a scratch build-override swapped the file's pinned `ghcr.io` tags -- which do exist but predate this session's B-070/B-071 changes -- for fresh local builds matching `docker-compose.yml`'s own build contexts, isolated under a distinct compose project so the real dev stack was untouched throughout): **AC1**: real `openssl s_client`/`curl -v` handshakes confirmed genuine TLS 1.3 on both `:443` and `:8443` (`issuer=CN=Caddy Local Authority`, the expected internal CA); direct host-port connections to `eami-api`/`eami-gateway`/`eami-ui`'s old ports confirmed refused after stopping an unrelated leftover dev-stack container that had briefly made the check ambiguous; `eami-proxy`'s own `:80` confirmed to only ever return a `308`, never content. **AC2**: self-signed cert confirmed live (`subject=` empty, internal-CA issuer) with the appliance fully functional on it, then a real generated test cert dropped into `/certs/custom/` + `eami-proxy` restarted, confirmed the served cert switched to the real one (`subject=CN=customer-supplied-test-cert`) with zero functional regression. **AC3**: a real bootstrap + real login (`200`, real signed JWT) over `https://.../v1/auth/login`; a real agent registered via the admin API and a real workflow-run call dispatched over `https://.../v1/gateway/workflows/.../run` through `:8443` -- confirmed reaching the real handler (real `400`s for a bogus workflow id, not blocked earlier). **AC4**: B-070's rate limiting confirmed working correctly *through* the proxy -- real per-account and per-agent `429`s with real `Retry-After` headers at the expected thresholds. **The critical anti-spoofing proof, run twice**: a single fixed account bombarded with a different forged `X-Forwarded-For` on every request still rate-limited normally (proves per-account limiting is unaffected by the header either way); then, definitively, 21 requests each with a **different** spoofed `X-Forwarded-For` **and** a different account collapsed into the *same* real per-IP bucket and tripped together -- if Caddy had trusted the spoofed values, every request would have looked like a new IP and the limiter would never have tripped at all. **AC5**: topology reasoning documented above and in `appliance/README.md`. Verification stack fully torn down afterward (containers, volumes, network); the real dev stack's containers (briefly stopped only to disambiguate the port-reachability check) restarted, confirmed unaffected.
  - **Known limitation at the time, closed by B-072 (2026-08-18, see immediately below):** `eami-collector`'s endpoint-agent-facing port (`8888`) was **not** covered by this brief -- confirmed against the task brief's own CONTRACTS/ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA, which named only the UI/API/gateway surfaces, never the collector. Logged with priority as its own scoped backlog item at the time; now done.
  - **Not touched, out of scope (disclosed, not silently skipped):** `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s `eami-proxy` service uses `build:` rather than a pinned `ghcr.io` image, a deliberate, commented exception to that file's own all-pinned-images convention -- this session has no CI/registry access to publish a real image, and a compose file referencing a tag that doesn't exist yet isn't actually runnable or verifiable. Publishing a real pinned image via the existing CI pipeline is a natural follow-up (not CI config this session was authorized to touch). `scripts/setup.sh` (a separate, older, non-appliance production setup path) was not updated for the new `EAMI_CERT_DIR`/`EAMI_PUBLIC_HOST` values -- investigated and found to already reference environment-variable names (`GATEWAY_API_PORT`, `VITE_API_URL`, etc.) that don't match `docker-compose.prod.yml`'s real current shape at all, suggesting it predates and was superseded by `eami-stack.sh`'s appliance-based flow; fixing that pre-existing staleness is a separate concern from this brief.

- **TLS + cert-trust distribution for eami-collector (B-072, 2026-08-18):** closes B-071's own disclosed gap -- `eami-collector`'s endpoint-agent-facing port (`8888`), reachable across the customer's whole internal network (not confined to the appliance's own docker network the way the other three surfaces are), had zero TLS and a single, static, shared `COLLECTOR_API_KEY` as its only authentication.
  - **The real threat closed, stated precisely:** before this brief, any device with LAN network position to the collector could passively read the shared API key and full report contents (including discovered AI-tool-usage details) off the wire in plaintext. After: that eavesdropping/plaintext-key-harvesting path is closed. **Not closed** (per-agent auth's job, deferred to B-073): an attacker who obtains the shared key through any *other* means (a compromised endpoint's own local config/registry, an insider) can still impersonate any other agent.
  - **Mechanism: extended `eami-proxy` rather than building native Go TLS into the collector.** `eami-collector` already had dormant, unused `TLSCertPath`/`TLSKeyPath` config fields (`cmd/collector/main.go`) -- left untouched. A 4th Caddy site block (`docker/caddy/docker-entrypoint.sh`), `${SITE_8888}` → `reverse_proxy eami-collector:8888`, reuses the exact same self-signed/customer-cert logic B-071 already built and reviewed. `eami-collector` no longer publishes a port directly in `docker-compose.prod.yml`; `eami-proxy` does.
  - **Cert-trust distribution -- the brief's single most important usability constraint, closed by extending `eami-agent`'s existing ADR-014 registry-fallback pattern, not inventing a new one:** new `CollectorConfig.CACertPath` (`internal/config/config.go`), with the identical Windows-registry-fallback treatment `CollectorURL`/`CollectorAPIKey` already get (`applyRegistry` gains `CollectorCACertPath`). `internal/collector/sender.go`'s `Sender.New` now returns `(*Sender, error)` (two `cmd/agent/main.go` call sites updated) and, when `CACertPath` is set, builds an `http.Client` with a custom `Transport{TLSClientConfig: &tls.Config{RootCAs: pool}}` -- `pool` is `x509.SystemCertPool()` **plus** the appliance's CA (additive, never replacing the OS trust store); unset behaves byte-identical to pre-B-072. A new install-time parameter (Windows MSI `COLLECTOR_CA_CERT_PATH` property; Linux/macOS `EAMI_COLLECTOR_CA_CERT_PATH` env/Jamf param) flows through the exact same per-invocation pipe as `COLLECTOR_URL`/`COLLECTOR_API_KEY` -- deliberately a **file path**, never inline PEM content, avoiding real quoting/length fragility of embedding multi-line certificate data in an `msiexec`/shell command line. `appliance/README.md` documents extracting the appliance's CA from the running `eami-proxy` container (`docker compose exec eami-proxy cat /data/caddy/pki/authorities/local/root.crt`) and staging it for installer invocations, alongside a scoping note on why per-agent auth wasn't also built here.
  - **Four real bugs found by this brief's own mandatory security review, all fixed before shipping:**
    1. **A real B-ID process mistake, caught by the reviewer, not self-noticed.** This work was built end-to-end under a freshly-minted "B-073" before the review flagged that `BACKLOG.md` already had a `B-072` entry -- reserved for exactly this scope, by this session's own prior work closing out B-071. Renamed throughout (code comments, tests, docs) rather than leaving a permanently-stale open B-072 sitting alongside an unreserved B-073. This is precisely the failure mode the user's own standing feedback memory on B-ID assignment exists to prevent (check existing reservations, not just the file's counter) -- the counter alone was insufficient here because the reservation was made in a *previous* session, not the current roadmap conversation the memory's own guidance anticipates.
    2. **A real pre-planting/TOCTOU vulnerability in the documented CA-staging path.** The Linux/macOS installer docs originally recommended `/tmp/eami-collector-ca.pem` -- world-writable. A local unprivileged attacker could pre-plant their own CA at that well-known path before the real (root-run) install, and the postinstall script would silently `cp` and permanently trust it, enabling a MITM of all collector traffic including the API key, invisible to the whole point of this design (no human watching for a cert warning). Fixed with a real ownership/permission check in both `postinstall.sh` and macOS `postinstall` before trusting any staged file: source must be root-owned and not group/other-writable, using correct **bitwise** (not numeric-magnitude) permission-bit testing -- verified with a standalone smoke test across representative octal modes (a naive `[ "$perm" -gt 644 ]` check would have missed e.g. mode `624`, numerically less than 644 but still group-writable). Documentation updated to stop recommending `/tmp`.
    3. **The Windows MSI had no persistent-copy step, unlike Linux/macOS.** The registry pointed straight at the original staged path; SCCM/Intune commonly clean up staging/download caches after a package finishes installing, which would silently break CA trust (falling back to the OS default store, failing every handshake) the next time the agent service restarted. Fixed with a new `CopyCollectorCACert` custom action (`Product.wxs`) copying into a permanent location under `INSTALLFOLDER`, verified with a real local `wix build` before ever attempting a live install.
    4. A misleading copy-pasted comment in `cmd/agent/main.go` claiming a short-lived, per-browser-connection native-messaging-host invocation was a "long-lived background service" (copied from the sibling `runLoop` call site, where that reasoning is actually correct) -- corrected to describe the function it's actually in.
  - **A second real bug, self-inflicted by this brief's own fix for #3 above, caught only by literally installing the MSI -- not by re-reading the XML:** the `SetProperty` computing the permanent registry path was scheduled `Before="CostFinalize"`, but `INSTALLFOLDER` isn't resolved to a real filesystem path until `CostFinalize` itself runs -- so the registry value came out as a bare relative filename (`collector-ca.pem`, no directory) even though the actual file copy (correctly scheduled after `InstallFiles`) succeeded and the file really was sitting at the right location. A real install + `Get-ItemProperty` readback caught what code review of the XML alone did not. Fixed by rescheduling to `After="CostFinalize"`; rebuilt, reinstalled, reconfirmed the registry value resolves to the full absolute path.
  - **Per-agent authentication: investigated, not silently skipped, deliberately deferred -- see `BACKLOG.md`'s new B-073.** Real scoping questions found: `api_keys`' schema has no agent-identity linkage (just a free-text label), no key-minting mechanism exists (`RegisterKey` is unreachable dead code), and a real distribution-logistics decision is needed (pre-generated key pool vs. self-enrollment) that would change today's "one identical value for every install command" zero-touch simplicity. Confirmed with the user before scoping this out of the current brief.
  - **Test coverage:** `eami-agent/internal/collector/sender_test.go` (new, 5 tests) uses a real `httptest.NewTLSServer` -- genuine TLS handshakes, not mocked transports -- proving both a positive case (trusted CA succeeds) and a negative control (an untrusted self-signed server genuinely fails the handshake, proving the positive case isn't accidentally permissive), plus error-path tests for a missing/invalid cert file. `eami-agent/internal/config/config_test.go` gained 3 tests mirroring the existing `CollectorURL`/`CollectorAPIKey` registry-fallback coverage for the new field (including the "YAML wins over registry" and "field is genuinely optional, not an error when absent" cases). **Verified 2026-08-18: `go build ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `go test ./...` clean across both the full `eami-agent` and `eami-collector` modules**, zero regressions in any pre-existing test.
  - **Live-verified end-to-end, including a real Windows MSI install driven through the actual documented `msiexec` flow -- not just Go unit tests -- against a real, isolated, fully-rebuilt instantiation of the stack (same scratch build-override pattern as B-071, reused):** real TLS 1.3 handshake inspected directly (`openssl s_client`) on the collector's new `:8888` listener, confirmed for both the self-signed default (`issuer=CN=Caddy Local Authority`) and a customer-cert override (`subject=CN=customer-supplied-test-cert`), and confirmed the port no longer speaks plaintext HTTP at all. **AC1/AC2**: a real `eami-agent-1.0.0-windows-amd64.msi`, built from the actual current agent binary and the fixed `Product.wxs`, installed silently via the real documented command line with `COLLECTOR_CA_CERT_PATH` pointing at a locally-extracted copy of the appliance's real CA -- service started automatically, **zero manual certificate approval at any point** (this is a background service; no human was watching for a warning). Confirmed not by inspection but by the collector's own container logs showing a real `"report buffered"` / `POST /v1/ingest status=202` from the live Windows machine's real hostname. **The negative control, run live, not just as a unit test:** clearing the registry's `CollectorCACertPath` and restarting the service produced **zero new requests at the collector at all** -- the TLS handshake failed client-side before any data was ever sent, proving true fail-closed behavior, not a silent plaintext fallback; restoring the correct value immediately resumed successful reporting on the very next scan cycle. **AC3**: confirmed the collector's own `/v1/ingest` ingestion logic is completely unaffected by this brief -- a separate, pre-existing, unrelated gap in `docker-compose.prod.yml` (no `COLLECTOR_SERVICE_KEY` configured for the collector→`eami-api` forwarder hop, confirmed absent from that file since before this brief via diff) caused an unrelated `401` on that *downstream* leg, not on the agent→collector path this brief actually changed. Full teardown/uninstall verified clean afterward (service, registry key, and every MSI-tracked file removed) -- one disclosed, minor known limitation: `CopyCollectorCACert`'s copied file lives outside WiX's own File table (by design, since it's sourced from an external staged path, not part of the MSI package), so it survives uninstall as a harmless orphan; not fixed this round.
  - **Known limitation, disclosed:** per-agent authentication remains a single shared key -- see B-073 above for the real scoping questions and why it wasn't folded into this brief.

- **Config hygiene cleanup (B-028/B-030/B-031, 2026-07-25):** three small, low-risk doc/script fixes discovered during the B-025/026/027 security work — none of them live vulnerabilities, all closed in one session, no application code touched.
  - **B-028:** `eami-api/eami-api.yaml` (`database.dsn`, `service_key`) and `eami-gateway/eami-gateway.yaml` (`postgres_dsn`, `api.service_key`) still shipped the literal `"changeme"` as example config — misleading now that B-025's `validate()` rejects that exact value at startup. All 4 replaced with `""` plus a short comment naming the real env-var override (`API_DB_HOST/NAME/USER/PASSWORD`, `API_SERVICE_KEY`, `GATEWAY_DB_HOST/NAME/USER/PASSWORD`, `GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY`) and noting `validate()` rejects empty/placeholder — mirrors `config.go`'s own `defaults()` comments ("no default ServiceKey/DSN shipped on purpose"), not a new convention. Confirmed via `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` that neither service's Dockerfile/default invocation actually depends on the YAML's literal values: both services always get their real DSN/service-key from env vars in the compose files, which `config.go`'s `Load()` unconditionally overlays onto whatever the YAML provided — so blanking the YAML defaults cannot change any running container's behavior.
  - **B-030:** `scripts/seed-db.sh` and `scripts/create-audit-partition.sh` both had `DB_URL="${DATABASE_URL:-postgresql://eami_app:devpassword@localhost:5432/eami}"` — the same silently-guessable-default pattern B-025 already closed in `docker-compose.yml`'s `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`. Both scripts now check `[[ -z "${DATABASE_URL:-}" ]]` first and `exit 1` with an actionable message ("DATABASE_URL must be set — see .env.example. Refusing to fall back to a guessable default connection string.") before ever constructing a connection string — same fail-closed contract as B-025's Go-side `validate()`. Each script's header comment updated to match (previously claimed a default/override relationship that no longer exists). `bash -n` syntax-checked clean on both.
  - **B-031:** `API_JWT_SECRET` — generated by `scripts/setup.sh`'s `gen_jwt_secret()`, documented in `.env.example` as an "HS256 fallback," wired through both compose files as an env var — but a repo-wide grep for `JWT_SECRET`/`JwtSecret`/`JWTSecret` confirmed zero Go code anywhere reads it; `eami-api/internal/auth`'s `Service` signs/verifies exclusively via RS256. Removed entirely, per this task's stated preference for full removal over a reserved-with-comment approach — confirmed first that nothing in `setup.sh`'s own flow (`start_stack`, `seed_database`, `print_summary`) depends on the variable's value beyond writing it to `.env`. Deleted: `gen_jwt_secret()` itself, its Step-5 generation call, `write_env`'s `api_jwt_secret` parameter and the `API_JWT_SECRET=...` heredoc line (remaining positional parameters renumbered/shifted at both the function signature and its one call site), `.env.example`'s whole `API_JWT_SECRET` block, and one line each from `docker-compose.yml`/`docker-compose.prod.yml`. `bash -n` syntax-checked `setup.sh` clean after the parameter-shift; re-grepped the full repo afterward and confirmed zero remaining references outside this session's own doc/log entries (`BUILT.md`/`BACKLOG.md`/`CONTEXT.md` — history, not live config).
  - **Verification:** no Go files were touched by this task (confirmed via `git diff --stat` against the 8 changed files, all YAML/shell/compose/env), so no `go build`/`go vet`/`go test` run applies. `docker compose config` (which parses and resolves the compose YAML, including all env-var substitution, without needing a running daemon) was run against both `docker-compose.yml` and `docker-compose.prod.yml` with a full set of required env vars supplied on the command line — both resolved cleanly (exit 0), and the resolved `eami-api` service block in each was inspected directly to confirm `API_JWT_SECRET` no longer appears while every other environment value (`API_SERVICE_KEY`, `API_DB_PASSWORD`, etc.) is unchanged. The Docker daemon itself was not running in this session (confirmed via `docker info`), so a live `docker compose up` re-verification of the running stack was not possible here — `docker compose config`'s clean resolution is the evidence produced for this task's AC #4, not a substitute for a live check if one is wanted later.
- **`POSTGRES_PASSWORD` fail-closed fix (B-025, 2026-07-24):** `docker-compose.yml` had `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:-devpassword}` in all 3 places it's consumed (`postgres`, `eami-gateway`'s `GATEWAY_DB_PASSWORD`, `eami-api`'s `API_DB_PASSWORD`) — an unset env var meant Postgres silently started with a guessable password. `docker-compose.prod.yml` had no textual fallback (bare `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD}`), so an unset var there passed an empty string through to the official Postgres image, which does refuse to start without a real password — but as a Postgres-container crash-loop, not a clean EAMI-level error. Both files' 3 occurrences (6 total) now use compose's native `${POSTGRES_PASSWORD:?POSTGRES_PASSWORD must be set — see .env.example}` syntax, which aborts `docker compose up`/`run` before any container is created if unset — confirmed fail-closed both by reading compose's documented `:?` semantics and by live-testing `docker compose config` with the var unset. `.env.example`'s `POSTGRES_PASSWORD=changeme` and `DATABASE_URL=postgresql://eami_app:changeme@...` are now blank with instructive comments (matching how `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY` was already documented); `API_SERVICE_KEY`/`GATEWAY_API_SERVICE_KEY` were already blank, just gained a clarifying "both services refuse to start if this is unset or a placeholder" comment. See the `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` sections above for the app-level `validate()` half of this fix (the same known-bad values are now rejected at the Go level too, not just relying on the compose/Postgres-image layer) and for the live verification against the currently-running local stack's real secrets.
- **`docker-compose.yml` build-context fix (2026-07-22):** `eami-ui` service had `build.context: ./eami-ui` while `eami-ui/Dockerfile` copies `api/openapi.yaml` (a repo-root path, needed for `generate-client`) — `docker compose up --build` failed with `COPY api/openapi.yaml /api/openapi.yaml: not found`. Fixed to `context: .` / `dockerfile: eami-ui/Dockerfile`. `eami-api`'s service was never affected — its Dockerfile has no path outside its own `./eami-api` context, so it wasn't a valid template to copy from. Verified with `docker compose build eami-ui` — clean. `docker-compose.prod.yml` unaffected (pulls a prebuilt `ghcr.io` image, no build context).
- **Docker is confirmed available on this machine as of 2026-07-22** (`docker build`/`docker compose build` both ran successfully this session) — corrects the "no Docker in this environment" notes elsewhere in this doc and in `BACKLOG.md` B-004, which were accurate when written but are now stale for this point specifically. Full-stack `docker compose up` end-to-end run still not performed.
- **Schema:** `schema/schema.sql` + `schema/migrations/001`–`007` (sequential, no gaps). Table references in Go store code cross-checked clean except `notification_channels` (dead, see above).
- **Multi-tenancy:** enforced at the application layer — every store query takes an explicit `org_id` param (e.g. `WHERE org_id = $1`), not Postgres RLS (RLS is used only for `audit_log` append-only enforcement per ADR-007). ADR-013 (org-per-schema vs. RLS) is still listed Pending in `DECISIONS.md`. Correctness currently depends on every handler remembering to pass `org_id` — no defense-in-depth. Not verified exhaustively this session.
- **Secrets/auth posture (from `tasks/TASK-051-security-findings.md`, all HIGH findings closed):** bcrypt cost 12 (TASK-066), JWT RS256 with explicit algorithm allowlist, constant-time API-key comparison, SHA-256-hashed API keys, `crypto/rand` throughout, JWT revocation persisted + issuer-validated (TASK-062/063), audit-log DB-error propagation fixed (TASK-064), audit chain verify endpoint added (TASK-065, `GET /v1/audit/verify`).
- **Toolchain gap:** this development machine has no `go` or `node`/`npm` installed. No build or test command in this doc was actually executed — all status above is from static source review. Treat every "STABLE" tag as "reads correct" rather than "verified green." **No longer accurate as of B-047 below** — `go` (1.26.5) is installed and on a documented path per `CLAUDE.md`'s toolchain note, and Docker is confirmed working; real `go build`/`vet`/`test` and real `npm`-via-Docker runs are now routine for this repo, not the exception.

- **B-047 (2026-08-06): security dependency updates + container hardening.** Closed 5 confirmed-reachable Go CVEs, one chi deprecation with header-spoofing implications, two containers running as root, floating base-image tags on all 4 service Dockerfiles, and one Dockerfile comment that actively misrepresented its own container's security posture. Every finding was re-verified against current code before fixing (not trusted from the audit brief as-is) — one line number had drifted (`router.go:127`→`:116`, code unchanged) and two of the brief's own assumptions turned out wrong, both surfaced and resolved with the user before building rather than silently worked around.
  - **CVE fixes (all verified via `govulncheck`, 0 reachable vulnerabilities post-bump, across all 5 modules):** `eami-gateway`: `jackc/pgx/v5` v5.6.0→v5.9.2 (GO-2026-5004/CVE-2026-33815/33816, SQL injection + memory-safety), `golang-jwt/jwt/v5` v5.2.1→v5.2.2 (GO-2025-3553/CVE-2025-30204, unbounded-allocation DoS in `ParseUnverified`), `golang.org/x/text`→v0.39.0 (GO-2026-5970, infinite loop — not picked up by plain `go mod tidy`, since pgx's own `go.mod` only floors it at v0.29.0; required an explicit `go get golang.org/x/text@v0.39.0`). `eami-api`: same pgx (v5.7.2→v5.9.2)/jwt/x/text fixes, plus `go-chi/chi/v5` v5.1.0→v5.3.0 (RealIP's spoofing-class CVEs, see below). `eami-agent`: `golang.org/x/sys` v0.21.0→v0.44.0 (GO-2026-5024/CVE-2026-39824, Windows `NewNTUnicodeString` integer overflow — confirmed present but unimported by any current code path, bumped anyway per the brief's own "cheap, removes latent risk" reasoning).
  - **Cascading discovery, not in the original brief:** pgx v5.9.2 and x/text v0.39.0 both declare `go 1.25.0` in their own `go.mod` — confirmed by fetching each version's real `go.mod` from the module proxy, not assumed. This forced `go.work` and both affected modules' `go` directives from `1.24`→`1.25.0` (the workspace-mode tooling auto-synced `go.work` during the `go get`/`go mod tidy` runs), which in turn forced `eami-api`/`eami-gateway`'s Dockerfile builder stage off `golang:1.24-alpine` (verified necessary, not just prudent: `eami-gateway`'s Dockerfile sets `GOTOOLCHAIN=local`, so an unbumped builder image would have hard-failed rather than auto-downloading a newer toolchain) and CI's 4 `go-version: '1.24'` pins in `.github/workflows/build.yml`. Both of the latter were out of this task's original `MAY MODIFY` list — surfaced to the user explicitly before touching either (Dockerfile bump self-evidently required by the CVE fix already approved; CI pin change required a deliberate, user-confirmed exception to `CLAUDE.md`'s "never touch CI config" hard rule) rather than silently done or silently skipped.
  - **chi `RealIP`→`ClientIPFromRemoteAddr`** (`eami-api/internal/api/router.go`): chi deprecated `RealIP` in v5.3.0 for mutating `r.RemoteAddr` and unconditionally trusting `X-Forwarded-For`/`X-Real-IP` with no configurable trust boundary (GHSA-3fxj-6jh8-hvhx and siblings — real IP spoofing, not just a style deprecation). **Verified functionally inert before touching it**: a repo-wide grep for `RemoteAddr`/`ClientIP`/`X-Forwarded-For` inside `eami-api` found zero consumers of the resolved client IP anywhere in production code — nothing reads it today, so the swap changes no observable behavior, only closes the deprecated/spoofable code path for whenever something eventually does. Chose `ClientIPFromRemoteAddr` (trust only the raw TCP source, not any header) over a header-trust variant because `eami-api` is reachable two ways simultaneously — via `eami-ui`'s nginx proxy (which does set `X-Forwarded-For`/`X-Real-IP`, confirmed in `eami-ui/nginx.conf`) and directly on its own published Docker port — so there is no single trusted ingress that would justify trusting a caller-supplied header. New `router_realip_test.go`, 2 tests, proving directly (not just by code-reading) that a forged `X-Forwarded-For: 10.0.0.1`/`X-Real-IP: 10.0.0.2` on a request with `RemoteAddr = "203.0.113.7:54321"` still resolves `middleware.GetClientIP` to `203.0.113.7`, and a header-less request resolves correctly too.
  - **Non-root containers (`eami-api`, `eami-gateway`, matching `eami-collector`'s existing correct su-exec-based pattern):** added `USER 65532:65532` (distroless's own nonroot convention UID, numeric so it needs no `/etc/passwd`/`/etc/group` lookup — required for `eami-gateway`'s `FROM scratch` runtime stage, which has neither file) to both. **A real, non-obvious regression found by this task's own mandatory code-review pass, not caught by the initial live-verification run:** both `eami-api` and `eami-gateway` self-generate and persist an RSA JWT signing key to disk on first boot (`auth.loadOrGenerateKey`/`identity.loadOrGenerateKey`, both pre-existing from B-026/B-002-era work, unchanged by this task) — `eami-api` to `API_JWT_KEY_PATH` (`/certs/api.key`, the `api_certs` named volume) and `eami-gateway` to `GATEWAY_JWT_KEY_PATH` (`/certs/gateway.key`, `gateway_certs`) when those env vars are set (both compose files set them), or to `eami-gateway`'s hardcoded fallback `/var/lib/eami-gateway/gateway.key` (`config.go`'s `validate()`) when `GATEWAY_JWT_KEY_PATH` is unset. A brand-new named or anonymous Docker volume mounted over a path with no pre-existing content in the image defaults to root:root ownership — which UID 65532 can't write to. Fixed for all three paths by pre-creating and `--chown=65532:65532`-ing empty directories in each Dockerfile's builder stage (which has a real shell/`mkdir`, unlike the `scratch`/distroless runtime stages) and `COPY`ing them into the runtime image — a fresh volume then inherits that baked-in ownership on first population, standard practice for non-root `scratch`/distroless containers with a runtime-writable path. The `/certs` half of this was caught and fixed during the task's own live-verification pass (both containers crash-looped on first rebuild with `permission denied` reading the just-created key file); **the `/var/lib/eami-gateway` fallback-path half was missed by that same live pass — both compose files set `GATEWAY_JWT_KEY_PATH`, so it was never exercised — and was only caught by the separate, mandatory code-review pass**, which reasoned through the config fallback chain rather than only checking what was already running. Fixed and **re-verified live against the actual failure scenario**, not just re-read: a throwaway `docker run` of the rebuilt image with `GATEWAY_JWT_KEY_PATH` deliberately unset and a fresh anonymous volume (the exact "any other deployment method" case the finding describes) produced `"identity: RSA keypair written" path=/var/lib/eami-gateway/gateway.key` and a clean boot; the real compose-managed containers were then rebuilt and redeployed with the same fix and confirmed still healthy via `docker top` (both processes run as UID 65532, not root) and a full live E2E pass (below).
  - **One-time migration note for this machine's pre-existing dev volumes** (would apply to any real upgrade of an already-running deployment, not just this dev box): the `api_certs`/`gateway_certs` named volumes already existed from prior sessions with root-owned key files inside — baking ownership into a *new* image only affects volume population on genuine first-use, not an already-initialized volume. Both were `chown -R 65532:65532`'d directly (via a throwaway `alpine` container mounting each volume) as a one-time fix; documented here since a real production upgrade to this image would need the identical step, not just a fresh rebuild.
  - **Digest-pinned base images, all 4 service Dockerfiles** (real digests fetched via `docker pull`+`docker inspect`, not hand-typed): `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` builders bumped `golang:1.24-alpine`→`golang:1.25-alpine` (required by the go.mod bump above) and pinned by digest; `eami-api`'s `gcr.io/distroless/static-debian12` runtime and `eami-gateway`'s builder-stage `ca-certificates` source pinned by digest (`scratch` itself has no manifest/tag to pin — nothing floats there by construction); `eami-collector`'s `golang:1.24-alpine`/`alpine:3.19` pinned by digest with **no version bump** (not exposed to any in-scope CVE, kept minimal-diff per `CLAUDE.md`'s no-unrequested-scope-creep rule); `eami-ui`'s `node:20-alpine` (both `development`/`builder` stages) and `nginx:alpine` pinned by digest.
  - **`eami-ui/Dockerfile`'s false nginx comment, corrected, not just deleted:** the old comment claimed nginx "runs as the default nginx user (not root) ... via the `USER nginx` directive in the base image configuration." **Verified empirically wrong, not just by inspection**: `docker inspect nginx:alpine --format='{{.Config.User}}'` returns empty (no `USER` set at all) and `docker run --rm nginx:alpine sh -c id` shows `uid=0(root)`. The real mechanism (also verified directly, via `docker run --rm nginx:alpine cat /etc/nginx/nginx.conf`): the base image's own `nginx.conf` sets `user nginx;`, which only the *worker* processes — the ones that actually accept connections and serve files — read and drop to; the master process stays root by design, since binding port 80 requires root privilege, and this repo's `eami-ui/nginx.conf` (which only replaces the `server{}` block, not the base `nginx.conf`) doesn't touch that directive. Comment rewritten to state this accurately; no runtime behavior change (no `USER` directive added — that would break port 80 binding).

Both decisions made explicitly with the user rather than assumed: consistent with the brief's own "Vite major-version migration" carve-out, react-router-dom got the identical treatment rather than being force-fit into a dependency-bump session as a one-off exception.
  - **`postcss` bumped 8.5.15→8.5.18** (patch-level, already inside `package.json`'s existing `^8.4.45` range — `npm install postcss@8.5.18` updated the declared range to `^8.5.18`) via the established Docker `node:20-alpine` builder-stage trick.

- **B-051 (2026-08-07): real database migration runner.** Closes the VM-appliance investigation's C7 finding: no mechanism existed to apply a schema change to an already-running, already-seeded database — `schema.sql` only ever ran once, via Postgres's own `docker-entrypoint-initdb.d`, on a genuinely empty data directory. `schema/migrations/001-010` were historical documentation of `schema.sql`'s evolution, never individually runnable.
  - **Tool choice: golang-migrate via its official `migrate/migrate` Docker image** (digest-pinned `v4.19.1@sha256:cc4ad8e1...`), not embedded as a Go library in any of the 5 existing service modules — zero new Go dependency added anywhere, matches this repo's established pattern of using official upstream images directly (`postgres:16-alpine`, `nginx:alpine`) rather than wrapping tools in custom binaries.
  - **Reconciliation, investigated not assumed:** read all 10 legacy migration files before deciding. Only `005_token_usage.sql`, `007_audit_partitions.sql`, and `008_paste_events.sql` are actually idempotency-guarded (`IF NOT EXISTS` throughout) — `001_init.sql`, `002`, `003`, `004`, `009`, and `010` all use bare `CREATE TABLE`/`ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`/`CREATE INDEX`/`CREATE TRIGGER` that error on any re-run (contradicting the task brief's own assumption that 007/010 were the guarded examples — 010 is actually unguarded, 005/007/008 are the real ones). Given a table-level diff between `schema.sql` and `001_init.sql` confirmed `schema.sql` is the accurate cumulative result, chose a **fresh baseline** over retrofitting 6 files with guards they never had: new `schema/migrations-v2/000001_baseline.up.sql` captures `schema.sql`'s current content verbatim, hand-rewritten with idempotency guards throughout (`CREATE TABLE/INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, `DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS` + recreate and `DROP POLICY IF EXISTS` + recreate since Postgres has no `CREATE TRIGGER`/`POLICY IF NOT EXISTS` form, `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` for the `model_pricing` seed, `add_retention_policy(..., if_not_exists => TRUE)`). `000001_baseline.down.sql` is a deliberate no-op (dropping every table is never the right response to a failed migration) — pairs with B-029's existing `backup-db.sh`/`RECOVERY.md` mechanism as the real rollback story instead. `schema/migrations/001-010` left in place, untouched, with a new `schema/migrations/README.md` marking them historical/non-runnable rather than silently ambiguous.
  - **`000002_add_orgs_table_comment`** — a deliberately trivial, genuinely harmless (`COMMENT ON TABLE orgs IS '...'`, pure metadata, zero structural/data impact, no application code reads table comments) second migration, proving the mechanism actually works end-to-end without making a real schema change — explicitly out of this brief's own scope. Flagged explicitly to the user as the chosen approach before building, per their own instruction to disclose rather than silently decide.
  - **`docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml`:** removed the old `./schema/schema.sql:/docker-entrypoint-initdb.d/01-schema.sql:ro` mount entirely — the new `migrate` service is now the *only* path, fresh installs and existing installs upgrading both go through the identical `migrate up` invocation, no fork. `migrate` runs once, applies pending migrations, exits; `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` gained `depends_on: migrate: condition: service_completed_successfully`, gating them from ever starting against a not-yet-migrated schema.
  - **New `scripts/migrate.sh`** — thin wrapper around `docker compose run --rm migrate ...` (reuses the exact same image/mount already defined in the compose files, not a second divergent definition) for the real-world "apply an update to an already-up stack" case. Supports `up`/`version`/`force VERSION` (force stamps a version without running SQL — for marking an already-correct database as caught up, e.g. this session's own dev database — requires interactive `y/N` confirmation, fails closed on non-interactive/empty input).
  - **A real MEDIUM security finding, found by the mandatory security review and fixed before commit:** `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` interpolated unescaped into a `postgres://` URL (in the new `migrate` service's `-database` flag, and in `scripts/migrate.sh`) can misparse or, on some golang-migrate versions, leak into error output if the password contains base64-reserved characters (`+`/`/`/`=`) — **not theoretical**: this session's own real dev `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` already contains a `+`, confirmed via direct inspection. Root cause fixed at the source: `.env.example`'s `POSTGRES_PASSWORD` generation guidance changed from `openssl rand -base64 24` to `openssl rand -hex 32`, matching `setup.sh`'s own generator (which already filters to alphanumeric-only) and every other secret in the same file. Defense-in-depth: `scripts/migrate.sh` gained a `urlencode()` bash function, applied before any DSN construction — re-verified live against the real `+`-containing dev password (`scripts/migrate.sh version` correctly returned `2`). The automatic-at-boot `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` `migrate` service path left as a documented residual — `migrate/migrate`'s minimal Alpine image has a shell but no reliable way to URL-encode from pure compose YAML without a fragile inline one-liner, and the risk is already substantially closed by the `.env.example` fix; same risk class already exists, unfixed, in `eami-api`/`eami-gateway`'s own pre-existing DSN construction (`config.go`), out of this brief's scope to touch.
  - **New standalone Go module `schema/migrationtest/`** — deliberately **not** added to `go.work`: doing so broke `eami-gateway`'s Docker build (that Dockerfile `COPY`s each workspace member's `go.mod`/`go.sum` individually into the builder stage, and `go mod download` fails if `go.work` references a path that doesn't exist on disk inside the container) — caught live during this session's own verification, not just reasoned about. Run via `GOWORK=off` (Go auto-detects the parent `go.work` for anything nested under the repo tree regardless of `use()` membership). 4 real-Postgres integration tests (`migrate_test.go`), each creating and dropping its own throwaway database (`eami_app` confirmed to have `CREATEDB` before relying on it, not assumed) rather than reusing one shared always-current schema the way this repo's other `_pg_test.go` files do: `TestMigrate_FreshDatabase_MatchesExpectedSchema`, `TestMigrate_ExistingSeededDatabase_AppliesNewMigrationWithoutDataLoss`, `TestMigrate_RunTwice_NoErrorNoDuplication`, `TestMigrate_FreshPathAndIncrementalPath_ProduceIdenticalFinalSchema`.
  - **Reviewer + security subagent passes, both mandatory, both ran as real independent sub-tasks** (the security review's first attempt hit a transient API connection error mid-run — retried cleanly, not silently skipped). **Security pass** found the one real MEDIUM finding above (fixed); traced and cleared: no injection risk in `scripts/migrate.sh`'s `force`/argument handling, no realistic un-migrated-startup window (Compose's `depends_on` correctly gates normal `up` invocations; bypassing it requires `--no-deps` or a raw `docker run`, which fails loudly with missing-relation errors, not silently), no dynamic-SQL injection risk in the baseline's partition-creation `DO` block (static literals only, no `EXECUTE`/string concatenation). **Code-review pass**, after mechanically diffing `000001_baseline.up.sql` against `schema.sql` (not eyeballing) and confirming zero content discrepancies, found 3 real issues, all fixed: `schema/migrationtest/go.mod` had pinned `golang.org/x/text v0.31.0`, reintroducing the exact GO-2026-5970 CVE B-047 (the immediately preceding session) had just bumped every other module to `v0.39.0` to fix — bumped, re-verified clean via `govulncheck`; the new test module had zero CI wiring — investigated and found this is actually a repo-wide gap (no Go module's tests run in CI at all, for any of the 5 existing services either), logged honestly as its own item, **B-052**, rather than narrowly wiring up just the new module; throwaway-database cleanup in `migrate_test.go` swallowed its own reconnection failure via `t.Logf` instead of `t.Errorf`, meaning a transient cleanup failure could leak a throwaway database without failing the test — fixed.
  - **Test coverage:** 4 new real-Postgres integration tests (`schema/migrationtest/migrate_test.go`, described above), plus manual verification beyond the automated suite: the baseline migration applied to a truly fresh Postgres (table count matched `schema.sql` exactly — 48 tables + `schema_migrations`); the raw SQL file re-run directly via `psql`, bypassing `migrate`'s own version tracking entirely, confirmed every single statement is a genuine idempotent no-op (`NOTICE: already exists, skipping` throughout, `model_pricing` row count unchanged). `go build`/`go vet` clean for `schema/migrationtest` (`GOWORK=off`) and all 5 existing workspace modules (confirmed unaffected by the `go.work` edit); `go test ./...` re-confirmed clean for `eami-api` and `eami-gateway` (no regression from the compose-file changes).

Separately, a fully isolated fresh `docker compose -p eami-b051-test` project (genuinely empty Postgres volume, real rebuilt `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` images) confirmed via actual container state transitions — not just compose config inspection — that `migrate` completes and exits 0 (both migrations applied: `1/u baseline`, `2/u add_orgs_table_comment`) strictly before `eami-gateway`/`eami-api` ever reach the `Starting` state, proving AC #5's gating requirement holds for real. - **B-052 / B-016 (2026-08-08): `go test` runs for real in CI for the first time, across all 6 Go modules, gated on a real Postgres — and the gate caught a real pre-existing bug on its very first run.**
  - **Confirmed the gap for real before building:** read `.github/workflows/build.yml` in full — 364 lines, builds/pushes 4 Docker images and cross-compiles/vets `eami-agent` binaries, but `go test` appeared zero times anywhere, for any of the 6 modules.
  - **New `test` job** (`.github/workflows/build.yml`): matrix over the 5 `go.work` modules (`eami-agent`, `eami-collector`, `eami-policy`, `eami-api`, `eami-gateway`), each with its own `timescale/timescaledb-ha:pg16` service container — chosen deliberately over the stock `postgres` image because the schema requires the `pgvector`/`timescaledb` extensions the stock image lacks, and it's the same image `docker-compose.yml` already uses. `schema/migrations-v2` is applied via the pinned `golang-migrate` CLI (`go install -tags postgres .../migrate/v4/cmd/migrate@v4.19.1`, matching the version pinned in `docker-compose.yml`'s `migrate` service) before tests run, mirroring B-051's real production migration mechanism rather than a second, divergent schema-loading path. **User decision, explicitly asked rather than assumed:** all 5 legs get a Postgres service uniformly, even the 3 (`eami-agent`/`eami-collector`/`eami-policy`) with zero real-Postgres tests today — rejected the conditional-per-module alternative because the maintenance cost of remembering to add it back the day a 3rd module gains a real-Postgres test outweighs the marginal CI-time savings.
  - **New `test-migrationtest` job**: `schema/migrationtest` (deliberately outside `go.work`, per its own `go.mod`/`go.work`'s comment), run with `GOWORK=off` against its own Postgres service — no pre-migration step needed, since its tests manage their own throwaway per-test databases via `CREATE DATABASE`/`CREATEDB`.
  - **`build-images` gained `needs: [test, test-migrationtest]` — a deliberate, user-confirmed design fork, not the default choice.** The alternative (tests running fully in parallel with image builds, red CI without blocking a push) was presented explicitly; the user chose gating specifically because B-051's update mechanism now assumes published GHCR images are trustworthy — a broken test must never let a broken image reach the registry, even at the cost of losing `max(build,test)` parallelism in favor of sequential `build+test` time. Real cost measured, not assumed: ~1m40s baseline (pre-B-052 run `31205156691`) → ~2m54s–3m12s post-change across 3 separate clean runs — judged acceptable given `push: true` only fires on `main`/tags.
  - **The gate caught a real bug on its very first real run, closing B-016 (queued since 2026-07-22, discovered during B-002 Brief 2's first-ever real `go test` pass in this repo):** `eami-api/internal/api/finops.go`'s `FinOpsSummary`/`FinOpsTimeSeries` both called `s.queries.DB()` with **no nil-guard**, unlike every other handler in the package (`agents.go`, `policies.go`, `approvals.go`, `auth.go`'s own `"if s.queries != nil"` convention, `tools.go`'s `toolQueries()` seam). `finops_test.go`'s `newFinOpsTestEnv` deliberately builds a `Server` with `s.queries == nil` (a validation-path-only unit-test fixture); any request that passes validation reached the unguarded call and nil-pointer-panicked — silently recovered into an opaque 500 by chi's `Recoverer`, masked for who knows how long by the tests' own weak `!= http.StatusBadRequest` assertions (they never checked for `500` specifically). Confirmed with real evidence before fixing anything, not from a third-party log summary a review tool produced: reproduced the exact panic locally with a full stack trace (`finops.go:269`, `TestFinOpsTimeSeries_ValidGranularities/hour`) via `go test -count=1`.
  - **A real process failure, caught and corrected in-session rather than shipped:** an initial "all 6 modules verified locally" pass had actually been a **false negative** — Go's test-result cache doesn't invalidate on environment-variable changes without `-count=1`, so `go test ./...` silently reused a stale, pre-fix cached "ok" for `eami-api` across a `TEST_DATABASE_URL` change. Discovered by deliberately re-running with `-count=1` and getting a different, real answer. Both CI `go test` invocations subsequently gained `-count=1` too, closing the identical risk inside the gate itself — the exact class of gap CI was built to catch, almost present in the CI wiring itself.
  - **Fix:** both handlers gained `if s.queries == nil { writeError(500, "internal_error", "finops store is not configured"); return }`, matching the package's own established pattern exactly — deliberately **not** a mock-DBTX/fake-store fix (the initial lead, sourced from an unverified third-party log-summary tool, was explicitly rejected by the user before being acted on, since a fake store risks masking other real gaps behind fabricated success responses). Two new tests, `TestFinOps{Summary,TimeSeries}_NoStoreConfigured_ReturnsCleanError`, assert the exact clean 500 — mirroring `tools_test.go`'s existing `TestToolHandlers_NoStoreConfigured_ReturnsCleanError` precedent, closing B-016's own "strengthen assertions" acceptance criterion, not just the panic.
  - **Mandatory code-review pass (general-purpose subagent, run as a real independent sub-task against the actual diff) found a real HIGH finding the original brief missed:** the identical unguarded `s.queries.DB()` pattern also existed in `eami-api/internal/api/paste_events.go:314` (`PasteEventsTimeSeries`) — the file's own doc comment even cross-references `FinOpsTimeSeries` as a structural sibling, yet only two of the three raw-`DB()` call sites in the package had been guarded. Never caught by existing tests because `paste_events_test.go`/`paste_events_read_test.go` always require a real Postgres connection or skip cleanly — none builds a nil-`s.queries` server the way `finops_test.go` deliberately does. **Verified directly before fixing** (read the actual line, confirmed no existing test exercises the nil path), not taken on the review agent's word. Fixed identically; new `TestPasteEventsRead_TimeSeries_NoStoreConfigured_ReturnsCleanError` (a self-contained black-box test needing no real database at all, proven by running it in isolation with no Postgres available). Two smaller findings from the same review also applied: CI's `go test` steps gained `-count=1` (above), and a `finops_test.go` comment overclaiming the new tests check "status+body" was corrected to "status" (they only assert the status code). One finding — 3 of 5 matrix legs standing up an unused Postgres service — was **not** applied: already an explicit, user-confirmed design decision (see above), not a bug the review surfaced fresh.
  - **AC1 (the gate actually blocks on failure) proven twice, not once:** organically, when the real B-016 bug failed `Test — eami-api` on run `31251999449` and `Docker — ${{ matrix.service }}` was correctly skipped as a result (no staged breakage needed — a genuine bug did the job); and deliberately, per the brief's own explicit instruction, via a one-line assertion flip in `eami-policy/policy_test.go` (`2e7e284`), pushed, confirmed red (`Test — eami-policy` failed, `Docker` skipped, run `31253303734`), then reverted (`8042bcc`) and confirmed green again (run `31253438387`).
  - **Security review:** no findings. The hardcoded CI-only Postgres password is an ephemeral, service-container-scoped, non-secret placeholder (not reachable outside the job's network namespace); `golang-migrate` is installed at a pinned version tag, not `@latest`; the `finops.go`/`paste_events.go` guards are purely defensive early-returns introducing no new attack surface or data flow.

Configuring branch protection itself is a separate, smaller follow-up, not done here (out of this brief's stated scope).
- **B-054 (2026-08-11): `scripts/setup.sh` now generates all 3 secrets `docker-compose.yml`/`.prod.yml` reference but `write_env()` never wrote.** Gap found and logged during B-053 (2026-08-08) while porting `setup.sh`'s secret-generation pattern into `appliance/scripts/eami-stack.sh`; left unfixed there deliberately since `setup.sh` was out of B-053's scope. Re-confirmed still present by reading `write_env()` directly before starting this task.
  - **Fix, `scripts/setup.sh` only:** `main()`'s "Generating secrets" step now also calls the existing `generate_secret` (`openssl rand -hex 32`) for `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY`, `GATEWAY_TOKEN_REVOKE_SERVICE_KEY`, and `TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` — identical entropy/method to the pre-existing `SERVICE_KEY`/`COLLECTOR_API_KEY`, not a new generator. `write_env()` gained 3 new positional parameters (`$8`–`$10`) and writes all 3 into the `.env`'s `eami-gateway` section (a single entry each is correct — both compose files reference these var names identically from both the `eami-gateway` and `eami-api` service blocks, unlike `SERVICE_KEY` which needs two differently-named variables for the same value). Confirmed via `eami-api/internal/toolcreds/toolcreds.go` that the cipher requires exactly a hex-encoded 32-byte key — `generate_secret`'s existing output format already matches, no new validation logic needed.
  - **Investigated whether this should become shared logic with `appliance/scripts/eami-stack.sh` (which already generates all 3 independently via its own `gen_hex_secret()`) rather than a third duplication — decided against it:** extracting shared logic would require editing `eami-stack.sh`, outside this task's `MAY MODIFY` scope, and duplicating small security-relevant secret-generation snippets across `setup.sh`/`eami-stack.sh` already matches this repo's own established B-025 precedent (duplicate over shared infrastructure for this class of helper). `eami-stack.sh` itself is untouched.
  - **Live-verified, twice:** (1) extracted `write_env()` and its generator dependencies into an isolated harness (main()'s prompt/stack-boot steps not exercised) and confirmed a fresh `.env` contains all 3 new keys, each exactly 64 hex characters (32 bytes) — matching every other `generate_secret`-produced value in the same file. (2) Booted a fully isolated throwaway stack (`docker compose -p eami-b054-test`, fresh Postgres volume, real rebuilt images) with that generated `.env`: both `eami-gateway` and `eami-api` started cleanly with no restart loop and no missing-key errors (`docker compose logs` grepped clean for `must be set|required|fatal|panic|failed`, only routine Postgres first-boot noise present). Went beyond config-acceptance to prove the features are actually functional, not just that the vars are non-empty: `GET /v1/gateway/episodes` returned `200` with the real generated `GATEWAY_EPISODE_READ_SERVICE_KEY` and `401` with a wrong one; `POST /v1/gateway/tokens/{jti}/revoke` returned `400` (past auth, into normal business-logic rejection of a fake jti) with the real `GATEWAY_TOKEN_REVOKE_SERVICE_KEY` and `401` with a wrong one; gateway's own startup log showed `"tool router ready","credentials_configured":true`, confirming `TOOL_CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` was accepted by the cipher. Throwaway stack and volumes fully torn down afterward (`docker compose -p eami-b054-test down -v`), confirmed via `docker volume ls`/`docker ps -a`.
