Restored via `docker compose up -d` (proper dependency ordering) plus `--force-recreate` on `eami-api`/`eami-gateway` (their auto-restarted containers had a stale/unreachable embedded-DNS network attachment even after Postgres came up healthy) — confirmed the real dev stack fully healthy again afterward, `dev@example.com`'s data and the `postgres_data` volume untouched throughout (never removed, only the already-dead process was restarted).
  - **No application code, `docker-compose*.yml`, or `.env.example` touched** — `scripts/setup.sh` is the only file changed, per this task's scope.
- **B-056 (2026-08-11): perf-seed tests no longer leak synthetic orgs into the shared dev database.** Logged during B-055 (2026-08-10, incidentally, while live-verifying that brief against the real dev DB and finding 30 leaked `paste-events-perf-*` rows dating to 2026-07-25) but misattributed at filing time to `paste_events_read_test.go:362`'s `seedPasteEventsPerfData` — investigation found that function does **not** leak (tested in isolation, zero leak every run; it already correctly used `t.Cleanup(pool.Close)`).
  - **Real root cause, found by direct empirical testing, not guessed:** `paste_events_test.go`'s `TestPasteEvents_ReportingQuery_UsesIndex_AtRealisticVolume` (B-032, 2026-07-25 — matching both the leaked orgs' exact naming, no `-read-` infix, and the leak's own dated history) has `defer pool.Close()` (a plain Go defer, scoped to the test function) alongside a `t.Cleanup`-registered `DELETE FROM orgs` for its two seeded orgs. `defer` unwinds when the test function itself returns; `t.Cleanup` callbacks are a *separate, later* teardown phase that only runs after the function body — including its own defers — has already fully completed. So the registered DELETE always ran against an already-closed pool, erroring immediately, with the error silently discarded (`_, _ = pool.Exec(...)`). Proven directly, not theorized: ran the test in total isolation (`-run`, single test, no timeout, no interruption) — clean `PASS`, exit 0 — and it still leaked both orgs (100,200 child rows each: 200 `endpoints` + 100,000 `paste_events`) every single time; the identical `DELETE` run manually via `psql` succeeded instantly with a clean cascade to zero remaining child rows, ruling out any FK/cascade explanation. A repo-wide grep confirmed no other real-Postgres test file in the module mixes `defer pool.Close()` with a `t.Cleanup`-registered row delete — an isolated, one-function bug.
  - **Fix, scoped as a rewrite rather than a one-line ordering patch, per explicit user direction:** both the buggy test and its non-buggy sibling (`seedPasteEventsPerfData`, converted too — seeding 200,000 synthetic rows into the shared dev DB at all is the underlying problem this item actually named, not just the one ordering bug) now create their own throwaway database per run, reusing `bootstrap_test.go`'s already-established `bootstrapTestPgConn`/`newThrowawayDB`/`applyMigrations` helpers directly (same `api_test` package, zero duplication) instead of `pasteEventsTestDSN`'s shared-dev-DB connection. The whole throwaway database — and everything seeded into it — is dropped by `newThrowawayDB`'s own `t.Cleanup`; the per-org `t.Cleanup` DELETE calls are gone entirely, removing the whole class of future ordering mistakes, not just this instance.
  - **Live-verified against all 3 scenarios the fix needed to hold under, including the two explicitly requested beyond a normal pass:** (1) normal run — all 3 affected tests pass, shared dev DB unchanged (still only `Dev Org`), zero leftover throwaway databases. (2) **simulated failed run** — a `t.Fatal` temporarily injected right after seeding (reverted immediately after): test fails as expected, cleanup still fires — zero leak in either the shared DB or a leftover throwaway database, confirming Go's `t.Fatal`→`runtime.Goexit` semantics still run registered cleanups. (3) **simulated interrupted run** — compiled the test binary directly and hard-killed it via `taskkill /F` mid-flight, twice (once during migration setup, once with one org's full 100,200 rows already inserted): in both cases the shared dev database's `orgs` table stayed untouched — the one remaining residual is the throwaway database itself being left behind, which no cleanup mechanism in any language can survive after a hard kill. This is the same already-accepted residual `bootstrap_test.go`'s own throwaway-DB pattern has always carried (not a new gap introduced here); both leaked throwaway DBs were uniquely named (`bootstraptest_<pid>_<rand>`), trivially identifiable, inspected, and manually dropped.
  - **Verification:** `go build ./...`/`go vet ./...` clean; `go test ./... -count=1` clean across the full `eami-api` module (including the full pre-existing suite, not just the 3 affected tests) — no regressions, comparable runtime to the pre-fix version (~23s for all 3 perf tests together, both before and after). Real dev database confirmed to still contain only `Dev Org` throughout and after this session's work.
  - **No application code touched** — `eami-api/internal/api/paste_events_test.go` and `paste_events_read_test.go` are the only files changed.
- **AI Provider Connector — Thread A Model 1 (2026-08-11): a new `ai_provider` `gateway_tools` type, dispatching to an external AI provider (Claude first) as a named tool through the real gateway dispatch path, exactly like a `rest_api` tool** (`claude/messages`, reusing MCP's existing async 202-then-SSE dispatch unchanged, non-streaming per this brief's own scope). This is the multi-provider adapter pattern from the Thread A investigation, Model 1: Claude is the first complete implementation of a real `Adapter` interface, not a special case bolted on. Also unblocks prompt redaction/tokenization architecturally (the gateway had no visibility into an agent's outbound LLM call before this) — that subsystem itself is not built here.
  - **Schema** (`schema/migrations-v2/000004_ai_provider_tools.up.sql`): `gateway_tools.type` CHECK widened to include `'ai_provider'`; new `provider TEXT` (nullable, deliberately no CHECK enum — validated at the Go layer against a small allowlist instead, so a second provider is one entry + one adapter file, not another migration); new `audit_mode TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'structural_metadata_only' CHECK (IN ('full','structural_metadata_only'))` — a string enum (not boolean) so a future `'redacted'`/tokenized mode is a value addition later, deliberately not accepted yet since nothing implements it.
  - **New `eami-gateway/internal/aiprovider` package:** `Adapter` interface (`Provider() string`, `Dispatch(ctx, Credentials, Request) (Response, error)`); `Router` (`Resolve` — org+name+type-scoped SQL, mirrors `toolrouter.Resolve`'s discipline exactly; `Dispatch` — decrypts via the reused `toolrouter.Cipher`, looks up the registry, calls the adapter); `ClaudeAdapter` (real `x-api-key`+`anthropic-version` headers — not the `Authorization: Bearer` `toolrouter.Forward` assumes for REST tools, since Claude's own auth scheme genuinely differs; POSTs the agent-supplied, already-Claude-shaped params to `https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages`); a disclosed duplicate of `toolrouter`'s `safeDialContext` SSRF guard (toolrouter is read-only scope this brief; same B-025/B-044 duplicate-rather-than-touch-frozen-code precedent). `Credentials{APIKey}` decoded from the same stored-JSON shape B-022 already uses, not a new credential shape. No request/response normalization across providers attempted (confirmed unrealistic without real per-provider translation work in the Thread A investigation, Part A) — the adapter abstracts *dispatch*, not request shape.
  - **`cmd/gateway/main.go` wiring, additive only** (B-044/B-046's `rest_api` dispatch logic itself untouched): a new `resolveAIProviderTool` (mirrors `resolveDynamicTool`'s fail-open contract), only queried when `resolveDynamicTool` didn't already match (code-review finding — the initial version paid a second DB round trip for every `mcp`/`database`-type dispatch too, not just genuinely-unregistered names; the fix means an already-registered `rest_api` tool still pays exactly one query, same as before this brief — a small residual extra query for `mcp`/`database`-type calls specifically was judged not worth further restructuring and is disclosed here, not silently accepted). Reuses `pc.ToolServerID` (Part 0's own finding: policy evaluation needed zero changes). One redaction point, applied once before the deny/escalate/allow switch: `auditEntry.Parameters = nil` when the resolved connector's `audit_mode != "full"` — scoped strictly to the `audit_log` write per AC5's literal wording; `approval_requests`/episodes keep showing full parameters unchanged (a human reviewer needs full visibility to decide, confirmed as correct existing behavior, not a bug, in the Thread A investigation Part 0 §6-7).
  - **`eami-api`:** `tools.sql.go`/`models.go` extended (not the frozen B-044/B-046 dispatch logic) with `Provider`/`AuditMode` on `CreateToolParams`/`UpdateToolParams`/`GatewayTool`; `CreateTool` defaults `AuditMode` to `"structural_metadata_only"` **at the store layer**, not only in the handler — a more robust guarantee (holds for any caller, not just ones that remember to set it; caught a real gap where two pre-existing tests constructing `CreateToolParams` directly would otherwise have violated the new CHECK constraint). `tools.go`'s `CreateTool`/`UpdateTool` validate `provider` against the allowlist and reject it outright for non-`ai_provider` types (code-review finding: an earlier version silently accepted+persisted `provider` on any type); validate `type=ai_provider` requires `auth_type="api_key"` at creation (the only shape the adapter understands, catching a misconfiguration at creation instead of on every real dispatch); `UpdateTool` fetches the target row's actual current type (reusing the existing `GetToolForTest` read, no new query) before honoring a `provider`/`audit_mode` PATCH, closing the identical gap on the update path. `tool_connectivity.go` gained an honest `ai_provider` case (matches the `mcp` case's own precedent: reports "not implemented" rather than fabricating a "connected" — testing it for real would spend provider API credits on every click and duplicate the adapter's own wire-format knowledge in a second file).
  - **`eami-ui`:** `ToolsPage.tsx`/`useTools.ts` — `ai_provider` added to the type union (`Omit<Tool,'type'> & {type: ToolType}`, since `type` is a generated-schema literal union `openapi.yaml` doesn't document yet, matching B-046's own undocumented-field precedent); a Provider dropdown (structured as a real list — `claude` today, a second provider is one more `<option>`, matching AC2's "ready for a second provider without UI rework") and an Audit-mode dropdown, defaulted to `structural_metadata_only`; Auth type forced to and locked at `api_key` when Type is `ai_provider` (code-review finding: the two selects were previously independent, so a user could leave a stale unrelated auth type selected with no visible sign until a confusing 400 on submit); `TypeBadge` gained a color for the new type (was falling through to the generic gray "unrecognized type" styling).
  - **Scope expansion 1, explicitly authorized mid-brief, matching B-039's own `mcp/handler.go` precedent for "a real gap found during this task's own investigation that makes an acceptance criterion unsatisfiable without the fix":** live verification found `approval.Router`'s resume logic (`resolve()`'s "approved" case) was hardcoded to the static `fwd *proxy.Proxy`, with **zero** awareness of `toolRouter`/`aiProviderRouter` — meaning an approved escalation for **any** dynamically-routed tool, `rest_api` (B-044) included, never actually reached the destination it was escalated for; it silently hit the static proxy instead. Not a new bug — B-044's own session had already found and explicitly disclosed this exact gap for `rest_api` without fixing it (`CONTEXT.md`: "`internal/approval/router.go` are completely untouched, so approved escalations keep using the static proxy exactly as before"). Fixed with a new `dispatchApproved` method: resolves the tool via `aiProviderRouter` then `toolRouter` (mirroring `main.go`'s own resolution order), falling back to the static proxy only if neither matches — closing the gap for both types at once, scoped to the resume dispatch path only, `Submit`/`Hold`/the LISTEN-NOTIFY mechanism itself untouched. `New()` gained two new nil-safe params; all 4 existing call sites (1 production, 3 test) updated to pass them (or `nil, nil`, preserving prior behavior exactly for tests that don't exercise resume dispatch).
  - **Scope expansion 2, also explicitly authorized, found by this session's own mandatory security review of expansion 1 — judged more serious than the routing bug it followed, since it's a bypassable safety guarantee, not a broken feature:** re-resolving by `(org_id, name)` at resume time (expansion 1's own design) meant a lower-privileged `admin`/`operator` role — which cannot itself approve or deny an escalation, and which the human approver has no visibility into having acted — could edit the escalated connector's `base_url`/`credentials`/`provider` (or, found by a **second** security review pass on THIS fix, `action_paths` alone, base_url/credentials untouched) during the hold window, silently redirecting the approved call to a different destination than the one actually reviewed. The approver-facing UI/API shows only agent/tool/action/justification/risk — never `base_url`, `provider`, or a credential fingerprint — so there was no way for a human to detect this from the approval screen itself.
    - **Fix:** at escalation time (`main.go`, already resolving the tool before policy evaluation), the resolved connector's ID and `approval.ComputeConfigHash(toolType, baseURLOrProvider, credentialsEncrypted, actionPathsJSON)` — SHA-256 over type + base_url-or-provider + the **encrypted** credential bytes (never plaintext; needs no cipher/key, and re-encryption's fresh GCM nonce means any credential touch, even resaving the identical secret, always changes the hash — confirmed by inspecting `toolcreds.Cipher.Encrypt`'s real nonce generation, not assumed) + a canonical (`encoding/json.Marshal`, always sorted-key) serialization of `action_paths` — are pinned into new `Request.ResolvedToolID`/`ResolvedConfigHash` fields, persisted by `Submit()` into two new `approval_requests` columns (`schema/migrations-v2/000005_approval_resolved_config.up.sql`). At resume, `dispatchApproved` fetches the connector **by ID** (a new `fetchResolvedConnector`, raw SQL, org-scoped — never by name, closing the delete-and-recreate-under-the-same-name variant too, since a different row has a different ID), recomputes the hash, and **fails closed** — refuses to dispatch, returns a clear error, records a new `resume_outcome` (`'dispatched'|'config_changed'|'connector_deleted'|'static_fallback'`, closing the audit gap: what actually executed at resume time is now recorded, not just the original pre-hold escalation entry) — if the connector is gone or the hash differs. `resolved_tool_id`'s FK uses `ON DELETE SET NULL` (deliberately different from this table's other FK columns like `policy_id`/`agent_id`, which have none — found live by this fix's own test suite, which tried to delete a referenced tool and got blocked by a naive FK; a historical approval record must never prevent an admin from deleting a tool).
    - **The `action_paths` gap was found live, not assumed:** this fix's own mandatory security review (run twice — once on the routing fix, once specifically re-checking the config-pinning fix) and an independent code-review pass **both independently converged on the identical finding**: the first version of `ComputeConfigHash` covered only `base_url`/credentials, missing that `action_paths` alone (unrelated to `base_url`/credentials) fully determines a `rest_api` tool's real per-action destination/method (`toolrouter.Forward`). Fixed in the same pass, with its own dedicated live-simulation test.
  - **Test coverage:** `eami-gateway/internal/aiprovider` (new package) — `Resolve` (real Postgres: found/not-found/wrong-type/cross-org), `Dispatch` (decrypt-and-route wiring, no real DB needed), `ClaudeAdapter` (real headers, real round trip via `httptest`, credential-never-in-error-message, response-size-cap, SSRF guard against the real dialer). `main_ai_provider_pg_test.go` — `resolveAIProviderTool` mirroring `resolveDynamicTool`'s existing test shape exactly (found/unregistered/wrong-type/cross-org/empty-input), plus a real-Postgres proof the fail-safe `audit_mode` default holds through the full insert-then-resolve path. `eami-api` — `tools_ai_provider_test.go`, provider/audit_mode validation on both `CreateTool`/`UpdateTool` (valid, unknown-provider-rejected, missing-provider-rejected, wrong-auth-type-rejected, wrong-type-rejected, invalid-audit-mode-rejected, omitted-leaves-unchanged) plus response-shape round-trip. `eami-gateway/internal/approval` — `router_dispatch_test.go`: the resume-routing fix (ai_provider/rest_api both resume to their real resolved destination, unregistered-tool/nil-routers still fall back exactly as before) **and** the TOCTOU fix's fail-closed behavior proven via **real mid-hold Postgres mutations, not mocks**: a real `UPDATE ... SET credentials_encrypted`, a real `UPDATE ... SET base_url`, a real `UPDATE ... SET action_paths`, and a real `DELETE` against the actual `gateway_tools` row while an approval sits `pending`, each followed by a real `dispatchApproved` call proving the resume is refused and the correct `resume_outcome` is recorded — this is what "actually change a connector mid-hold and confirm the resume rejects it, don't just reason about it" meant at the automated-test level, in addition to the live proof below. `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -count=1` clean across all three affected Go modules (`eami-gateway`, `eami-api`, `schema/migrationtest`) throughout, including the full pre-existing suites — no regressions at any point. Real `npm run type-check`/`npm run build` clean for `eami-ui`.
  - **Reviewer + security subagent passes: three full rounds, mandatory per the standing rule (new credential type, new external-network dispatch surface, real API keys to a third party), each finding real, distinct issues before the next was clean.** Round 1 (base feature): code review found 4 issues (a `schema.sql`/sqlc concern investigated and found overstated — `tools.sql.go` was never actually sqlc-generated, no `tools.sql` query source exists — disclosed not fixed; 2 real `CreateTool`/`UpdateTool` type-validation gaps, fixed; 1 minor extra-query inefficiency for `mcp`/`database` dispatches, disclosed); security review found nothing (traced credential handling, the SSRF-guard duplicate, org-scoping, input validation, and the audit-redaction logic — confirmed all correct as designed). Round 2 (resume-routing fix, scope expansion 1): security review — explicitly mandated to check destination-integrity — found the real TOCTOU gap described above (8/10 confidence, full exploit trace); code review found 3 more issues (2 real `provider`/`audit_mode` type-consistency gaps on `CreateTool`/`UpdateTool`, fixed; 1 UI polish gap, fixed). Round 3 (config-pinning fix, scope expansion 2): security review and code review **both independently found the identical `action_paths` gap** described above, fixed in the same pass; security review confirmed org-scoping/no-internal-TOCTOU/no-misroute/credential-hash-safety all hold given the fix, and found nothing further against its explicit "can any role still redirect an approved action" mandate.
  - **Live-verified end-to-end against a real, isolated `docker-compose` stack (`-p eami-aiprovider-test`, fresh Postgres, real rebuilt images), with a real Anthropic API key — a real one-off operational mistake caught and corrected mid-verification, disclosed not glossed over:** an early rebuild omitted the `-p` project flag, silently rebuilding a *different* project's image tag and leaving the throwaway stack's actual running image stale for several verification steps (surfaced when a pinned `resolved_tool_id` kept coming back NULL despite the DB-level query working correctly in isolation) — re-diagnosed from first principles (confirmed the exact SQL directly via `psql`, ruling out a query bug, before suspecting the build) and corrected by rebuilding with the correct `-p` flag for all three images. Once corrected: **AC1** — a real agent `tool_call` for `claude/messages` on a directly-allowed connector reached the real Claude API and returned a real response (`"AI_PROVIDER_TEST_OK"`). **AC2** — provider/credentials/audit_mode configured per-connector via the real `eami-api` CRUD, response never contains credentials. **AC3** — a real `tool_names:["claude"]` escalate policy correctly escalated the call (real `approval_requests` row, `status=pending`). **AC4, the full standard, not partial** — submitted, visible, approved via the real admin decide API, **and the original call genuinely resumed**: a second full cycle after the fix returned a real Claude response (`"RESUME_FIX_VERIFIED"`) over the live SSE stream, with `resume_outcome='dispatched'` recorded. **The fail-closed behavior itself, live, not just via Go tests** — a third cycle: escalate, then a real `PATCH` (the exact attacker/operator-role action) swapping the connector's credentials while the request sat pending, then approve — the resume was genuinely refused, `resume_outcome='config_changed'` recorded, matching the automated test suite exactly. **AC5** — `audit_log.parameters` confirmed `null` for the default-mode connector's call and fully populated for the explicit `full`-mode connector's call, in the real database. **AC6** — grepped all three services' real logs for the actual API key's distinctive suffix across the entire session: zero matches. Every throwaway resource (org, agent, 2 connectors, policy, 3 approval requests, the isolated stack's containers/volumes/network, the helper container, the on-disk API key file) confirmed torn down/deleted afterward; the real dev stack confirmed untouched throughout (still only `Dev Org`).
  - **Out of scope, as specified:** streaming (Model 2), Gemini/OpenAI adapters themselves (the interface supports adding them; not built), redaction/tokenization (architecturally unblocked, not built), transparent SDK passthrough (Model 2).
- **B-059 (2026-08-12): Multi-Hop Workflows, Brief 2 — execution engine, per-hop TOCTOU pinning, static per-step parameters.** Makes a B-058-defined workflow (Brief 1: definitions only, zero execution) actually run: each step dispatched through the exact same, completely unmodified `dispatch()`/policy/audit path a standalone MCP call uses. Output→input mapping between steps and durable escalation pause/resume are both explicitly deferred to later, unscoped Thread B briefs.
  - **New `eami-gateway/internal/workflow` package:** `resolve.go` (`Resolve(ctx, pool, orgID, workflowID)` — reads `workflows`/`workflow_steps`/`workflow_step_params` directly via the gateway's own pool, mirroring `toolrouter.Resolve`'s established pattern of gateway-side reads of eami-api-owned tables in this shared-Postgres deployment model; returns `ErrDanglingStep` before any step executes if one references a deleted connector — B-058's `ON DELETE SET NULL` state); `connector.go` (a by-ID `gateway_tools` read, deliberately duplicated from `approval/router.go`'s unexported `fetchResolvedConnector` rather than imported, matching this repo's own established B-044/B-025 duplicate-small-security-helper precedent); `executor.go` (`Executor.Run` — the per-step loop); `http.go` (`POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{workflowId}/run`, agent-JWT authenticated via the same `identity.Manager.Validate`+`registry.LookupByName` pattern `mcp.Handler.parseBearer` already uses, synchronous — the HTTP response blocks until the run finishes).
  - **Per-hop TOCTOU pinning is what calling the existing `dispatch()` closure once per step already gives, unmodified, for free — this brief's own central design question, resolved by inspection rather than new mechanism.** `dispatch()` (`cmd/gateway/main.go`) already does its own complete resolve→policy→TOCTOU-pin(if escalating)→dispatch cycle inside one call; calling it N times in sequence, once per step, means each step independently re-resolves and re-pins its own connector immediately before its own policy evaluation — there is no shared "resolve everything upfront" phase for a later step's connector edit to hide behind undetected. Upfront pinning (resolving every step's connector once at workflow start) was considered and rejected: it's strictly weaker, since a later step's connector could still be edited during an earlier step's escalation hold without upfront pinning ever re-verifying that at the actual dispatch moment — at which point it's just per-hop pinning again, with a wasted, misleading earlier snapshot.
  - **Escalation mid-chain reuses `Hold()` unmodified, automatically, as this brief's explicit interim behavior** (a real durable pause/resume mechanism is later-brief scope): because `dispatch()` itself is called verbatim per step, a step whose policy escalates transparently blocks inside `Submit()`+`Hold()` exactly as a standalone MCP call would, with zero special-casing in the new execution code. The whole run — and the HTTP request that triggered it — blocks for up to `holdTimeout`; no durable persistence survives a process restart mid-hold.
  - **`Executor.runStep`'s per-step contract:** resolves the step's connector by ID (informational only — for `workflow_run_steps`' own audit record, via the exact same `approval.ComputeConfigHash` B-057 already exports); runs an independent, informational pre-dispatch `policy.Evaluate` call (recorded as `projected_decision`, purely so the audit trail can show "this step escalated" even though `dispatch()`'s own return value alone can't distinguish that from "always allowed" — never used to skip or alter what `dispatch()` itself enforces); calls the real, unmodified `dispatch()`; classifies the real outcome into `allowed` / `denied` (an `*mcp.PolicyDeniedError`) / `blocked` (any other dispatch error — escalation denied/timed out, or a downstream failure), with the raw error always preserved in `error_detail` regardless of the coarser bucket. The first non-`allowed` step stops the run — no partial-failure recovery or step-skipping, explicit v1 scope.
  - **New schema (`schema/migrations-v2/000007_workflow_execution.up.sql`):** `workflow_step_params` (1:1 with `workflow_steps`, a deliberately *separate* table rather than a new `workflow_steps` column or reuse of the already-present `input_mapping` — ~~that column stays untouched, reserved for the later output→input-mapping brief~~ **closed by B-063, see immediately below**; conflating "admin-typed static constant" with "value derived from a prior step's response" would collide two concepts that will likely need to coexist per step once mapping ships); `workflow_runs`/`workflow_run_steps` (the execution audit trail, separate from B-058's static definition) — `workflow_id`/`workflow_step_id`/`resolved_tool_id` all `ON DELETE SET NULL`, mirroring `approval_requests`' established precedent throughout: a run is a historical record, and deleting the definition or connector it ran against must never be blocked by that history.
  - **`eami-api`: new `internal/api/workflow_step_params.go` + `internal/store/workflow_step_params.sql.go`** (new files — `workflows.go`/`workflows.sql.go` from B-058 are completely untouched, per that brief's own boundary carried forward into this one). `PUT`/`GET /v1/gateway/workflow-steps/{stepId}/params`, same role gating as `workflows`' own write/read groups. `UpsertWorkflowStepParams` is one atomic `INSERT...SELECT...WHERE EXISTS` statement combining the org-ownership guard (`workflow_steps` → `workflows.org_id`, since `workflow_step_params` has no `org_id` column of its own, matching `workflow_steps`' identical no-`org_id` shape) with the write itself — no separate check-then-write TOCTOU window. **Known minor limitation, disclosed not fixed:** a literal JSON `null` body passes the handler's "must be an object" validation (unmarshals into a nil map without error) and degrades gracefully to "no params set," rather than being explicitly rejected like an array/scalar body is (confirmed those ARE rejected, via a passing test) — low severity, no security impact, not blocking.
  - **Test dispatch harness — a real, disclosed simplification, not a mock:** `dispatch()` itself is an unexported closure local to `main.go`'s `run()` — not importable into `internal/workflow`'s tests, and refactoring it to be testable would itself be exactly the kind of change to B-057's logic this brief must not make. `eami-gateway/internal/workflow/testenv_test.go` reconstructs dispatch()'s real shape (real `policy.Evaluator`, real `audit.Writer`, real `episode.Recorder`, real `approval.Router` with real `Submit`/`Hold`/`LISTEN`/`NOTIFY`, real `aiprovider.Router`) using only exported constructors — the same real pipeline `main.go` itself wires, just reassembled at the test-file level. Test connectors are all `type=ai_provider` dispatched via a fake `Adapter` (mirroring `approval/router_dispatch_test.go`'s own `fakeProviderAdapter` pattern), not `rest_api` via `toolrouter.Forward` — found live, not assumed: the first test version used `rest_api` tools pointed at a local `httptest.Server`, and `toolrouter`'s real, correct SSRF guard (B-023-class) rejected every call as loopback, exactly the same reason `router_dispatch_test.go`'s own "genuinely dispatches and succeeds" proofs already use `ai_provider` instead of `rest_api`.
  - **Test coverage:** `eami-gateway/internal/workflow` — 10 real-Postgres integration tests covering all 6 acceptance criteria: full multi-step execution with real per-step static params (AC1); a middle step's policy denial stopping the run before a later step's `workflow_run_steps` row is ever created (AC2); escalate→approve→resume and escalate→deny→stop, both via the real `Hold()`/`pg_notify` mechanism with a background goroutine + `waitForPendingApproval` polling helper (AC3); two distinct TOCTOU scenarios kept deliberately separate (AC4) — a non-escalating connector edit between steps is *correctly* picked up by the next step (proves no accidental upfront-caching bug, not a vulnerability, since nothing was ever "reviewed" against a stale snapshot for an allow-path step) versus the real attack, an escalating step's own connector edited specifically during its hold window, which correctly fails closed via B-057's completely unmodified `dispatchApproved` mechanism; `workflow_run_steps` audit-trail accuracy inspected directly against real inserted rows (AC5); no output→input mapping, proven by a distinctive-marker-leak check across two real fake-adapter calls (AC6). Plus `eami-api/internal/api/workflow_step_params_test.go` — 3 real-Postgres/real-HTTP tests (round-trip incl. full-replace-not-merge, cross-org rejection via the atomic upsert guard, non-object-body rejection). **Verified 2026-08-12 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -count=1` clean across both `eami-gateway` and `eami-api`, full pre-existing suites included, no regressions.**
  - **A real test-authoring bug reproduced a THIRD time this session (the identical B-056-class `t.Cleanup`-registered-DELETE-after-a-plain-`defer pool.Close()` ordering bug B-058's own session already found and fixed twice) — found and fixed again, in the new `eami-api` params test file:** confirmed via a real leaked-org check against the shared dev database, fixed identically (`t.Cleanup(func(){ pool.Close() })` registered before any org-seeding call so LIFO ordering runs the real DELETE first), re-verified clean, dev database confirmed back to just `Dev Org`.
  - **Reviewer + security passes: the automated code-review subagent failed on a platform session-limit outage mid-task (not a finding — the identical class of tooling failure B-046's own session already hit and disclosed) — substituted with a direct manual review, disclosed here rather than glossed over.** That manual pass found and fixed one real bug, a direct analog of the exact issue B-039 already found and fixed for the MCP async path: `workflow/http.go`'s `HandleRun` originally passed `r.Context()` straight into `Executor.Run` — since this handler is synchronous (unlike MCP's `ServeMessages`, which returns immediately after a 202 and dispatches in a detached goroutine), the request context stays live for the whole run in the normal case, but a client disconnect mid-`Hold()` (reproduced live, organically, during this session's own testing via a killed `curl` client) cancels that context, and every subsequent DB write depending on it — including the final run-status `UPDATE` — then silently fails, leaving `workflow_runs.status` stuck at `'running'` forever. Fixed identically to B-039's own precedent: `context.WithoutCancel(r.Context())`. Security review (this session, a direct adversarial trace-through specifically aimed at the brief's own stated bar — "the same rigor as B-057's original adversarial proof"): confirmed the workflow runner's own pre-dispatch connector read and config-hash computation are purely informational and structurally cannot be substituted into or weaken `dispatch()`'s own completely independent enforcement (the runner never branches on its own read, only records it); confirmed cross-org scoping holds throughout `Resolve`/`HandleRun` (workflow resolution is always scoped to the authenticated agent's own resolved org via `registry.LookupByName`, never client input); zero exploitable findings.
  - **Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh both before and after the context-cancellation fix:** a real 2-step workflow (`Test` connector, allow → `manual_verify_tool` connector, matches a real pre-existing escalate policy) executed via a real agent JWT against `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{id}/run` — step 1 genuinely dispatched to a real external endpoint (`postman-echo.com`) carrying its own real static params (set via the new `PUT .../params` endpoint, round-tripped through a real `GET`); step 2 genuinely blocked the HTTP request on a real pending `approval_requests` row (confirmed mid-flight via a direct query). **The real TOCTOU attack, not simulated:** while step 2's approval sat pending, its connector's `base_url` was edited via a real `PATCH /v1/gateway/tools/{id}` call to an attacker-controlled-looking URL, then approved via the real `POST /v1/approvals/{id}/decide` — resume correctly refused (`resume_outcome='config_changed'`, step `outcome='blocked'` with a clear error message, run `status='failed'`), proving B-057's completely unmodified mechanism engages correctly for an escalation that originated from inside a workflow run, not just a standalone call. A second run, connector restored and no tampering, completed successfully end-to-end (`status='completed'`, both steps `outcome='allowed'`, step 2's real dispatch carrying its own distinct static params, visibly different from step 1's — confirming AC6 live as well as by test). **The context-cancellation bug reproduced and fixed live, before/after:** killed the triggering `curl` mid-hold, confirmed (pre-fix) the run stuck at `'running'` forever even after the approval was later decided; after the fix, repeated the identical kill-mid-hold scenario and confirmed the run correctly reached `'completed'` once decided, with the original client long gone. All gateway/API container logs confirmed clean of errors/panics throughout; shared dev database confirmed to contain only `Dev Org` before, during, and after this session's work.
  - **Dependencies:** B-058 (workflow definitions, read via a new gateway-side `Resolve`, never modified), B-057 (TOCTOU pinning mechanism — `ComputeConfigHash`, `dispatchApproved` — reused via the unmodified `dispatch()` closure, not reimplemented), B-039 (`Hold()` blocking mechanism, reused unmodified). **Out of scope, as specified:** output→input mapping, durable pause/resume for escalation, chain-aware approval UI (the approver sees the same single-step card as any other escalation today), any UI for triggering/viewing workflow runs (live verification used direct API calls throughout), branching/conditional logic.
- **B-063 (2026-08-13): Multi-Hop Workflows, Brief 3 — output→input mapping.** A later step's parameter can now be extracted from an EARLIER step's real recorded result within the same run (the Freshservice JSON-Parser-node pattern) — the piece that makes Thread B a real data pipeline, not a sequence of unconnected calls. Re-verified the MCP Discovery investigation's D9 finding directly against current schema before building: **zero new tables, zero new columns, zero new migration** — `workflow_steps.input_mapping JSONB` (B-058) and `workflow_run_steps.result JSONB` (B-059) already existed for exactly this purpose. Expression library: `github.com/tidwall/gjson` — pure read-only JSON-path querying, no eval/code-execution capability.
  - **CRITICAL FINDING, surfaced in the plan before any code was written, changed the file scope from what the brief itself assumed:** `workflow_steps.id` was NOT actually stable across a save — `eami-api/internal/api/workflows.go`'s `UpdateWorkflow` unconditionally deleted every step row and reinserted fresh ones (brand-new UUIDs, `input_mapping` never even passed through `InsertWorkflowStep`) whenever a request included a `steps` array, which the current UI does on every edit-panel submit, including an unrelated rename. Extraction wiring set up today would have been **wiped by the very next save**, not just misdirected by a reorder (the brief's own AC2 framing) — worse than anticipated, and not achievable within the brief's stated `MUST NOT MODIFY: B-058's core CRUD` boundary as literally written. **Presented to the user as a genuine scope-boundary fork rather than decided unilaterally** (same category as B-044/B-059's own presented forks) — approved: a minimal, additive touch preserving 100% of existing behavior for any caller that doesn't use the new field.
  - **`eami-gateway/internal/workflow`:** `types.go` — `ExtractionRef{FromStep, Path}`, `Step.InputMapping`. `resolve.go` — now also reads `input_mapping`. New `params.go` — `resolveParams(step, priorResults map[uuid.UUID]StepResult)` merges static params with extracted values; **extraction overwrites a static value for the same key — the documented, deliberate precedence rule**; a `from_step` absent from `priorResults` (covering "future step," "different workflow," and "different run" identically, since all three are simply absent from the map) or an unresolved `gjson` path both fail the WHOLE step cleanly, no partial substitution. `executor.go` — `Run` accumulates `priorResults` per-run, purely in-memory (never a DB read-back — the entire mechanism AC4's cross-run/cross-workflow isolation rests on); `resolveParams` runs after connector resolution and before dispatch, recording `outcome='blocked'` with a clear `error_detail` on failure exactly like the existing dangling-connector path — **dispatch is never called**.
  - **`eami-api`:** `WorkflowStepDTO` gained `ID`/`InputMapping` (new `ExtractionRefDTO`); `InsertWorkflowStep` takes a caller-assigned `id` (was DB `DEFAULT`) plus `inputMapping []byte`; new `resolveStepIDs` reuses a submitted step's id only if it matches a currently-existing step of THAT workflow (`ListWorkflowSteps` scoped by `workflow_id`, itself already org-verified via `GetWorkflow` earlier in the handler) — anything else mints fresh, identical to today's behavior for a caller that's never sent this field.
  - **A second real bug found live during this brief's own verification (not part of the originally approved plan, fixed the same session):** `workflow_step_params.workflow_step_id` has `ON DELETE CASCADE` — so even with step-id reuse working, the delete-then-reinsert still cascade-deleted a step's *static* params the instant its row was deleted, even though the same id got reinserted moments later. Reproduced live: an unrelated sibling-step edit silently zeroed a step's static params to `{}`. Fixed by capturing each reused step's current params (existing B-059 `GetWorkflowStepParams`) before the delete, re-writing them (existing B-059 `UpsertWorkflowStepParams`) inside the same transaction right after the reinsert — no new SQL.
  - **Reviewer + security passes, both mandatory, both clean.** Code review found and both got fixed: `go.mod` had `tidwall/gjson` mis-marked `// indirect` (`go mod tidy` not run, cosmetic); the same real step id echoed on two different submitted steps in one request would hit `workflow_steps`' primary-key uniqueness (23505, a code `isForeignKeyViolation` doesn't recognize) and leak a raw Postgres error as a 500 — fixed with an explicit duplicate-id check in `validateWorkflowSteps`. Security review, matching B-042's own rigor for the AC4 isolation claim: independently re-derived that `resolveParams` has zero DB calls anywhere in its path, that `priorResults` is a fresh function-local map per `Run()` call (no cross-run/cross-org aliasing possible), and that a step genuinely cannot reference itself or a not-yet-executed step (traced the exact ordering: a step's own `priorResults` entry is written only after that step's own `resolveParams` call already completed); independently re-traced `resolveStepIDs`' SQL scoping and confirmed airtight. Zero exploitable findings.
  - **Test coverage:** `eami-gateway/internal/workflow/params_test.go` (7 pure unit tests: static-only unaffected, nested-path extraction, unresolved-reference failure covering future-step/cross-workflow/cross-run identically, non-"allowed" prior step, path-not-found, precedence, no param-map aliasing). `mapping_test.go` (3 real-Postgres end-to-end tests: a real extracted value genuinely received by a real downstream call (AC1); a real path-not-found failure confirmed `outcome='blocked'` at the DB level with the downstream adapter never called (AC3); a REAL second workflow's real step id substituted as `from_step`, confirmed to never resolve (AC4, real data)). `eami-api/internal/api/workflows_test.go` gained 5 tests: `id` survives a pure reorder (AC2); `input_mapping` survives an unrelated field edit; an echoed id from a *different* workflow is never reused (real HTTP round trip); the duplicate-echoed-id regression guard; static params survive a reused-step-id sibling edit (the second bug's regression guard). **Verified 2026-08-13 with a real toolchain: `go build`/`go vet`/`go test ./... -count=1` clean across the full `eami-gateway` and `eami-api` modules.**
  - **Live-verified end-to-end against the real `docker-compose` stack, rebuilt fresh (multiple times, as review/verification findings were fixed):** a real 2-step workflow (`b046-live-verify`/`echo_post`, real `postman-echo.com` dispatch) with step 1's real static `lookup_key` param and step 2's real `input_mapping` extracting it — run via a real agent JWT against `POST /v1/gateway/workflows/{id}/run`: step 2's real downstream HTTP request genuinely carried the exact value extracted from step 1's real response, traced through the real `workflow_run_steps.result` column directly via `psql`. Separately confirmed the params-carry-forward fix live (step 1's params intact after an unrelated step-2 edit) and AC3 live (a broken path failed the run cleanly, `outcome='blocked'`, a precise error naming the exact param/path/step). Test workflow deleted afterward; shared dev DB confirmed unaffected.
  - **Dependencies:** B-058 (`input_mapping` column, extended not modified), B-059 (execution engine/`workflow_step_params`, both reused), the MCP Discovery investigation (D9). **Out of scope, as specified:** any UI for defining extraction expressions (a real variable-picker UX is a separate, later polish brief — this brief is the backend mechanism only); branching/conditional logic; the visual canvas redesign.
